# MONTH OF APRIL

*April 1. Our Venerable Mother Mary of Egypt:*

*On the 1st day: our venerable mother Mary of Egypt.*

*At "Lord, I have cried," stichera of the Venerable One, Tone 6, Podoben: "Having laid aside all earthly hope" (Всѐ ѿло́жше):*

The defilement of defilements previously contracted hindered thee from beholding the venerable things; yet thy feeling and thy God-wise conscience concerning the deeds done wrought in thee a turning unto better things. For looking upon the icon of the blessed Maiden of God, having repented of all thy former transgressions, O all-praised one, with boldness thou didst venerate the precious Wood.

Having joyfully venerated the holy places, thou didst receive from thence a most saving viaticum of virtue. And exceedingly didst thou speed upon a good journey, having crossed the pathway of the Jordan; and in the dwelling-place of the Forerunner thou didst fervently take up thine abode, and by thy way of life didst darken the fierceness of the passions, thinning out the carnal strivings of the body by abstinence, O ever-memorable mother.

Having settled in the wilderness, thou didst piously blot out the images of thy passions, having inscribed in thy soul that most Godlike depiction: the forms of virtues. And so greatly didst thou shine forth, that even upon the waters thou didst walk lightly with thy footsteps, and didst rise above the earth in thy supplications unto God. And now with boldness, O glorious Mary, standing before Christ, pray for our souls.

*Glory... Tone 2. Idiomelon:*

The snares of the soul and the passions of the flesh thou didst cut down with the sword of abstinence, and the mental sins of thought thou didst choke by the silence of fasting, and with the streams of thy tears thou didst water all the wilderness, and didst make to sprout forth for us the fruits of repentance. Therefore, O venerable one, we celebrate thy memory.

*Both now... Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion:*

Beholding Thee nailed unto the Wood of the Cross, O Jesus, she who knew not wedlock weeping said: O sweet Child, why hast Thou left me alone who bare Thee, O unapproachable Light of the Father Who is before all beginning? Make haste and be glorified, that they who glorify Thy divine passions may receive divine glory.

*Troparion, Tone 8:*

In thee, O mother, was that which is according to the image saved with certainty: for having taken up the Cross, thou didst follow Christ, and by thine actions didst teach us to despise the flesh, for it passes away, but to care for the soul, a thing immortal. Therefore, O venerable Mary, thy spirit also rejoices with the Angels.

*Canon, Tone 4.*  
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*

Having traversed the deep of the Red Sea on foot with dryshod steps of old, Israel by the hands of Moses stretched out crosswise conquered the power of Amalek in the wilderness.

*Troparia:*

Cleanse the defilement of transgressions from my humble soul by Thy mercy, O Christ; drive away the mist and darkening of passions, by the prayers of Thy venerable one.

Having defiled the nobility of the soul by carnal passions, thou didst again cleanse it by abstinence; thou didst enlighten thy mind, having made thy soul radiant by the torrents of thy tears.

Thou didst flee from the Egypt of passions as from a sinful spring, and being delivered from the cruel defilement of Pharaoh, thou hast now inherited the land of dispassion, and dost choir with the Angels unceasingly.

*Theotokion:*

Beholding thine icon, O Sovereign Lady, pure Birthgiver of God, and the Word Who was born of thy most pure virginal womb, the glorious one fervently entreats thee to be a guarantor unto this.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*

Thy Church makes merry in Thee, O Christ, crying aloud: Thou art my strength, O Lord, and my refuge and my firm support.

*Troparia:*

Festering and putrefied were the wounds of thy soul, but with the fountain of thy tears thou didst fervently wash them away.

The demonic hosts were vanquished by thee, and the wanton strivings of the passions thou didst wound with tears.

As a morning cloud and as a dropping dewdrop didst thou become, pouring forth the waters of saving repentance unto all.

*Theotokion:*

Having thee as protectress, O pure one, and as salvation and strength, the venerable one venerated the precious Wood of the Holy Cross.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "Guiding unto wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*

Having bridled all carnal wantonness by fasting labors, thou didst display the manly mindset of thy soul; for longing to behold the form of the Cross, O ever-memorable one, thou didst crucify thyself unto the world, whence also unto zeal for an blameless life thou didst fervently raise thyself up, O all-blessed and glorious Mary. Pray unto Christ God to grant remission of transgressions unto those who honor thy holy memory with love.

*Theotokion:*

As a Virgin and alone among women, who without seed didst bear God in the flesh, all generations of mankind call thee blessed: for the fire of the Godhead took up His abode in thee, and as an infant thou dost nourish with milk the Creator and Lord. Therefore, the angelic and human race, we worthily glorify thy most holy Nativity,[^5] and with one voice cry aloud unto thee: Pray unto Christ God to grant remission of sins unto us who venerate thy most holy childbirth with faith.

*Stavrotheotokion:*

The ewe-lamb, beholding the Lamb, Shepherd, and Deliverer upon the Cross, wept aloud, and bitterly lamenting cried out: The world indeed rejoices, receiving deliverance through Thee, but my womb burns, beholding Thy Crucifixion, which Thou endurest for the tender mercy of Thy lovingkindness, O longsuffering Lord. O Mother, abyss and inexhaustible spring, take pity and grant remission of transgressions unto those who hymn Thy divine passions with faith.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*

Beholding Thee lifted up upon the Cross, O Sun of righteousness, the Church stood in her station, worthily crying aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparia:*

Thou didst withdraw, fleeing from those in the world and from all sweet things; and alone thou didst purely unite thyself unto the One alone, by extreme abstinence and the endurance of thy deeds.

The movements and burnings of the flesh thou didst truly dry up by abstinence; whence thou didst adorn thy soul, O all-glorious Mary, with divine visions and actions.

By thy virtuous power, with tears and extreme fasting and with prayer and heat, with frost and nakedness, thou didst become a precious receptacle of the Holy Spirit.

*Theotokion:*

Having fled unto thine icon, and having in paradise quired with Him Who was born of thee, O Virgin Mary, through thee she hath now found immortal life.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*

Thou, my Lord, hast come as a light into the world, a holy light turning those who hymn Thee with faith out of gloomy ignorance.

*Troparia:*

Thou didst follow after Christ rejoicing, bearing thy cross upon thy shoulder, O Mary, and thou didst wound the demons.

Thou hast shown us the medicine of repentance; thou hast shown also the path leading again unto unperishing life.

Be thou unto me an invincible and honorable protectress, and deliver me from passions and every disease by thy supplications unto the Lord.

*Theotokion:*

Looking upon thine icon, O pure Sovereign Lady, and ever praying unto thee, the venerable one put to shame the assaults of passions.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*

I will sacrifice unto Thee with the voice of praise, O Lord, the Church cries aloud unto Thee, having been cleansed from the demonic blood by the blood that flowed for mercy's sake from Thy side.

*Troparia:*

With sweat thou didst wash away the defilement of sin, and gazing with thy mind toward uncorrupt glory, thou hast now found good fruit for thy labors, O glorious one.

Thy life was shown forth as an example unto all sinners, O Mary, that those who have sinned immeasurably in life may arise and cleanse their defilement with tears.

Show compassion upon my humble soul, O Lover of mankind, which I have defiled by unclean deeds of the desires of my flesh; but do Thou have mercy upon me, O God, by the prayers of the venerable one.

With all thy soul and heart thou didst love the living and incarnate God the Word born of the Virgin, who brought a voice unto thee, O venerable one.

*Kontakion, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who wast lifted up upon the Cross" (Вознесы́йся):*

Having fled the mist of sin, and having illuminated thy heart with the light of repentance, O glorious one, thou didst come unto Christ, and didst bring His all-immaculate and holy Mother as a merciful intercessor. Whence also thou hast found remission of transgressions, and dost ever rejoice with the Angels.

*Ikos:*

The serpent that of old wrought evil in Eden against Eve through the deception of the tree, thou didst cast down into the pit by the wood of the Cross, O glorious Mary: And having fled pleasures, thou didst desire purity; whence also thou wast accounted worthy to enter within the bridal chamber of thy Master with the virgins, worthily to delight with them. Fervently pray unto Him, therefore, that He may grant the remission of many sins, and vouchsafe us His life, to rejoice ever with the Angels.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*

In the Persian furnace, the Abrahamic youths, burning with love of piety rather than with flame, cried aloud: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!

*Troparia:*

Having trodden the sorrowful and narrow path, O virgin,[^6] and having made thy soul radiant with the beauty of virtues, thou didst attain unto heavenly, endless life, where Christ is the unending Light.

Having trampled upon all temporal things in the world, thou dost now choir with all the angelic hosts, singing: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!

The snares and weapons of the enemy were all brought to naught by thy strong fasting, and thy prayer, O venerable one, and thy tears. And now also the assault of passions is driven away, O venerable Mary.

*Theotokion:*

Having truly brought forth God without wedlock, Who is bodiless, and having remained truly a Virgin: by thy power, O all-pure one, thou hast driven away the armies of passions and demons.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*

Daniel, having stretched forth his hands, stopped the gaping mouths of lions in the pit; and the Children, lovers of piety, girded with virtue, quenched the power of fire, crying aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparia:*

Having illuminated all thy mind with the radiance of virtues, O glorious Mary, knowing God; for having thinned out the flesh by much fasting, with pious thought thou didst sing rejoicing: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

Having protected thyself with the sign of the Cross, with thy dryshod feet thou didst cross the Jordan waters faithfully, O Mary; and having partaken of the Body and Blood of the eternal Christ, thou didst say: Now lettest Thou Thy handmaid depart![^7]

The divine priest Zosimas, the initiate of grace, when he saw thee, O glorious one, having crossed the rushing current of the Jordan with dryshod feet, being held with fear and trembling, rejoicing sang: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Theotokion:*

Through thee, O all-undefiled Sovereign Lady, she shook off all corruption and defilement, and was clothed in the garment of immortality; and through thee the venerable one cried aloud unto thy Son: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*

A cornerstone uncut by hand was cut out from thee, O Virgin, from that unhewn mountain: even Christ, Who joined together the parted natures; therefore rejoicing, we magnify thee, O Theotokos.

*Troparia:*

Now art thou truly filled with incorruptible and divine food, and dost delight in the intelligible and never-setting light in the heavenly habitations, where the angelic ranks pray unto God for us.

Having loathed fleeting and corruptible glory, O Mary, thou hast inherited blessed glory and life; pray unto Christ ever for those who celebrate thy most holy memory.

Behold my sorrow, O venerable one, and the groaning of my heart; behold the narrowness of my life. Save me from my sin, and show compassion upon my soul, by thine intercessions unto the Lord.

*Theotokion:*

O pure Sovereign Lady Theotokos, salvation of sinners: accept this supplication, and deliver me from my sins as I flee unto thy Son, by the prayers of thy venerable one.

*Exapostilarion, Podoben: "O Unvarying Light" (Свѣ́те неизмѣ́нный):*

An example of repentance was given unto us, O Mary; for by thy fervent compunction thou didst reverse defeat, having acquired the Mother of God as protectress: with whom do thou pray for us.

*Theotokion:*

The Light that shone timelessly from the Father before the ages, in time hath now in the end appeared from thee, O Virgin, for the salvation of the world: unto Whom cease not to pray for thy people.

*[Typographical Ornament]*  
*April 2. Our Venerable Father and Wonderworker Titus:*

*On the same month, on the 2nd day: our venerable father and wonderworker Titus.*[^8]

*At "Lord, I have cried," stichera, Tone 8, Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder" (Ѡ пресла́внагѡ чꋋдесѐ):*

O God-wise Titus our father, bearing thy cross, thou didst follow Christ, and didst subdue all passions of the soul. Whence thou didst receive grace from on high, to heal the passions of those who run unto thee, to calm infirmities, and to drive away spirits; therefore we celebrate and call blessed thy memory.

O all-blessed Titus our father, being sacredly instructed by monastic conduct, and enlightening thy mind by divine partakings, thou didst truly receive the most holy chrism of priesthood by the bidding of the Divine Spirit; and thou didst gallantly serve God and thy Creator upon earth, even as an Angel.

O venerable Titus, inspired by God, having illuminated thy soul with the light of Orthodoxy, thou didst diminish the dark mist of heresy. And having shone forth as a most radiant star, thou dost enlighten the ends of the world with thy radiant miracles always. Therefore, celebrating, we feast thy memory.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*

Unclean are my thoughts and deceitful are my lips, and all my deeds are defiled; and what shall I do? How shall I meet the Judge? O Sovereign Lady Virgin, pray unto thy Son and Creator and Lord, that He may receive my spirit in repentance, as He alone is compassionate.

*Stavrotheotokion:*

The undefiled heifer, seeing the young bull willingly nailed upon the Tree, weeping piteously cried aloud: Woe is me, O most beloved Child! What hath the ungrateful Hebrew gathering rendered unto Thee, desiring to make me childless of Thee, O dearly beloved?

*Canon, whose acrostic is: Let Titus the pillar of monks be praised.[^9] Tone 8.*  
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*

Having traversed the water as dry land, and having escaped the Egyptian evil, the Israelite cried aloud: Let us sing unto our Deliverer and God!

*Troparia:*

Since I have been grievously wounded by carnal passions and corrupted by pleasures: O Titus, who art a treasure of dispassion, save me by thy prayers.

As a divine chosen vessel of the Spirit, thou didst offer thyself unto God from thy mother's swaddling-bands, and didst become an initiate and servant of the unwedded Birthgiver of God.

Having shone forth as the sweetness of abstinence, thou didst cast away the bitterness of passions; whence, O father, sweeter than honey and the honeycomb dost thou delight our souls.

As a disciple of the new Paul do we praise thee, O Titus: for thou wast shown forth as a zealous emulator of his struggles, O God-blessed one, and with him hast received crowns.

*Theotokion:*

The timeless Word of the Father, without a husband thou didst bear, O sole Mother of God, as thou art higher than all creation.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*

Thou art the firm support of those who run unto Thee, O Lord; Thou art the light of the darkened, and my spirit hymns Thee.

*Troparia:*

As an ointment of sanctification compounded from fasting spices, O venerable one, thou didst assemble thyself as an odor of sweet fragrance unto God.

Having abstinence as a weapon and prayer as a shield ever, O venerable one, as a reprover of the powers of darkness thou didst put them to shame.

Meditating in the laws of fasting, thou didst drown the passions like another host of Pharaoh's men by the torrent of thy tears.

*Theotokion:*

I ever beseech thee, O Sovereign Lady, that by thy prayers He Who was born of thee may be merciful unto me in the Day of Judgment.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 1, Podoben: "The Angelic Choir" (Ли́къ а́гг҃льскій):*

Having fasted upon earth as one without a body, O Titus, thou didst slay the enemy, and together didst cast down the assembly of heresies by thy Orthodox teachings. Therefore today, celebrating thy most holy memory, we joyfully hymn Him Who hath magnified thee.

*Theotokion:*

O hope of Christians, most holy Virgin, Whom thou didst bear as God beyond mind and word: cease not to pray together with the heavenly powers to grant remission of all our sins and amendment of life unto those who ever glorify thee with faith and love.

*Stavrotheotokion:*

The undefiled ewe-lamb, beholding the Lamb and Shepherd hung dead upon the Tree, weeping said, crying aloud as a mother: How shall I endure Thy descent which is beyond word, O my Son, and Thy voluntary passions, O most good God?

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*

I have heard, O Lord, the mystery of Thy dispensation; I have understood Thy works, and I have glorified Thy Godhead.

*Troparia:*

All the festering putrefaction of sin thou didst burn away, O blessed one, and with the arrow of abstinence thou didst wound the demonic hosts.

Having wounded thy soul with the desire for purity, thou didst fervently follow Christ, and didst enter into the chamber of glory, O all-blessed one.

Like a most fragrant garden of flowers and an animate paradise of virtues, thou didst blossom by abstinence, wherewith thou dost nourish all who honor thee.

Having acquired thee as a lawgiver of abstinence and a guileless image of meekness, as a true Moses and David, we call thee blessed, O father.

Thee Who art higher than the Cherubim and Seraphim do we hymn, O all-undefiled one; for the Master before Whom all things tremble, thou didst bear in the flesh in thine arms.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*

Why hast Thou cast me away from Thy face, O Never-setting Light, and an alien darkness hath covered me, the wretched one? But turn me back, and direct my paths toward the light of Thy commandments, I pray Thee.

*Troparia:*

Having quenched all passions by the dew of thy ascetic labors, O blessed Titus, richly kindled by the divine fire of love and faith, thou hast become a lamp of abstinence, and the enlightenment of dispassion even today.

As a cluster of grapes, by divine cultivation, O father, thou didst rear thyself, and didst cast thyself into the winepress, and didst press thyself out by the sufferings of abstinence; and having filled the cup of spiritual fasting, thou dost gladden the hearts of thy flock.

Having valiantly endured the assaults and wounds of the invisible enemies, thou wast shown forth as a pillar of patience, establishing thy flock with the divine staff, nurturing it upon pastures of abstinence and beside the water, O blessed one.

*Theotokion:*

Having thee as a haven, a wall, and a firm support, and as our protectress, and an unashamed entrance unto the Kingdom: we pray that we may not fall away from our hope, by thy maternal supplications, O all-undefiled one.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*

Cleanse me, O Savior, for many are mine iniquities, and lead me up from the depth of evils, I pray Thee; for unto Thee have I cried, and do Thou hear me, O God of my salvation.

*Troparia:*

Having dispelled the mist of passions and the gloomy darkness, the day of the Orient and the light upon thy paths, thou didst shine forth, O venerable one, from many ascetic struggles and afflictions endured for God's sake.

Thou wast wholly enraptured unto God by the desire for abstinence, having forsaken the world and the ruler of this world; thou didst seize the delight of the transcendent Kingdom, O God-receiver.

Thou didst gather into thy soul the divine wealth of the Spirit: blameless prayer, chastity of purity, prolonged vigilance, and the labors of abstinence, whereby thou wast known as a house of God.

Thou didst wisely pass beyond the baseness of earthly matter, and by immaterial prayer thou didst give wings to thy mind, and wast shown forth as an heir of the adoption on high through the perfection of thy life.

*Theotokion:*

Truly thou didst conceive the Divine Word in thy womb, and didst supernaturally bring Him forth, O all-undefiled one; make Him merciful by thy prayers, to deliver all thy servants from perils.

*Kontakion, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Ꙗ҆ви́лсѧ є҆сѝ):*

Having forsaken the turmoil of life, and having quietly lived out all thy life in wisdom, thou didst pass over unto God, O wonderworker, our father, venerable Titus.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*

The Children who came from Judea in Babylon of old, by faith in the Trinity trampled down the flame of the furnace, singing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparia:*

Thou wast not defiled by sins, but wast shown unto us as a river of abstinence, O father, drowning the passions and cleansing defilement from those who cry in faith: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

Now conversing with the bodiless choirs, O blessed of God, and continually enjoying the radiance of the banquet on high, show compassion upon those who sing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

In the confession of the faith, O father, thou didst remain unshaken; for depicting Christ in the flesh and bowing down before Him depicted, thou didst cry: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

Bearing the wounds of the Master upon thy body, O father, do thou also heal me who have been wounded by the arrow of Beliar, as I cry: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*

Beyond nature and word thou didst bear the Master of all in thy womb, and thou dost nourish from thy breasts Him Who prepares a table in due season for all: Whom do thou earnestly pray for us, O pure one.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*

The King of heaven, Whom the angelic hosts hymn: praise and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparia:*

Having acquired thee truly as an animate pillar and an image of abstinence, O father, we all honor thy memory as is due.

The choir of monks rejoices, and the assembly of the venerable and the righteous dances for joy; for worthily hast thou received a crown with them.

Adorned with virtues, thou hast reclined in the chamber of ineffable glory, singing a song unto Christ unto all ages.

Clothed in sacred vesture and offering unbloody sacrifices, thou didst offer thyself as a sacrifice unto God forever.

*Theotokion:*

Having conceived in thy womb Him Who was before thee, O Mother of God, pure Virgin, thou didst ineffably bear God and King.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*

Let us, O people, gloriously honor the pure Mother of God, who received the fire of the Godhead in her womb unconsumed; with hymns let us magnify her.

*Troparia:*

Thou wast shown forth from God as a physician of those ailing in passions, and as a destroyer and expeller of the cunning demons, O blessed one; therefore do we call thee blessed.

Thou didst forsake the earth as an abode of corruption, O father, and didst settle in the land of the meek, and with them dost rejoice, delighting in divine sweetness.

Having thy struggles as a blameless rule of faith and an image of abstinence, we continually magnify the Creator in hymns.

Today is a feast day, for Titus convenes the flocks of monks unto spiritual rejoicing and banquet, and unto the food of incorruptible life.

*Theotokion:*

He Who made His abode in thee, O all-undefiled one, was born, casting down the manslayer who of old maliciously tripped our first parents, and He hath saved us all.

*[Typographical Ornament]*  
*On the same month, on the 3rd day: our venerable father Nicetas the Confessor, Hegumen of the Medikion Monastery:*[^10]

*At "Lord, I have cried," stichera, Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Е҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва):*

Guiding souls by the word, thou wast shown forth as a divine builder and a faithful mystagogue, sowing good and saving seeds, and reaping abundant fruit, O God-bearer, offering this with joy unto thy Master; before Whom as thou now standest, O blessed one, remember this thy flock which ever honors thee, O God-inspired one.

Having been meek and simple, thou wast shown forth as a warrior through zeal for Orthodoxy; for girded with faith as with breastplates, and with abstinence as with a spear, O God-wise Nicetas, thou didst refute every blasphemous heresy, venerating the divine icon of the Savior and serving, O God-bearer, clearly following the patristic statutes.[^11]

When in bitter exiles and in most dark places the most dark tormentor shut thee up with bestial cruelty, bearing the paradise-dwelling in thy mind, rejoicing in spirit, O father, thou didst endure; whose goodly beauty thou hast now truly been accounted worthy to behold, O blessed one, receiving the recompense of thy sufferings.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*

Thou didst contain the uncontainable God strangely, roomily without confinement in thy womb, Who became man out of goodness, O all-pure Bride of God. Therefore I pray thee, deliver me from the passions that constrain me, that walking by the narrow and straight path, I may attain unto that which leads unto life, O Virgin.

*Stavrotheotokion:*

When the undefiled ewe-lamb beheld her Lamb willingly drawn to the slaughter as a man, weeping she said: Dost Thou now make me childless who bore Thee, O Christ? What is this that Thou hast done, O Deliverer of all? Yet do I hymn and glorify Thine utter goodness that is beyond mind and word, O Lover of mankind.

*Canon, whose acrostic, without the Theotokia, is: Thy life, all-radiant with purity, O father.*[^12] *Tone 2.*  
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*

The supremely armed power of old laid low the entire army of Pharaoh in the deep; and the incarnate Word destroyed all-malicious sin, the most glorious Lord, for gloriously hath He been glorified.

*Troparia:*

Illumined by the light of divine radiance, O father, thou didst sanctify thy life unto God, having chosen from childhood the light-bearing brightness of virginity, whereby thou wast likened unto the purity of the bodiless ones.

Anointed by a divine teacher and by the honorable Spirit in Godlike manner, thou didst converse purely in the pattern of monks, and didst abide illuminated by a most radiant dawn, O God-bearer, having acquired a most pure life.

Strengthened by the power of the Cross, thou didst wither the carnal passions, and didst enlighten thy mind, O all-wise Nicetas, by diligence in spiritual teachings, bearing the unstolen wealth of divine contemplation as fruit.

Having blossomed with chastity and purity, O blessed one, thou wast elevated to sacred leadership; for thou wast adorned with the most radiant vesture of priesthood, being known, O venerable one, as a dispenser of the divine mysteries O venerable one, as a dispenser of the divine mysteries.

*Theotokion:*

Thou wast shown forth as higher than every visible and invisible creation, O pure Ever-Virgin; for thou didst bear the Creator, as He was pleased to be incarnate in thy womb: Whom do thou pray with boldness, to save those who hymn thee.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*

The wilderness hath blossomed like a lily, O Lord: the barren church of the Gentiles, by Thy coming, wherein my heart is established.

*Troparia:*

Having acquired a heavenly citizenship, O venerable one, light-like and radiant, O Nicetas, thou hast become a lamp of piety, adorned with many gifts.

Having put to death the mind of thy flesh, O venerable one, by virtue thou didst divinely quicken thy soul unto the divine, and wast accounted worthy of incorruptible life.

Rejoicing, thou didst pass through thy life, O glorious one, brimming with virtues, and rich in fair gifts, wherein thy heart was established.

Thou didst shine forth like the sun unto the ranks of monks, adorned with virtues, O God-wise Nicetas, venerable one; therefore rejoicing do we call thee blessed.

*Theotokion:*

Him Who enlightens the ends of the earth didst thou bear in the flesh, O all-pure one, Who is uncreated and before all co-unoriginate with the Father; therefore in faith do we honor thee as the Mother of God.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Speedily anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ):*

Thou didst dwell in the mountains of stillness, O wise one, and wast clearly reared in the citadels of abstinence; having been exalted from both, thou didst forsake earthly pleasures,[^13] and didst attain unto heavenly life, settling in the habitation wherein thou prayest unto God for us.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*

As the all-undefiled one who didst bear the pre-eternal God, unceasingly pray Him together with the bodiless hosts, to grant remission of transgressions and amendment of life unto us before the end, who sing unto thee with faith and love as is due, O only all-hymned one.

*Stavrotheotokion:*

As the all-blameless Mother of Christ God, a sword pierced through thy most holy soul when thou didst behold thy Son and God willingly crucified; Whom do thou not cease to pray, O blessed one, to grant us forgiveness of transgressions.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*

Thou hast come from the Virgin, not an envoy nor an Angel, but Thyself incarnate, O Lord, and hast saved me, the whole man the whole man; therefore I cry unto Thee: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparia:*

Shown forth by purity of soul as an uprooter of passions, O God-bearer, thou didst eradicate all soul-corrupting delusion of the fighters against God, O most honorable sacred mystagogue.

Having received the crown of victory, O most rich one, thou dost now rest in the heavenly habitations, O blessed one, having shaken off the passions and clothed thyself in dispassion, O father.

Thou didst offer thy life unto Christ as a blameless sacrifice, O father, filled with divine love, and adorned with the beauty of fasting, O most blessed one.

Thou didst clearly refute the doctrines of soul-destroying heresy, O God-wise one, honoring the divine icon of Christ, and of the Mother of God, and of all the saints, O most sacred one.

*Theotokion:*

As a boast, as the dominion and firm foundation of the world, as a tranquil haven, and as the cause of joy, do we glorify thee, O all-pure one, all of us adorning ourselves in thy glory.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*

Thou hast become a Mediator between God and men, O Christ God; for through Thee, O Master, we have access from the night of ignorance unto Thy Father, the Prince of Light.

*Troparia:*

As a meek one, thou didst settle in the land of the meek, O wise Nicetas, having been in truth a champion, O ever-memorable one, brightly adorned with the crown of confession.

Thou hast now passed over unto the divine Light, O venerable one, having been a beloved son of light; rejoice gladly in joy and beauty among the angelic choirs.

Thou hast now become a partaker of the ineffable sweetness of paradise; for thou wast shown forth as a most tranquil haven for the storm-tossed, and didst become a nourisher of the hungry, O blessed one.

*Theotokion:*

Remembering thy generations, we now call thee blessed; for through thee, O all-blameless one, have we truly received ineffable blessedness and unending life.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*

Wallowing in the abyss of sin, I call upon the unsearchable abyss of Thy tender mercy: lead me up from corruption, O God!

*Troparia:*

Thou hast obtained the blessed hopes, O all-blessed father, having become an heir of eternal blessedness and of divine illumination.

Possessing the twofold light-bearing lamps of thy confession and thy fasting, thou hast found room in the heavens, O God-bearer Nicetas, most rich in gifts most rich in gifts. Enjoying endless and divine sweetness in the heavenly tabernacles now, O father, pray unto the Master and Lord of all for us.

*Theotokion:*

Now I flee unto thee, O all-pure one; save me by thy supplications and preserve me; for whatsoever thou desirest thou canst do, as the Mother of the Almighty.

*Kontakion, Tone 2, Podoben: "Seeking the highest" (Вы́шнихъ и҆щѧ̀):*

Having acquired a heavenly mind and life, thou shinest brightly like the sun with the radiance of deeds, enlightening those who are in worldly darkness, O father Nicetas; and leading all unto God, pray unceasingly for us all.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*

The God-opposing command of the lawless tyrant raised a high flame; but Christ spread out spiritual dew for the pious Children, He Who is blessed and supremely glorified.

*Troparia:*

Thy meekness greatly humbled the cruelty of the tormentor into the dust, slaying him by frequent supplications; for the Lord knows how to do the will of those who fear Him.

The thunder of thy doctrines and the light-bearing lightning of thy life made the world clear, illuminating the faithful with God-spoken radiances, both of words and of deeds, O sacred mystagogue, blessed of God.

Invigorated by the strength of the Comforter, thou didst escape the demonic hosts and heretical snares, O God-wise one; and thou hast ascended unto heavenly heights, now dwelling together with the choirs of the bodiless ones.

*Theotokion:*

Thou wast a sacred temple of Him Who created all things by His will, O Virgin; for thou didst contain Him Who is uncontainable by all, Who renews the world; therefore we proclaim thee as the Mother of God.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*

The fiery furnace in Babylon of old divided its action by divine command, consuming the Chaldeans, but bedewing the faithful who sang: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparia:*

Having in every way surpassed human nature, O thrice-blessed one, thou hast now drawn near unto the intelligible Light, O all-honorable one, accounted worthy of vision of God and filled with illumination, crying: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

Clearly clothed in the mortification of passions, thou didst go forth unto life-bearing rejoicing, O all-blessed one, inflamed with piety and irradiated with virtues, crying: Supremely exalt the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

Having learned every virtue from infancy, O God-wise one, at the conclusion of Thy struggles thou wast shown forth adorned with the crown of grace, and didst receive the prize, singing: Supremely exalt the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

From on high dost thou now look down upon us, O father, standing before thy Master, named after victory,[^14] having much boldness, and asking spiritual salvation for thy flock, O glorious and sacredly revealed one.

*Theotokion:*

Thou didst conceive without seed Him Who is inseparable from the Father, Who dwelt in thy womb in God-manly manner, and didst ineffably give birth, O all-pure Mother of God; therefore we know thee as the salvation of us all.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*

The Son of the unoriginate Begetter, God and Lord, incarnate of the Virgin hath appeared unto us, to enlighten the darkened, to gather the dispersed; therefore the all-hymned Mother of God do we magnify.

*Troparia:*

As pure thou wast accounted worthy of divine radiance, shining with the forms of virtues, and visibly adorned with the crown of confession; therefore all of us call thy festive festival blessed.

Prudently didst thou accomplish thy life, O blessed one, guided by the commandments of the Savior; and now that the shadow hath passed away, most joyfully hast thou worthily obtained eternal life, O most sacred one, blessed of God.

Thou didst swiftly pass beyond the turmoil of fleeting things, and rejoicing didst hasten unto the tranquil haven of Christ's Kingdom, O Nicetas; whereunto do thou guide us, O father, who call thee blessed and honor thy memory.

*Theotokion:*

The tabernacle of testimony prefigured thee, wherein were the tablets, the jar, and the golden ark; for as that tabernacle held those things, so also didst thou contain in thy womb the unoriginate Word being incarnate, O Mother of God.

*[Typographical Ornament]*  
*On the same month, on the 4th day: our venerable fathers Joseph the Hymnographer, and George of Maleon:*[^15]

*Stichera, Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Е҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва):*

Thou didst take up the Cross upon thy shoulders, and wast crucified unto the world, and didst follow the footsteps of Him Who created all things out of non-being, even as the Word of God said; and having shaken off worldly love and all attachment, from thence thou didst also blossom with virtues, and by divine deeds didst receive the eternal inheritance, O father.

Thou wast shown forth as a mystical trumpet, rousing all unto spiritual song; and as a God-moved cithara, praising the divine ranks, glorifying all the saints, and proclaiming their victories; for from the saving fountains thou didst draw an abyss of words, watering the house of God.

Thou wast shown forth as a treasury of virtues; inflamed with love, thou didst seek out all those who were well-pleasing unto God in life, celebrating them with sacred hymns, and emulating their divine achievements in faith: having become a partaker with them in a divine manner, thou wast also shown forth as a co-partaker of their delight.

*Other Stichera, of Saint George, Tone 4, Podoben: "Of the heavenly ranks" (Небе́сныхъ чинѡ́въ):*

By abstinence, O father George, thou didst subject the passions unto reason, having become a fair vessel of dispassion, O most blessed one, receptive to life-giving radiances, and an expeller of demons, and an admirable physician of passions.

The pure alabaster jar of the Divine Spirit, the fountain of wonders, the pillar of monks, meek and an imitator of the meek Christ, an earthly Angel and a heavenly man: let George be praised today!

Directing the ship of thy soul unto the tranquil haven, fleeing from the tumults of life as from the waves of the sea, by aspirations toward God thou didst fly up from the earth, and wast shown forth in the flesh like unto the Angels.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*

As thou art the help of helpless men, as thou art the hope of all the hopeless, and the resolver of dire things, O Mother of God: be thou a helper unto me be thou a helper unto me, delivering me from sins and perils as thou art merciful, O all-pure one.

*Stavrotheotokion:*

Standing by the Cross of thy Son and God, and beholding His long-suffering, weeping the pure Mother said: Woe is me, O sweetest Child! What are these things that Thou sufferest unjustly, O Word of God? Even that Thou mightest save mankind.

*Canon of Saint Joseph, Tone 2.*  
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*

Come, ye peoples, let us sing a hymn unto Christ God, Who divided the sea and guided the people whom He led forth from the bondage of Egypt; for He hath been glorified.

*Troparia:*

In song let us honor the hymnographer, singing his honey-flowing hymns, even Joseph, who from songs hath passed over into eternal songs.

Even as the preaching of the Prophets and Apostles traversed the whole earth, so also the singing of thy lips raises the world unto the glory of the God of all.

Having dedicated thyself from infancy unto Christ, O Joseph, thou didst live ascetically; therefore also He enriched thee with divine gifts: with hope and love, and with pure faith.

*Theotokion:*

The divine word of the Prophets of old proclaimed thee as the gate of God and as the holy mountain, O Virgin Mother of God, adornment of the divine passion-bearers.

*Canon of Saint George, whose acrostic is: I fervently sing thy sufferings, O father.*[^16] *Tone 2. Same Heirmos.*  
*Ode 1. Troparia:*

Enlightened by the thrice-radiant God-originate brightness, O venerable one, thou didst forsake the world and the mists of passions, and wast adorned with a most radiant life, O father.

Guided by divine Providence, O George, thou didst tread the path leading unto heaven, having acquired the only Benefactor and all-bountiful One as thy helper.

Visibly directed by God, O blessed one, and enriched with the grace of adoption, thou didst accomplish thy citizenship in venerable and righteous manner, O father.

*Theotokion:*

Enriched by thy firm support and protection, confessing thee as the Mother of God, O all-pure one: from the tempest of life are we saved, O all-undefiled Virgin.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*

Establish us in Thee, O Lord, Who by the Tree didst put sin to death, and plant Thy fear in the hearts of us who hymn Thee.

*Ode 3. Troparia:*

Through love of purity thou didst implore God to grant helpers; and having received thine undefiled fathers, thou didst obtain thy hope, O father.

Thou didst show forth the knowledge of true love, and house, and action, and wisdom, bestowed upon thee by God, even as thou didst open the door of the ark unto all.

Taken by barbarian hands, O most rich one, even as of old the noble Joseph in Egypt, with faith and purity didst thou preserve the gift.

*Theotokion:*

O Virgin, thou wast known as a fair paradise having in the midst the Tree of incorruption, Jesus Christ the Lord; therefore do we call thee blessed.

*Canon of Saint George, Ode 3. Same Heirmos.*  
*Troparia:*

The path of life that abides unto the endless age, O all-blessed one, thou didst tread rejoicing without turning aside, and didst settle in the eternal habitations.

Thy life was radiant, and thy word seasoned in deed with divine salt, O George, was shown forth as a divine garden of divine husbandry.

Accounted worthy by baptism to be a son of God, O all-blessed one, thou didst inherit the paternal treasure by the purity of thy life.

*Theotokion:*

Be thou the establishment, and refuge, and protection, O Virgin Birthgiver of God, unto those who flee unto thee in faith, and who proclaim thee as the Mother of God.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Ꙗ҆ви́лсѧ є҆сѝ дне́сь):*

Thou wast shown forth as meek and quiet, humble-minded, trembling at the divine words, fulfilling the commandments of thy Master, Who looked down upon thee, O father, and settled thee in the mansion on high.

*Glory... Another Sessional Hymn, of Saint George, Tone 3, Podoben: "Awed by the beauty of thy virginity" (Красотѣ̀ дѣ́вства):*

Thou didst desire the most comely Christ, and didst hate the bodily passions, having lived angelically upon earth, O George. Therefore do we celebrate thy holy dormition, and kiss the shrine of thy relics, wherefrom thou pourest forth healings unto us, O father, who honor thee with love.

*Both now... Theotokion:*

As an uncultivated vine, O Virgin, thou didst sprout the most fair cluster of grapes, pouring forth for us the wine of salvation, gladdening the souls and bodies of all. Therefore, ever blessing thee as the cause of good things, with the Angel we cry unto thee: Rejoice, thou that art full of grace!

*Stavrotheotokion:*

The unwedded pure one and Thy Mother, O Christ, beholding Thee dead hanging dead upon the Cross, maternally weeping said: What hath the lawless and ungrateful Jewish assembly rendered unto Thee, which enjoyed many and great gifts of Thine, O my Son? I hymn Thy divine condescension.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*

I have heard, O Lord, the report of Thy dispensation, and I have glorified Thee, the only Lover of mankind.

*Troparia:*

Christ showed thee forth, O venerable father, as discerning judgment and a moderate rule for monks.

Thou didst show thy life to be a bridge of humility, O God-wise one, leading across unto true life.

Having foreseen thee, the Master blessed thee, O father, as one who gives in due season the measure of wheat of the word.

*Theotokion:*

A Light hast Thou shone forth, O Lord, unto those sitting in night and darkness of transgressions, carried in the womb of the pure Virgin.

*Canon of Saint George, Ode 4. Same Heirmos.*  
*Troparia:*

Adorned with the beauties of virtue and with light-bearing ways, thou didst fly up unto God, O father George.

Thou didst flee the humility of corruptible things, O father George, passing over unto stillness by a God-befitting life.

The torrents of iniquity were not able to trouble thy soul, for the torrents of sweetness gave thee to drink.

Ever heeding divine instructions, O most wise one, thou didst cast away unstable thoughts, O father George.

*Theotokion:*

Hymning Him Who was incarnate in thy womb, O pure one, we also glorify thee as the Mother of God.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*

O Dispenser of light and Creator of the ages, O Lord, guide us in the light of Thy commandments; for we know no other God save Thee.

*Troparia:*

While being upon earth, thou didst faithfully hymn the saints, O venerable one; being now with them and hearing their songs thou dost rejoice, unceasingly glorifying the Lord.

Drawing all unto repentance and confession, O wise one, thou didst write down every fault for thyself, offering prayers unto God to obtain forgiveness.

Applying thyself in all thy life unto holy labors, thou wast as a true merchant, gathering for thyself words and deeds as precious stones.

*Theotokion:*

Deliver us from temptations and dire thoughts, O pure one from all wrath, all sin, famine and pestilence, and from eternal torment, O Virgin.

*Canon of Saint George, Ode 5. Same Heirmos.*  
*Troparia:*

Blessedness and immortal life didst thou obtain, O wise father, ascending by divine ascents, O all-blessed one, strengthened by hope, love, and faith.

Adorning thyself with gracious humility, O venerable one, thou wast manifestly exalted unto the height of virtues, O God-wise one, having become a threshing-floor of divine husbandry.

By abstinence didst thou strictly show forth the mortification of the body, and didst acquire a pure heart, whereby thou wast accounted worthy, O father, to see Him Who is invisible to all.

*Theotokion:*

As an unashamed and unapproachable mystery beyond mind unto all, O Lady Maiden, is that of thy divine childbearing; for thou didst bear unto us Him Who is truly God.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*

Wallowing in the abyss of sin, I call upon the unsearchable abyss of Thy tender mercy: lead me up from corruption, O God!

*Troparia:*

By the variegated multitude of thy songs, shining forth like stars upon the firmament of Christ's Church, thou drivest away soul-destroying slumber.

The prayer of thy word, O venerable one, was unto those grieving in soul and body even as a sponge, cleansing away all sorrow of opinion.

Unto those hungering for the saving word and sated with bodily afflictions, O venerable one, thou didst generously grant twofold healing.

*Theotokion:*

As I wander upon the paths of life and frequently fall into pathless ways through sins, O Lady, guide me unto the path of repentance.

*Canon of Saint George, Ode 6. Same Heirmos.*  
*Troparia:*

Thou wast shown forth as a fervent expeller of evil spirits, O most glorious father George, manifestly strengthened by the cooperation of the Holy Spirit.

Adorned with the gift of dispassion, O George, thou wast most radiantly vested in the robe of chastity, driving away the turmoil of passions.

Thou didst pass through the flaming weapon, O wise one, and wast accounted worthy of the sweetness of paradise, clad, O George, in the invincible armor of the Cross.

*Theotokion:*

Upon thee have I placed my hope, O Mother and Ever-Virgin of my salvation; and thee do I set forth as a firm and unshakable protectress of my life.

*Kontakion, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who wast lifted up" (Вознесы́йсѧ):*

An inexhaustible fountain of repentance, a dispenser of unending consolation, and an abyss of compunction art thou, O Joseph; grant us tears of divine repentance, whereby, weeping here, we may find consolation from God, asking thy help, O holy one.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*

The Hebrew children in the furnace of old were most philosophic; for from a God-receiving soul, theologizing with their lips they sang: O supremely divine God of our fathers and our God, blessed art Thou!

*Troparia:*

Thou didst take care, even after the sleep of death, not to have the eyes of thy soul closed; for thou singest hymns with those who sing: O supremely divine God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

Thou didst receive the crown of martyrdom, as having diligently called blessed the choirs of unnumbered martyrs; and unceasingly, O father, thou singest with them: O supremely divine God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

As one who is the healing of great sins, which thou didst wisely set forth as confession for those who sing thy hymns, O father: O supremely divine God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Theotokion:*

With the pure radiances of thy light, O Virgin Mother of God, guide with light my soul that hath been grievously darkened by wicked transgressions, since thou art the gate of Light, that I may hymn thee.

*Canon of Saint George, Ode 7. Another Heirmos:*

When the golden image was served on the plain of Dura, Thy three children disregarded the godless command; and cast into the midst of the fire, being bedewed they sang: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparia:*

Having strengthened thy soul with the power of Christ, O father George, by His might thou didst drive away the hosts of those who hated thee, putting them to flight by the holy Cross, preserved wherewith thou didst sing: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers!

Blessed art thou, and well shall it be with thee now, O most blessed one, receiving the recompense of thy sufferings and struggles, and crying unto the Lord with the Angels, O George: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers!

Thou wast a radiant dwelling of Christ, ready for wonders and wisdom, O father, filled with holiness, and shining with faith and grace, and crying, O George: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*

A candlestick of Light, a radiant cloud, and a sanctuary wast thou shown forth to be, O unwedded one; for thou didst ineffably receive the Holy of Holies, the Word, Whom hymning we cry unto thee: Blessed art thou who didst bear God in the flesh!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*

Unto God Who descended into the fiery furnace unto the Hebrew children, and turned the flame into dew, sing ye works of the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparia:*

Knowing the present life truly to be as a passing shadow, thou didst struggle to receive that which is incorruptible, O wise one; therefore thou didst build up multitudes for the honorable Mother, for the salvation of their souls.

Possessing uprightness toward the meek and poverty of spirit, thou didst show forth all manner of courage; so as not only to establish thyself to serve the Creator, O father, but also all who look upon thee.

Thy guilelessness and the outpouring of thy love, O thrice-blessed one, set forth an image of Christ, the Lover of mankind, unto Whom thou didst unceasingly sing: Supremely exalted unto all ages!

*Theotokion:*

O fearful mystery! O awe-inspiring hearing! How does the Virgin, by an ineffable union, give birth unto the Word Who became like unto us, remaining as before childbirth, pure unto the ages!

*Canon of Saint George, Ode 8. Same Heirmos.*  
*Troparia:*

Unto indestructible life, unto unfading sweetness, O father, hast thou passed over, unto ever-existing glory, and unto the never-setting Light; for thou wast shown forth as the husbandry of God, Whom we supremely exalt unto all ages.

As a servant of Christ, as a faithful minister and steward of His mysteries, thou wast an unerring guide, O most wise George, healing the passions of souls and bodies.

As a ripe cluster of grapes, thou didst offer thyself, O father, as full fruit of fasting unto the threshing-floor of Christ; pressed out in the winepresses, O most blessed one, thou pourest forth for us the wine of compunction in thy memory.

*Theotokion:*

The fruit of thy womb was shown forth as the cause of incorruptible life unto me, and of perpetual sweetness, O pure Birthgiver of God. Therefore rejoicing I cry unto thee the voice of the Archangel: Rejoice, O Virgin!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*

Unto God the Word from God, Who by ineffable wisdom came to renew Adam, who fell grievously into corruption through eating, and Who for our sake was ineffably incarnate of the Holy Virgin, let us, the faithful, with one mind magnify in hymns.

*Troparia:*

Having inscribed the forms of virtues as upon tablets in thy pure soul, thou didst actively offer them unto all the saints, doubly exalting each of the virtues bringing in the singing of virtues, worthily offering praise from deeds.

The angelic choirs, the apostolic assembly, the Mother of God with the Forerunner, the prophetic company, the ranks of priests, with the fasters the armies of martyrs, O Joseph, now crown thee with commensurate praises.

Cease not to remember thy rational flock, but even as thou didst intercede when alive, O wise one, so also be thou one who grants incorruptible life, delivering us from bodily and spiritual passions unto Christ by thy prayers, O father.

*Theotokion:*

Cease not to pray for us unto thy Son, O Virgin, Christ God, the Lover of mankind; for thee have we acquired as hope, and by thy protection are we delivered from perils and passions, and transgressions and sicknesses, who glorify thee in faith.

*Canon of Saint George, Ode 9. Same Heirmos.*  
*Troparia:*

Strengthened by faith, and firmly hedged about by Christ's power, O all-blessed one, thou didst break the snares of the devil, and having escaped all their hunting and cunning wiles, thou standest before the Master of all, rejoicing.

Offering all thy desire unto God, O blessed one, and enjoying the divine beauty which every nature desires, by contemplation and action, and by the radiance of thy life, thou didst adorn thyself, O father; therefore do we call thee blessed.

Cease not to pray earnestly unto Christ, O father, for us who celebrate thy memory in faith, that we may be saved from misfortunes and grievous sorrows, and tempest, and perils, and passions, and that He may grant peace unto the world, O all-blessed one.

*Theotokion:*

The incarnate Word, Who before was bodiless, as He is God from God, didst Thou alone, all-hymned one, bear for the renewal and salvation of all earthborn; therefore, all we the faithful magnify thee in hymns.

*[Typographical Ornament] On the same month, on the 5th day: Holy Martyrs Theodulus and Agathopus, and those with them:*[^17]

*At "Lord, I have cried," stichera, Tone 4, Podoben: "Of the heavenly ranks" (Небе́сныхъ чинѡ́въ):*

The sufferings and labors of the divine pair were joined by faith, giving wings to their minds toward commensurate zeal, earnestly imploring Christ and saying: O Thou Who hast glorified the saints as good, establish us also in Thy fear.

As namesakes of their callings, O wise passion-bearers, having received divine actions and accomplished them most suitably, ye served God in upright paths, and truly drank the saving cup of martyrdom.

Traversing as it were another sea unto the land of dispassion, ye worthily entered into the heavenly Kingdom, where the stream of delight flows and the perpetual, unchangeable Light of Christ's martyrs shines forth.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*

Quench, O Virgin, by the bedewing of thy divine and voluntary rain, the fiery impulses of my soul-destroying passions, that I also, as the children, may give thanks unto God Who is from thee, glorifying and blessing thy goodness, O Lady.

*Stavrotheotokion:*

When the blameless Virgin beheld her Lamb stretched out upon the Cross, weeping she cried: O my sweet Child, what is this new and most glorious sight? How art Thou Who holdest all things in Thy fist nailed in the flesh upon the Tree?

*Canon, whose acrostic is: I sing the beauty of Thy martyrs, O Christ. Joseph.*[^18] *Tone 4.*  
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*

The Red Sea's deep of old did Israel traverse with dryshod feet, and by the cross-stretched hands of Moses vanquished the power of Amalek in the wilderness.

*Troparia:*

Adorned with the divine crown of martyrdom, enlightened with intelligible beauty, ye stand before the Master, praying for us, O most fair great martyrs.

Fairly making yourselves familiar unto God, O wise martyrs, ye piously withdrew yourselves from unseemly malice and rejoicing were numbered with the choir of passion-bearers.

Being filled with the waters of the Life-creating Spirit, by beneficence, O holy ones, obeying divine commandments, ye gave drink unto those who of old were melting in the heat of ignorance.

*Theotokion:*

Thou didst bear in thy womb the Savior and Creator of all, the Lord, Whom Agathopus together with the glorious Theodulus proclaimed as being God and man, O all-blameless Virgin Mother.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*

Thy Church rejoices in Thee, O Christ, crying: Thou art my strength, O Lord, and my refuge and my firm establishment!

*Troparia:*

At the wounds of thy scars the enemy stumbled and fell, and was manifestly exposed, O martyr and passion-bearer Theodulus.

Having strengthened thy soul with sacred love toward God, O martyr Agathopus, thou didst show all the power of the enemy to be feeble.

Pouring forth healings unto the faithful from ever-flowing streams, O glorious ones, by divine power ye dry up the fountain of passions.

*Theotokion:*

Thou didst bear the Deliverer Who delivers men from the captivity of the enemy, O pure one, clothed in our flesh.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "The Wisdom [and Word]" (Премꙋ́дрости):*

Brethren by the grace of the Spirit, unanimous in pious faith, being holy and truly of great name, passing your lives in prayers and fastings, ye drew people unto divine knowledge. Therefore, noble soldiers, ye manly captured delusion, and having lawfully suffered ye received crowns. Pray ye unto Christ God to grant remission of transgressions unto those who celebrate your holy memory with love.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*

Having conceived Wisdom and the Word in thy womb without being consumed, O Mother of God, thou didst bear unto the world Him Who holds the world, and in thine arms didst carry Him Who bears all things, the Nourisher of all and Creator of creation. Therefore I pray thee, O all-holy Virgin, and faithfully glorify thee, that I may be delivered from transgressions; and in the day of judgment, when I am to stand before the face of my Creator, O Lady, pure Virgin, then grant me thy help: for whatsoever thou desirest thou canst do, O all-hymned one.

*Stavrotheotokion:*

Beholding the Lamb and Shepherd and Deliverer upon the Cross, the ewe-lamb, crying out weeping and bitterly lamenting, exclaimed: The world indeed rejoices, receiving redemption through thee, but my womb burns, beholding Thy crucifixion, which Thou endurest for tender mercy's sake, O long-suffering Lord, abyss of mercy, and inexhaustible fountain: have mercy, and grant remission of transgressions unto those who sing Thy divine passions in faith.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*

Beholding Thee lifted up upon the Cross, O Sun of righteousness, the Church stood in her place, worthily crying: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparia:*

Shining forth as most radiant lamps by the brightness of martyrdom, the glorious martyrs enlighten the fullness of the universe, darkening the evil-mindedness of delusion.

The Angels marveled at the blessed and prudent pair that received the crown of martyrdom, and men praised the valor of their endurance.

Rejoicing, Theodulus cried: I run in the way of Thy testimonies, O Lord, and richly enjoy Thy radiance, together with steadfast Agathopus.

*Theotokion:*

Purified by the incarnation of the Master Who shone forth from thy womb, O Mother of God, the glorious martyrs drank the saving drink of martyrdom, rejoicing.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*

Thou, my Lord, hast come as a light into the world, a holy light turning from gloomy ignorance those who hymn Thee in faith.

*Troparia:*

Thou hast manifestly set thy feet upon the uprightness of honorable martyrdom, O most praised Agathopus, tripping the enemies.

Having acquired in your hearts the water of life that flows, O passion-bearers, ye received a glorious end by water.

Ye dried up the torrent of idolatrous threatening, O sufferers, cast into the depth of the sea and willingly put to death.

*Theotokion:*

O Mother of God, heal the afflictions of my soul, who didst bear the all-good Word Who blesses all.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*

I will sacrifice unto Thee with the voice of praise, O Lord, the Church cries unto Thee, cleansed of demonic gore by the blood that for mercy's sake flowed from Thy side.

*Troparia:*

Being filled with living waters, ye received a praiseworthy end by the drowning of the waters; and ye drowned in them the prince of malice.

Preserved by the grace and power of God, ye turned not aside to sacrifice unto vile idols but offered yourselves as a pure sacrifice unto the Master.

The weapons of the enemy grew feeble against you; for the sharpened arrows of your courage, O sufferers, ye valiantly plunged into his heart.

*Theotokion:*

Heal the passions of my soul, O all-undefiled one, who didst bear the Fountain of dispassion; and put a stop to the torment of those who unceasingly trouble my heart, O pure one.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*

In the Persian furnace the Abrahamite children, burned more by love of piety than by the flame, cried: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!

*Troparia:*

By divine understanding thou didst clothe thyself in the Levitical vesture,[^19] making it sacredly brighter by the radiance of martyrdom; and now thou standest, O Agathopus, rejoicing before the Word.

Adorned with the ministry of the first martyr, O blessed Agathopus, thou wast accounted worthy to serve the sacrificed Word, crying: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!

Thinking one thing in unanimity, O marvelous ones, ye endured one form of death for Christ, crying: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!

*Theotokion:*

Having found thee, O pure one, pre-adorned with the brightness of virtue, the Word Who covers the heavens with virtue dwelt in thee, O all-undefiled one, and made human nature heavenly.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*

Daniel stretched forth his hands and shut the lions' gaping mouths in the pit; and girded with virtue, the children, lovers of piety, quenched the power of fire, crying: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparia:*

By thy words thou didst darken delusion, O wise Theodulus, and illuminated by gracious wisdom thou didst shine forth, proclaiming piety before the judgment seat, and stopping the mouths of the impious, crying: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

The deep of the sea could by no means hide your bodies, O holy ones; for by divine command it gives them forth onto dry land uncorrupted, pouring forth marvelous wonders unto us who cry: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

Wholly enslaved for the sake of Him Who appeared in the form of a servant, O glorious one, even after death thou dost show forth providence unto orphans showing forth providence unto orphans and widows, O blessed one, fulfilling the ordinance of the law, as a wise servant of the Lord, O most blessed Theodulus.

Thessalonica honors your bonds and wounds and struggles; for ye were shown forth as her foster-children, and in her ye truly received a blessed end as sufferers, and now ye honorably call blessed the Church of the firstborn.

*Theotokion:*

Without changing His nature, Christ put on the whole man from thee, O Sovereign Lady joyful in God; Whose Passion Theodulus and Agathopus steadfastly depicted, crying: All ye works of the Lord, bless ye the Lord!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*

A cornerstone cut without hand from an uncut mountain—even thee, O Virgin—was Christ, Who joined together the parted natures; therefore rejoicing, we magnify thee, O Birthgiver of God.

*Troparia:*

"Behold, what is good now, or what is pleasant?" cried the passion-bearers, "save to live in the city of the living God, having preserved the bond of piety indissoluble even unto death!"

Having finished the course of martyrdom, O sufferers, the choir of the hosts on high received you, crowned with unfading crowns, O glorious ones.

Today glorious Thessalonica brightly celebrates, calling every city and land unto your memory with high proclamation, O Agathopus and Theodulus.

As radiant luminaries of ineffable glory, O passion-bearers, pray, O most honorable ones, since ye have boldness, that those who ever celebrate your memory in faith may be accounted worthy thereof.

*Theotokion:*

With the light of thy tender mercy, illumine me who lie in the darkness of ignorance, O thou who alone didst bear the Giver of light, the Deliverer and Lord Who crowns the saints.

*April 6. Our Father among the Saints Eutychius, Archbishop of Constantinople:*[^20]

*Stichera, Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Є҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва):*

Unto the rational flock and sheep of Christ, O blessed Eutychius, the lot appointed thee as an invincible champion for the Church of God, driving far from her the impious Nestorius, who blasphemed against the most glorious and pure Mother of God, asserting that she is not truly the Birthgiver of God; whom thou didst cast out.

Upon the grass of the right faith, and upon the living water of true wisdom, and upon the fair green herb, thou didst wisely guide thy flock to dwell in the pasture planted by God: food of incorruption, unfailing and inexhaustible, with mouth uttering divine words of eternal life and an indissoluble mind.

Cutting down roots and thorns with thy sickle of teaching, with thy plow thou didst cut through abominable heretical words and hearts, and didst sow the divine commandments, thine uprightness and rectitude; and having shown these forth as fruitful, thou hast thy talent doubled, and this thou bringest unto thy Master.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion, Same Podoben (Подо́бенъ то́йже):*

Other sovereign refuge, and tower of strength, and unconquerable wall, we truly have not acquired save thee, O all-pure one; unto thee do we flee, and unto thee do we cry: O Birthgiver of God, help, lest we perish; show forth thy grace upon us, and the glory of strength, and the greatness of thy tender mercy!

*Stavrotheotokion:*

Beholding Thee nailed unto the wood of the Cross, O Jesus, the unwedded one weeping said: O sweet Child, why hast Thou left me alone who bare Thee, O unapproachable Light of the Father before all ages? Make haste and be glorified, that they who glorify Thy divine Passion may receive divine glory.

*Troparion, Tone 4:*

A rule of faith and image of meekness, a teacher of temperance did the reality of things show thee forth unto thy flock; therefore thou didst acquire high things through humility, and rich things through poverty, O father Eutychius; pray unto Christ God that our souls may be saved.

*Canon, whose acrostic is: "I marvel at wonderworking Eutychius." By Theophanes. Tone 6:*[^21]

*Ode 1. Heirmos:*

Traversing the deep on foot as on dry land, Israel, beholding the pursuer Pharaoh drowned, cried: Let us sing a song of victory unto God!

*Troparia:*

Having become an isle of prosperity through virtues, and illuminated with radiant beams of God's gifts, deliver me by Thy prayers, O wise one, from the misfortune of passions, that I may hymn thee.

As a heavenly planted branch in the courts of God, thou didst put forth abundant fruits of virtuous deeds together with miracles, O truly blessed Eutychius.

Having made thy mind the ruler, O venerable one, thou didst restrain the tyranny of passions; therefore, lifted up by the wings of dispassion, thou wast brought unto the very habitations of God.

*Theotokion:*

Having received thy Creator beyond understanding incarnate from thy seedless womb even as He willed, O pure one, thou wast truly revealed as the Sovereign Lady of creation.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*

There is none holy as Thou, O Lord my God, Who hast exalted the horn of Thy faithful, O Good One, and established us upon the rock of Thy confession.

*Troparia:*

Thou wast a temple of the Holy Spirit, O God-bearer, having cleansed the bodily mire of passions through great diligence, O glorious hierarch Eutychius.

By participation in that day thou wast shown to be of immaterial brightness, and by the light of true espousals thou drivest away the mist of the harm of passions, O ever-memorable one.

Giving forth the glory of doctrines of temperance, by unceasing prayers thou didst truly attain unto the measure of the stature of Christ, O glorious hierarch Eutychius.

*Theotokion:*

The wonder of thy divine childbearing, O pure one, surpasses every order of nature; for supernaturally thou didst conceive God in thy womb, and having given birth thou remainest Ever-Virgin.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 3, Podoben: "Of divine faith" (Бж҃твенныѧ вѣ́ры):*

Clothed with divine power, thou didst lay bare the strength of the deceiver; thou didst work [many][^22] marvelous wonders; and being unjustly driven away from thy flock, thou didst again return unto it glorified, O venerable father; pray unto Christ God to grant us great mercy.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*

Thou wast the divine tabernacle of the Word, O only all-pure Virgin Mother, who in purity didst surpass the Angels; me, who am become dust beyond all men, defiled by carnal transgressions, cleanse by the divine waters of thy prayers, granting great mercy, O pure one.

*Stavrotheotokion:*

The undefiled ewe-lamb of the Word, the incorrupt Virgin Mother, beholding Him Who sprang without travail from her suspended upon the Cross, maternally lamenting cried: Woe is me, my Child! How sufferest Thou willingly, wishing to deliver mankind from the passions of dishonor?

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*

Christ is my power, God and Lord, the honorable Church divinely sings, crying out with a pure mind, keeping festival in the Lord.

*Troparia:*

Illuminated by the beam of divine grace, O all-wise one, thou didst darken every heretical opinion, and didst make clear the assemblies of the Orthodox people.

Thou didst not faint, enduring persecutions for the faith; for thou hadst as a fellow champion Christ Who was persecuted with thee, O all-honorable hierarch.

Having exalted them, Christ set the steps of thy soul upon the rock of His truth, O blessed one, and tripped up the haughty reasonings of the enemies.

*Theotokion:*

Truly unutterable and incomprehensible unto those upon earth and in heaven, O Ever-Virgin Birthgiver of God, are the mysteries of thy divinely becoming childbearing.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*

With Thy divine light, O Good One, illumine with love, I pray, the souls of those who awake early unto Thee, that they may know Thee, O Word of God, the true God, Who callest them from the gloom of sins.

*Troparia:*

Valiantly enduring separation from the flock entrusted unto thee, O father, thou wast unjustly exiled; and straightway thou didst run back unto it rejoicing, greatly praised by all.

Not in riddles dost thou behold the glory of God which thou didst desire from infancy, O blessed Eutychius, but rather face to face now, the bodily mirrors having been dissolved, O our father.

Thy soul was filled with divine waters, O father; it truly poured forth rivers of teachings for the Church, O wise Eutychius, and thou waterest her furrows, O blessed one.

*Theotokion:*

Save those who from the soul confess thee as the Birthgiver of God, O good and well-famed one; for we have acquired thee as an invincible protection, who art truly the Birthgiver of God.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*

Beholding the sea of life surging with the storm of temptations, fleeing unto Thy calm haven I cry unto Thee: Lead up my life from corruption, O Most Merciful One!

*Troparia:*

The Trinity made Her abode in thee as in one pure from passions, and filled thee with splendors and gifts, O God-wise Eutychius, wherewith thou ever drivest away every disease.

Thou wast exalted, having truly acquired in thy heart uplifting humility; and thou didst utterly humble all the demonic wiles, O most glorious hierarch of the Lord.

Having crossed the sea of life without waves, thou didst reach the haven of the heavenly Kingdom, O father, having saved for God the cargo of thy soul, O praiseworthy one.

*Theotokion:*

O all-pure, tenderhearted Sovereign Lady, who didst bear for mankind the Helmsman and Lord, calm the turbulent and cruel disturbance of my passions, and grant stillness unto my heart.

*Kontakion, Tone 8:*

All ye people singing faithfully, with love let us call divine Eutychius blessed, as a great shepherd, and minister, and all-wise teacher, and expeller of heresies; for he prays unto the Lord for us all.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*

The Angel indeed made the furnace drop dew for the venerable youths, while the command of God consuming the Chaldeans persuaded the tormentor to cry: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparia:*

Having acquired a soul white as snow by the whiteness of virtues, O father, thou wast likened unto the Angels; therefore thou dancest with them, rejoicing and crying: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

As a blameless sacrifice, thou didst offer thyself from infancy unto thy Master, O venerable one, truly through the mortification of pleasures; therefore rejoicing thou criest: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

The streams of thy tears, O father, quenched the fiery burning of carnal pleasures, and became the dew of healings unto those who ever faithfully cry unto Christ: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*

We men have known thee, O all-undefiled one and Ever-Virgin Mother, as salvation; for the Son Who is inseparable from the bosom of the Father took flesh from thee; therefore blessed art thou unto generations of generations.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*

Out of the flame Thou didst pour forth dew for the venerable ones, and the sacrifice of the righteous one Thou didst consume with water; for Thou doest all things, O Christ, only by willing; Thee do we exalt unto all ages.

*Troparia:*

Having shaken off the slumber of despondency, through care for virtues thou didst keep vigil, O father, until thou wast shown to be an habitation for the God of Jacob, Whom we exalt unto all ages.

Sheltered by the shield of piety, and having received the honorable commandment as a two-edged sword, thou didst steadfastly cut down all the heretical hosts, O God-wise one.

Thou didst offer bloodless sacrifices unto God with humility of heart, O father Eutychius, unto Him Who was sacrificed for thy sake as a sheep, Whom we exalt unto all ages.

*Triadicon:*

The one nature of the Trinity, the innumerable power surpassing all times and the concept of nature, we ever Orthodoxly proclaim and exalt unto all ages.

*Theotokion:*

In giving birth thou didst escape travail, O pure one, and having given birth thou didst again remain a Virgin; for Jesus, God and man, makes thee Mother of God; Whom we exalt unto all ages.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*

It is impossible for men to see God, upon Whom the ranks of Angels dare not gaze; but through thee, O all-pure one, the incarnate Word was revealed unto men, Whom magnifying together with the heavenly hosts, we call thee blessed.

*Troparia:*

Having acquired the greatest things by small things, and well purchased incorruptible things by corruptible things, thou didst receive an unshakable kingdom, and didst obtain unpassing glory, adorned with the beauty of thy virtues.

Purely didst thou finish thy life, and thou wast shown forth as a servant of the pure Virgin, O Eutychius, most true hierarch of God, pillar of Orthodoxy, foundation of the faith, wall of the chosen people, O divinely blessed one.

Thou didst subject carnal wisdom unto the mind, and didst live upon earth as bodiless in the flesh, O Eutychius; therefore with the bodiless divine hosts thou standest rejoicing before the Master of all and God.

As one who now lives together with Angels, and dwells equally with patriarchs, martyrs, and venerable ones, pray with them unto the Lord to grant remission of sins unto all who celebrate thy sacred memory on earth, O wise one.

*Theotokion:*

Having ineffably conceived in thy womb the Son Who shone forth timelessly from the Father, thou didst bear Him in thy womb, having ineffably conceived, O pure Mother of God, undefiled Virgin; pray unto Him as the Lover of mankind, O all-pure one, on behalf of thy servants, that He may save our race.

*April 7. Our Venerable Father George, Bishop of Mytilene:*[^23]

*Stichera, Tone 4, Podoben: "O all-praised martyrs" (Всехва́лнїи мꙋ́ченицы):*

O father George, thou didst honorably bow down before the honorable and all-pure icon of Christ, O all-wise one, in no wise terrified at the insolence of the fighters against God, being strengthened by His power; Whom do thou now implore to grant unto our souls peace and great mercy.

O father George, thou didst endure the fury of the impious who raged senselessly, whom also thou didst see exalting themselves in evil arrogance, and again grievously crushed by divine providence, stirred up unto vengeance upon the senseless, O glorious one.

O father George, having become a fellow worker with God as a priest and husbandman, thou didst bring unto the Master as a hundredfold fruit the souls of those saved by thy word; and now pray that there be granted unto our souls peace and great mercy.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*

Rejoice, unburnt bush, [who didst bear] the Angel of Great Counsel; rejoice, O thou who alone didst give birth; rejoice, good pleasure of the honorable Trinity; rejoice, establishment of earthly ones; rejoice, who didst put forth uncultivated the ear of wheat of paradise, the King and Lord, Who roots out the sprouts of wickedness!

*Stavrotheotokion:*

When the ewe-lamb and undefiled Sovereign Lady beheld her Lamb upon the Cross having neither form nor comeliness: Woe is me! she cried, lamenting: Whither has Thy beauty departed, O Sweetest One? Where is Thy majesty? Where is the shining grace of Thy form, O my most beloved Son?

*Canon, Tone 8.*  
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*

Let us send up a hymn, ye people, unto our marvelous God, Who delivered Israel from bondage, singing a song of victory and crying: Let us sing unto Thee, the only Master!

*Troparia:*

Shine forth a beam of tender mercy unto me, O Christ, who am filled with transgressions and grievous sins, O Compassionate One, and drive away the gloom of my blindness by the supplications of Thy hierarch.

Thou didst cultivate the land of temperance with thy labors and sweats, O wise one, and sowing the wheat of the Spirit in thy heart, thou didst yield the ear of dispassion, O blessed George.

Taking thy cross upon thy shoulders, Thou didst forsake earthly beauties unto the world-ruler of darkness, having followed Christ; and Thou didst acquire the treasure of His immaterial Kingdom.

*Theotokion:*

Having thy protection as a weapon, and a firm haven, and a wall, and a stronghold, and a divine foundation, and a bridge, and a shelter, O Bride of God, we are delivered from all perils.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*

There is none holy as the Lord, and none righteous as our God, Whom all creation hymns: there is none righteous above Thee, O Lord.

*Troparia:*

Ascending unto the height of virtues, O blessed one, and worthily looking down into the depths of the Spirit, and having become a receptacle of vision, O venerable one, thou dwellest with the angelic choirs.

Having become poor in spirit, O wise one, thou wast enriched with the immaterial Kingdom, wherein thou didst faithfully prepare eternal treasures for thy flock; therefore do we call thee blessed.

In simplicity, O venerable one, and in gentleness of soul, thou wast likened in thy manners and upright deeds unto Jacob, and by the purity of the spirit thou wast shown forth as another mind seeing God.

*Theotokion:*
