Without father didst thou give birth unto Him Who was without mother before thee, and as an infant didst thou suckle Him Who nourishes all things; Whom do thou implore that they may be saved who faithfully hymn thee as truly the pure Birthgiver of God.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*

Shining with the height of humility, thou didst utterly shatter the enemy's pride and fight valiantly; and being merciful, O venerable father, by abundant giving thou didst satisfy the hungry. Therefore after death thou didst inherit food that grows not old, rejoicing unto the ages, O blessed hierarch George; implore Christ God to grant remission of transgressions unto those who celebrate thy holy memory with love.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*

As a Virgin and the only one among women, who didst bear God in the flesh without seed, all we generations of men call thee blessed for the fire of the Godhead made its dwelling in thee, and as an infant thou didst suckle the Creator and Lord. Therefore, both the angelic and human race worthily glorify thy most holy childbearing, and with one voice we cry unto thee: Pray unto Christ God to grant remission of sins unto those who bow down in faith before thy most holy childbearing.

*Stavrotheotokion:*

The ewe-lamb, beholding her Lamb and Shepherd and Deliverer upon the Cross, cried out weeping, and bitterly lamenting exclaimed: The world indeed rejoices, receiving deliverance through Thee; but my womb burns, beholding Thy crucifixion, which Thou endurest for the tender mercy of Thy loving-kindness, O longsuffering Lord, abyss of mercy, and inexhaustible fountain; have compassion and grant remission of transgressions unto those who hymn Thy divine Passion in faith!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*

From the overshadowed mountain—the only Birthgiver of God—the prophet divinely foresaw Thee about to become incarnate, O Word, and with fear he glorified Thy power.

*Troparia:*

Thou didst save the ship of thy soul; from the passions of material tempest thou didst hasten into the haven and stillness of temperance, O venerable father.

Having acquired an angel-like mind, thou didst listen to the song of the Trinity from the Angels, being in ecstasy of thy mind, O all-praised one.

Armed with the spiritual sling, as another Goliath thou didst cast down the proud prince of darkness like David, O hierarch, and by thy divine faith.

*Theotokion:*

Hope and protection of the world, O Birthgiver of God, pray unto Him Who was incarnate of thee on behalf of thy servants; for having acquired thee as a holy place of propitiation, we are delivered from sin.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*

O Thou Who hast enlightened the ends of the earth from the night of ignorance by the knowledge of God, illumine me with the dawn of Thy love for mankind, O Lord!

*Troparia:*

All knowing thee as a pillar of purity and key of chastity, O ever-memorable one, may we obtain mercy by thy prayers!

Through God-beholding foresight thou wast clothed in the sacred rank, by Him Who called thee, as blameless, unto this for the blameless Virgin.

Possessing bountiful mercy given unto all, and ever dwelling with thee, thou wast for the poor a workshop of rich nourishment.

*Theotokion:*

Having conceived the heavenly Master in thy womb O all-undefiled one, thou wast higher than the heavens and all creation.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*

Grant me a radiant robe, Thou Who clothest Thyself with light as with a garment, O Most Merciful Christ our God!

*Troparia:*

The All-Holy Spirit, having found an abode in Thee, drove away legions of evil spirits by thy prayers.

Thou wast shown forth as a river of stillness and a stream of meekness, from which we drink of the torrent of delight, O venerable father.

Having put to death the carnal pleasures, thou didst live in the Spirit; and raise me up also, who am fallen through passions, O glorious one.

*Theotokion:*

In thy womb, in time, beyond all speech, O Ever-Virgin, thou didst conceive the Word, God the Word, Who shone forth timelessly from the Father.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*

Thou Who didst dew the youths in the furnace, and didst preserve after childbearing the Virgin who bare Thee: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparia:*

By the illumination of the Divine Spirit thou didst beforehand perceive thy departure hence unto better things; and now thou dancest, O venerable one, in those mansions.

As a star appearing unto thee, enlightened in senses by thy life and divine radiance, a star announced beforehand thy departure, O venerable one.

Clothed worthily, thou didst enter into the bridal chamber of the Kingdom on high, and didst recline with the Bridegroom, O venerable one, at the banquet of immaterial sweetness.

*Theotokion:*

The heavenly hosts worthily attend thee as the Mother of the Life of all, and hymn thee, O Sovereign Lady, as the protection of our souls.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*

The God-proclaiming youths in the furnace, trampling the flame together with the fire, sang: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparia:*

Having a guided heart, O father, drive away the dark tempest of my passions, and guide me unto the haven of thy stillness.

Planted by the divine outgoings of the love of God, Thou didst grow as a tree of life, and didst yield the fruits of piety in due season.

As a physician of incurable diseases and expeller of unclean spirits, O blessed George, heal also the disease of my sin.

*Theotokion:*

Who shall express thy divine childbearing, O Birthgiver of God, Mary? For every nature of earthly beings and of Angels is astounded, and they call thee blessed.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*

That which was revealed upon the mountain unto the lawgiver in the fire and the bush, the childbearing of the Ever-Virgin for our salvation as faithful, we magnify with unceasing hymns.

*Troparia:*

With the streams of thy tears, O venerable one, thou didst blamelessly cultivate a paradise of dispassion, and bearing forth abundant fruit, thou didst nourish the divine flock with virtues.

Thou didst offer a pure service unto God, and having finished the upright course of the faith, thou hast now received the crown of righteousness from the hand of Christ, the Chief of athletes.

Today the multitude of the faithful leaps and dances, the herds of monastics, the assemblies of priests; for they all have found in thee an image of salvation and a portrayal of the rule of life.

*Theotokion:*

Thy childbearing was of God and man, O all-undefiled Maiden, as He is God and the Creator of the ages; cease not to pray unceasingly to save the souls of those who hymn thee.

*April 8. Holy Apostles Herodion, Agabus, Asyncritus, Rufus, Phlegon, and Hermes, and those with them:*[^24]

*Stichera, Tone 4, Podoben: "As one valiant" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*

As winged eagles ye traversed all the earth, sowing venerable teachings, uprooting the tares of deceit by grace, and producing abundant fruit, O all-praised ones; which the spiritual storehouses hold unto the ages, richly preserved for the immortal Husbandman.

Let us call blessed Herodion, Agabus, and Asyncritus, Rufus also, and the divine Phlegon, who are fruitful branches of the vine, dropping down for us saving sweetness, gladdening the thirsty hearts of those who with true faith celebrate their divine solemnity.

Having become heavens high in understanding, proclaiming the glory of our God Who was incarnate of His own will, O God-beholding Apostles, guides of the pious, foundations of churches, unshakable pillars, havens of piety, ministers of ineffable mysteries, light-guides of our souls.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*

O thou who didst contain in thy womb the uncontainable God, Who in His love for mankind became man and took our compound nature from thee and visibly deified it: despise me not now who am in affliction, O pure one, but show mercy quickly, and deliver me from diverse enmities and the harm of the evil one.

*Stavrotheotokion:*

When she beheld Thee nailed upon the Cross, O Lord, the Ewe-lamb and Thy Mother was astounded, and cried out: "What is this vision, O much-desired Son? Has the unfaithful and lawless synagogue rendered these things unto Thee, which enjoyed Thy many wonders? But glory unto Thine ineffable condescension, O Master!"

*Canon, whose acrostic is: "Unto the apostles we weave a divine song. The work of Joseph":*[^25]

*Tone 3. Ode 1. Heirmos:*

O Thou Who of old didst gather the waters by Thy divine nod into one gathering, and didst divide the sea for the people of Israel: this is our God, and He is exceedingly glorified; unto Him alone let us sing, for He is glorified!

*Troparia:*

Let us harmoniously celebrate the all-honorable memory of the holy Apostles, and let us hymn them with gladness of soul; for they pray unto the most holy Word to grant us cleansing and mercy.

Having driven away the rot of polytheistic madness by the salt of the divine word, ye healed all the souls that were formerly foolish and weak, and presented them saved unto the Creator of all; therefore ye are glorified.

Let glorious Herodion, Rufus, and Phlegon, Agabus, and Asyncritus together, and Hermes be called blessed with hymns, for they became blessed friends of our Deliverer, and wise Apostles, and divine preachers.

*Theotokion:*

O merciful Mother of God, praise of Apostles, glory of passion-bearers, and establishment of the faithful: establish, I pray thee, my mind, which is tossed by the assaults of the deceiver and darkened by transgressions.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*

O Thou Who broughtest all things from non-being, which are created by the Word and perfected by the Spirit, O Most High Almighty: establish me in Thy love!

*Troparia:*

Having become the eyes of the Church, ye opened the eyes of many to behold the incomprehensible beauty, O God-beholding disciples of the divine Word.

Thou wast shown forth as a radiant house of the Trinity, O most glorious Rufus, and by the word of grace thou didst cast down pagan shrines and build churches unto the Lord.

Having renewed hearts by thy divine word, O wise Rufus, and having sown saving teachings, thou didst reap as salvation an abundant ear of grain of those who were saved.

*Theotokion:*

He Who is unapproachable by divine essence, O all-pure one, has now become approachable unto me, and by the assumption of flesh was entirely united out of the utmost tender compassion.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who wast raised up" (Вознесы́йсѧ):*

Having drunk from the fountain of wisdom, ye made wise those who formerly through lack of understanding perniciously served idols, O wise ones; therefore ye are called blessed unto ages of ages, standing before the throne of the Holy Trinity. But pray earnestly, O most glorious ones, that we may obtain forgiveness of our transgressions.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*

For after God, O Mother of God, I, the lowly one, flee unto thy divine protection, and falling down I pray: have mercy, O all-pure one, for my sins have gone over my head, and I fear the torment, O Sovereign Lady, and tremble; make supplication, O pure one, unto thy Son, to deliver me from them.

*Stavrotheotokion:*

Beholding Thee hanging upon the Cross, O Christ, she who bare Thee in the flesh at the end of times, Who wast begotten of the unoriginate Father, cried out: "Woe is me, O most beloved Jesus! How is it that Thou, Who art glorified as God by Angels, art now willingly crucified by lawless men, O Son? I hymn Thee, O Long-suffering One!"

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*

Thou hast shown firm love toward us, O Lord, for Thou didst give Thine Only-Begotten Son unto death for us; therefore we cry unto Thee, giving thanks: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparia:*

Bringing divine healing unto the faithful nations, ye gave over the evil land of impiety unto destruction, O wise ones, and built up souls in the faith; therefore ye are called blessed.

The city of Patras was manifestly clothed in power through thee, O blessed Herodion; for thou wast the first to adorn its throne, enlightened by the divine outpourings of light of the Holy Spirit.[^26]

The city of Patras was truly enriched with thee as a great citizen, O blessed Herodion, guiding its people in righteousness unto the Sion on high; therefore it honors thee with divine hymnody.

*Theotokion:*

By the sacred Word of the Father thou didst conceive the Word, delivering mortals from all irrationality delivering mortals from all irrationality, O Virgin Birthgiver of God; therefore with divine words we ever magnify thee with a loud voice.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*

Thou Who art invisible wast manifested on earth, and Thou Who art incomprehensible didst willingly dwell with men; and rising early at dawn unto Thee, we hymn Thee, O Lover of mankind!

*Troparia:*

Ye proclaimed the Word Who came upon the earth to save the straying one, O divine Apostles, and ye delivered all men from deception.

In marvelous manner the grace of prophecy was richly poured out upon thee from God, O God-wise Agabus; for Thou dost loudly proclaim the telling of things to come.

Thou didst appear of old, foretokening unto Paul the plots, and bonds, and afflictions, O prophet Agabus, when thou didst bind thine own hands with his belt.

*Theotokion:*

Thou alone upon earth hadst a marvelous childbearing, reconciling us who are singular unto the one unoriginate Father; therefore we honor thee alone as the Mother of God.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*

The lowest abyss of sins has surrounded me, and my spirit fails; but stretching forth Thy high arm, O Master, save me as Thou didst Peter, O Helmsman!

*Troparia:*

The Word set you as radiant lamps, O God-beholding Apostles, enlightening the universe, dissolving the darkness, and pointing out the saving way.

Proclaiming the death of the Divine One, he brought those put to death unto life: the wise Asyncritus, praise of Apostles, and firm rampart of Hyrcania.

Hyrcania found thee, O Asyncritus, as a river full of the waters of the Spirit; and having been watered with thy venerable outpourings, she brought forth most fair shoots unto the Master.

*Theotokion:*

The ark of the Law prefigured thee, who didst receive the fulfillment of the divine Law, and who barest Him in the flesh, and who remained an Ever-Virgin after childbearing even as before childbearing, O Bride of God.

*Kontakion, Tone 2, Podoben: "Seeking the highest" (Въ вы́шнихъ и҆ща̀):*

Ye were shown forth as disciples of Christ and all-venerable Apostles, O glorious Herodion, Agabus, and Rufus, Asyncritus, Phlegon with Hermes; ever pray unto the Lord to grant the forgiveness of sins unto us who hymn you.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*

Formerly the venerable youths did not bow down before the Persian golden image, but the three sang in the midst in the midst of the furnace: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparia:*

Having well founded the fullness of the Gentiles upon the rock of faith, ye manifestly enlightened all those who of old venerated stones, O God-proclaiming ones.

Thou didst burn up every thorn of deception by the fire of thy divine words, O holy Phlegon, and hearts wretchedly extinguished thou didst manifestly rekindle by the warmth of the Spirit.

Having thee as their greatest protector, and excellent teacher, and lamp, and guide, O glorious Phlegon, the inhabitants of Marathon honor thee unceasingly.[^27]

*Theotokion:*

Thou wast a new paradise for us, having possessed the Tree of life, O pure one; by partaking of Which, O pure Birthgiver of God, He brought to life those put to death through eating.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*

The Babylonian furnace did not burn the youths, nor did the fire of the Godhead consume the Virgin; therefore with the youths let us faithful cry out: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparia:*

Let Agabus, Phlegon, and Asyncritus, Rufus, and glorious Hermes, and Herodion be called blessed today, having become divine Apostles of the Word, and luminaries of the world, and teachers of the nations.

The most glorious Hermes was the guide and enlightenment of all Dalmatia; for upon her he came like a radiant sun, performing marvelous wonders, and enlightening hearts that formerly did not see.[^28]

Having obtained the Word as a lamp which thou barest, O glorious Hermes, thou didst enlighten those sitting in the night of grievous evils; and thy great healings and signs drew the multitude of the unbelieving unto faith.

*Theotokion:*

We thy servants have thee as protection, O all-pure one, and as an unashamed mediatress before thy Son and God; save us from perils and cruel misfortunes, that with faith and love we may ever glorify thee.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*

Her who was prefigured by the bush and the fire in Sinai unto Moses the lawgiver, who conceived the divine Fire unconsumed in her womb, the all-radiant and unquenchable lamp: her who is truly the Birthgiver of God, honoring her with hymns, we magnify.

*Troparia:*

Let us faithfully form a most sacred choir, with oneness of soul, warmly calling blessed Hermes and Herodion, Rufus, Agabus, and great Asyncritus, and the divine Phlegon, as eyewitnesses of the Word.

As mystical olive trees, manifestly God-proclaiming in truth, they anointed our souls and our faces with grace, and delivered them from the godless flood and the darkness of folly; therefore they are worthily called blessed.

Deliver us from many possessions, and from the slavery of the enemy, and from the ancient supplanter, O wise Apostles of the Deliverer; and show forth the paths leading by divine hope unto the heavenly Kingdom.

O six-fold lamp of the holy disciples, earnestly pray unto the three-sunned Radiance to enlighten our souls who celebrate your all-radiant memory, and to deliver us from darkness and eternal condemnation.

*Theotokion:*

As a fiery tongs, thou didst receive in thy womb the spiritual Coal Which burns up our passions, and enlightens our souls, and dissolves the darkness of idol-madness, O pure and much-hymned Virgin.

*April 9. Holy Martyr Eupsychius of Caesarea:*[^29]

*Stichera, Tone 8, Podoben: "O martyrs of the Lord" (Мꙋ́ченицы гдⷭ҇ни):*

Torn by iron scrapers even unto thine inward parts, O valiant martyr, thou didst remain unbending, having the eye of thine understanding fixed on God Who strengthened thee, O Eupsychius, and Who put to shame the lawless; pray unto Him to save those who in faith honor thy memory today.

By the stroke of the sword thy divine head was severed, O valiant martyr; and instead of blood, thou didst marvelously pour forth milk and water, drawing the foolish unto knowledge, having received ineffable glory, and through divine intercessions granting great mercy unto all.

Courageously Eupsychius finished his sufferings and struggles, conquering the foolish by patience, and astonishing the unbelieving by the infliction of unbearable torments; therefore after his contest he is called blessed. By his prayers, O Lord, grant great mercy unto all.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*

My thoughts are unclean, and my lips deceitful, and all my deeds defiled; and what shall I do? How shall I meet the Judge? O Sovereign Lady Virgin, pray unto thy Son and Creator and Lord, that He may receive my spirit in repentance, as He alone is tenderly compassionate.

*Stavrotheotokion:*

Unable to endure seeing Thee asleep upon the Tree, O Child, so that Thou mightest grant divine and saving vigilance unto those who of old fell into the sleep of destruction through the fruit of transgression, the Virgin cried out weeping, whom we magnify.

*Canon, whose acrostic is: "Courageously I sing the sufferings of Eupsychius. The work of Joseph":*[^30]

*Tone 8. Ode 1. Heirmos:*

Having passed through the water as dry land, and having escaped the Egyptian evil, the Israelite cried: Unto our Deliverer and God let us sing!

*Troparia:*

As I courageously sing of thy sufferings, O blessed martyr Eupsychius, I pray thee, having boldness toward the Lord: snatch my soul from the tyranny of the passions.

Having thine understanding directed unto God, O blessed one, thou didst despise all the fair things of life; and having reigned over the passions, thou wast shown forth all-fair, O Eupsychius.

Truly thou wast patient when tested by torments and pains, astonishing all the godless, and recognized as a true sufferer.

*Theotokion:*

Having in the likeness of flesh borne Him Who came from thee, He renewed our nature that had grown old, O Maiden, and made the dead incorruptible.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*

Thou art the establishment of those who flee unto Thee, O Lord; Thou art the light of the darkened, and my spirit hymns Thee!

*Troparia:*

As a rational sheep, the foolish men brought thee unto the impious ruler, who foolishly served demons.

When thou wast raised up unto the height of thy martyrdom, an Angel appeared and made thee bold, O passion-bearer Eupsychius.

Thou didst remain constant in prison, confessing God and the Lord, and by His command wast shown forth as its keeper.

*Theotokion:*

The choir of sufferers sings a song of thanksgiving unto thee, O pure Maiden, having become victorious through thine intercessions.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 2, Podoben: "Thy tomb" (Гро́бъ тво́й):*

Having courageously finished the good fight, thou didst conquer the enemy adversary, O martyr, receiving honors and wonders from God; therefore the whole Church of the faithful calls thee blessed, honoring thee today with divine praise.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*

Guide us unto the path of repentance, who have ever turned aside unto the pathless wastes of evils, and have provoked the most good Lord to anger, O unwedded, blessed Mary, refuge of despairing men, dwelling-place of God!

*Stavrotheotokion:*

Beholding Thee dead, stretched out upon the Cross, O Christ, the all-undefiled Mother cried out: "O my Son, co-unoriginate with the Father and the Spirit, what is this Thine ineffable dispensation, whereby Thou hast saved the creation of Thine all-pure hands, O Bountiful One?"

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*

I have heard, O Lord, the mystery of Thy dispensation, I understood Thy works, and I glorified Thy Godhead!

*Troparia:*

Rejoicing, O martyr, thou didst distribute all thy possessions unto the poor, having acquired in return the non-stealable riches of martyrdom and ageless joy.

Holily enlightened, thou didst convict the darkness of the unhallowed who tried to compel thee to be led away through the deception of evil demons.

Those who clung to delusion were astonished at thee, who wast adorned with guileless ways, pouring forth words of life, O worthy of honor!

*Theotokion:*

Thou art holier than the heavenly hosts; show my mind pure, O Virgin, which has been subject to carnal passions.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*

Illumine us with Thy commandments, O Lord, and by Thy high arm grant us Thy peace, O Lover of mankind!

*Troparia:*

Thou didst patiently endure the wounds of the flesh, suspended, O blessed Eupsychius, intimately united to the Master of all creation.

Thou dost sing unto Christ while scraped along thy ribs, O martyr, and flayed of thy skin, most patient with manly mind.

While thou wast hymning Him, an Angel appeared unto thee, training thee for the struggles which thou didst finish, having been made strong in the Lord.

*Theotokion:*

Ever pray for all those who place their hopes in thee, O Sovereign Lady, that we may be delivered from the coming condemnation.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*

I will pour out my prayer unto the Lord, and unto Him will I declare mine afflictions; for my soul is filled with evils, and my life has drawn near unto Hades, and I pray like Jonah: Lead me up from corruption, O God!

*Troparia:*

The weight of torments indeed burdened thee, but it could not submerge thee, O martyr; for thou didst have Christ and God as Helper and Helmsman, guiding thee unto the quiet haven of His Kingdom, O Eupsychius!

Having shown the adversaries thy fair spiritual wealth, O martyr, thou wast shown forth terrible unto them, astonishing their malicious mind by thy patience; and from Christ thou didst receive the recompense of victory for thy struggles.

By thy blood poured out in righteousness, thou didst drown all the demonic host, and the multitude of the impious was engulfed; while the assembly of the faithful was richly watered, O Eupsychius, adornment of holy martyrs!

*Theotokion:*

Raise up all of me who am fallen, as one who opened the door to passions, and by thy good intercession lead me into the entrances of repentance, and save me, O thou who didst bear the Savior of all and Lord!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*

The Hebrew youths boldly trampled the flame in the furnace, and changed the fire into dew, crying out: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages!

*Troparia:*

Thou wast numbered with the assembly of martyrs and the choir of the divine bodiless hosts; having kept thy body from the irrationality of passions, thou didst fight valiantly in eminent manner, O thrice-blessed sufferer of Christ!

O thy good merchandise! For by the bitterness of blood thou didst purchase the Kingdom on high, O wise one, in which thou also singest: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages!

Thou fallest wounded by the sword, O martyr, and dost trample down Belial who boasted beyond measure, and with incurable wounds thou didst cut down him who fiercely assaulted thee, through thy firm understanding.

*Theotokion:*

The Prophet Daniel named thee a mountain through the Divine Spirit, O Virgin, from which Christ the Stone was cut without hands, and wrought the destruction of the deception of idols.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*

Having become victors over the tyrant and the flame by Thy grace, cleaving earnestly unto Thy commandments, the youths cried out: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparia:*

Thou wast a conqueror of tyrants and of unclean spirits, and the glory and praise of faithful men, O sufferer Eupsychius, of those who praise thee with sacred hymns and exceedingly exalt Christ unto all ages!

The sacred people of Caesarea hymn thee, O all-blessed one, for lying therein, O martyr, thy body pours forth streams of healings, drowning passions and divinely dewing the hearts of the faithful.

Having gone forth manfully unto the judgment seat of contests, O martyr, thou wast scraped even unto thy limbs and unto thine innermost structure; yet thou wast shown forth completely unbending, unshakable; therefore thou hast received the crowns of victory.

*Theotokion:*

O merciful Virgin, who didst bear the merciful Word, show mercy at least unto me who am unmerciful, despairing through immeasurable transgressions and not turning back, but having spent my life in slothfulness.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*

We confess thee truly as the Birthgiver of God, saved through thee, O pure Virgin, magnifying thee with the bodiless choirs!

*Troparia:*

Thou wast raised upon the wood, portraying the Passion of the Passionless One, Who diminished the passions of the pious out of tender mercy, O martyr Eupsychius.

A great wonder was seen at thy decease; for instead of blood, milk with water flowed forth when thy head was severed, O martyr!

By thy members, O all-blessed one, the one exceedingly glorified God was glorified in the glorification of thy memory, even as thou didst foretell.

Today the Church recognizes gladness in thy divine memory, O Eupsychius; therein save by thy prayers all who hymn thee.

*Theotokion:*

Enlighten me with the light of repentance, O Virgin, who am darkened by the night of sin and covered with the gloom of despondency.

*April 10. Holy Martyrs Terence, Pompeius, and those with them:*[^31]

*Stichera, Tone 4, Podoben: "As one valiant" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*

The company of many names of sufferers steadfastly endured diverse torments, and rejoicing passed unto our one God; it rejoices with myriads of sacred bodiless powers, because they conquered the many thousands of the serpent by steadfast resistance and by the grace of the Spirit.

Let most glorious Maximus and great Terence, all-wise Pompeius, Macarius, and the divine Africanus be honored with hymns, and with them the rest of the company of martyrs, who by their own blood purchased the Kingdom on high, and are filled with uninterrupted glory and filled with uninterrupted glory.

Neither famine nor tribulation, neither life nor death, O glorious ones, was able to separate you from the love of the Creator; therefore ye have inherited the heavenly Kingdom, and inexhaustible sweetness, and unending gladness; but ask also for us grace-given cleansing and mercy.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*

O all-pure one who didst contain in thy womb the uncontainable God, Who in His love for mankind became man, and received from thee our compound nature and deified me: despise me not now who am in affliction, but swiftly show compassion, and deliver me from diverse enmities and from the harm of the deceitful one.

*Stavrotheotokion:*

When the ewe lamb who bore Thee saw Thee, the Lamb and Shepherd, upon the Tree, she lamented and maternally addressed Thee: "O beloved Son, how art Thou suspended upon the Tree of the Cross, O Long-suffering One? How are Thy hands and Thy feet pierced with nails by the lawless, O Word, and Thou hast poured forth Thy blood, O Master?"

*Canon, whose acrostic is: "Save me, O multitude of fair-victorious martyrs. The work of Joseph":*[^32]

*Tone 8. Ode 1. Heirmos:*

Having passed through the water as dry land, and having escaped the Egyptian evil, the Israelite cried: Unto our Deliverer and God let us sing!

*Troparia:*

O glorious martyrs, entreat Christ Who has a multitude of tender mercies, that the multitude of my evils may be diminished, so that I may praise your solemnity.

Serving the living and true God, O passion-bearers, ye by no means brought honor unto lifeless gods, and offered yourselves as a living sacrifice unto the Giver of life.

The godless proclaimed a god-hated decree; and in the midst of the tribunal, forty martyrs proclaimed the incarnate Word, Who delivered the world by His guilelessness.

*Theotokion:*

I hymn thee, O Virgin, for thou alone, O all-hymned and all-pure one, didst give flesh unto God from thy precious blood, and in a manner transcending speech didst dreadfully give Him birth.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*

Thou art the establishment of those who flee unto Thee, O Lord; Thou art the light of the darkened, and my spirit hymns Thee!

*Troparia:*

With their sacred mouths the sufferers proclaimed the Lord before the tyrants, suffering patiently.

Unto being cut in pieces for the sake of Christ, the servants and sufferers gave themselves over, having loved Him through the kindling of divine love having loved Him through the kindling of divine love.

Those who were with divine Terence broke the bond of the snare of idolatrous service for the sake of Christ.

*Theotokion:*

Save me, O pure Mother who didst bear the most good Lord; calm the turbulent tempest of my soul.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who wast lifted up" (Вознесы́йсѧ):*

The forty passion-bearers of Christ, armed with the power of Christ, overthrew the demonic regiments through faith; and dying, they passed rejoicing unto an immortal end, and they ask for us remission of transgressions, who ever celebrate their solemnity.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*

Being a lover of sin, I beseech thee who didst bear the sinless God Who takes away the sins of the world, O all-pure one: have mercy on my much-sinning soul, and cleanse my many sins; for thou art the cleansing of sinners, and the salvation and protection of the faithful.

*Stavrotheotokion:*

Beholding Thee, Who wast begotten of the unoriginate Father and in the end born in the flesh from her, hanging upon the Cross, O Christ, she cried weeping: "Woe is me, most beloved Jesus! How art Thou, Who art glorified as God by the Angels, now willingly crucified by lawless men, O Son? I hymn Thee, O Long-suffering One!"

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*

I have heard, O Lord, the mystery of Thy dispensation, I understood Thy works, and I glorified Thy Godhead!

*Troparia:*

Ye were adorned, O martyrs, by the outpouring of pains, shining brighter than sapphire and gold, through the exhibition of steadfast struggles.

Having received life-bearing operation from the Life-originating Trinity, drive away every sickness, every deadness of souls and bodies.

"We will not deny the Lord, and unto senseless demons we will not sacrifice," the God-wise ones cried out, when examined with pains and torments.

*Theotokion:*

Beset by temptations, I call upon thee, O all-undefiled Birthgiver of God, for help: have compassion and save me, O thou who didst bear the Savior Word.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*

Rising early, we cry unto Thee: Lord, save us; for Thou art our God, besides Thee we know none other!

*Troparia:*

Ye cast down the strength of delusion, O passion-bearers, all-praised through the tearings of endurance.

Having dried up the depth of delusion by the streams of your blood, O blessed ones, ye watered every soul shown forth as a multi-flowered pasture, O divine martyrs, pouring forth the sweet fragrance of the Holy Spirit.

*Theotokion:*

Thy Son, O all-pure one, Who was born from thee as the Lover of mankind, appeared as the Deliverer of the human race.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*

I will pour out my prayer unto the Lord, and unto Him will I declare mine afflictions; for my soul is filled with evils, and my life has drawn near unto Hades, and I pray like Jonah: Lead me up from corruption, O God!

*Troparia:*

Strengthened by Thy might, O Jesus, the honorable and fair-victorious martyrs cast down the enemy's power, and were truly shown forth strong, fighting in the weakness of the flesh, and overthrowing the strong one.

Having conquered the multiform serpent, and having truly crushed his head with thy feet, O Terence, martyr of Christ, partaker with the holy Angels: thou hast received a victorious crown upon thy head.

A river of healings the martyrs pour forth, and cleanse passions, and wash away the defilement of souls, and submerge the regiments of demons; and it fills the hearts of all the pious with grace.

*Theotokion:*

The Prophet Habakkuk foresaw thee, O Birthgiver of God, as an overshadowing mountain, from which God, in a manner transcending speech, came forth incarnate, and saved the world which was heavily held fast by the tempest of grievous sin.

*Kontakion, Tone 2, Podoben: "In prayers" (Въ мл҃твахъ):*

The honorable memory of the martyrs has arrived today, gladdening all things, of all-wise Terence and his company; let us therefore warmly gather together, that we may receive healings; for they received grace from God the Holy Spirit to heal the infirmities of our souls.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*

The Hebrew youths in the furnace boldly trampled the flame, and changed the fire into dew, crying: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages!

*Troparia:*

Having acquired ears well obedient unto divine fulfillment, O wise ones who possess divine commands, ye sang with all zeal: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages!

O passion-bearers, ye opposed divine laws unto the commands of the lawless who ordered you to deny Christ, unto Whom we cry: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages!

Having surrendered your members unto punishments, ye renounced the body for the sake of the love of Him Who appeared upon earth and took flesh, unto Whom ye sing, O martyrs: Blessed art Thou, O God art Thou blessed!

*Theotokion:*

Having ineffably conceived Christ in Thy womb, O all-undefiled one, by the fruit of Thy womb thou dost set upright the forebears who corrupted below through the transgression of inappropriate sin.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*

Sevenfold did the Chaldean tyrant madly kindle the furnace for the pious; but having seen them saved by a greater power, he cried unto the Creator and Deliverer: Ye youths, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparia:*

At the tyrant's tribunal, God-wise Terence, and Africanus, and Maximus and Pompeius, Alexander and Zeno, and glorious Theodore, together with a threefold ten and threefold units who suffered with them, having confessed Christ the Savior of all, valiantly suffered.

Tortured for the sake of Christ, and beaten with ox sinews, and unmercifully scraped, O sufferers, cut with tridents, and cast unto beasts, and having their ribs burned with red-hot irons, they cried: Ye priests, bless; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto ages!

Bearing the word of life, the fair-victorious martyrs saved those who had been put to death in delusion, and put the enemies to death; and portraying Christ's death and His radiant Sufferings, through diverse torments they received a blessed end, singing unto Him unto all ages.

*Theotokion:*

Bring to life my deadened soul, raise up the fallen, heal the wounded, O all-undefiled one, by the spear which pierced the divine ribs of the Savior Who was incarnate from thy womb, Whom youths bless, priests hymn, and people supremely exalt unto all ages.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*

Every ear was filled with dread at the ineffable condescension of God, that the Most High willingly came down even unto the flesh, having become man from the virgin womb; therefore we faithful magnify the most pure Birthgiver of God.

*Troparia:*

The shrine of the martyrs pours forth healing, ever and most richly watered from the spring of the Divine Spirit; come, let us draw from it, sanctifying body, heart, and soul, and faithfully magnifying Christ the Savior, O lovers of the martyrs.

Like flowers of the divine tabernacle, like uncorrupt roses ye blossomed, and filled the Church with honorable sweet fragrance, having driven far away the foul-smelling delusion of the enemy by divine grace, O forty martyrs of Christ.

Today, ye faithful, let us faithfully praise and call blessed Maximus and Alexander together, and Theodore, Zeno, and Pompeius, Africanus and Terence, with the other sufferers, singing of their struggles.

The earth indeed received your blood and your suffering bodies, and heaven your divine spirits; the angelic hosts honorably assembled at your victory, O wise ones, and Christ crowned you as victors.

*Theotokion:*

Enlighten my soul, O door of light, which is darkened by grievous transgressions, obscured and in peril; and show me forth a partaker of the divine day, that I may glorify thee, the unashamed protectress of the faithful.

*April 11. Holy Hieromartyr Antipas, Bishop of Pergamum in Asia:*[^33]

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*

A sacred martyr, and an acceptable hierarch, and a true shepherd, an immovable pillar, a step of piety, a rampart of the Church, beauty of hierarchs, and fountain of wonders thou wast, O Antipas; therefore we bless thee piously with hymns, and celebrate thy divine solemnity today.

By the rudder of thy words, O glorious one, like an excellent pilot, thou didst steer the ship of the Church, sailing above the waves of delusion, and by the all-defiled wiles of the stranger not submerged; having suffered valiantly, and having conquered the enemy, thou didst pass unto Christ, Whose Sufferings thou didst emulate.

Thy relics ever pour forth wisdom, O God-inspired hierarch; and thou wast truly a sweet fragrance of Christ, having cast down the foul stench of delusion patiently as a sufferer, O martyr, by resistance; therefore we pray thee: remember all who remember thee in faith, having acquired boldness before the Lord.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*

Shower upon me, O Sovereign Lady, the abyss of thy mercy, and as thou art merciful, water my heart scorched by the burning heat of passions, O Maiden; and grant that I may unceasingly pour forth drops of compunction, I pray thee, through which I may be counted worthy of consolation, O pure one, which those who have sincerely wept are to receive.

*Stavrotheotokion:*

Beholding Christ the Lover of mankind crucified, O all-pure one, and His ribs pierced with a spear, weeping she cried out: "What is this, O my Son? What have the ungrateful people rendered unto Thee in return for the good things Thou didst do for them? And dost Thou hasten to leave me childless, O most beloved One? I marvel, O Tender-hearted One, at Thy voluntary crucifixion!"

*Troparion, Tone 4:*

*A partaker of the ways:*

*Or this one, the same Tone:*

Thou didst abolish the idol-deception, O Antipas, and having trampled down the power of the devil, before the god-fighters thou didst boldly confess Christ; therefore, taking up thine abode in the heights with the angelic ranks, offering doxology unto the Master of all, thou dost present a prayer of thanksgiving for us, bestowing the grace of healing. For this reason we honor thee, O hieromartyr Antipas: pray unto Christ God that He may save our souls.

*Canon, whose acrostic is: "I honor Antipas, radiant among martyrs. The work of Joseph":*[^34]

*Tone 4. Ode 1. Heirmos:*

Traversing the Red Sea deep with unwetted feet, ancient Israel of old, by the cross-form hands of Moses, conquered Amalek's power in the wilderness.

*Troparia:*

Illumined by the radiance of the three-sunned grace, shine forth upon those celebrating this thy radiant feast, delivering them from the gloom of passions, O martyr Antipas.

The Word of God showed thee forth, O all-wise one, as a true witness of His Sufferings, destroying impiety and conquering every deceit of demons.

Having put to death the carnal mind by many fastings, thou wast adorned with the sacred vestment of the divine priesthood; and bloodlessly thou didst sacrifice Him Who for thy sake became a sacrifice.

*Theotokion:*

With the splendors of thy light, O all-undefiled Maiden, enlighten my heart held fast in the night of passions and temptations, O thou who ineffably didst cause the Sun of righteousness to dawn upon all.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*

Thy Church rejoices in Thee crying: "Thou art my strength, O Lord, and my refuge and establishment!"

*Troparia:*

Thou didst pour forth rivers of teaching that water the faithful, and the turbid waters of polytheism thou didst dry up.

By the power of Christ, O blessed one, thou didst cut down the power of the persecutors, and didst patiently suffer, and wast crowned as a martyr.

Those who served demons thou didst persuade to honor the true God, Who out of the riches of His tender mercy took mortal flesh, O venerable one.

*Theotokion:*

O pure one, thou didst bear the Deliverer Who delivers mankind from the enemy's captivity; therefore we piously glorify thee.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 3, Podoben: "Of divine faith" (Бж҃твенныѧ вѣ́ры):*

Kindled with the divine coals of divine love, O martyr Antipas, thou didst quench the flame of godlessness; and having been cast into a red-hot caldron, thou didst pass unto the unwaning Light, O venerable father: entreat Christ God to grant us remission of sins.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*

He was not separated from the Divine Nature when He became flesh in thy womb, but having become man He remained God, the one Lord Who preserved thee after childbirth as an all-undefiled Virgin Mother even as before childbirth: earnestly entreat Him to grant us great mercy.

*Stavrotheotokion:*

Beholding suspended upon the Cross the One Who without pain sprouted forth from her, the undefiled Ewe Lamb of the Word, the incorrupt Virgin Mother, lamented in a manner befitting a mother and cried: "Woe is me, my Child! How dost Thou suffer willingly, wishing to deliver man from the passions of dishonor?"

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*

Beholding Thee, the Sun of righteousness, lifted up upon the Cross, the Church stood in her place, worthily crying: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparia:*

The opposition of the exceedingly lawless did not shake the pillar of thy heart, O martyr passion-bearer; therefore, kindled by zeal for the faith, thou wast shown forth stronger than fire.

Awakening those held fast by the slumber of impiety through the teachings of the Holy Spirit, O all-praised one, thou didst raise them unto the understanding of true knowledge.

Thou didst humble the demonic haughtiness through thine earnest elevations unto God, O Antipas, and by divine ascents being deified, and recognized as godlike.

*Theotokion:*

After childbirth even as before childbirth thou wast preserved, O incorrupt Virgin Mother; for thou didst bear the Creator of all, Who willingly took a human body from thee.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*

Thou, my Lord, hast come as a light into the world, a holy light turning from the gloom of ignorance those who hymn Thee in faith!

*Troparia:*

Thou didst ascend the mountain of virtues, O blessed one, and didst enter into the knowledge of the uttermost darkness, and didst converse with God.

By the bonds of thy words, O hierarch, thou didst catch out of the depth of godlessness the men who had gone astray, and by grace didst save them.

Thou didst surrender thy body to be devoured by fire, richly receiving from on high from God the divine holy dew.

*Theotokion:*

He Who is in the form of God, the transcendent God, took up His dwelling in thy womb, the Lord Who assumed my form, O all-undefiled one.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*

"I will sacrifice unto Thee with a voice of praise, O Lord," the Church cries unto Thee, having been cleansed from demonic blood by the blood which out of mercy flowed from Thy ribs.

*Troparia:*

Having crucified thy members through abstinence, O blessed one, having struggled in fasting, thou wast crowned with martyrdom's contests, which thou didst accomplish through firmness of mind, O Antipas.

Thou art a pillar before the judgment seat, preaching the Incarnation of Him Who emptied Himself for thy sake, and stood before Pilate's judgment seat, and by the Cross put the enemy to death.

The church is a place of healing for the sick, wherein, O blessed martyr, thy patient body lies, pouring forth the divine radiance of the gifts of the Spirit.

*Theotokion:*

Holy Jesus, Who rests in the saints, took up His dwelling in thy womb, O Virgin; glorify those who with true faith glorify Him, O Maiden.

*Kontakion, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who wast lifted up" (Вознесы́йсѧ):*

Thou wast a fellow-enthroned of the Apostles and the adornment of hierarchs, O blessed one; glorified through martyrdom, thou didst shine forth like the sun enlightening all, O sacred Antipas, and didst dissolve the deep night of godlessness; therefore we honor thee as a divine hieromartyr and granter of healings.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*

In the Persian furnace, the Abrahamite youths, burned more by love of piety than by flame, cried out: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!

Portraying the three youthful youths, O wise Antipas, thou didst stand in the midst of the fiercely heated bronze vessel as in the midst of a furnace, crying: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!

Shown forth as a partaker of the unapproachable Light, enlightened by God thou didst pass by the darkness of deceitful spirits, calling out: Blessed art Thou, O my God and Lord!

Through the working of the Spirit, O Antipas, thou dost calm the diverse passions of men, O martyr, conforming thyself unto Christ's Sufferings, calling out: Blessed art Thou, O my God and Lord!

*Theotokion:*

Incarnate in truth from thy virginal blood, O Maiden, the Word co-unoriginate with the Father, setting upright our ruin: Blessed art thou among women, O all-undefiled Birthgiver of God!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*

Stretching out his hands, Daniel shut the lions' open jaws in the pit; and the youths, lovers of piety, girded with virtue, quenched the power of fire, crying: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparia:*

Now not in mirrors, nor in dark riddles dost thou behold the ultimate of good things, but rather face to face, the shadows having passed away, crying out: We joyfully glorify Thee; bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

The lawless burned with fire as a lamb the divine sheep of Christ the Chief Shepherd, who well tended His flock, O all-wise Antipas, as he sang: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

Upon the rock of the divine commandments thou didst establish the steps of thy heart; by the winds of cruel torments and by flattering tempests thou wast not shaken, singing, O all-wise Antipas: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Theotokion:*

As the Mother of the Creator of all, as the divine temple of the Almighty, as the untilled earth that sprouted forth the uncultivated ear of grain, O Virgin Mother, preserve the faithful who cry: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*

The cornerstone uncut by hand was cut from thee, the unhewn mountain, O Virgin: Christ Who joined the separated natures; therefore rejoicing, we magnify thee, O Birthgiver of God!

*Troparia:*

Hierarchically thou didst finish thy holy and sacred course, having most sacredly completed thy most sacred life, rejoicing thou didst hasten unto Him Whom thou didst love, O most sacred martyr Antipas.

Adorned with the forms of virtues, and illumined with the glory of martyrdom, O all-blessed one, thou didst pass unto the sovereign Lord, deified by participation, O God-acceptable one.

Kindled with the love of the Lord, O martyr Antipas, with zeal thou didst enter into the red-hot vessel, burned as a whole burnt offering, and didst offer thyself as an acceptable sacrifice unto God, O father.

The sacred shrine of thy sacred relics pours forth myrrh of healings, O blessed one, driving away foul-smelling passions, and fragrantly perfuming the places of those who glorify thy memory.

*Theotokion:*

Holding in thy arms Christ Who holds all things by His nod, O pure one, entreat Him as God and Master to show compassion upon us who ever confess thee as the pure Birthgiver of God, O undefiled one.

*April 12. Our Venerable Father and Confessor Basil, Bishop of Parium:*[^35]

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera, Tone 8, Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder" (Ѡ҆ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*

With praises make bright the memory of radiant Saint Basil, and the assemblies of the Orthodox shall be filled with joy; for he loved righteousness, and chastity, purity, and meekness, and ever honorably and completely honored gentleness.

Emulating divine Paul, in exiles patiently, O holy Basil, thou didst end thy life, inexhaustibly comforted in necessities and afflictions, and ever journeying from place to place; but now thou hast found from God a sorrowless and unchanging habitation and delight.

As an acceptable hierarch, thou wast counted worthy to look upon things that never set, and to behold the transcendent things of the spiritual habitation, clothed in majesty, girded with righteousness, and face to face thou beholdest the beauty of the splendor of the intelligent powers, O exceedingly wealthy one.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*

Save me, O all-pure Birthgiver of God, who ineffably didst bear Christ the Savior for thee alone have I acquired as protectress, and an unassailable wall, and shelter and rejoicing, and the divine consolation of my soul. Deliver me, therefore, from the unsleeping worm and the eternal fire, O Mother of Christ God.

*Stavrotheotokion:*

"What is this sight beheld, which is seen by mine eyes, O Master? Holding all creation, Thou art lifted up upon the Tree, and Thou diest, Who givest life unto all!" the Birthgiver of God cried weeping, when she beheld Him Who ineffably shone forth from her, God and Man, lifted up upon the Cross.

*Canon, Tone 8:*

*Ode 1. Heirmos:*

Having traversed the water as dry land, and having escaped the Egyptian evil, the Israelite cried: Let us sing unto our Deliverer and God!

*Troparia:*

The ineffable and three-hypostatic Light shone upon thee, O father, in the unshakable Heavenly Kingdom, and the rejoicing of the righteous worthily received thee.

The gates of Eden were opened unto thee, and as holy thou hast been joined unto the saints, and hast been united unto the Church of the firstborn, O worthy of glory.

Having suffered lawfully, and having kept the priesthood pure unto the end, thou wast counted worthy to rejoice together with the heavenly priests.

*Theotokion:*

Having united Himself hypostatically unto flesh which He took from thee, the Creator of all came forth ineffably, O Mother of God, and was pleased to draw near unto men.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*

Thou art the establishment of those who flee unto Thee, O Lord; Thou art the Light of the darkened, and my spirit hymns Thee.

*Troparia:*

All-marvelous and most glorious, thy memory joyfully gladdens the assemblies of the Orthodox.

Having put off the cloud of the body, O venerable one, and having been loosed from bonds, thou didst draw near unto God.

A blessed end and the delight of the elect, O venerable one, as one chosen thou hast worthily received.

*Theotokion:*

Having thee as helper, I shall not be put to shame, O most pure Mother of God; having thee as protectress, I shall be delivered from mine enemies.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 3, Podoben: "Of divine faith" (Бж҃твенныѧ вѣ́ры):*

As a great sun unto the universe, thou didst shine forth by the courses of thy virtues; by the rays and splendors of wonders thou didst enlighten the gatherings of the faithful, and didst consume the gloom of passions. O father Basil, entreat Christ God to grant us great mercy.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*

Thou wast the divine tabernacle of the Word, O only most pure Virgin Mother, who in purity didst surpass the angels. Cleanse me, who above all have become dust, defiled by fleshly transgressions, with the divine waters of thy prayers, O pure one, bestowing great mercy.

*Stavrotheotokion:*

The undefiled Ewe Lamb of the Word, the incorrupt Virgin Mother, seeing Him Who without pain budded forth from her hanging upon the Cross, maternally lamenting cried out: "Woe is me, my Child! How dost Thou suffer willingly, wishing to deliver man from the passions of dishonor?"

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*

I have heard, O Lord, the mystery of Thy dispensation; I have understood Thy works, and have glorified Thy Divinity.

*Troparia:*

By thy prayers unto God, thou didst cast down the mist of the ill-minded heresies, and didst gladden the Church, O father.

In the heavenly tabernacles the Umpire of the contest settled thee, O father, having accepted thine unflagging resistance against the adversaries.

For the sake of the traditions kept by the Church of God, thou didst endure banishments, and receiving the victory, thou didst finish thy course.

*Theotokion:*

Entreat God, Whom thou didst bear, O pure Virgin, to grant remission of transgressions unto thy servants.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*

Rising early at dawn we cry unto Thee: Save us, O Lord! For Thou art our God, besides Thee we know none other.

*Troparia:*

Divine grace having taken up its dwelling within thee, O God-bearer, showed thee forth as a most faithful shepherd of the Church.

Having put to death the carnal mind of thy flesh and the passions, thou didst follow after the Spirit of life, O glorious one.

Having accomplished thy life soberly and righteously, O God-wise one, Thou didst receive the recompense of virtues.

*Theotokion:*

The cleansed, most holy temple, the fountain of dispassion thou didst bear, O all-immaculate Virgin Mother.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*

Cleanse me, O Savior, for many are my transgressions; and lead me up from the depth of evils, I pray, for unto Thee have I cried, and give ear unto me, O God of my salvation!

*Troparia:*

Enlightened by the beauty of the heavenly dwellings, thou didst leave the throne that is upon earth, O venerable one; and as most meek, thou didst inherit the incorruptible inheritance in the land of the meek.

Deriding the deception of blasphemous heretics, thou didst most zealously teach the divine and pious understanding of the Orthodox to venerate the Savior's icon, O worthy of marvels.

Thy desire toward God was fulfilled, O most glorious one, for thou didst pass unto Him divinely rejoicing; and now, O father, thou beholdest the brightness and beauty of the saints.

*Theotokion:*

Mankind was delivered from mortality and corruption, for ineffably thou didst bear the Life-giver according to nature, O all-immaculate Virgin, for the benefaction of those who faithfully praise thee.

*Kontakion, Tone 2, Podoben: "Seeking the highest" (Вы́шнихъ и҆щѧ̀):*

Illumined with the light of the Highest, O blessed one, thou dost enlighten all who with love honor thy venerable suffering, O father Basil, sacred sufferer: pray unceasingly unto Christ God for us all.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*

The Hebrew youths in the furnace boldly trampled upon the flame, and turned the fire into dew, crying: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages!

*Troparia:*

O the ineffable rejoicing, which thou wast counted worthy to receive, O all-blessed one, dwelling in the immaterial Light, and singing: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages!

Fairly adorned with the crown of divine splendor, O all-blessed one, thou rejoicest crying with the hosts on high: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages!

Being an initiate of the Trinity, a minister of heavenly mysteries, make the Master merciful unto us who cry: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*

The ancient curse thou didst turn into blessing, O pure one, having borne the Son of God, unto Whom we all cry: Blessed art Thou unto all ages!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*

Victors over the tyrant and the flame, having become such by Thy grace, strictly keeping Thy commandments, the youths cried: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparia:*

Delivered from the afflictions of this life and from cruel exile,[^36] thou hast received joy and rejoicing beyond words, O all-blessed one, singing: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto the ages!

Warmly opposing with spiritual teachings, O father, thou didst easily undo the tongue-harming blather of the blasphemers, O sacred initiate, singing: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto the ages!

How fair is the place wherein thou hast settled, O sacredly revealed one; how fair, O father, is the habitation where thou abidest, singing: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto the ages!

*Theotokion:*

All we faithful have acquired thee as protectress, O most pure one, and being delivered from cruel afflictions by thy prayers, we cry unto Christ: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto the ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*

Truly we confess thee as the Birthgiver of God, saved through thee, O pure Virgin, magnifying thee with the bodiless choirs.

*Troparia:*

Passing this thy life among the venerable,[^37] O father, and having pleased God by thy works, thou hast received the Heavenly Kingdom, O wise one.

Having finished the course, kept the faith, and accomplished the contest, O Basil, rejoicing thou hast received the crown of righteousness.

Thou hast received the divine calling, O all-blessed one, and the perfect fulfillment of desires; therefore now cease not to pray for thy flock.

*Theotokion:*

Turn not away from the stream of my tears, O Virgin who didst bear Christ, Who wipes away every tear from every face.

*April 13. Holy Hieromartyr Artemon, Presbyter of Laodicea:*[^38]

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera, Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the heavenly hosts" (Небе́сныхъ чинѡ́въ):*

Living upon earth, thou wast united with the bodiless hosts, and with them thou unceasingly servest the Trinity, O God-blessed Artemon; whence, walking ever as a heavenly man, thou teachest earthly men the things on high by divine wonders.

Enlightened by divine radiance, thou didst waste thy body with many torments, and didst pass unto the immaterial dawn; loosed by which through thy supplications, O wise one, thou hast dispelled the dark night of passions, enlightening our souls.

For those who faithfully celebrate thy memory, O hieromartyr, and who sing the many contests of thy venerable martyrdom, cease not ever to pray that they may be preserved from misfortunes and afflictions, and that they may be delivered from future corruption, O Artemon, martyr and preacher of God.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*

Having slipped by rotten habits, I lie prostrate, and I flee unto thy calm, O Virgin; deliver me from the storm of the adversaries and from manifold temptations, that I may unceasingly hymn thy grace, O Birthgiver of God, Mother Ever-Virgin.

*Stavrotheotokion:*

Standing by the Cross of thy Son and God, and beholding His long-suffering, the pure Mother wept and said: "Woe is me, my sweetest Child! Why dost Thou suffer these things unjustly, O Word of God, that Thou mayest save mankind?"

*Canon, whose acrostic is: Alphabetical:*[^39]

*Tone 8. Ode 1. Heirmos:*

Having traversed the water as dry land, and having escaped the Egyptian evil, the Israelite cried: Let us sing unto our Deliverer and God!

*Troparia:*

Opening the treasures of understanding, the Giver of these words richly bestows grace upon those who in faith praise His sufferer Artemon.

Wounded from thy youth by the arrow of Christ's sweetest love, O wise Artemon, bodily passion did not cut thee off from this love.

Stooping diligently over the Scriptures, O Artemon, from the divine depth thou didst wisely draw the wealth of the best understanding, wherewith thou hast enriched all the faithful.

*Theotokion:*

By thy childbirth, we earthborn have truly been lifted up from earth to the heavenly height, O pure Birthgiver of God, and have been numbered with the angelic choirs.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*

Thou art the establishment of those who flee unto Thee, O Lord; Thou art the Light of the darkened, and my spirit hymns Thee.

*Troparia:*

Clothed with the power of the Spirit, thou didst conquer the armies aligned against the Creator, O most valiant Artemon.

Thou didst quench the furnace of godlessness, O venerable one, with the rain showers of thy suffering blood, O most glorious Artemon.

Seething with divine longing, and kindled with divine love, O venerable one, thou didst suffer for the Savior.

*Theotokion:*

Thy childbirth loosed the guilt of our sins, O pure one, and bound all the faithful with righteousness.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Speedily anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ):*

Having striven in sacred confessions, and having ended thy life as a martyr thou didst ascend unto that life, O wise Artemon, receiving an unfading crown from God. Therefore we celebrate thy holy memory, crying: Remember us, O hieromartyr, standing before Christ!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*

By thy divine childbirth, O pure one, thou didst renew the mortal nature of earthborn men that had decayed in passions, and thou didst raise all up from death unto the life of incorruption. Therefore, as is due, all we bless thee, O most glorified Virgin, even as thou didst foretell.

*Stavrotheotokion:*

When thy most pure Mother beheld Thee lifted up upon the Cross, O Word of God, maternally lamenting she cried: "What is this new and strange wonder, O my Son? How dost Thou, the Life of all, taste death, wishing to give life unto the dead, as Thou art tender-hearted?"

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*

I have heard, O Lord, the mystery of Thy dispensation; I have understood Thy works, and have glorified Thy Divinity.

*Troparia:*

Thy sacrifice, O glorious one, ascended unto the heavenly altar, and has now become acceptable unto the Master of all.

Having lifted up thy mind unto the better vision, O venerable Artemon, Thou wast taught the mysteries of Christ, for which Thou didst also suffer.

Held fast upon the immovable rock of the Creator's love, O venerable one, thou wast not shaken at all in mind by the assaults and disturbances of the enemies.

*Theotokion:*

Possessing thee, O Sovereign Lady, as an inexhaustible treasure of gifts, we all receive from thee the unstinting gifts of wonders.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*

Enlighten us by Thy commandments, O Lord, and by Thy high arm grant us Thy peace, O Lover of mankind!

*Troparia:*

Opposing the waves of godlessness, O wise one, thou didst save obedient people, guiding them unto the haven.

Shining forth as a bright lamp in the night of deception, O venerable one, with the light of the knowledge of God through virtues, thou didst direct men toward the Light.

Having become an initiate of Christ's ineffable mysteries, O venerable Artemon, thou didst offer thyself unto Him as an acceptable sacrifice through suffering.

*Theotokion:*

Cleansing me from the defilement and foulness of passions, O Bride of God, by thy prayers present my soul pure unto God.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*

Cleanse me, O Savior, for many are my transgressions; and lead me up from the depth of evils, I pray, for unto Thee have I cried, and give ear unto me, O God of my salvation!

*Troparia:*

Lawfully having suffered for Christ, O Artemon, thou didst refute the impious decree of the lawless enemies; therefore thou wast shown forth as a lawful martyr, crowned with divine victories.

Having dried up the turbid streams of godlessness by the fire of thy words, thou didst pour forth clear rivers of the faith of the knowledge of God unto the faithful, O venerable Artemon.

Having armed thy mind against the contending serpent, O glorious one, by a noble struggle thou didst cast him down and show him to be dead, O Artemon, and from the Lord thou hast received the crown of victory.

*Theotokion:*

We mortals have become immortal by the divine grace of thy childbirth, and from the first corruption we have been delivered, O pure Birthgiver of God, and we have been adorned with the garment of incorruption.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*

The youths who went down from Judea to Babylon in days of old trampled upon the flame of the furnace by their faith in the Trinity, singing: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Troparia:*

Kindled with the divine fire of the Master's love, O glorious martyr, thou didst willingly and zealously enter the contest of labors, crying: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

Clothed with the Comforter's power, O martyr, like the Apostles thou didst conquer the hosts of deception by noble endurance, singing: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

Having adorned thy life with chastity and thy body with purity, thou didst purely perform the sacred ministry unto Christ, O Artemon, singing and chanting songs of thanksgiving: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Theotokion:*

That He might draw all unto heavenly glory, the Creator of all was pleased to be born of thee, O Birthgiver of God, and as Lover of mankind and Benefactor God, to live in the flesh with us.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*

The King of Heaven, Whom the hosts of angels hymn, praise and supremely exalt unto all ages!

*Troparia:*

As thou didst worthily prefer the Heavenly King above all earthly things, rejoice together with Him unto the ages, O Artemon!

The assaults of the torturers in no wise drew thee away from His love, O arrow-bearer,[^40] whose soul was exalted unto the Creator.

Enlightened by the flashes of divine light, O passion-bearer, having entered into the unwaning altar of mysteries as an initiate of ineffable things.

*Theotokion:*

The One Who took human nature from thee beyond mind and word, O Birthgiver of God, made the sons of men His own.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*

Truly we confess thee as the Birthgiver of God, saved through thee, O pure Virgin, magnifying thee with the bodiless choirs.

*Troparia:*

Having passed through the winter of godlessness, O martyr, thou didst reach the springtime of piety, and wast counted worthy of the light of the Sun of glory.

Having foreseen with the spiritual and intelligent eyes the Lord standing at thy right hand, thou wast not shaken, O Artemon, by the waves of torments.

As thou hast been counted worthy of the divine and ineffable glory, O passion-bearer, together with the passion-bearers and disciples of Christ: entreat Him,[^41] as He is tender-hearted.

*Theotokion:*

From on high, O Birthgiver of God, visit our poverty through thy tender mercy, and deliver us from corruption and misfortunes.

*April 14. Our Father among the Saints and Confessor Martin, Pope of Rome:*[^42]

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera, Tone 8, Podoben: "What shall we call you" (Что̀ ва́съ нарече́мъ):*

What shall we now proclaim thee, O Martin? All-glorious teacher of Orthodox teachings, true supreme keeper of the sacred divine commandments, most true rebuker of falsehood, most valiant fellow-worker of the Word, most sacred celebrant of sacred things, most honorable worker of wonders: pray that our souls may be saved!

What shall we now call thee, O Martin? A river filled with spiritual waters, ever watering souls unto intelligent fruitfulness; a candlestick shining forth the light of piety; a mountain pouring forth divine rejoicing; an expounder of divine words, a rebuker of every heresy: pray that our souls may be saved!

What shall we now proclaim thee, O Martin? All-praised sacred minister of the true Tabernacle, God-befitting mediator between God and creation, cup pouring forth divine drink, lamp as holding the word of life; proceeding from the West, yet appearing in the East: pray that our souls may be saved!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*

Unto whom hast thou become like, O wretched soul, never looking up unto repentance, and not fearing the fire that awaits the wicked? Arise, and call upon the only speedy protectress, and cry aloud: O Virgin Mother, pray unto thy Son and our God to deliver me from the snare of the deceitful one!

*Stavrotheotokion:*

When the Ewe Lamb beheld her Lamb willingly stretched out upon the Wood of the Cross, maternally suffering she cried with weeping: "O my Son, what is this strange sight! Giving life unto all as Lord, how art Thou put to death, O Long-suffering One, granting resurrection unto earthly men? I glorify Thy great condescension, O my God!"

*Canon, the work of Joseph:*[^43]

*Tone 8. Ode 1. Heirmos:*

The cleaving rod cleaved the deep that was not cleaved, and the sun beheld land which it had not seen; the water drowned the cruel enemy, and Israel traversed the impassable deep; and the song was chanted: Let us sing unto the Lord, for gloriously has He been glorified!

*Troparia:*

Many afflictions didst thou endure for Christ God, O Martin, and now thou hast departed unto the sorrowless life, having fought the good fight; therefore ease the cruel sicknesses of my soul, that I may sing of thee, enlightened by thy prayers.

Two essences, and two wills, and dual operations bearing, O blessed one, didst thou teach concerning the One of the honorable Trinity, Christ the transcendent God; and those who do not honor Him thus thou didst entirely reject, O most sacred Martin.

Those who foolishly and irrationally fell sick with heresy, minding one will in the one Christ, thou didst openly refute, O Martin, adorned with true understanding and faith, and rejoicing didst cry aloud: Let us sing unto the Lord, for gloriously has He been glorified!

*Theotokion:*

He Who is invisible according to divine nature was seen as an Infant from thee, having become Man, bearing in truth dual wills and operations in one Hypostasis; entreat Him, O all-immaculate one, to save all of us who sing of thee with love.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*

Plant Thy fear, O Lord, in the hearts of Thy servants, and be Thou an establishment unto us who in truth call upon Thee!

*Troparia:*

Filled with divine zeal, O all-blessed one, thou didst gather a sacred council, O Martin, and didst establish the teaching of the Church.

In the midst of the council thou didst reject Pyrrhus, Sergius, and Theodore, and Cyrus,[^44] and those like unto them, who blessed the ill-glorifying, O father.

Driven by force from Rome, thou didst go forth as a sun emitting rays, O venerable one, enlightening all Orthodox men.

*Theotokion:*

Him Who is equal in honor and co-enthroned with the Father, thou didst bear beyond word, bearing two operations and two wills, O all-immaculate Maiden.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who wast lifted up" (Вознесы́йсѧ):*

Adorned with sacred confessions, and having ended thy life as a martyr, thou didst ascend unto the heavens, O wise Martin, crowned with an unfading crown from God. Therefore we celebrate thy holy memory, crying: Remember us, O sacredly blessed one, standing before Christ!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*

After God I flee unto thy divine protection, lowly as I am, and falling down I pray: Have mercy, O most pure one, for my sins have risen above my head, and I fear the torment, O Birthgiver of God, and I tremble; make supplication, O pure one, unto thy Son to deliver me from them.

*Stavrotheotokion:*

She who in the last times bore Thee in the flesh, Who wast begotten of the unoriginate Father, seeing Thee hanging upon the Cross, O Christ, cried: "Woe is me, my most beloved Jesus! How art Thou, Who art glorified as God by angels, now willingly crucified by lawless men, O Son? I hymn Thee, O Long-suffering One!"

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*

Thou didst mount upon horses, Thine Apostles, O Lord, and didst take their reins in Thy hands; and Thy riding became salvation unto those who faithfully sing: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparia:*

Unjustly driven from thy throne, O wise one, a righteous man by unrighteous men, thou wast pleased to suffer, that the churchly teaching might remain safe and unharmed, O father.

Standing in the midst of the exceedingly lawless as a lawful sufferer, O God-acceptable hierarch, and insulted by them and mocked, and wretchedly dragged along, thou didst remain unswerving in mind.

As the arrows of infants didst thou account the darts of all those who afflicted thee, O father Martin; and having suffered patiently, thou wast driven and beaten, as is known, and shut up in prisons.

*Theotokion:*

One of the Trinity, known in two essences with divine-humanity, thou didst bear, O all-immaculate Virgin, as God and Man, bearing wills proper unto both natures.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*

From the night of ignorance, wandering ever upon the soul-destroying path, by the light of Thy knowledge, O Lord, guiding me beforehand, direct me unto the path of Thy commandments.

*Troparia:*

By thy most sacred word, O father, thou didst openly establish the immaterial teaching, and didst cast down the heretical hosts, enduring exiles, and prisons, and many afflictions.

Thou didst not give slumber unto thine eyelids, O venerable one, until thou becamest a dwelling for God; and thou didst scatter all the delusion of heresy, having striven as a martyr.

Hostilely, like wild beasts, the enemies dragged thee with chains, O Martin, having bound thee mockingly in the midst of the city, reproaching and blaspheming, and falling shamelessly upon thee.

*Theotokion:*

Having borne the uncircumscribed Word in the flesh without knowledge of wedlock, O Virgin, put to death the passions of my flesh, and give life unto my soul that is deadened by transgressions.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*

The abyss of sins and the storm of transgressions trouble me, and drag me into the deep of violent despair; but stretch forth Thy strong hand unto me as unto Peter, O Helmsman, and save me!

*Troparia:*

The crafty one was crushed beneath thy sacred feet, O God-bearer, and the doorless mouths of crafty men are stopped by thy word; and the divine teaching is shown forth, shining more brightly than the sun.

The deceivers stretch thee out unsparingly and bind thee with cords, while thou dost convict every deception and tear asunder the heretical union by the bond of thy divine teachings, O most sacred hierarch, father Martin.

Thou didst stand before the tribunal of the judges,[^45] confounding the delusion of those who proclaim one will in Christ, O father, and visibly adorning thyself with crowns of martyrdom, O God-blessed Martin.

*Theotokion:*

The laws of nature are dissolved in thee, for ineffably thou bearest the Creator of all, O all-hymned Birthgiver of God, as both God and Man, having two wills and operations, O Virgin Mother.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*

The children having arrived from Judea in Babylon of old, by faith in the Trinity trampled down the flame of the furnace, singing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparia:*

Bereft of the dual operations and wills of Christ, O father, they deprived thee of thy throne, removing thee by a distant exile, who didst cry aloud: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

Having passed through long-enduring prisons as a keeper of the blameless faith, thou didst diminish the heretical darkness, and didst enlighten the faithful to sing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

Having trusted like a lion, O father, thou didst boldly leap upon the lawless ones—Theodore, and Cyrus, and Sergius, and Pyrrhus—and those like-minded with them, severing them from the holy Church of Christ.

*Theotokion:*

Ineffably born of thee, O all-immaculate one, in two wills and two operations, He saves those who are minded thus and cry: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*

The God-proclaiming children in the furnace, trampling the flame together with the fire, sang: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparia:*

Constrained, O father, by infirmities, and cruel exiles, and multiform torments, thou didst receive the crowns of martyrdom as a martyr.

Being driven out, O Martin, thou wast counted worthy of the God-chosen beatitude, and becamest near unto righteousness, having driven out the unrighteous heresy from the midst.

Having adorned the divine throne of Peter, and on his rock preserved the Church unshaken, O Martin, with him thou hast been glorified.

*Theotokion:*

More holy than the heavenly hosts, and higher than the Cherubim, O Mother of God, save me, and make me superior to fleshly passions.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*

Thou didst surpass the ordinances of nature, having conceived the Creator and Lord, and becamest the door of salvation unto the world; therefore, O Birthgiver of God, unceasingly we magnify thee.

*Troparia:*

Having been a most sacred celebrant, and both a confessor and a martyr, with the patriarchal choirs thou ever rejoicest worthily, O Martin; therefore in faith we call thee blessed.

Like a radiant sun, O blessed one, thou didst shine forth unto the east from the west; with the beams of piety thou didst enlighten the whole earth, and didst drive away the profoundest darkness of those who fall into heresy.

Let us stand in the house of our God, proclaiming the sufferings, and the struggles, and the confessions of our undefiled father Martin, and let us bless him with love.

Thy holy departure we celebrate today with splendor; fill with joy the thoughts of those who praise thee, O God-wise hierarch, wherein remember us all.

*Theotokion:*

Obedient unto the voices of the Fathers, O pure one, faithfully we confess the One of the Trinity born of thee in two natures and wills, and unceasingly we magnify thee.

*April 15. Holy Apostles Aristarchus, Pudens, and Trophimus of the Seventy:*[^46]

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera, Tone 1, Podoben: "O all-praised martyrs" (Всехва́лнїи мч҃ницы):*

By the word of divine knowledge, O all-wise ones, ye completely delivered the people from ignorance, and brought them as saved ones unto the Word Who shone forth from the unbegotten Father, O Apostles; entreat Him to grant unto our souls peace and great mercy.

Let us praise Pudens, and clearly Aristarchus, and divine Trophimus, the true table-companions of Christ; for they dissolved the winter of delusion by the warmth of divine grace, and have now taken up their abode in the unwaning radiance, receiving the reward of their labors, and being divinized by ineffable communion.
