Following Paul, your glorious teacher, O glorious ones, ye endured many afflictions, being shut up in prisons, partaking of sufferings; and at the end, having your heads severed with the sword, ye suffered. Entreat Christ to grant unto our souls peace and great mercy.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*

Woe is me! What shall become of me? Mind, and soul, and body have I defiled with transgressions. What shall I do? How shall I escape the unendurable flame, and the unbreakable and eternal bonds? But, O all-immaculate one, before the end entreat thy Son to grant me remission.

*Stavrotheotokion:*

The undefiled Virgin, seeing Him of old upon the Tree Whom she bore from her seedless womb, unable to endure the inward wounding, tearing her hair, said: "O Thou Who by Thy nod upholdest the whole creation, how art Thou lifted up upon the Cross as one condemned, desiring by all means to save mankind?"

*Canon, whose acrostic is: "Unto the divine disciples a divine song I chant." The composition of Joseph. Tone 6:*[^47]

*Ode 1. Heirmos:*

Traversing the deep on foot as upon dry land, Israel, seeing Pharaoh the pursuer drowned, cried aloud: Let us sing a song of victory unto God!

*Troparia:*

Standing before the throne of God as servants, as ministers of Christ, as spiritual luminaries, ask enlightenment for us who honor your memory, O Apostles.

Having appeared on earth in His tender mercy, He chose you as disciples and ministers with many others, proclaiming unto all His divine will.

Joining the most sacred choir of the sacred seventy disciples of Christ, with Paul ye enlighten the whole earth with the teachings of divine light.

Most sacred and glorious Pudens, and wise Aristarchus with Trophimus, shone forth upon the earth like radiant stars, driving away the gloom of delusion.

*Theotokion:*

That He might unite earthly things with the heavenly through the multitude of tender mercy, O all-pure one, God took flesh from thee, and remained what He was even after the Incarnation.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*

There is none holy as Thou, O Lord my God, Who hast exalted the horn of Thy faithful, O Good One, and established us upon the rock of Thy confession.

*Troparia:*

Being with glorious Paul, in him ye traversed the whole world like stars with the sun, driving away the darkness of ignorance by the light of the sacred proclamation.

By all manner of bodily sufferings the disciples of the Word took away painful sin from the midst, betrothing painless rest on high unto the faithful.

By divine struggles truly ye destroyed all the enemy's malice; and casting down the idol temples, ye made men temples unto God.

*Theotokion:*

Having nourished with milk the Nourisher of all, O Virgin, Who was born without corruption from thy most pure womb, and rebuilding mankind who was shattered by corruption.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 1, Podoben: "Thy tomb, O Savior" (Гро́бъ тво́й сп҃се):*

Having shone forth upon the world in a God-befitting manner like the morning dawn, by faith ye drove away the deep darkness of delusion, O Apostles, and enlightened souls. Therefore the whole radiant Church sings today of your light-bearing memory, magnifying Christ.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*

O Hope of Christians, all-holy Virgin, Whom thou didst bear as God beyond mind and word, unceasingly entreat with the heavenly hosts to grant remission of all our sins and amendment of life unto those who ever glorify thee in faith and love.

*Stavrotheotokion:*

The undefiled ewe-lamb, seeing the Lamb and Shepherd hanging dead upon the Tree, weeping said, crying aloud like a mother: "How shall I endure Thine ineffable condescension, O my Son, and Thy voluntary sufferings, O all-good God?"

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*

Christ is my power, God and Lord, the pure Church sings in God-befitting manner, crying aloud from a pure mind, keeping festival in the Lord.

*Troparia:*

The most radiant Trinity kindled as a thrice-radiant lamp for the sacred ones Pudens, Trophimus, and Aristarchus, enlightening the earth with the divine word.

Ye were shown forth as wise traveling companions unto Paul, O glorious ones, and with joy ye endured multiform persecutions and afflictions with him for the sake of the life to come.

By the streams of your blood suffering ye cleansed all creation, and by Christ's power ye abolished the defiled blood offered unto demons, O Apostles.

*Theotokion:*

O my strength, all-pure one, and refuge, and unconquerable wall, who art an intercessor before God, deliver me from eternal flame and Gehenna.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*

With Thy divine light, O Good One, enlighten with love the souls of those who awake early unto Thee, I pray, to know Thee, O Word of God, as the true God, calling them from the gloom of sin.

*Troparia:*

Having cast down the carved images and idol temples, O glorious ones, by grace ye built up the hearts of men like a tabernacle fallen down by transgressions, as God-proclaimers.

Truly being the most glorious horses of God, having troubled the sea of godlessness, by your divine preaching ye gave life unto those previously drowning therein.

Visited by God through your divine visitations unto those who were in the disease of idol-madness, ye saved those who were drowning, having become saviors unto us.

*Theotokion:*

Sacredly the sacred Isaiah of old, enlightened by the divine Spirit, cried aloud: "Behold, a Virgin shall receive God Incarnate, for His ineffable tender mercy!"

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*

Beholding the sea of life surging with the storm of temptations, running unto Thy calm haven I cry unto Thee: Bring up my life from corruption, O Greatly Merciful One!

*Troparia:*

Let all-wise Trophimus, and Aristarchus together, and marvelous Pudens, as disciples of the Word and sacred companions of Paul, be praised with hymns.

Filled with the waters of the Spirit, O all-wise ones, truly ye watered every soul with teachings, taking them from the midst of the fire of delusion by divine grace.

United in the Spirit, ye never had separation in bodies; for together with Paul the preacher ye were beheaded in your heads, and with him ye are glorified.

*Theotokion:*

Marvelous is thy conception, marvelous, O Maiden, and fearful is thy childbearing beyond understanding, reconciling unto God those who were estranged of old, and restoring the primal beauty.

*Kontakion, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Ꙗ҆ви́лсѧ є҆сѝ дне́сь):*

This honorable apostolic feast has appeared, enlightening us and driving away sinful gloom, unto those who cry: The Light has appeared, and redemption has come!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*

The angel made the furnace dew-bearing for the venerable children, but the command of God, which consumed the Chaldeans, persuaded the tormentor to cry: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparia:*

Ye quenched the furnace of ungodliness with the showers of honorable preaching, as Apostles; and as divine sacred sufferers, by the streams of your blood ye burned up the matter of idols,[^48] O wise ones; therefore ye are glorified.

Ye were shown forth as second disciples of Christ after the twelve disciples, O glorious ones; for having traversed the whole earth with Paul, ye endured many afflictions, and with him received a sacred end.

Ye were adorned with your beautiful wounds, O Aristarchus and Trophimus, and shone forth more brightly than any gold, O glorious ones, with sacred Pudens, truly ever entreating Christ for us.

*Theotokion:*

Thou wast shown forth as blessed of the Holy Spirit, having given flesh unto the Angel of Great Counsel, Who sent forth His divine disciples into the world like divine lightning, O pure one, to enlighten the faithful.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*

From the flame Thou didst pour forth dew upon the venerable ones, and by water didst burn up the sacrifice of the righteous one; for Thou dost all things, O Christ, solely by willing them: Thee we highly exalt unto all ages!

*Troparia:*

Cutting down the roots of godlessness with the divine sickle, ye planted the true faith and perfect knowledge in pious hearts, O God-proclaiming ones.

With the divine word ye renewed hearts, and sowing the seed of knowledge, O wise ones, ye reaped a fruitful ear of corn, the multitude of saved ones, O Apostles.

Let the unyielding pillars of Christ's Church, the divine clusters of the divine vine, dropping down the sweetness of life—Aristarchus, and Pudens, and Trophimus—be honored.

*Theotokion:*

All shattered mankind hast thou renewed, O Virgin, by thy new childbirth renewing the bonds of nature beyond nature, O thou who art full of grace, Mother of the living God.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*

It is not possible for men to see God, upon Whom the angelic ranks dare not gaze; but through thee, O all-immaculate one, the Word Incarnate became visible unto men, Whom magnifying, with the heavenly hosts we call thee blessed.

*Troparia:*

The strength of the proud one was taken away, and his exalted raging fell; for having wrestled with him through bodily weakness, the saints conquered and destroyed him, and delivered the faithful from his tyranny.

Most truly ye appeared as dust unto delusion, and as guileless instructors unto men, and becamest fellow-citizens of the angels, singing with them: Holy, Holy, Holy, Father, and Son, and Holy Spirit!

Healing bodily infirmities and spiritual diseases, O Apostles of the Lord, and working marvelous wonders, ye drew all who were in ignorance unto the knowledge of the true light; therefore ye are called blessed.

Now in the heavens ye enjoy the celebration of the immaterial Mind, O Apostles, having received the blessed hope: O Trophimus, preacher of God, all-excellent Pudens, and glorious Aristarchus, make remembrance of us!

*Theotokion:*

From the flame of Gehenna deliver me, O thou who hast borne the unendurable Fire beyond understanding, and kindle my soul unto the love of God, O all-pure Maiden, who gavest flesh unto Him Who came to cast the fire of knowledge upon the earth, O full of God's grace.

*April 16. Holy Virgin-Martyrs Agape, Irene, and Chionia:*[^49]

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera, Tone 4, Podoben: "As one valiant" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*

Incorrupt virginity and all-marvelous martyrdom ye brought unto Christ, O honorable virgins, having cast down the godless raging of delusion by the power of the Cross and with manly understanding; therefore Christ's whole Church celebrates your holy, light-bearing, and solemn memory.

At Chionia's suffering, and Agape's resistance, and the martyr Irene's unconquerable strength, how the blessed angelic ranks marveled: how by manly wrestlings, having shattered the invisible enemy, they richly received the crown of victory from the hand of the Life-giver.

Being perfected by fire with inflamed mind, ye quenched the flame of worldliness; and keeping the spiritual lamp unquenched, ye entered with Christ into the heavenly bridal chamber; and with the sparks of wonders ye burned up the passions, O passion-bearers, and suffered valiantly, conquering the hosts of demons.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*

With the showers of the All-Holy Spirit, O all-pure one, dew my thought, who didst bear Christ, the Drop that washed away the measureless iniquities of men innumerably by His compassions; dry up the fountain of my passions, and make me worthy of the stream of the living food evermore by thy prayers.

*Stavrotheotokion:*

When the ewe-lamb who bore Thee beheld Thee, the Lamb and Shepherd, upon the Tree, she lamented and cried aloud unto Thee like a mother: "O my beloved Son, how art Thou suspended upon the Tree of the Cross, O Long-suffering One? How are Thy hands and Thy feet pierced with nails by the lawless, O Word, and Thou hast poured forth Thy blood, O Master?"

*Canon, the composition of Joseph. Tone 8:*[^50]

*Ode 1. Heirmos:*

Let us sing unto the Lord, Who led His people through the Red Sea, for He alone has been gloriously glorified!

*Troparia:*

Entreat grace from heaven for me, O martyrs of the Lord, who praise your light-bearing solemnity on earth.

Having torn asunder the attachment of fleshly bonds by the divine Spirit, the virgins bound themselves unto Christ with God-wise love.

Thou didst guide Thy virgins unto the heavenly havens, O Savior, who passed without wetting their feet through the unstable deep of torments.

Having pleased the Word Who shone forth from the Virgin with honorable bodies, O maiden-virgins, with Him ye are glorified.[^51]

*Theotokion:*

Ineffably thou bearest Him Whom the Father begot ineffably, and with thy breasts dost nourish the Nourisher of all things, O blessed Lady.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*

Thou art the establishment of those who flee unto Thee, O Lord; Thou art the light of the darkened, and my spirit hymns Thee.

*Troparia:*

Watered by the showers of the divine Spirit as ears of corn of martyrdom, by divine tilling the virgins blossomed forth.

Having loved the invisible things more than the visible, ye conquered both visible and invisible enemies.

Ye were not terrified by the fire, being inflamed with the divine fire of the Spirit; therefore ye pour forth the divine dew of healings.

*Theotokion:*

Tear asunder the cruel handwriting of my transgressions, O Sovereign Lady, who didst loose the bonds of Hades by thy seedless childbearing.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*

Having betrothed yourselves unto the Lord in a God-befitting manner, as a gift ye offered your blood and slaughter unto Him, O passion-bearing maidens, and worthily attained the divine bridal chamber, unceasingly filled with ineffable enlightenment. Therefore, spiritually keeping your holy and honorable memory, we glorify the Savior, and faithfully cry aloud: Entreat Christ God to grant remission of sins unto those who honor your holy memory with love!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*

Having conceived Wisdom and the Word in thy womb unconsumed, O Mother of God, thou didst bear unto the world Him Who upholds the world, and in thine arms didst carry Him Who bears all things, the Nourisher of all and Creator of creation. Therefore I pray thee, O all-holy Virgin, and faithfully glorify thee, that I may be delivered from transgressions; and in the day of judgment, when I am to stand before the face of my Creator, O Sovereign Lady, pure Virgin, thy help then grant me; for thou canst do all things whatsoever thou desirest, O all-hymned one!

*Stavrotheotokion:*

Beholding the Lamb, Shepherd, and Deliverer upon the Cross, the ewe-lamb cried aloud weeping, and bitterly lamenting, cried: "The world indeed rejoices, receiving redemption through Thee, but my womb burns as I behold Thy crucifixion, which Thou endurest for the tender mercy of compassion, O Long-suffering Lord, abyss of mercy and inexhaustible fountain! Have mercy and grant remission of transgressions unto those who sing of Thy divine passions in faith."

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*

I have heard, O Lord, the mystery of Thy dispensation; I have understood Thy works, and I have glorified Thy Divinity.

*Troparia:*

Having separated themselves from bodily desires by divine desire, Christ's virgins became an abode of the Spirit.

Having marshaled yourselves against the adversary with God-wise concord, ye received divine victory and were counted worthy of divine crowns.

Sacredly, like guileless lambs, ye were led to be sacrificed, O all-honorable ones, neither crying out nor calling aloud, but put to death by your own voluntary choice.

*Theotokion:*

Thou hast borne the Incarnate Lord, O Mistress of all creation; entreat Him, O all-hymned one, to deliver me from the domain of the enemy's craftiness.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*

Enlighten us by Thy commandments, O Lord, and by Thy high arm grant us Thy peace, O Lover of mankind!

*Troparia:*

Together the God-wise virgins rose early unto Christ, the Unwaning Light, and were enlightened more brightly than the radiance of the sun.

Manfully ye surrendered your limbs unto torments, tormenting the tyrant by your endurance, O praiseworthy passion-bearers.

Ye appeared as a sacred flower of martyrs, granting the fragrance of divine knowledge unto the Church, O all-praised ones.

By your words the boastful one was struck dumb,[^52] and was vanquished by your endurance, and delivered unto destruction, O all-praised ones.

*Theotokion:*

Pray, O Maiden, that a good end may come unto me, that I may escape the endless torments awaiting me, and be saved.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*

I will pour out my prayer unto the Lord, and unto Him will I declare my sorrows, for my soul is filled with evils, and my life has drawn near unto Hades; and like Jonah I pray: Bring me up from corruption, O God!

*Troparion:*

Having endured bodily pains and the torment of fire manfully, Christ's passion-bearers and brides—Agape, and Chionia, and Irene—obtained a beauteous crown and ineffable glory with the martyrs.

*Troparia:*

Dragged upon the earth like stones, O virgins, ye cast down the whole edifice of delusion; and by the power of the Word ye were built up into the walls of the Church, joyfully standing before the Creator of all creation.

The shrine of Chionia, Irene, and Agape pours forth healings, being richly watered from the fountain of the Comforter, and stays diseases; and it waters those who draw near in piety with sacred grace.

*Theotokion:*

Adam put off the deadness of the garments of skin by the Incarnation of Thy Son, O Virgin Mother of God, and was clothed in the garment of divine glory, joyfully praising thee as the all-immaculate Mother of God.[^53]

*Kontakion, Tone 4:*

Strongly didst thou arm thy soul with faith, O Irene, manifestly putting the evil one to shame, and hast brought multitudes of ten thousands of people unto Christ, O blessed one; and wearing a purple robe dyed from thy blood, thou rejoicest now with the angels.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*

The Hebrew children in the furnace boldly trampled upon the flame and changed the fire into dew, crying aloud: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages!

*Troparia:*

Neither the onsets of wild beasts, nor the surging of the unstable fire, nor the severing of limbs, nor multiform diseases did Christ's maidens fear with valiant mind.

Having the eye of the heart directed toward the saving God and King, Christ's lambs separated themselves from all the enemy's array, and conquered manfully.

Be merciful unto Thy servants, O All-good One, by the supplications of Chionia, and pure Irene, and honorable Agape, granting us remission of sins for their sake.

Unto the only Good One ye accounted the beauty of life as a dream, crying aloud: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages!

*Theotokion:*

Put to death, O pure one, the sin that lives within me, and make me worthy to obtain the ageless life, O thou who didst bear the Life and Him Who put the serpent to death.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*

The God-speaking children in the furnace, trampling the flame together with the fire, sang: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparia:*

Taught in divine things most gloriously, at the time of struggles they took on a manly character:

Christ's lambs, having suffered steadfastly.

Darkened by the drunkenness of dark sin, the persecutor showed himself worthy of mockery; but Christ's virgins appeared as light.

Ye showed yourselves truly to be harps clearly giving voice to God's song of divine confession, testimony, and faith, O maiden-virgins.

Established by the love of the Master, ye cast down all the strongholds of the devil, O passion-bearers of Christ, and now ye are called blessed.

*Theotokion:*

Bearing the prefigurations of thy childbearing of old, O Virgin Mother, the furnace burned not the youths, even as the divine fire burned not thy womb.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*

Truly we confess thee to be the Mother of God, saved through thee, O pure Virgin, with the bodiless choirs magnifying thee.

*Troparia:*

Ye steered your course unto divine havens, O virgins, by the pilotage of Christ our God, having passed through the assaults of fierce waves.

Shining like stars, like lamps, Irene, Agape, and Chionia richly enlighten all the ends of the universe.

Ye were joined unto the choirs of the bodiless ones, O passion-bearers, having valiantly conquered the bodiless enemies in the flesh.

Your memory, radiant with divine and light-bearing gifts, O passion-bearers, enlightens the thoughts of those who praise you.

*Theotokion:*

O blessed Virgin, who didst bear the all-good Word, adorn my soul that is embittered by sin.

*April 17. Our Venerable Father Symeon of Persia, and our Venerable Father Acacius, Bishop of Melitene:*[^54]

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of Saint Symeon, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ знаме́нїе):*

Brighter than the sun, enlightened has thy memory shone forth unto the faithful, enlightening creation with divine splendors, O all-blessed Symeon, and driving away the gloom of passions and the darkness of demons;

therefore we call thee blessed, and suppliantly sing of thee as a universal luminary and as a fervent intercessor.

O wise martyr, having acquired heavenly things with earthly things,[^55] and enduring things with transient things, and incorruptible glory with corruptible things, thou didst receive them rejoicing; and by torments and by wounds patiently borne for the sake of the Word of God, thou didst receive crowns and the Kingdom, wherein rejoicing with those who suffered with thee, O glorious one, pray for all who praise thee in faith.

The arrows of thy words wounded the assemblies of the lawless, O martyr Symeon; therefore the heavenly clouds gave forth a voice, and the choirs of the bodiless ones joyfully assembled, marveling at the endurance of thy struggles; therefore we also, rejoicing, honor thy holy dormition in faith, glorifying the Savior.

*Other Stichera, of Venerable Acacius, Tone 8, Podoben: "O marvelous wonder" (Ѡ҆ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*

As a God-received hierarch, thou beholdest the unwaning tabernacles of the noetic realm, and wast counted worthy to see the things above the world. Clothed in holiness, invested with righteousness and majesty, face to face thou beholdest the beauty of noetic things, O blessed one.

Venerable Father Acacius, having given wings to thy mind unto God by faith, thou didst abhor the confused mingling of the world, and taking up thy cross, didst follow the All-seeing One, by the power of the Divine Spirit enslaving the incontinence of the body unto thy mind through works of fasting.

A champion of the divine Council and cloud of theologians, together with Cyril, O Acacius, thou didst deliver fierce Nestorius unto destruction, having exposed his folly, O blessed one, and put his council to shame with spiritual words;[^56] therefore every soul calls thee blessed, celebrating thy divine memory in faith.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion, in the same Tone and Podoben (Гла́съ и҆ подо́бенъ то́йже):*

Save me, O all-pure Sovereign Lady, who didst bear Christ the Savior ineffably; for thee alone have I acquired as an intercessor, an impregnable wall, a protection and rejoicing, and the divine comfort of my soul. Deliver me, therefore, from the unsleeping worm and from the eternal fire, O Mother of Christ God!

*Stavrotheotokion, in the same Podoben (Подо́бенъ то́йже):*

Beholding Thee nailed upon the Cross, O Jesus, and voluntarily accepting the Passion, the Virgin and Thy Mother, O Master, cried aloud: "Woe is me, sweet Child! How dost Thou endure wounds unjustly, O Physician Who didst heal human weakness and deliver all from corruption by Thy tender mercy?"

*Canon of Venerable Symeon, the acrostic whereof is: "I crown thee, the blessed Symeon, with songs." The composition of Joseph. Tone 5:*[^57]

*Ode 1. Heirmos:*

Horse and rider into the Red Sea Christ shook off, Who crushes battles with a high arm; and He saved Israel, who sang a song of victory.

*Troparia:*

Invested with a crown of incorruption and standing before the throne of the Lord, O all-blessed one, ever save by thy prayers those who honor thy holy suffering in faith.

An honorable vessel of the Divine Spirit wast thou shown to be, O God-bearing Symeon; anointed therewith, thou didst shepherd a rich people with the staff of divine teachings upon the water of understanding, O martyr.

Thou didst stay the rushing torrents of delusion by the divine streams of thy sweet words and by the drops of thy blood, O glorious flock;[^58] therefore in faith we sing of thee.

*Theotokion:*

Thou bearest in thine arms, O Virgin, Him Who bears all things, and dost nourish as an infant the Giver of milk; Him therefore entreat to show compassion unto all thy people who sing of thee in faith.

*Another Canon, of Saint Acacius, the composition of Joseph. Tone 8:*

*Ode 1. Heirmos:*

The cleft rod divided the indivisible deep, and the sun beheld the earth which it had not seen; the water drowned the fierce enemy, and Israel passed through the impassable, and a song was sung: Let us sing unto the Lord, for He has been gloriously glorified!

*Troparia:*

Having conquered all the malice of the serpent in the guilelessness of thy soul, as thou didst live, O wise Acacius, snatch me away from malice and deadly passions, and adorn me with good ways, that I may praise thee who didst fight the good fight.

Beauteous Melitene brought thee forth as a most radiant shoot, O great Acacius, yielding sweet fruits, and sweetening the hearts of the faithful more than honey, driving away the soul-corrupting former bitterness of passions by divine grace.

Being the offspring of thy parents, O Acacius, like holy Samuel thou wast offered unto God, surrendered from early childhood to be nurtured in the holy house, and to serve God the Almighty, Who enlightened thy soul with divine deeds.

*Theotokion:*

O thou who didst give flesh unto the incomprehensible God, and didst reconcile unto Him us who had apostatized:

O all-hymned pure one, heal the passions of my heart by the Passion of Thy Son, I pray, and save me who ever sing: For He has been gloriously glorified!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*

Thou Who didst establish the earth upon nothing by Thy command, and didst suspend its weight unrestrained, establish Thy Church upon the immovable rock of Thy commandments, O Christ, Who alone art good and the Lover of mankind!

*Troparia:*

Beautifying the motions of thy soul and walking in the path leading unto God, thou didst turn away from all evil lawlessness, and wast taken up unto the life-bearing door of eternal rest, O glorious one.

Having removed thy mind far from fleshly passions, O Father, thou didst offer bloodless sacrifices unto the Word Who sacrificed Himself for our sake; and proclaiming His divine Incarnation which is beyond word, thou wast slaughtered like a guileless lamb.

Having passed thy life in holiness, O all-rich one, thou dwellest now with the saints, having enlightened thy soul by sufferings, and shone forth brighter than the sun, O blessed one, having become a son of the light.

*Theotokion:*

Thou hast delivered us from the ancestral curse, O pure and ever-blessed one, having given flesh unto the Word Who crowned all with purple: the adornment of passion-bearers, and the cleansing of sinners.

*Another Canon, of Saint Acacius. Ode 3. Heirmos:*

Thou art the establishment of those who flee unto Thee, O Lord; Thou art the light of the darkened, and my spirit hymns Thee.

*Troparia:*

Thou didst bow down unto the life-originating law, O venerable one, and from thy soul didst hate all deadly iniquity.

The Holy Spirit ordained thee a priest, O wise one, when thou hadst become wholly radiant by spiritual deeds.

Thy tongue poured forth teachings like a river, O wise one, drying up the turbid stream of delusion by divine power.

*Theotokion:*

O impassable gate, open unto me the gates of repentance, O pure one, and direct me unto straight paths.

*Sessional Hymn of Saint Symeon, Tone 1, Podoben: "Thy tomb, O Savior" (Гро́бъ тво́й сп҃се):*

Like the sun has thy holy memory shone forth unto us, O wise hierarch Symeon, and that of those who suffered with thee, illuminating the faithful; and celebrating it today, we cry aloud: Entreat the only Lover of mankind for us, O passion-bearers!

*Glory... Another Sessional Hymn, of Saint Acacius, Tone 3:*

*Podoben: "Of the divine faith" (Бж҃твенныѧ вѣ́ры):*

A crown of victory from the Lord for thy temperance didst thou receive rejoicing, being adorned with confession, O God-bearer; and thou didst expose all the vanity of heresies by thy divine commands. O venerable Father, entreat Christ God to grant us great mercy!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*

He departed not from the Divine Nature when He became flesh in thy womb, but being God, He became man and so remained; and after childbearing He preserved thee a Virgin Mother all-immaculate, even as thou wast before childbirth, the only Lord: Him earnestly entreat to grant us great mercy.

*Stavrotheotokion:*

The undefiled ewe-lamb of the Word, the incorrupt Virgin Mother, seeing Him Who sprouted without sorrow from her suspended upon the Cross, weeping as a mother, cried aloud: "Woe is me, my Child! How dost Thou suffer voluntarily, desiring to deliver man from the passions of dishonor?"

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*

Perceiving Thy divine self-emptying, O Christ, Habbakuk foresightedly cried aloud unto Thee with trembling: For the salvation of Thy people, to save Thine anointed, art Thou come!

*Troparia:*

Bound in thine old age,[^59] thou didst loose the craftiness of the evil one, O all-rich initiate of sacred mysteries; having suffered valiantly, thou didst receive crowns of incorruption.

With the arrows of thy words thou didst wound the assemblies of the lawless, O hieromartyr Symeon, and with the fire of thy blood didst burn up the deceit of polytheism.

Let us form choirs, blessing the God-bearing Symeon, and glorious Ananias, and the martyrs suspended with them, who suffered manfully.[^60]

*Theotokion:*

From thee the Sun of Righteousness, having shone forth beyond understanding, enlightened the universe, O maiden full of divine joy, and destroyed the winter of delusion.

*Another Canon, of Saint Acacius. Ode 4. Heirmos:*

I have heard, O Lord, the mystery of Thy dispensation; I have understood Thy works, and I have glorified Thy Divinity.

*Troparia:*

An emulator of the divine apostles wast thou, O Father Acacius; for with their authority to bind and to loose wast thou enriched.

A harp of the Spirit didst thou appear, proclaiming saving teachings, destroying the frantic delusion of the foolish Nestorius.

By thy most radiant prayer, O Father, thou didst grant rain unto the earth, and didst water it, which had been dried up in summer, O blessed one, furrows.

Thy cheeks were adorned, O venerable one, breathing the fragrance of divine commands unto the faithful, and driving away foul-smelling delusion.

*Theotokion:*

Quicken me who am slain, and turn me back quickly who have strayed, O Sovereign Lady, who art the unerring path and the guide of all.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*

Thou Who clothest Thyself with light as with a garment, unto Thee I awake early and unto Thee I call: Enlighten my darkened soul, O Christ, for Thou alone art compassionate!

*Troparia:*

A pillar and foundation of God's Church didst thou appear by thy suffering, O all-blessed one; unyielding and unshaken preserve it unto the ages by thine intercessions.

With hymns we bless thy life, thy persecutions and resistances, O all-rich Symeon, for the sake of which thou wast counted worthy of a blessed end.

Having become a shepherd by the chrism of divine anointing, thou wast sacrificed for the flock, O blessed one, like a choice ram, and didst make glad the Church of the firstborn.

*Theotokion:*

May I find thee, O Sovereign Lady, delivering me in the hour of judgment from condemnation and rescuing me from the torments awaiting me, as I faithfully glorify thee.

*Another Canon, of Saint Acacius. Ode 5. Heirmos:*

Why hast Thou cast me off from Thy face, O Light Unwaning, and alien darkness has covered me, wretched one? But turn me back, and direct my paths unto the light of Thy commandments, I pray Thee!

*Troparia:*

Marvelous was thy life shown to be, for thou wast invested with the gifts of the Divine Spirit; by prayer thou didst stop the senseless surgings of the river, setting a stone as its bar and boundary, O hierarch Acacius.[^61]

In the midst of the Council thou wast divine, a champion of the fathers and of the sacred ordinances; with the divine Cyril, O blessed one, thou didst confess the pure Virgin to be the Mother of God, conquering the deceitful Nestorius.

Sweeter than honey was thy word known unto the people of Melitene, sweetening their souls with sacred confessions, and exposing the bitterness of sin, O venerable Father Acacius.

*Theotokion:*

Not emptying at all the bosom of the Father, the Most High was seen held as an infant in thy bosom, O Virgin, clothing Himself with my great poverty through the richness of His tender mercy, the only Lover of mankind.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*

Raging with a soul-corrupting storm, O Master Christ, calm the sea of passions, and bring me up from corruption, for Thou art compassionate!

*Troparia:*

Full of divine comfort didst thou appear, O blessed Symeon, having received a blessed end by the sword with the holy martyrs who suffered brightly.

Youths and elders, monks and priests, having fought the good fight for Christ, were slaughtered like lambs.

Having lived a blessed life, O sufferers, ye received a blessed end from Christ, being perfected by the sword rejoicing.

*Theotokion:*

From thee, O all-pure one, the Creator shone forth, and with the dawn of divine knowledge enlightened those who were in the night of ignorance.

*Another Canon, of Saint Acacius. Ode 6. Heirmos:*

Cleanse me, O Savior, for many are mine iniquities, and bring me up from the depth of evils, I pray Thee; for unto Thee have I cried, and do Thou give ear unto me, O God of my salvation!

*Troparia:*

Unto the pure Mother of God, an honorable temple didst thou raise up; for beforehand thou hadst made thyself a temple of the Spirit, O holy Acacius; and having consecrated it, thou didst show forth a river of wonders:

Thy holy voices, which thou didst utter unto thy flock, singing most radiantly like a swallow, O Acacius; do thou preserve it immutably, having received the law of thy tongue.

When the emperor saw thee coming humbly like a servant, he fulfilled thy requests; for he recognized the venerable servant of the heavenly King,[^62] O God-bearing Father.

*Theotokion:*

Heal the defeats of my mind and the haughtiness of my heart by thy mercy, O pure one, I pray, and deliver me from the sin that holds me fast, as I bless thee in faith.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*

The exalted Lord of the fathers quenched the flame and dewed the children who sang in unison: Blessed art Thou, O God!

*Troparia:*

Showing forth understanding as a lamp, thou wast known as a light unto those sleeping in the darkness of ignorance, O venerable one, and didst destroy the darkness of fire-worshipers, having suffered most gloriously.

In the midst of the martyrs thou wast shown forth leading their company; perfected with them by the sword, thou didst leap for joy, O God-bearing Symeon, crying: Blessed art Thou, O God!

We honor the assembly of the numerous martyrs, gathered honorably from diverse cities: divine celebrants of sacred mysteries, and God-bearing monks, who were divine, and God-bearing monks.

*Theotokion:*

Heal the passions of my heart, O all-immaculate one, calm the confusion that storm-tosses my mind,[^63] and save me who sing: Blessed art Thou, O God!

*Another Canon, of Saint Acacius. Ode 7. Heirmos:*

The children who came from Judea in Babylon of old trampled down the flame of the furnace by their faith in the Trinity, singing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparia:*

Adorned with perfect mercy, O Father, thou wast generous unto the poor and known as health unto all the sick, an intercessor for those who sing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

Seeing the flies afflicting the sick, O Father, thou didst drive them away, commanding them by no means to approach them again, O Acacius,[^64] crying: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

Like a cluster of grapes pressed out, thou didst mingle for us the wine of wisdom, gladdening hearts with great understanding, and directing them to cry: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*

As the cause of our salvation do all of us, thy servants, ever sing of thee, O Virgin full of God's grace, and in faith we call thee blessed, saying: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*

Unto Thee, the Creator, the children in the furnace, forming a universal choir, sang: All ye works of the Lord, praise the Lord and highly exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparia:*

Shown forth as a spiritual coal, O Symeon, thou didst not serve the fire, O all-blessed one; but having burned up the bitter matter of polytheism, thou didst pass over unto the unwaning radiance, O venerable one.

Heal our infirmities, O martyr, drive away the gloom of our hearts, and show us forth as partakers of eternal life by thine intercessions unto the Master of all.

Let us sing of the honorable army, the divine assembly of martyrs, numbering a hundred,[^65] whose leader was valiant Symeon, as a shepherd and great pillar of the Church.

*Theotokion:*

The unburnt bush that was seen prefigured thy miraculous conception and childbearing, O all-pure one; for having received the divine fire in thy womb, thou wast not burned at all; therefore we sing of thee.

*Another Canon, of Saint Acacius. Ode 8. Heirmos:*

The God-speaking children in the furnace, trampling the flame together with the fire, sang: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparia:*

Standing before the Lord with the choirs on high and with all the saints, pray, O Father, that He may cleanse and save us!

When frogs were raising a clamor while thou wast offering prayer, by thy quiet word thou didst rebuke them not to croak, and didst cause them to remain silent, O Acacius.[^66]

Having written words against wanton Nestorius like precious stones, thou didst establish the Church upon the rock of blameless faith, O blessed one.

*Triadikon:*

In faith let us cry aloud unto the Trinity with the ranks on high: Holy, Holy, Holy art Thou, Father, and Son, and Holy Spirit!

*Theotokion:*

Being a fountain of incorruption, O maiden, vouchsafe thy healing unto me who am corrupted by the many drownings of sin.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*

Rejoice, O Isaiah! A Virgin has conceived in the womb and borne a Son, Emmanuel, God and man, Dayspring is His name: Him magnifying, we call the Virgin blessed!

*Troparia:*

As acceptable incense wast thou offered unto the King of all, as a sacred slaughter, as a choice acceptable sacrifice, as myrrh, as an offering, as a sweet-smelling rose, O venerable hierarch.

Having become an establishment unto all the faithful, O hierarch, thou wast slaughtered like a ram, and hast passed over unto the noetic kingdoms; and thou standest before the King of all and God, O blessed one, radiant with divine glory.

Thy holy memory has shone forth unto us today like a great sun, O wise Symeon, and that of the martyrs with thee: with them ask of Christ grace and mercy for us, and deliverance from transgressions.

*Theotokion:*

With bright splendors, O all-pure Mother of God, illuminate my mind which is held fast by the darkness of grievous ignorance, that having obtained divine enlightenment, I may ever proclaim thy greatness, O Virgin.

*Another Canon, of Saint Acacius. Ode 9. Heirmos:*

Thou didst transcend the laws of nature, having conceived the Creator and Lord, and becamest the door of salvation unto the world; therefore, O Mother of God, unceasingly we magnify thee!

*Troparia:*

By thy fervent prayer, O Father, thou didst open the shut heavens, and didst make water flow forth most gloriously from the stone unto the glory of the Savior Who glorified thee, O Acacius.[^67]

How greatly didst thou shine forth, a sun from Melitene, by the dawn of thy words, and didst receive the radiance of wonders, O Acacius, illuminating all who piously call thee blessed.

Adorned with the wisdom of words and with the splendors of many healings, O Acacius, and shining with the virtues of hierarchical dignity, thou didst pass over from worldly things unto enjoyment above this world.

enjoyment.[^68]

*Troparion:*

Thy divine memory today, O all-blessed Acacius, enlightener of our hearts, summons ascetics to sing of thy sufferings, thy struggles, and thy virtuous deeds.

*Theotokion:*

Thou hast truly been revealed as the fair dwelling-place of Him Who enlightened all things, O all-immaculate Virgin; therefore enlighten me who have been darkened by malice, that in faith I may magnify thee.

*April 18. Our Venerable Father John, Disciple of Saint Gregory of Decapolis:*

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera, Tone 8. Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder" (Ѡ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*

*Stichera:*

O venerable Father John, having given wings to thy mind toward God by faith, thou didst abhor unstable worldly confusion; and taking up thy cross, thou didst follow the All-seeing One, subjecting the disobedient body unto thy mind through struggles of abstinence, by the power of the Divine Spirit.

Like a radiant sun through the light of virtues and the splendors of miracles didst thou find Gregory, O most glorious John; thou didst enlighten the eyes of the soul and drive away dark passions; and having become light, O Father, rejoicing thou didst pass over unto unwaning radiance, O all-blessed one.

Thou didst spend all thy life in much stillness, in weeping and in tears, having been a monk true in faith, adorned with poverty, beautified with abstinence, and showing thyself a stranger and pilgrim upon earth; therefore hast thou found the enjoyment of good things, O wondrously worthy of praise.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*

Having taken flesh from thy blood, the pre-eternal God showed thee forth as an intercessor for mankind, O pure one; therefore deliver thy servants from every affliction and distress, and from the snares of the deceitful enemy, and vouchsafe them to partake of the radiance of the elect, even all who glorify and worship thee.

*Stavrotheotokion:*

"What is this visible sight that is seen with mine eyes, O Master? Bearing all creation, Thou art lifted up upon the Tree, and Thou diest, Who givest life unto all!" weeping, the Mother of God said when she beheld Him lifted up upon the Cross, God and man, Who shone forth ineffably from her.

*Canon, Tone 8. Ode 1. Heirmos:*

The wonderworking rod of Moses, striking the sea crosswise and dividing it, once submerged Pharaoh the charioteer; but it saved Israel, the fleeing foot-traveler, as he sang a hymn unto God.

*Troparia:*

Ever troubled by the tempest of passions and now submerged by the tumult of thoughts, by thy prayer, O venerable one, guide me unto the pursuit of dispassion and unto the saving haven, O blessed John.

Having truly turned thy will toward heaven, O venerable one, living angelically upon earth, O Father, thou didst mortify thy carnal mind through abstinence; therefore hast thou been revealed as a dwelling-place of God, O marvelous John.

Desiring to obtain the good things that surpass the mind, O blessed one, thou didst find a guide who stirred thee up unto divine struggles, O God-wise John: the glorious Gregory, whose footsteps thou didst follow.

*Theotokion:*

Co-eternal and only-begotten with the Father and the Spirit, the Word of God appeared from thee upon earth as man, O Virgin Mary, Mother of God, and saved all the world, who sing a hymn unto the Lord.[^69]

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*

O Christ, Who in the beginning didst establish the heavens with understanding and didst found the earth upon the waters: establish me upon the rock of Thy commandments, for none is holy save Thee, the only Lover of mankind!

*Troparia:*

Beholding the heavenly beauties through sobriety of mind, thou wast by no means seized by material cares; but made light by the wings of poverty, O Father, thou wast wholly transformed.

Having become a lover of stillness, O thrice-blessed John, following the stillness-loving shepherd Gregory, thou didst zealously forsake the tumults in this life, and by unceasing prayers didst attain divine serenity.

Thou didst subject thyself unto the law of the Spirit, O God-wise one, having put to death the law warring against it through steadfast abstinence, fasting, diligent prayers, and vigils, O blessed one.

*Theotokion:*

Thou hast truly been revealed as the bright, impassable door unto us of Him Who reigns unto all ages: through which He Himself passed, leaving thee sealed again, O all-immaculate one.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 1. Podoben: "While the soldiers were guarding Thy tomb" (Гро́бъ тво́й, Сп҃се):*

Having buried the deceitful snares of demons through many acts of abstinence and supplications, O Father, lying dead in the tomb thou hast remained incorrupt; therefore rejoicing we celebrate thy most holy memory, with gladness of heart magnifying Christ.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*

O unwedded and pure Virgin Mother of God, sole intercessor and protection of the faithful: deliver all from afflictions, sorrows, and grievous distresses, who place their trust in thee, O Maiden, and save our souls through thy divine prayers.

*Stavrotheotokion:*

Beholding Thee dead, stretched out upon the Cross, O Christ, the all-immaculate Mother cried aloud: "O my Son, co-unoriginate with the Father and the Spirit, what is this ineffable dispensation of Thine, whereby Thou hast saved the creation of Thy most pure hands, O compassionate One?"

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*

Thou art my strength, O Lord, Thou art also my power, Thou art my God, Thou art my rejoicing, Who without leaving the bosom of the Father hast visited our poverty; therefore with the Prophet Habakkuk I cry unto Thee: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind!

*Troparia:*

Thou wast shown forth as a rock of endurance, O blessed one, ever beaten by contrary waves, yet by no means shaking the thoughts of piety; therefore the Master placed thee as a most precious stone in eternal treasures.

The Father of mercies received thee, as one who truly loved Him and abode in His commandments, O Father; and having made thee a partaker of the Son by grace, He vouchsafed thee eternal glory, O God-wise John.

As one merciful and humble-minded, meek, filled with love and faith, and having thy soul established in hope, O John, thou didst shine forth both by word and by deeds in the midst of monks, walking in this life, O ever-memorable one.

*Theotokion:*

Thou art the praise of the faithful, O unwedded one, thou art the defense, thou art the refuge of Christians, a wall and a haven; for thou dost bear supplications unto thy Son, O all-immaculate one, and dost save from distresses those who in faith and love acknowledge thee to be the pure Mother of God.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*

Why hast Thou cast me away from Thy presence, O unwaning Light, and an alien darkness hath enveloped me, the wretched one? But turn me back, I pray Thee, and direct my paths unto the light of Thy commandments!

*Troparia:*

Having nurtured thee like a gentle lamb upon the pasture of abstinence, the Great Shepherd settled thee in the eternal courts, O blessed John, and showed thee forth as a partaker with His sheep on the right hand, O venerable one.

Thou didst steadfastly bear the burden and the heat of the day, O Father, having labored well in the vineyard of divine virtues; therefore in the evening of thy departure from this life, thou didst receive a worthy reward.

Thou wast truly accounted worthy to dwell with the company of the fasting ascetics, O Father, and it was granted thee to rejoice with the choir of the venerable, having been richly adorned with the heights of contemplation and practical philosophy,[^70] O all-praised one.

*Theotokion:*

Having conceived the uncreated Life and given birth unto the Redeemer and King for the world, raise me up who have been put to death by the sting of death and transgression, O Virgin, and guide me unto the light, O pure one.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*

Cleanse me, O Savior, for many are mine iniquities, and lead me up from the depths of evils, I pray Thee; for unto Thee have I cried, and hear me, O God of my salvation!

*Troparia:*

By the drops of thy tears thou didst dry up the abyss of passions, and didst trample down all the feeble power of the noetic enemy, O blessed John, being strengthened by Christ.

Like radiant, shining gold tried by the fire of abstinence, O most glorious one, Christ placed thee in the incorrupt treasures of eternal life, O Father.

Having found Gregory shining upon earth with virtues like a sun, and enlightening the eyes of thy soul with his spiritual splendors, thou wast shown forth as a most radiant star.

*Theotokion:*

A divine and truly all-honorable dwelling-place of God, let us piously hymn the all-pure Mary; for she contained God, having received Him Who cannot be contained and is incomprehensible.

*Kontakion, Tone 4. Podoben: "Thou Who wast lifted up upon the Cross" (Вознесы́йся):*

Having adorned thy life with abstinence, O Father, thou didst mortify the flesh; therefore also didst thou conquer the assaults of the enemy, O all-blessed one, and hast passed over unto God into painless and eternal life as a worthy heir, O John: unto Whom pray thou that we may be saved.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*

The children who went down from Judea in Babylon of old trampled down the flame of the furnace by faith in the Trinity, singing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparia:*

Meditating upon the second coming of the Judge, O wise one, thou didst cast away the sleep of despondency from the eyes of thy soul, and being saved didst sing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

From early youth thou didst choose the good portion, O wise Father, to serve the Lord with fear, and to fulfill His ordinances: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

Having become full of spiritual days, like ripe wheat in the granaries on high God placed thee, O God-bearer, as thou sangest: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*

O thou who art the Lady of the world, who didst bear God incarnate: save my soul foolishly enslaved by unfitting sins, O blessed and pure one, that I may glorify thee.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*

The Chaldean tyrant furiously kindled the furnace sevenfold for the godly; but seeing them saved by a higher power, he cried unto the Creator and Deliverer: Ye children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparia:*

Having well inherited the grace of thy glorious shepherd like Elijah's mantle, O Father John, with it thou didst cleave the abyss of pleasures; and being saved without being drowned, thou wast shown forth as a fellow citizen of the angels, crying out with them: Ye people, supremely exalt the Lord unto all ages!

Piously imitating the all-good Lord Who became poor for our sake, O Father John, thou didst maintain poverty like the birds, and didst acquire the wealth of eternal good things, singing: Ye priests, bless; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

Rejoicing, thou didst pass over unto ceaseless life, unto incorrupt joy, unto gladness surpassing the mind, O most rich one, and unto fair splendor, unto the angelic dwelling, crying aloud: Ye children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt the Lord unto all ages!

*Theotokion:*

Overshadowed by the Spirit, O all-immaculate Mother of God, thou didst truly receive in thy womb the unoriginate and co-eternal Son, incarnate in thee, O pure one, as a benefaction for those who sing in faith: Ye children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*

Heaven was amazed at this, and the ends of the earth were astounded, that God appeared in the flesh unto men, and thy womb became more spacious than the heavens; therefore the hierarchies of angels and men magnify thee as Mother of God!

*Troparia:*

Having renewed the furrow of thy soul with the plow of prayer, O John, thou didst sow the virtues of piety and the seeds of life; therefore hast thou reaped the ear of God-like healings and divine gifts, with which thou dost nourish those who sing of thee.

Having finished the course of fasting and kept the faith, O blessed one, truly filled with spiritual maturity, thou didst receive from Christ the crown of the venerable, O God-wise one; and now thou hast found rest in radiant dwellings with the righteous, O most glorious one.

Thou hast reaped the rewards of thy labors, having passed over unto painless and pure perfection, unmixed with any alteration, change, worse state, or contrary turning; dwelling therein, O Father, remember before the Master those who in faith sing of thee.

*Theotokion:*

Having become the Mother of the Deliverer, O all-pure one, thou hast summoned back us who were cast out from the heavenly life and had wretchedly fallen into death, and hast vouchsafed us to run unto our first fatherland; therefore, O Mother of God, unceasingly we magnify thee.

*April 19. Our Venerable Father John of the Ancient Caves:*

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera, Tone 5. Podoben: "O venerable father" (Преподо́бне ѻ́ч҃е):*

*Stichera:*

O venerable Father John, wondrously worthy of praise, wounded by divine desire for Christ God, Who for thy sake willingly sojourned as a pilgrim upon earth and was clothed in flesh from the Virgin, thou didst reach a foreign land, O Father; thou didst utterly estrange thyself from all the sweet things of the world, O wise and all-blessed one; thou didst live as a stranger, acquiring virtues: all-night standing, meditation upon death; and thou didst bridle the passions through thy abstinence; a zealot wast thou shown forth of those who fasted before thee, with whom also thou hast been glorified.

O venerable Father John, most glorious one, having learned in the flock of divine Chariton, thou didst draw forth grace like a sponge and a jar,[^71] emulating his divine way; and having completed thy life in stillness, thou wast accounted worthy of the honorable priesthood, having been a worthy celebrant, ever sacrificing thyself like a blameless lamb; and cleansed by divine radiances and enlightened in mind, thou didst ascend in thought unto mystical visions, unto surpassing blessedness.

O venerable Father John, all-blessed one, strengthened by the power of the Trinity, thou didst flee all heretical harm; thou didst cleave unto Him Who truly is, O wise one, by contemplation and divine action, and hast attained all things desired: unto the highest divine glory, O blessed one, which thou didst truly receive worthily after thy repose; and rejoicing with the angelic choirs, thou art robed with them and standest before God; therefore remember those who worthily celebrate thy memory, O wondrously worthy of praise.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*

Unto whom hast thou become likened, O wretched soul, ever increasing in worse things and foolishly adding more arrows to thy wounds, so that the wound has covered all the body? Not meditating that the Judge is drawing near, before Whom thou shalt stand to receive condemnation and torments according to thy deeds. But turn back and fall down before the Virgin, crying: "O Lady, Lady, despise me not who have angered the good and compassionate God, Who was born from thee for the salvation of mankind, and was crucified in the flesh!"

*Stavrotheotokion:*

When the all-pure one beheld suspended upon the Tree the Cluster of sweetest fruit which she had brought forth like a vine, pierced with a spear in His divine side, she said: "What is this, O my Son and my God? How dost Thou Who healest all infirmities and sufferings endure sufferings, being dispassionate according to Thy divine nature? Why have the ungrateful people rendered these things unto Thee, the Benefactor, instead of benefits?" cried the all-pure one: "Yet by His sufferings pray thou unceasingly that I may be freed from passions, that I may glorify thee!"

*Canon, Tone 4. Ode 1. Heirmos:*

Traversing the deep of the Red Sea with dryshod feet, Israel of old conquered the power of Amalek in the wilderness by Moses' hands stretched out crosswise.

*Troparia:*

Strengthened from youth by hope toward God, thou didst hasten unto the good God, O blessed John, having been established through the best monastic fasts.[^72]

Filled with rich and light-bearing grace, and dwelling in honorable places, O all-blessed one, thou didst wisely set in thy heart good ascents unto virtues.

Guided by the power of the Spirit, thou didst flee unto the sheepfold of God-bearing Chariton and didst abide therein, having become a sacred minister, an enlightened teacher.

Having estranged thy mind from earthly things, O venerable one, thou didst fasten it unto heavenly good things, O John, and didst obtain the most radiant splendor that comes from thence.

*Theotokion:*

From thee, O favored and all-immaculate one, Christ the Sun of righteousness appeared unto us, and with light enlightened those who formerly sat in dark realms.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*

Thy Church rejoices in Thee, O Christ, crying: Thou art my strength, O Lord, my refuge and my foundation!

*Troparia:*

Having mortified the carnal mind through steadfast abstinence, O Father John, thou didst draw near unto the life-giving Spirit. *(Twice)*

Having a divine and light-bearing radiance in thy heart, O blessed Father John, it took up its dwelling brightly.

*Theotokion:*

Thou alone wast the Mediatress of good things beyond nature for those upon earth, O Mother of God; therefore unto thee do we cry: Rejoice!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8. Podoben: "The Wisdom" (Прему́дрости):*

Having wounded thy soul with divine desire, O blessed one, thou didst depart into a foreign land, having forsaken wealth, luxury, and splendor, and possessing nothing in this life; and having lived most honorably in foreign lands, thou didst wither thy body through the labors of abstinence, and having lived manfully in stillness, thou didst attain the perfection of virtues, O venerable one; therefore in faith we cry unto thee: Pray thou unto Christ God to grant remission of sins unto those who in love honor thy holy memory!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*

As a Virgin and the only one among women who without seed didst bear God in the flesh, all generations of mankind call thee blessed; for the fire of the Godhead dwelt in thee, and like an infant thou dost feed with milk the Creator and Lord; therefore both the angelic and human race worthily glorify thy most holy childbearing, and in unison we cry unto thee: Pray thou unto Christ God to grant remission of sins unto those who in faith bow down before thy most holy childbearing!

*Stavrotheotokion:*

Beholding the Lamb and Shepherd and Deliverer upon the Cross, the Ewe-lamb cried out weeping, and bitterly lamenting, exclaimed: "The world indeed rejoices, receiving deliverance through Thee; but mine inward parts are aflame, beholding Thy crucifixion which Thou endurest in Thy loving-kindness of mercy, O longsuffering Lord, abyss of mercy, and inexhaustible fountain: take pity, and grant remission of transgressions unto those who sing in faith of Thy divine sufferings!"

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*

For the sake of love for Thine image, O compassionate One, Thou didst stand upon Thy Cross, and the nations melted away; for Thou, O Lover of mankind, art my strength and my praise!

*Troparia:*

Nurtured in God-illumined lands and enlightened by radiant effusions, O Father, thou didst live in virtues and acts of abstinence, O God-wise one.

Having estranged thyself from thy fatherland and thy kinsmen, thou didst hasten unto Christ of thine own free will, taking up the cross, O God-wise and blessed one.

Mortified unto all visible things by the life-giving Cause, thou didst live in abstinence, O most wise John, unto Whom also thou didst depart.

*Theotokion:*

He Who dwelt beyond nature in thy God-receiving womb, O Virgin, the same also after childbearing preserved thee as a Virgin by divine power.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*

Thou, my Lord, hast come as a light into the world, a holy light turning back from dark ignorance those who sing of Thee in faith!

*Troparia:*

Having acquired a pure mind through stillness, O Father, by the power of the Holy Spirit thou didst escape the snares of the enemy. *(Twice)*

By the grace of God, in wisdom and teachings thou didst live, O God-manifest one, having become a priest, an all-blessed hierarch.[^73]

*Theotokion:*

All generations piously call thee blessed, O all-pure one, who didst bear Him Who is truly blessed beyond nature and word.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*

I will sacrifice unto Thee with the voice of praise, O Lord, the Church cries unto Thee, having been cleansed from the demons' blood by the blood that flowed in mercy from Thy side!

*Troparia:*

Having become a lover of divine wisdom, O divinely inspired one, thou wast crowned by her with a crown of good things, in all ways having become a disciple of light-bearing Chariton. *(Twice)*

Thou didst establish thy feet upon the rock of Christ, ascending by deeds of virtues, O God-wise one, setting ascents of divine understanding in thy heart, O Father.

*Theotokion:*

From the first curse of our mother Eve have we been delivered by thy childbearing: for having given birth unto Christ, O Virgin Mother, in place of this thou didst pour forth blessing unto all.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*

In the Persian furnace, the children of Abraham, burning with the love of piety rather than with the flame, cried out: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!

*Troparia:*

Clothed with divine power as with a garment, thou wast able to escape every heretical assault, O all-blessed one, crying: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord! *(Twice)*

The Lord Who for our sake sojourned as a stranger upon earth, the same in thy peregrination preserved thee,[^74] O blessed one, as thou criedst: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!

*Theotokion:*

Rejoice, O consecrated divine dwelling of the Most High: for through thee joy was given unto those who cry, O Mother of God: Blessed art thou among women, O all-immaculate Lady!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*

Stretching out his hands, Daniel shut the lions' mouths in the den; and girded with virtue, the children, lovers of piety, quenched the power of the fire, crying: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparia:*

Fair in body and soul and in thy piety, O venerable one, thou wast an instrument of divine understanding, closest unto the commandments, and a pipe of pure singing, chanting: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord! *(Twice)*

Thou hast passed over unto immortal life and unending blessedness, O venerable one, and wast accounted worthy to obtain incorrupt rejoicing, singing, O blessed one: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Theotokion:*

Shaking off the mortal dust, we have become incorrupt through thy divine childbearing, O all-pure one: for thou didst bear the natural and true and eternal Life, O pure Mother of God; therefore all of us call thee blessed, O Mary, Bride of God!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*

A cornerstone was cut out from thee, the unhewn mountain, O Virgin, not cut by hand of man: Christ, Who joined together the natures that had stood apart; therefore rejoicing thee, O Theotokos, we magnify.

*Troparion:*
Having found an all-blessed end through the struggle of abstinence, O Father John, thou didst depart unto Christ; and after death thou hast settled in the passionless and sorrowless life, O sacred one. *(Twice)*

*Troparion:*
Thou wast vouchsafed to settle in the land of the meek, O father, having become an imitator of silent and God-bearing men, with whom manifestly thou hast received delight, O venerable and blessed one.

*Theotokion:*
Thou hast gushed forth for us the Fountain of immortality, O Virgin, having given birth to Christ, the Water of forgiveness, through Whom we are cleansed from every defilement of sin, O Bride of God.

*April 20. Our Venerable Father Theodore Trichinas:*[^75]

*At "Lord, I have cried", stichera, Tone 8.*
*Tone 8, Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder!" (Ѽ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*

*Sticheron:*
O venerable father Theodore, thou didst offer thyself as a chosen gift unto God, having acquired all-night vigil and compunction, divine meditation, utter humility, hope, faith, and undivided love, sobriety and mercy, true dispassion, and prayer; these showed thee to be a most radiant pillar.

*Sticheron:*
O venerable father Theodore, seeking with godly understanding to be clothed in the true garment and the robe of salvation, thou didst not cease to cover thy body always with haircloth rags, and thou didst strip bare the snares of him who stripped us bare, being strengthened by the power of the Holy Spirit.

*Sticheron:*
Unto our God Who was incarnate of the Virgin Mother for our sake and became poor, thou, as poor in spirit, didst offer thyself, a living sacrifice and an acceptable whole-burnt offering, O all-blessed one, and an honorable gift; therefore thou didst also receive heavenly delight; unto Him pray unceasingly, we beseech thee, to have mercy on us.

*Glory, and now, Theotokion:*
Taking flesh from thy blood, the pre-eternal God showed thee forth, O pure one, as an intercessor for mankind;

...therefore deliver thy servants from every affliction and circumstance, and from the snares of the deceitful enemy; and vouchsafe those who glorify thee and worship before thee to partake of the radiance of the elect.

*Staurotheotokion:*[^76]
Beholding Thee nailed upon the Cross, O Jesus, and accepting the Passion willingly, the Virgin and Thy Mother, O Master, cried aloud: "Woe is me, O sweet Child! How dost Thou endure unjust wounds, O Physician, Who hast healed the infirmity of man, and delivered all from corruption by Thy tender mercy?"

*Canon, Tone 2.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Of old, the overwhelming power laid low the entire army of Pharaoh in the deep; and the Word made flesh destroyed all-wicked sin: the supremely glorified Lord, for gloriously is He glorified.

*Troparion:*
Enriched with divine gifts, thou didst bring thyself as a most precious gift unto the Creator, O father; therefore lighten my thoughts as I praise this day thy radiant memory and equal-to-the-angels life.

*Troparion:*
Having fed upon divine goodness, thou didst take the Cross upon thy shoulder in ascetic manner, O Theodore, and didst follow Him Who for thy sake willingly endured the Crucifixion, O blessed one, having put to death the carnal passions.

*Troparion:*
Stoutly arraying thyself against the enemy, O father, and fortified with the weapon of the Cross, thou didst humble him by humbling thyself and being wisely exalted; therefore thou hast been revealed as a victor and a peer of the venerable.

*Theotokion:*
For the sake of the Savior, O Virgin, we bless thee in songs as the all-immaculate among women and the Mother of God, and we honor thee, crying in harmony: "Rejoice, renewal of the ancestral fall!"

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
The wilderness, the barren church of the Gentiles, blossomed like a lily by Thy coming, O Lord, wherein my heart is established.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst blossom in the lineage of abstinence, O venerable one, like a fragrant rose, shedding sweet fragrance on our hearts through thy divine life, O most glorious one.

*Troparion:*
Strengthened by the might of the Savior, thou didst journey along the narrow, hard, and sorrowful path, O glorious one, and didst reach the broad expanse of the Sion on high.

*Troparion:*
Thy tomb is wondered at, shedding fragrant myrrh by grace, O venerable one, and proclaiming that thou hast become a sweet fragrance, O sacred father.

*Theotokion:*
Thy womb became the bridal chamber of immortality, from which the Bridegroom and Lord shone forth, O all-pure Virgin, and gathered unto Himself the choir of the venerable.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4. Podoben: "Thou Who wast lifted up" (Вознесы́йсѧ):*
As pure, approaching the Pure One, through frequent prayers and purity, thou wast purified in the Spirit, O Theodore; whence, now dwelling with the angels, thou singest the Thrice-Holy hymn unto the Master of all; therefore pray to Christ, O venerable one, to have mercy on those who honor thee with love.

*Glory, and now, Theotokion:*
Under thy protection do I, the humble one, flee, O Theotokos, falling down before thy divine protection, I pray: have mercy, O all-pure one, for my sins have surpassed my head, and I fear torment, O Lady, and tremble; make supplication, O pure one, unto thy Son, to deliver me from them.

*Staurotheotokion:*
She who in the end gave birth in the flesh to Thee, Who wast begotten of the unoriginate Father, seeing Thee hanging upon the Cross, O Christ, cried aloud: "Woe is me, O most beloved Jesus! How is it that Thou, Who art glorified as God by the angels, dost now willingly submit to be crucified by lawless men, O Son? I hymn Thee, O longsuffering One!"

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Thou didst come from the Virgin, not an envoy nor an angel, but Thyself, O Lord, incarnate, and didst save me, the whole man; therefore I cry unto Thee: glory to Thy power, O Lord.

*Troparion:*
By the frequent strokes of abstinence, thou didst abolish the passions of Egypt,[^77] and didst guide toward the heavenly land those who follow thee piously and venerably, O Theodore.

*Troparion:*
By virtuous weavings, O Theodore, thou didst fashion for thyself a pleasing and most pure robe, clothed in which thou didst enter the bridal chamber on high.

*Troparion:*
With radiant assurance thou didst accomplish thy course, O Theodore, covering thy flesh with coarse haircloth garments, whence spiritual joy shone upon thee through the Holy Spirit.

*Theotokion:*
In the dread hour of the Judgment, save and deliver me, O all-pure one, and snatch me from Gehenna, that I may call thee blessed and duly honor thee, O immaculate one.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
O Bestower of light and Creator of the ages, O Lord, guide us in the light of Thy commandments: for beside Thee we know no other God.

*Troparion:*
Having embraced an angel-like life upon the earth, O venerable one, through fasting and every affliction thou didst put to death the uprisings of passions, O God-wise Theodore.

*Troparion:*
Ever meditating upon the hour of Judgment, thou didst cast away from thy soul the sleep of despondency, rising early unto God, and being adorned with divine love.

*Troparion:*
As a true monk aspiring to the mansions on high, thou didst raise up on earth monasteries for those who live the monastic life, in which, having been benefited through thee, they honor thee, O venerable one.

*Theotokion:*
Alone thou didst give birth to hypostatic Life, O all-pure one, the Creator and Master of all; loving Him, the multitude of venerable fathers lived venerably.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Wallowing in the abyss of sin, I call upon the unfathomable abyss of Thy tender mercy: lead me up from corruption, O God.

*Troparion:*
Illumined by superior vigils, thou didst strip thy soul of all earthly things, and in quietude didst devote thyself to unceasing meditations, O Theodore.

*Troparion:*
By the brightness of thy radiant life, all under heaven was enlightened, O blessed one, proclaiming thee to be a light-bearing star of the Orient Sun.

*Troparion:*
Pressed hard by frost and cold, thou wast warmed by contemplation toward God, and didst drive away the winter of the passions by divine warmth, O Theodore.

*Theotokion:*
The beautiful Word loved thee who didst appear as good and beautiful, O pure one, and took up His abode within thy womb for our deliverance, who are faithful.

*Kontakion, Tone 4. Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Ꙗ҆ви́лсѧ є҆сѝ дне́сь):*[^78]
Thou hast appeared most marvelous in life, O Theodore, all-wise father, having exchanged haircloth rags for more than the precious treasures of the earth; therefore thou hast received a heavenly garment; pray unceasingly for us, O venerable one.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The God-opposing decree of the lawless tyrant raised high the flame; but Christ spread out a spiritual dew for the pious children, He Who is blessed and supremely glorified.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst become fragrant with myrrh by thy virtuous vows, O Theodore, and from thy precious relics thou gushest forth fragrant myrrh for us who honor thee with faith, filling souls and hearts with sweet scent, O venerable one.

*Troparion:*
As an innocent lamb, nurtured upon the pasture of abstinence, O venerable one, thou didst settle in the heavenly fold to rejoice with the Chief Shepherd; therefore ever remember thy sheep ever remember.

*Troparion:*
With drops of thy tears thou didst quench the burning furnace of the body, O sacred one, being kindled by the fire of the Holy Spirit; thou wast a light of valor and a light unto those in darkness, O father.

*Theotokion:*
The bush showed thee forth, enveloped with fire, O all-immaculate one, yet in no wise burned; for thou didst bear the Fire of the Godhead, Who kept thee unconsumed, O pure one, and after childbearing a Virgin incorrupt.[^79]

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Unto God Who descended into the fiery furnace for the Hebrew children and turned the flame into dew, sing works as the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst wash away the defilement of the soul with streams of tears, and didst inherit the torrent of delight and the land of the meek, as meek and guileless, O venerable one, and visibly adorned with righteousness.

*Troparion:*
Ever enlightened by divine gazings toward God, thou didst pass through passionate thoughts without stumbling, and through all bodily darkening, and didst pass over unto the light of dispassion, O father.

*Troparion:*
That thou mightest draw near unto true desire, thou didst show thyself abstinent, humble-minded, and longsuffering; and truly with a garment of haircloth rags didst thou cover thyself, exposing the demonic deceit.

*Theotokion:*
Adorned with the beauty of virtues and with golden tassels, O all-immaculate Virgin, thou didst bear the Word Who is fairer than all the sons of men, Whom the multitude of the venerable hymns unto the ages.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
The Son of the unoriginate Father, God and Lord, incarnate of the Virgin, hath appeared unto us to enlighten the darkened and to gather together the dispersed; therefore the all-hymned Theotokos do we magnify.

*Troparion:*
Having crucified thy flesh by the fear of thy Master, thou wast put to death unto the world, O father Theodore; and now thou hast departed rejoicing unto the life of the living, O thrice-blessed one, deified by communion and continually filled with light.

*Troparion:*
A festive day and full of consecration, we celebrate this day thy divine memory; for thou didst show thyself to be truly a son of light and of the day, and in the never-setting light thou dwellest rejoicing.

*Troparion:*
O radiant lamp, namesake of the gift of God,[^80] God-shining luminary, never-setting dawn, storehouse of the myrrh of virtues and immovable pillar of those living monastic life, deliver thy flock by thy prayers from every affliction.

*Theotokion:*
Thy womb became a dwelling of light, O Virgin, of the Sun of glory Who enlightened the world with the rays of Godhead; O thou who art full of joy among women, boast of the venerable, pure and most blessed one.

*April 21. Holy Hieromartyr Januarius and his companions; and the Holy Hieromartyr Theodore of Perge:*[^81]

*At "Lord, I have cried", stichera of Januarius, Tone 4.*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "As one noble" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*

*Sticheron:*
A most sacred priest and a lawful contender wast thou, O Januarius; entering into heaven itself not with the blood of others, but rather with thine own, where Jesus is the Forerunner of all; unceasingly looking upon Him and beholding Him, O father, Whom the angels behold.

*Sticheron:*
Cut down with the sword, cast into fire, given over to wild beasts, O wondrously worthy one, and shut up in prison for many days, thou didst remain unshaken in mind through divine power, and didst finish the contest, having suffered lawfully, O sacred wonderworker, companion of the bodiless hosts.

*Sticheron:*
Let us honor in harmony Desiderius and Proculus, Socius, Faustus, Eutychius, glorious Januarius, and wise Acutius,[^82] as wise celebrants of sacred mysteries, as crown-bearing martyrs, as citizens of the Holy City, and as true intercessors for those who call them blessed.

*Other Stichera, of Saint Theodore, Tone 4. Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*

*Sticheron:*
Standing before the unjust tribunal, O great-martyr Theodore, thou didst rebuke the godlessness of idols; and being unjustly beaten, stretched out, O most blessed one, upon a red-hot iron griddle, cruelly roasted, yet bedewed with the dew of the Spirit Who firmly strengthened thee to destroy the fierce fury of the bitter adversary.

*Sticheron:*
A manifest chariot of our God didst Thou become, having Him borne within thy pure heart having Him borne within thy pure heart, O blessed one; and tied to a chariot, thou didst loose from bonds and bring unto the love of Christ those who had bound thee; cast with them into the furnace, thou wast not burned, O Theodore, magnifying the Lord with them, O wondrously worthy one.

*Sticheron:*
Imitating Him Who stretched forth His hands upon the Cross, O blessed one, thou wast crucified, O Theodore, and didst remain hanging for three days until thou didst commend thy soul into the hands of God; thou didst finish the course of suffering, O crown of martyrs, foremost pillar of the Church, adornment of the faithful, companion of the angels.

*Glory, and now, Theotokion:*
Rain down upon me, O Lady, the abyss of thy mercy, and water my heart which is scorched by the heat of passions, like a fertile land, O Maiden, and grant that I may unceasingly shed drops of compunction, I pray, whereby I may be vouchsafed comfort, O pure one: that comfort which those who have wept sincerely shall obtain.

*Staurotheotokion:*
Beholding Christ, the Lover of mankind, being crucified, and His side pierced with a spear, the all-pure one wept and cried aloud: "What is this, O my Son? What have the ungrateful people rendered unto Thee in return for the good things Thou didst do for them? And dost Thou hasten to leave me childless, O most beloved One? I marvel, O compassionate One, at Thy voluntary Crucifixion."

*Canon of Saint Januarius.*
*Tone 4. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
O Thou Who wast born of the Virgin, drown the tripartite nature of my soul, I pray, in the deep of dispassion, as of old the mighty captains,[^83] that in the deadening of the body, as on a timbrel, I may sing a song of victory unto Thee.

*Troparion:*
One with the hosts of immaterial angels, and holily enlightening thy soul through communion with the unapproachable Light, enlighten my heart as I praise this day thy radiant feast, O blessed one.

*Troparion:*
A light-bearing army, a sacred choir suffered patiently with thee, O wise celebrant of sacred mysteries, with whom thou didst utterly overthrow the crafty serpent, having endured with courage the manifold tortures.

*Troparion:*
Following wisely the footsteps of Christ, the great and chief Shepherd, Thou didst brighten the vestment of thy hierarchal office with the dyes of martyrdom; therefore we honor thee with uprightness of mind.

*Theotokion:*
