Blessed are the people, O Theotokos, who glorify thee as the true Mother of God, and who ever call thee blessed, O pure one, even as thou didst foretell, prophesying holily when thou didst bear Christ within.

*Another Canon, to Saint Theodore, Tone 4. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
I shall open my mouth, and it will be filled with the Spirit, and I shall utter a word unto the Queen Mother, and I shall appear radiantly keeping festival, and rejoicing I shall sing her wonders.

*Troparion:*
An acceptable gift and beloved unto the Master, thou didst appear as an adornment of martyrs, O Theodore; therefore as a gift do I offer a hymn unto thee; grant me thy help.

*Troparion:*
Fair in body and adorned in spirit, thou didst set forth unto the combats against the deceiver; and having destroyed him, thou didst receive the crown of victory.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst despise the words of the wicked tyrant, O Theodore; and didst manifestly show thyself to be a sincere servant of the Lord, a soldier of Christ the King, and a perfect martyr.

*Theotokion:*
He Who is unapproachable by nature dwelt within thy womb, O all-immaculate one, and through unutterable mercy showed us to be dwellers of Paradise, from which we were cast out through the envy of the serpent.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
The bow of the mighty has become weak, and the weak have girded themselves with strength: therefore is my heart established in the Lord.

*Troparion:*
Fortified with the radiance of the Holy Spirit, thou wast manifested in the midst of the furnace, O most blessed one, imitating the pious youths of old.

*Troparion:*
The ranks of sacred powers held festival with thee in the midst of the furnace, O God-wise one, with whom thou didst hymn the Benefactor, remaining unconsumed.

*Troparion:*
A honeyed river of thy honey-flowing teachings was thy tongue truly, O all-wise Januarius, delighting the minds of those who look upon thee.

*Theotokion:*
Rend the bonds of my sins, O Bride of God, who didst shatter the bonds of Hades by thy childbearing, and fill all things with joy.

*Another Heirmos:*
Establish thy choir of singers who have gathered together, O Theotokos, living and plentiful fountain, and in thy divine glory vouchsafe them crowns of glory.

*Troparion:*
Having become a temple of the Holy Spirit, O wise one, thou didst destroy the ruinous carved temples, and by thy words didst rebuke the abominable offerings of the lawless, O Theodore.

*Troparion:*
With fury and wrathful intent the lawless judge beat thee as thou didst uphold the laws of our God, O Theodore; beholding Him before thee, thou didst remain unshaken.

*Troparion:*
Thou wast eagerly laid upon the red-hot iron griddle, O sufferer, burning with zeal for God the Almighty, and being kindled with His divine love, O Theodore.

*Theotokion:*
He Who is timeless by nature was seen under time from thee, born in the likeness of flesh, overlooking my temporal transgressions through unutterable compassion, O all-immaculate one.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4. Podoben: "Thou Who wast lifted up" (Вознесы́йсѧ):*
Having dyed the vestment of thy priesthood in the dipping of thy blood, thou didst manifestly show it to be more radiant, O servant of Christ; therefore thou hast entered into eternal mansions, pouring out an ocean of healings for those who honor thee, and ever praying unto God to grant the forgiveness of transgressions unto all.

*Glory, in the same Tone [Tone 4]:*
Come, let us praise the namesake of saving gifts and heir of that blessedness, as the imitator and martyr of Christ God, keeping festival this day in honor of his precious memory, and crying aloud unto him: O great-martyr, pray that the forgiveness of sins be granted unto us.

*And now, Theotokion:*
Under thy protection do I, the humble one, flee, O Theotokos, falling down before thy divine protection, I pray: have mercy, O all-pure one, for my sins have surpassed my head, and I fear torment, O Lady, and tremble; make supplication, O pure one, unto thy Son, to deliver me from them.

*Staurotheotokion:*
She who in the end gave birth in the flesh to Thee, Who wast begotten of the unoriginate Father, seeing Thee hanging upon the Cross, O Christ, cried aloud: "Woe is me, O most beloved Jesus! How is it that Thou, Who art glorified as God by the angels, dost now willingly submit to be crucified by lawless men, O Son? I hymn Thee, O longsuffering One!"

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
For the sake of love toward Thine image, O compassionate One, Thou didst stand upon Thy Cross, and the nations melted away: for Thou, O Lover of mankind, art my strength and my praise.

*Troparion:*
Let us honor in harmony the sacred deacon and sufferer Socius, and sacred Faustus, great Proculus, and Desiderius.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst by no means slumber with the slumber of impiety, O martyr Proculus; with divine vigils thou didst put to sleep all the malice of the godless.

*Troparion:*
Even as Daniel, so in the midst of the beasts with the fellow-sufferers did Christ our God save thee, O Januarius, bringing thee forth unharmed.[^84]

*Theotokion:*
Let us magnify the only-blessed one, through whom He Who is all-good truly blessed all, being incarnate of her.

*Another Heirmos [Canon of Saint Theodore]:*
He Who sits in glory upon the throne of the Godhead, Jesus the most divine, came on a light cloud, and with His incorrupt hand saved those who cry: "Glory to Thy power, O Christ!"

*Troparion:*
As thou didst ascend the iron griddle, a fiery angel stood by and bedewed thee, keeping thee unconsumed, O Theodore, with stream-like pourings, and with an earthquake and a rushing noise, through terrible wonders.

*Troparion:*
While thou didst faithfully work miracles, Dioscorus, who was devoted to delusion, through sudden torture was rolled like a stone unto faith, and was joined unto the choir of martyrs, being perfected by fire.[^85]

*Troparion:*
Placed in custody as a guardian of the Creator's commandments, thou didst keep unto God the faith which thou hadst from infancy, and didst abhor those who did not keep it, as fools, through thy perfect understanding, O most glorious one.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst endure the trial of painful tortures, and wast vouchsafed to heal many sicknesses, O martyr, having become an heir of the sorrowless mansions and of the truly sorrow-free life and delight.

*Theotokion:*
Without travail didst thou bear the bodiless God, O Maiden, Who loosed the birth-pangs of Eve the first mother, and delivered from the pangs of Hades those who sin and have a painful life.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Thou, my Lord, hast come into the world as a Light, a holy Light turning from gloomy ignorance those who hymn Thee with faith.

*Troparion:*
With sacred blood thou didst make thy vestment more radiant, O exceedingly rich one, and didst come unto the Church on high as a priest.

*Troparion:*
When thy most precious blood was poured out upon the earth, thou didst become a river of healings, drying up the torrents of passions.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst enlighten by thy prayer the unjust judge who had been blinded, imitating the compassion of the Good Lord of all, O exceedingly rich one.[^86]

*Theotokion:*
We sing of thee, through whom the unapproachable Light, the Master of all, shone forth upon those in darkness, O Theotokos, and we call thee blessed with love.

*Another Heirmos:*
All things were amazed at thy divine glory: for thou, O Virgin who hast not known wedlock, didst hold in thy womb the God Who is over all, and didst bear the timeless Son, [bestowing peace upon all who hymn thee] granting peace unto all that hymn thee.

*Troparion:*
By thy zealous deeds thou didst rebuke the godless deceit; therefore thou wast bound unto a chariot, yet thou didst remain unharmed when fiery steeds took thee up, a fiery chariot, O Theodore, even as glorious Elijah of old.

*Troparion:*
The lawless one was enraged, and cast thee into a burning furnace together with Socrates and divine Dionysius;[^87] with whom thou didst holily sing the prophetic song, O valiantly wise Theodore.

*Troparion:*
Let divine Dionysius be praised in hymns together with valiant Socrates: having been pierced with a spear in the midst of the furnace, they became steadfast martyrs, having obeyed the divine instruction of Theodore.

*Theotokion:*
O truly and genuinely most-immaculate sole Mother of God, strengthen me to hold sway over the passions that have enslaved me, so that with a free mind I may rejoicingly call thee blessed, the salvation of the faithful.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
I entered into the depths of the sea, and the tempest of many sins hath drowned me; but as Thou art God, bring up my life from the abyss, O greatly merciful One.

*Troparion:*
Having seen the words and signs wrought through thee, O sacred initiate, amidst the assembly of the lawless, Dioscorus[^88] came from his soul unto the Creator, being earnestly enlightened.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst appear before thy end, O hierarch, and after thy divine demise, working a multitude of miracles, having received the gift of the Holy Spirit from infancy.

*Troparion:*
Now with sacred concord let us all hymn Faustus and Socius, Desiderius with Eutychius, and wise Acutius, together with God-bearing Proculus.[^89]

*Theotokion:*
Unto the Benefactor of mankind, God the Word is incarnate from thy pure blood: Him do thou earnestly beseech, O Mother of God, that our souls may be saved.

*Another Heirmos:*
Celebrating this divine and all-honorable feast of the Mother of God, come, O godly-minded, let us clap our hands and glorify God Who was born of her.

*Troparion:*
When[^90] by the divine visitation the fire of the furnace was quenched, thy heart, O most blessed one, was filled with boldness, being refreshed by the nod toward the Creator.

*Troparion:*
When the tormentor heard the words of the holy one who bare thee, O Martyr Theodore, he delivered her unto the sword, as she divinely finished her course in martyrdom.

*Troparion:*
Having learned, as a youthful maiden, that she was to die with her son, Philippa came with haste[^91] into the midst of the tribunal unbidden, receiving the glory of the martyrs.

*Theotokion:*
Behold the holy mountain, behold the alabaster box of myrrh of the Spirit, the bridge leading all unto God, the Virgin Mother of God, whom we call blessed.

*Kontakion, Tone 2:*
With the anointing of the priesthood and the blood of martyrdom have ye been adorned, O glorious Januarius and Theodore; and ye shine everywhere, dancing in the highest: watch over us who have come into your temple, and therein unceasingly cry: Guard us all, praying unto God the Lover of mankind.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The three youths in Babylon, having turned the tormentor's command into foolishness, cried aloud in the midst of the flame: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers.

*Troparion:*
Thou wast likened unto the divine youths, O Januarius, dancing around in the midst of the flame through the descent of an angel, and singing unto the Savior in mystical hymns: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers.

*Troparion:*
Preserving thy mind free from passion by divine grace, even before thy suffering thou didst heal all manner of diseases, being known as shepherd and martyr and great wonderworker.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst stand before the tribunal of the tormentors, O martyr, suffering patiently, putting the wicked to shame, and showing the truth unto all who were deceived.

*Theotokion:*
Put to death my carnal motions, O most pure one who didst give birth unto Life; revive my soul, O pure one, deadened by passions and many sins.

*Another Heirmos:*
The godly-minded worshipped not the creature rather than the Creator, but valiantly trampling down the threat of fire, they rejoiced, singing: Blessed art Thou, O all-hymned Lord and God of our fathers.

*Troparion:*
Bedewed by the dew of the Spirit even as the three youths, unburned in the midst of the fire, O godly-minded Theodore, thou didst sing with them: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers, Who art praised above all.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst refute the Hellenic delusion, O Martyr Theodore, by the word of grace flowing richly from thy lips, and thou didst cry aloud: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers.

*Troparion:*
She that bare thee knew thy name and thy suffering even before thy conception, O Theodore, great and much-suffering martyr, crying aloud: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers.

*Theotokion:*
Be thou my purification, O all-immaculate one, unto Him Who was born of thee, mediating for me remission of cruel debts, and the divine entrance into the Kingdom of God, and enjoyment of delights, and communion of light.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
O almighty Redeemer of all, having descended into the midst of the flame, Thou didst bedew those who lived piously, and didst teach them to sing: All ye works, bless and praise the Lord.

*Troparion:*
Let great Proculus be praised, with Acutius and Eutychius, and divine Desiderius, Faustus and Socius, the valiantly wise, glorious martyrs of the Savior.

*Troparion:*
The blessed choir of sufferers gained thee as their leader, O glorious sacred initiate; it trampled down the prince of demons, and destroyed the exalted pride of godlessness.

*Troparion:*
Even as the youths thou didst trample upon the fire, and didst bridle the assaults of the beasts, even as Daniel most excellently, and like Paul thou wast beheaded, having piously finished thy course.

*Theotokion:*
He Who ineffably dwelt in thy womb showed thee forth as a dwelling-place for Himself, O all-pure Bride of God; unto Whom unceasingly we cry: All ye works of the Lord, bless the Lord.

*Another Heirmos:*
The childbirth of the Mother of God saved the pious youths in the furnace, being prefigured then, but now fulfilled in act; it raises up all the universe to sing unto Thee: Hymn the Lord, all ye works, and exalt Him above all unto all ages.

*Troparion:*
Having been lifted up upon the cross, thou didst spend three days suspended thereon, O Theodore, ever mindful of the Passion of thy Master that lasted three hours, visibly portraying it: Hymn the Lord, all ye works, and exalt Him above all unto ages.

*Troparion:*
Thou wast laid upon the tree, receiving painful wounds, and didst wound the serpent adversary, who by the tasting of the tree wrought painful death for Adam and his descendants, O Theodore; therefore do we honor thee unto all ages.

*Troparion:*
Perge cries aloud of thy struggles, pains, suffering, wounds, blessed passion, and honorable death, whereby thou didst pass unto immortal life, O Theodore, crying: Bless the Lord, all ye works, and exalt Him above all unto ages.

*Theotokion:*
O depth of the ineffable mystery! He Who of old fashioned man with His hand is fashioned, becoming incarnate, having entered into the womb of the Ever-Virgin; and He saves me who cry with fear: Hymn the Lord, all ye works all ye works, and exalt Him above all unto ages.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Eve through the disease of disobedience brought in the curse; but thou, O Virgin Mother of God, through the budding of thy pregnancy didst make blessing blossom forth unto the world: therefore do we all magnify thee.

*Troparion:*
With gladness let us magnify the honorable struggles, contests, and scourgings of Januarius, his courage even unto blood, his afflictions and violent death, and the immeasurable deep of his miracles.

*Troparion:*
Thou wast seen on the height of honorable martyrdom, O glorious one, even as an unsetting sun, leading the multitude of martyrs as divine stars; and now he enlightens the ends of the earth with the brightness of great sufferings.

*Troparion:*
Thy light-bearing memory hath shone forth with rays of the Divine Spirit, illuminating the whole world and driving away the gloom of evils; therefore on it we beseech thee, O divinely inspired one: Enlighten all who hymn thee.

*Theotokion:*
By the radiance of the Light within thee, enlighten my blinded soul, and by thy hand, O Maiden, guide me to walk in the paths of the saved and flee stumbling-blocks of delusion, that in faith I may call thee blessed.

*Another Heirmos:*
Let every earthborn mortal leap for joy, being enlightened by the Spirit; and let the nature of the bodiless minds celebrate, honoring the sacred festival of the Mother of God, and let it cry aloud: Rejoice, O all-blessed Mother of God, pure Ever-Virgin!

*Troparion:*
Following the footsteps of Christ, O all-wise one, thou didst endure the crucifixion of the First Martyr; receiving bonds and wounds of nails, and from earth thou didst ascend unto heaven, receiving as a crown-bearer the honor: the Kingdom of God that hath no end.[^92]

*Troparion:*
Thou didst appear as brightly shining gold, O Theodore, standing in the midst of the fire through the descent of the honorable angel; inflamed with longing for the Master, thou didst remain unconsumed, holily and rejoicingly singing the song of salvation with him.

*Troparion:*
Let us stand piously in the house of our God, in faith blessing His champion, who contended with a manly mind, overcame the insolence of the enemy, and received from above the crown of victory from the hand of Christ.

*Troparion:*
Thou wast taken up unto the mansions on high, rejoicing, O Martyr Theodore, and didst reach the ranks of the bodiless powers, having in the body valiantly conquered the bodiless demons; and thou prayest for those who honor thee in faith, that they may receive deliverance from debts.

*Theotokion:*
Enlighten the eyes of my soul, O pure Mother of God, lest the heavy darkness of sin overtake me, nor the abyss of despair cover me; but do thou thyself save and guide me, O shameless Mediatress of the faithful.

*The same month, on the 22nd day. Our Venerable Father Theodore the Sykeote:*[^93]

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera, Tone 2, Podoben: "O venerable father" (Преподо́бне ѻ̓́ч҃е):*

*Sticheron:*
O venerable father, holy Theodore, thou wast sanctified from the womb and wast known unto God even as Jeremiah of old. A star foretold thy birth; by the radiance of thy life the whole inhabited world was truly enlightened, and demonic darkness vanished; plagues were driven away from every age, and the grace of great healings poured forth. Therefore we cry unto thee: Ever entreat Christ to grant unto the world peace and great mercy.

*Sticheron:*
O venerable father, wondrously revered Theodore, by steadfast temperance and the fire of prayer having consumed all the matter of sin, thou didst receive the divine dawn, didst house within thee prophetic grace, and didst appear all light-bearing, radiating beams of healings, foreseeing the future even as Elisha of old, loosing the bonds of barrenness, and driving away demonic legions by the power of the Holy Spirit, and praying that peace and great mercy be granted unto the universe.

*Sticheron:*
O venerable father, all-honorable Theodore, sun that hast illumined the whole earth in countless deeds and signs, an honorable gift unto Christ God, a proven degree of the priesthood, pattern of monastics, confirmation of those who flee unto thee in faith, precious stone, and temple of the Trinity, basin pouring forth an abyss of healings: ever pray unto Christ that peace and great mercy be granted unto the universe.

*Glory, both now, Theotokion:*
Unto whom hast thou become likened, O my soul, ever advancing in worse things, and foolishly adding more arrows unto thy wounds, as though the wound were merely bodily? Reflecting not that the Judge draws near, before Whom thou shalt stand to receive condemnation and torments according to thy deeds. But turn, and fall down before the Virgin, crying: O Lady, Lady, despise me not who have angered the compassionate God Who was born of thee for the salvation of men, and was crucified in the flesh.

*Stavrotheotokion:*
When the unplanted Cluster of Grapes Whom thou didst bring forth as a vine was seen by thee hanging upon the Tree and pierced in His divine side with a spear: "What is this," she said, "O my Son and my God? How dost Thou Who healest all infirmities and sufferings endure passions, being passionless according to Thy divine nature? What have the thankless people rendered unto Thee, the Benefactor, in return for Thy benefits?" so cried the most pure one. But do thou unceasingly beseech Him by His sufferings to free me from passions, that I may glorify thee.

*Troparion, Tone 2:*
Being known as sanctified from swaddling clothes, and shown forth full of graces, thou didst illumine the world with miracles, and didst drive away a multitude of demons, O sacred celebrant Theodore; therefore pray unto the Lord on our behalf.

*Canon, The work of Joseph. Tone 4. Ode 1. Heirmos:*[^94]
Unto Him Who of old guided Israel fleeing from the bondage of Pharaoh, and fed him in the wilderness, let us sing, unto our Redeemer God, for He hath been glorified.

*Troparion:*
The noetic Sun showed thee forth as a radiant star unto the firmament of the Church, O all-wise one, ever emitting rays of miracles and destroying the mists of passions.

*Troparion:*
From thy mother's womb the Creator, O venerable one, even as He sanctified Jeremiah of old, made thee a vessel of the Spirit, meet for good use, driving away evil spirits, O Father Theodore.

*Troparion:*
By the sweat of temperance thou didst quench the flame of the passions, and through the raining down of prayers thou didst pour forth streams of miracles, visibly extinguishing the burning fever of diseases, O venerable Theodore.

*Theotokion:*
The uncultivated cluster blossomed forth from thy womb, O Virgin Mother, pouring forth the sweetness of forgiveness and immortal gladness, O pure one, unto those made bitter by the intoxication of cruel evils.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
Thou that establishest the thunder and createst the wind, establish me, O Lord, that I may truly hymn Thee and do Thy will; for there is none holy as Thou, O our God.

*Troparion:*
How marvelous is thy life, O godly-minded one! How awesome are thy miracles! How lofty is thy intimacy and inclination toward God, and thine unswerving ascent, O all-glorious Theodore!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst restrain the rush of the passions, O most blessed one, by painful abstinences,[^95] and didst destroy the crafty snares of the demons through the might of the All-Holy Spirit, O Theodore.

*Troparion:*
Fleeing unto thy precious shrine, we draw from it treasures of healings, and oceans of miracles, and ungrudging gift, as we hymn thee, O Theodore.

*Theotokion:*
The laws of nature are renewed in thee, O Virgin; for beyond nature thou dost give birth unto the Creator, and beyond word unto the Master: Him do thou earnestly entreat to save our souls.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*
Being given unto men as a gift named of God,[^96] for the deliverance from many diseases, thou didst work great and awesome miracles, healing every plague and sickness, and driving away legions of demons by thy word. Therefore, having also worthily shepherded the Church of Christ, thou wast a fellow-citizen of the Apostles, O Hierarch Theodore: entreat Christ God to grant remission of sins unto those who lovingly honor thy holy memory.

*Glory, both now, Theotokion:*
Having fallen into manifold temptations from enemies visible and invisible, held fast by the tempest of my countless sins: as unto my fervent protection and shelter, O pure one, I flee unto the haven of thy goodness. Therefore, O most pure one, earnestly beseech Him Who without seed was incarnate of thee, on behalf of all thy servants who unceasingly pray unto thee, O most pure Mother of God; entreating Him ever to grant remission of sins unto those who worthily hymn thy glory.

*Stavrotheotokion:*
The Ewe, beholding the Lamb and Shepherd and Redeemer upon the Cross, cried aloud weeping, and bitterly lamenting, exclaimed: The world indeed rejoices, receiving redemption through Thee, but my womb burns seeing Thy Crucifixion, which Thou endurest in Thy tender mercy, O longsuffering Lord! O abyss of mercy and unfathomable fountain, have compassion and grant remission of sins unto those who in faith hymn Thy divine Passion.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
I heard Thy report, O God, and was afraid;

...I understood Thy works, and was filled with dread, O Lord, for the earth is full of Thy praise.

*Troparion:*
Being visibly a torrent filled with the waters of the divine gifts of the Spirit, thou dost water the whole Church, O Father Theodore.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst choke the enemy with the sinews of thy painful afflictions, O most blessed one, and didst destroy his crafty hosts by the sword of thy prayers, O Theodore.

*Troparion:*
Meditating upon the law of God at the outgoings of temperance, thou didst grow up as a divine tree, yielding the good fruit of virtuous deeds, O Theodore.

*Theotokion:*
Thou hast been shown blessed among all generations, having given birth unto Christ Who makes blessed those who serve Him, O Virgin Mother, pure Lady.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Make the light of Thy commandments shine forth upon me, O Lord, for my spirit seeks Thee early and hymns Thee; for Thou art our God, and unto Thee do I flee, O King of peace.

*Troparion:*
The timeless Light showed thee forth as an inextinguishable lamp and an unquenchable candlestick, O Theodore, unto those held fast by the gloom of the passions and blackened by many sins.

*Troparion:*
From early childhood thou hadst the Great-Martyr George[^97] guiding thee well upon the path; and unto him thou wast likened with all zeal and love toward the Lord, O venerable father.

*Troparion:*
Thou hadst blameless temperance, unfailing patience, faith and hope and humility, love and steadfastness surpassing man; therefore thou didst find abundant grace from God.

*Theotokion:*
By the sprinkling of thy mercy, O most pure one, water my mind that is wasted by the flame of sin, and having quenched it, kindle the lamp of my heart, O Gate of Light.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
The tempest of thoughts having overtaken me, drags me down into the deep of immeasurable sins; but do Thou, O good Helmsman, guide me beforehand as the Prophet, and save me.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst shake off the sorrow of earthly things, and didst pour out all thy longing toward the Master, taking delight in His goodness and the splendors flowing thence, O father.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst melt the frost of sin, being filled with cold, standing in the midst of the open air as one bodiless, O father. Thou didst endure the assaults of the air, being warmed by divine grace.

*Troparion:*
Approaching thy sacred shrine, we are filled with the sweet fragrance of divine knowledge, O hierarch, being delivered from the stench of the passions, as we lovingly honor thee, O wonderworking father, venerable one.

*Theotokion:*
The surge of passions troubles me, the abyss of despair tempest-tosses my heart. Having given birth unto Christ the Helmsman and Savior, O Virgin, deliver and save me by thy prayers.

*Kontakion, Tone 3, Podoben: "Today the Virgin" (Дѣ́ва дне́сь):*
As upon a fiery chariot, having ascended by virtues, O God-bearer, thou didst mount up unto heavenly dwellings: as an angel thou didst live with men, and as a man thou dost dance around with the angels. Therefore thou hast been shown forth as a divine receptacle of miracles, O venerable Theodore.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
From the Persian furnace, the Abrahamic youths, inflamed rather by the love of piety than by the flame, cried aloud: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord.

*Troparion:*
As a true hierarch, thou didst offer the unbloody sacrifice in the Spirit. And by the mortification of the passions, O most blessed one, thou didst offer thyself in faith as a sweet-smelling sacrifice unto the Master.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst quench the fire of pleasures by the sweat of temperance, and during drought thou didst cause rain-clouds to stand over the earth: through thy prayers, O wonderworker, even as Elijah, the shut heavens were opened by thy supplications.

*Troparion:*
Sanctified from infancy, through prayerful valor thou didst make barren wombs fruitful with many children; unto the blind thou didst give sight, and unto the lame swift running, O thrice-blessed Theodore.

*Theotokion:*
God chose thee as the beauty of Jacob,[^98] and dwelt in thee, and preserved thee after giving birth a Virgin, even as thou wast before birthgiving, O Virgin Mother of God.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Earth, and all that is therein, sea, and all springs, heaven of heavens, light, and darkness, frost, and heat, ye sons of men, ye priests, bless the Lord, and exalt Him above all unto ages.

*Troparion:*
Having established the ascents of thy soul upon the rock of endurance, thou wast not overcome by adverse assaults, nor shaken by the wiles of the enemy; but thou didst mount up unto divine height, praying for those who honor thee in faith.

*Troparion:*
A stream issues forth from thy shrine of all manner of healings, and washes away the defilements of the passions, and cleanses the festering of cruel afflictions, and waters the hearts of all the faithful who lovingly honor thee, O Theodore.

*Troparion:*
Let Theodore the great now be praised: named after divine gifts,[^99] initiate of Christ, beauty of fasters, adornment of priests, fountain of miracles, honorable alabaster box of the myrrh of the Spirit.

*Theotokion:*
The sacred voices of the prophets figuratively preach thee as the gate, and mountain, and shadow, and holy land, and radiant cloud, from whom Christ the Sun and Light-giver shone forth unto those sitting in darkness and shadow, O Virgin.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
For He that is mighty hath done great things unto me, and holy is His Name, and His mercy is from generation to generation unto them that fear Him.

*Troparion:*
As the morning, as a bright day, thy festival hath appeared, enlightening the hearts of us who faithfully praise thy venerable sufferings, O Father Theodore.

*Troparion:*
Having become a companion of the angels, apostles, martyrs, venerable and righteous, O father and hierarch, thou dost ever entreat Christ on our behalf in heaven.

*Troparion:*
A day of salvation, a feast of gladness hath appeared; let us all run together, having purified our souls: Theodore the hierarch summons us.

*Theotokion:*
Awesome is thy birthgiving, O Mother of God, Mother of Christ; therefore we call thee blessed, O pure one, and honorably glorify thee, generations of generations unto the ages. Amen.

*The same month, on the 23rd day. Holy, Glorious Great-Martyr, Victory-bearer and Wonderworker George:*[^100]

*If it be the patronal feast of the temple, or if the superior wish, we perform a vigil.*

*At Little Vespers, at "Lord, I have cried," Stichera, Tone 4, Podoben: "Of the celestial ranks" (Ю́ныхъ чинѡ́въ):*[^101]

*Sticheron:*
The radiant cause of truth is come, and renews the creation through the pious knowledge of Christ the Life-giver; therefore, clearly enlightened in understanding, the Lord's champion trampled down the craftiness of the enemy.

...craftiness.[^102] *(Twice)*

*Sticheron:*
Thou didst soldier for the King of ages, O all-wise one, and didst cast down all the hosts of the lawless, O thrice-blessed George, and wast shown forth as a steadfast champion, by the grace given unto thee from God, O wonderworker; therefore pray that we may be saved.

*Sticheron:*
Thou dost pour forth streams of healings unto those who hasten unto thee, O thrice-blessed George, and thou art a helper of the souls and bodies of those who with fervent faith flee unto thy protection, O right marvelous martyr of Jesus, adornment of champions.

*Glory, in the same tone, Idiomelon:*
O warrior George of the Great King, rejoice and be glad! For having well-pleased God and disregarded all things, thou didst receive eternal life in the heavens; for thy body drives away every disease from men. For Christ Whom thou didst love, He glorifies thee, O blessed one.

*Both now, of the Feast.*

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 4, Idiomela:*
*Sticheron:*
Clap your hands, all ye nations, shout unto God, ye lovers of the martyrs; for He was lifted up upon the Tree, and descending into the tomb, He overthrew hades and raised the dead with Himself, as we cry unto Him: Alleluia, alleluia, alleluia, unto Christ Who is risen!

*Verse: The righteous man shall flourish like a palm tree.* [LXX Ps. 91:13a]

*Sticheron:*
Dancing together in choir, let us bring a singing of songs unto Christ our Savior, for the valiant champion, having conquered, hath cast down the tyrant enemy. Therefore let us all sing with unceasing voices: Alleluia, alleluia, alleluia, unto Christ Who is risen!

*Verse: They that are planted in the house of the Lord, shall flourish in the courts of our God.* [LXX Ps. 91:14]

*Sticheron:*
Thou hast appeared as the beauty of martyrs and the glory of the righteous, O crown-bearer George; for having trampled down the folly of the enemy upon earth by courage of mind and wisdom of soul, thou dost cry with us: Alleluia, alleluia, alleluia, unto Christ the Giver of life!

*Glory, in the same tone:*
Come, ye lovers of the martyrs, let us offer a singing of hymns unto Christ Who is risen from the tomb. For today the noetic spring hath shone forth unto us, granting blossoms unto rational beings: the universal memory of George, the wise great-martyr. Worthily honoring him, we receive fountains of healings; with whom also now let us entreat him, to pray unceasingly unto Christ the Savior, to grant peace unto the universe, and great mercy unto our souls.

*Both now, of the Feast:*
*Troparion:*

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
Thou didst fight the good fight, O passion-bearer of Christ, through faith; and thou didst expose the impiety of the torturers, and didst offer thyself as an acceptable sacrifice unto God. Therefore thou didst also receive the crown of victory, and by thy holy prayers, thou dost grant unto all forgiveness of transgressions.

*At Great Vespers, "Blessed is the man:" At "Lord, I have cried," we set 8 verses.*
*Stichera of the Feast, 3; and of the Saint, 5.*
*Tone 4, Automelon: "As one valiant among martyrs" (Ꙗ̓́кѡ до́блѧ въ мꙋ́нцѣхъ):*

*Sticheron:*
As one valiant among martyrs, O passion-bearer George, having gathered together today we praise thee; for having finished the course, thou didst keep the faith, and didst receive from God the crown of thy victory: Whom do thou beseech, to deliver from corruption and perils those who celebrate in faith thy right honorable memory.

*Podoben (Подо́бенъ):*
Trusting with steadfast intention, thou didst willingly dare, as a lion, O glorious one, unto suffering; having indeed despised the body as that which is about to perish, thou didst wisely care for the incorrupt soul. And thou wast tried by manifold kinds of tortures, as gold refined sevenfold, O George.

*Sticheron:*
Thou didst suffer for the Savior, and by death voluntarily likening thyself unto His death, O glorious one, thou reignest with Him radiantly, robed in a bright purple garment of blood, adorned with the scepter of thy sufferings, and majestic with the crown of victory, unto unpassing ages, O Great-Martyr George.

*Sticheron:*
Having armed thyself with the breastplate of faith, and with the shield of grace, and with the spear of the Cross, thou wast invincible unto adversaries, O George; as a divine warrior, having conquered the demonic hosts, thou dost dance in choir with the angels; and thou dost protect and sanctify the faithful, and dost save those who call upon thee.

*Sticheron:*
As a most radiant star, shining as the sun upon the firmament of heaven do we know thee, and as a precious pearl, as a gleaming stone, as a son of the day, as a valiant one among martyrs, as a champion of the faithful in perils do we glorify thee, as we celebrate thy memory, O victory-bearer George.

*Glory, Tone 6:*
Thou didst live worthily of thy name, O warrior George; for taking the Cross of Christ upon thy shoulder, thou didst well cultivate the earth that had been smoothed by the devil's deceit, and having uprooted the thorn-like service of idols thou didst plant the vine of the Orthodox faith. Therefore thou dost pour forth healings for the faithful throughout the whole universe, and hast been revealed as a righteous husbandman of the Trinity: pray, we beseech thee, for the peace of the world, and for the salvation of our souls.

*Both now, of the Feast.*

*Entrance. Prokeimenon of the day, and the Readings.*

*The Reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah (Chapter 43):*[^103]
Thus says the Lord: All the nations are gathered together, and princes shall be gathered out of them. Who will declare these things among them? Or who will declare to you things from the beginning? Let them bring forth their witnesses, and let them be justified; and let them hear, and speak truth. Be ye My witnesses, and I too am a witness, says the Lord God, and the Servant Whom I have chosen: that ye may know, and believe Me, and understand that I am He: before Me there was no other God, and after Me there shall be none. I am God, and beside Me there is no savior. I have declared, and have saved; I have reproached, and there was no strange god among you. Ye are My witnesses, and I am the Lord God, and even from the beginning I am He: and there is none that delivers out of My hands. I will work, and who shall turn it back? Thus says the Lord God, the Holy One of Israel, Who delivers you. [LXX Isa. 43:9–14a]

*The Reading from the Wisdom of Solomon (Chapter 3):*[^104]
The souls of the righteous are in the hand of God, and no torment shall touch them. In the eyes of the foolish they seemed to die, and their departure was taken for misery, and their going from us to be utter destruction, but they are in peace. For though they be punished in the sight of men, yet is their hope full of immortality. And having been a little chastised, they shall be greatly rewarded, for God proved them, and found them worthy for Himself. As gold in the furnace hath He tried them, and received them as a whole burnt offering. And in the time of their visitation they shall shine, and run to and fro like sparks among the stubble. They shall judge nations, and have dominion over peoples, and the Lord shall reign over them unto the ages. They that put their trust in Him shall understand the truth, and such as be faithful in love shall abide with Him; for grace and mercy are upon His holy ones, and visitation is among His elect. [Wisdom 3:1–9]

*The Reading from the Wisdom of Solomon (Chapter 4):*[^105]
Though the righteous happen to die early, yet shall he be in rest. For honorable age is not that which consists in length of time, nor is it measured by number of years; but wisdom is the gray hair unto men, and an unspotted life is ripe old age. He pleased God, and was beloved of Him, and living among sinners he was translated. He was taken away, lest wickedness should alter his understanding, or deceit beguile his soul. For the fascination of wickedness obscures that which is good, and the roaming of desire perverts the innocent mind. Being perfected in a short time, he fulfilled long years; for his soul pleased the Lord: therefore He hastened away from the midst of wickedness. And the people saw it, and understood it not, neither laid they this up in their thoughts: That grace and mercy are upon His holy ones, and visitation is among His elect.

*At the Litija, the Sticheron of the Temple, and of the Saint, Idiomela. Tone 4. By Anatolios:*[^106]

*Sticheron:*
The radiant warrior George, in his glorious suffering gladdens the heavenly hosts, and rejoicing now with them, he hath stirred up the faithful on earth to celebrate, and for us who are gathered together for his sake to keep feast, for the servant of Christ is come. Therefore let us worthily honor him, who unceasingly prays unto the God of all, to grant us great mercy.

*Sticheron:*
Having cultivated that which is best by valiant sufferings, thou didst offer the fruits of thy labors unto Christ, O most blessed martyr George; having confirmed thy desire by faith, and cast away fear by hope, thou didst acquire heavenly love, O most praised one. Having therefore boldness, by acceptable entreaties ask peace for our souls, cleansing of sins, and great mercy.

*Tone 4:*
*Sticheron:*
Come, ye assemblies of the faithful, come, let us compose a festival; for the all-radiant memory of the passion-bearer George hath appeared today, invisibly enlightening our hearts, shining with virtues. Therefore with one mind singing, let us say: Rejoice, O warrior of Christ the Great King! Rejoice, all-radiant boast of the faith! Rejoice, all-luminous and most rich one! On our behalf, O most blessed one, pray unto Christ God, the Master of all, that we may be preserved from the temptations of the evil one, and that He may save our souls.

*Glory, Tone 5. By Theophanes:*[^107]
Spring hath shone forth, come, let us take our delight! The Resurrection of Christ hath shone forth, come, let us be glad! The memory of the passion-bearer hath appeared, enlightening the faithful. Therefore, ye lovers of feasts, come, let us mystically celebrate it: for He, as a good soldier, having acted valiantly against the torturers, put them to shame; having become an imitator of the Passion of Christ the Savior, He spared not His earthen vessel, but exposed it naked, exchanging it through tortures. Unto him let us cry aloud:

...O passion-bearer, pray that our souls may be saved!

*Both now, of the Feast.*

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 4, Podoben: "Being called from on high" (Зва́нный свы́ше):*
*Sticheron:*
The peoples praise with psalms together and with hymns thy glorious memory, O George; for it hath shone forth comely and light-bearing, adorned with glory and grace. Therefore the angelic ranks now dance, and the martyrs with the Apostles praise the painful sufferings of thy struggles, and sing unto Him Who hath glorified thee, Christ the Savior, our God: Whom do thou beseech, to save and enlighten our souls.

*Verse: The righteous man shall flourish like a palm tree, he shall be increased like a cedar in Lebanon.* [LXX Ps. 91:13]

*Sticheron:*
Clothed in the armor of Christ, thou wast found, O George, of them that sought thee not; for being kindled with fire for Christ, and mocking the godless fury of the vain gods, thou didst then cry aloud unto the lawless: "I soldier for my King, Christ! Neither beasts, nor wheels, nor any other torment shall ever be able to separate me from the love of my Christ": Whom do thou beseech, to save and enlighten our souls.

*Verse: They that are planted in the house of the Lord, shall flourish in the courts of our God.* [LXX Ps. 91:14]

*Sticheron:*
Heeding not the manifold instruments, nor the diverse torments, nor the terrible weapons, O crown-bearer George, thou didst martyrlike finish the course of piety; therefore thy most radiant memory do we crown with flowers of song, and with faith we kiss thy venerable relics; but as thou standest ever before the throne of Christ our God, cease not to pray, to save and enlighten our souls.

*Glory, in the same tone, Idiomelon. By the Studite:*[^108]
Let us spiritually praise, brethren, the endurance of the noetic adamant, the glorious martyr George; whom being kindled for Christ, perils forged, and torments sharpened, and diverse tortures wore away his body naturally corruptible: for desire conquered nature, urging the lover through death to pass over unto the desired Christ God, and Savior of our souls.

*Both now, of the Feast.*

*At the Blessing of the loaves, Troparion, Tone 4:*[^109]
As deliverer of captives, and defender of the poor, physician of the ailing, champion of kings, O victory-bearer, Great-Martyr George, entreat Christ God that our souls may be saved. *(Twice) And "O Theotokos and Virgin:" once. And the rest.*[^110]
*At Matins, at "God is the Lord," the Troparion of the Feast, twice.*
*Glory, of the Saint; Both now, of the Feast.*
*After the first Kathisma reading, Sessional Hymn of the Feast.*
*Glory... Both now... the same.*
*After the second Kathisma reading, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "While the soldiers were guarding Thy tomb" (Гро́бъ тво́й):*

*Sessional Hymn:*
The radiant spring of grace has shone forth, the Resurrection of Christ has illumined all; and with it there shines forth now the all-festal and light-bearing day of the martyr George. Come, all ye who bear candles divinely, let us celebrate brightly!

*Glory, in the same tone, Podoben: "The angelic choir" (Ли́къ а̓́гг҃лскїй):*
*Sessional Hymn:*
Having hated the army upon earth, O martyr, thou didst desire the glory in the heavens; and thou didst endure afflictions and a violent death. Therefore today we celebrate thy right holy memory, offering praise unto Christ, O most blessed George.

*Both now, of the Feast, or Theotokion:*
All we who flee with love unto thy goodness know thee as the Mother of God, who wast revealed as a Virgin truly even after giving birth; for we sinners have thee as an intercessor, thee have we gained as salvation in afflictions, the only all-immaculate one.

*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Of divine faith" (Бж҃е́ственныѧ вѣ́ры):*
*Sessional Hymn:*
Being kindled with a burning love for piety, thou didst manly-mindedly overthrow the treasures of delusion; and having confessed Christ in the tribunal, O all-blessed victory-bearer George, right glorious martyr, entreat Christ God to grant us great mercy.

*Glory, Tone 4, Podoben: "Joseph was amazed" (Оу̓диви́сѧ):*
*Sessional Hymn:*
Having diligently cultivated the seeds of the divine commandments, thou didst piously disperse all thy riches unto the poor, having gained the glory of Christ instead of them, O right glorious one. Therefore, trusting, thou didst go unto the struggles and unto long afflictions, O martyr George; and having become a partaker of the Passion and Resurrection of the Passionless One, partaking of His Kingdom, pray now for us.

*Both now, of the Feast, or Theotokion:*
We have known the Word of the Father, Christ our God, Who was incarnate of thee, O Virgin Mother of God, only pure one, only blessed one; therefore unceasingly singing of thee, we magnify thee.

*Songs of Ascents, the 1st Antiphon of Tone 4.*
*Prokeimenon, Tone 4:*
The righteous man shall flourish like a palm tree.
*Verse: They that are planted in the house of the Lord, shall flourish in the courts of our God.*

*Let every breath:*
*Verse: Praise God in His saints.*
*Gospel according to Luke, Section 106 [Luke 21:12–19].*[^111]
*After Psalm 50, Sticheron, Idiomelon, Tone 6:*
Today the whole universe is enlightened by the rays of the passion-bearer, and the Church of Christ, adorned with flowers, cries unto thee, O George: O favorite of Christ, and fervent protector, cease not to pray for thy servants!

*Canon of the Feast, on 6; and two canons of the Saint, on 8.*
*The Canon of the Saint, the work of Kyros Theophanes. Tone 2.*[^112]
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
In the deep of old, the overwhelming power laid low the whole host of Pharaoh; but the incarnate Word destroyed all-wicked sin, the most-glorified Lord, for gloriously has He been glorified.

*Troparion:*
Unto those who with fervent faith and love call upon thee, O martyr of Christ, crown-bearer George: standing most radiant before the honorable throne of the Master of all, preserve them by thy prayers and intercessions.

*Troparion:*
Thou wast a noble cultivation unto Christ, O George, cultivated by martyr deeds; and as a right glorious treasure, the Establisher of the contest placed thee in the heavenly treasuries, as one who valiantly fought.

*Troparion:*
Steadfastly and patiently thou didst hasten unbidden unto the struggles, O most blessed George; and having overthrown the fierce host of the torturers, thou wast an intercessor for all who call upon thee.

*Troparion:*
Having now received the blessed life hidden in Christ, O George, for which thou didst struggle unto blood, pray, O passion-bearer, that those who sing of thee in faith may be delivered from every affliction.

*Theotokion:*
Thou wast a noetic heaven, O Mother of God, who didst contain in thy womb the Heavenly Word, through Whom all things were made: heaven and earth, and the things higher than these; therefore with boldness pray to save those who sing of thee.

*Another Canon, the work of Kyros Cosmas. Tone 4.*[^113]
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Having passed through the Red Sea with unwetted feet, and having chased away the watery deep, seeing the captains and enemy horsemen drowned therein, they sang with gladness: Let us sing unto our God, for He has been glorified!

*Troparion:*
Thou hast finished the course of sacred sufferings, and going unto heaven, thou wast shown forth as an incorrupt crown-bearer. Flourishing fairly, O George, thou hast appeared as a champion, and thou dost dance with the angels: let us sing unto our God, for He has been glorified! *(Twice)*

*Troparion:*
By the desire of good things yet more, thou wast shown forth desirable unto the heavenly choirs. By thy divine bodily sufferings, O George, thou didst astonish the ranks of the bodiless; dancing with them, thou criest: Let us sing unto our God, for He has been glorified!

*Theotokion:*
The rod of Aaron that budded, which was of Jesse, prefigured thee, O all-pure one, the root that sprouted, who didst make the incarnate God shine forth as a flower unto the world: Whom do thou pray for us, cease not ever, O Virgin, who flee unto thee.

*Katavasia:*[^114]
*It is the Day of Resurrection, let us be radiant, O ye people!*

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
The wilderness has blossomed as a lily, O Lord, the barren church of the nations, by Thy coming, wherein my heart has been established.

*Troparion:*
Shining with the crown of suffering, O glorious one, pray that they who piously call upon thee may be delivered, and entreat God to deliver us from every necessity, O blessed one.

*Troparion:*
Illumined with rich splendors, O all-wise one, drive away from us dark sorrow, and the mist of passions, who praise thee in faith.

*Troparion:*
Confirmed by hope and love, and fenced about with faith, O George, and strengthened by the power of Christ, thou didst cast down the delusion of idols.

*Theotokion:*
Thou hast given birth unto Him Who has enlightened the ends of the earth, O all-immaculate one, the Bodiless One with flesh, and co-unoriginate with the Father before all things: therefore in faith we honor thee as the Mother of God.

*Another Heirmos:*
As the barren church of the nations has brought forth, and the synagogue great in children has grown weak, unto our marvelous God let us cry aloud: Holy art Thou, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
Quietly thou didst distribute thy riches unto the poor, and being anointed with the oil of love, in body and soul martyrlike, O George, thou didst together conquer the torturer.

*Troparion:*
Established by grace, and kindled with the fire of zeal, thou didst appear unto them that asked not for thee, thou wast found of them that sought thee not, for Christ's sake, O George, being angered against the deceit of idols.

*Theotokion:*
As thou bearest in thy arms, O Mary, and nourishest from thy breasts Him Who nourishes all breath: Whom we honor and preach as the Mother of God, pray thou unto Him, O pure one, for all of us.

*Katavasia:*[^115]
*Come, let us drink a new drink:*

*Kontakion of the Feast, and Ikos:*
*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*

*Tone 8, Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*
*Sessional Hymn:*
Having manly-mindedly contended for the forms of piety, and overthrown the delusion of impiety, O martyr, thou didst trample down the arrogance of the enemy. And being ever kindled with divine desire, thou didst quench the godless fury of the torturers. Therefore thou didst worthily receive the crown as reward for tortures, and thou dost grant healings unto those who hasten with faith, O passion-bearer George: pray unto Christ God to grant remission of transgressions unto those who lovingly celebrate thy holy memory.

*Glory:*
*Sessional Hymn:*
Distributing earthly wealth on earth unto all, O wise martyr, thou didst inherit heavenly wealth through thy labors; for having clothed thyself with the honorable Cross as with a breastplate, thereby thou didst put to shame the arrogance of the torturers. Therefore by prayers thou dost grant divine gifts unto those who ask of thee, the benefactions of healings, O victory-bearer George: pray unto Christ God to grant remission of transgressions unto those who lovingly celebrate thy holy memory.

*Both now, of the Feast.*

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Thou didst come from the Virgin, neither an intercessor nor an angel, but Thyself, O Lord, incarnate, and didst save me, all of man; therefore I cry unto Thee: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
We sing of thy struggles, O most blessed George, whereby thou didst scatter the services of idols, and didst abolish all demonic delusion, O right glorious one.

*Troparion:*
Calm the tempest of temptations and perils, and drive away every evil assault of demons from those who sing of thee, O right marvelous warrior of Christ!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst shine forth as a light-bearing star, O George, through courage of soul and steadfastness of faith, driving away dark delusion and saving those who sing of thee.

*Theotokion:*
Thou didst give birth unto the Essential Salvation, O Virgin, which by the riches of goodness and natural benignity saves man, and rebuilds the corrupted image.

*Another Heirmos:*
I heard of Thy truly right glorious dispensation, O Christ: that being immortal God, Thou didst liken Thyself unto mortal men, and didst remain what Thou wast; and therefore I glorify Thy power!

*Troparion:*
Dread before the tribunals of the torturers manifested the unlearned simplicity of the words of thy simple wisdom, O George; and Christ was made known as God unto them that knew Him not.

*Troparion:*
By the word of the faith of Christ, O all-wise George, thou didst resolve for the wise men the intertwining of foul fables; and thou didst mock the gods who are workers of passion, O champion of the Trinity.

*Troparion:*
Having heard the sound of thy words, the torturers were put to shame; for mocking their gods, thou didst show them to be a laughingstock, preaching Christ God crucified in the flesh, Who abolished soul-corrupting delusion.

*Theotokion:*
Thee, Ever-Virgin, and truly only Mother of God, do we glorify, whom the bush mingled with fire without being consumed prefigured unto Moses the beholder of God, O all-immaculate one.

*Katavasia:*[^116]
*Upon the divine watch:*

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
O Enlightenment of them that lie in darkness, Salvation of the despairing, O Christ my Savior: early I seek Thee, O King of peace; enlighten me with Thy radiance, for beside Thee I know no other God!

*Troparion:*
As thou hast martyr's boldness toward the Master, earnestly ask salvation for those who sing of thee, exchanging the diseases of soul by thy supplications, O martyr, passion-bearer, most blessed George.

*Troparion:*
Dancing with the angelic choirs as a martyr in the company of dancers, and being filled with immortal and blessed glory, O crown-bearer, snatch away from every tempest those who flee unto thy protection.

*Troparion:*
Deliver us from every necessity, as an intercessor, driving away sinful and perilous manifold confusion, receiving the prayers of George Thy favorite, O Christ.

*Theotokion:*
Thou didst pay the debt of the first mother Eve, O Mother of God; for thou didst clothe in flesh the Savior of the world Who was born of thee. Therefore we all call thee blessed, O Virgin full of grace, all-immaculate one.

*Another Heirmos:*
Thy enlightenment, O Lord, send down upon us, and loose us from the mist of transgressions, O Good One, and grant Thy peace!

*Troparion:*
Wrestling martyrlike, O George, manly-mindedly opposing the causes of manifold delusion, and stripped of the world, thou didst hold sway over the cruel world-ruler. *(Twice)*

*Troparion:*
Having fenced thyself, O George, with the weapon of the Cross, with the hope of faith, and with sovereign love, thou didst blunt the sharpness and all the weapons of the cruel fighter.

*Theotokion:*
The virginal womb put to shame the understanding of the torturers; for the Child probed with His hand the soul-corrupting wound of the asp, and from and casting down the proud pillar, subdued him under the feet of the faithful.

*Katavasia:*[^117]
*Let us wake at early dawn:*

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*[^118]
Wallowing in the abyss of sin, I call upon the unfathomable abyss of Thy compassion: bring me up from corruption, O God!

*Troparion:*
Beyond nature and word and thought, thy valiant deeds which thou didst bravely accomplish are ceaselessly sung, O most blessed martyr George.

*Troparion:*
Having become a companion of the heavenly host, O blessed one, and beholding the divine manifestation as far as is attainable, save those who honor thee with faith.

*Troparion:*
Deified by those divine archetypes, and receiving the radiance of miracles, thou dispensest gifts unto them that have need, O most blessed George.

*Theotokion:*
Now I flee unto thee, O all-immaculate one: save me by thy supplications, and preserve me; for whatever thou desirest, thou canst do, as the Mother of the Almighty.

*Another Heirmos:*[^119]
Foreshadowing the three-day burial, the Prophet Jonah cried out, praying in the belly of the whale: Deliver me from corruption, O Jesus, King of hosts!

*Troparion:*
With boldness thou didst cry out unto the Creator, O George: Deliver not Thy servant unto the lawless, but grant me strength to destroy their insolence!

*Troparion:*
Heeding not the councils of the tormentors, O George, of thine own will thou didst run manly to the struggles; and with honorable victories thou wast crowned by Christ God.

*Troparion:*
After the trial of thy virtue, turning their threat unto wonder at the miracle, O George, the deceitful tormentors marveled at the Giver of life, Christ, Whom thou didst proclaim.

*Theotokion:*
Anciently indeed the serpent deceived me and slew me through Eve my first mother; but now, O pure one, He Who created me hath through thee recalled me from corruption.

*Katavasia:*[^120]
*Thou didst descend into the nethermost parts:*

*Kontakion, Tone 4. Podoben: "Thou Who wast raised up" (Вознесы́йся):*[^121]
Cultivated by God, thou hast shown thyself a most honorable husbandman of piety, gathering for thyself the sheaves of virtues; for having sown in tears, thou reapest with rejoicing; having suffered unto blood, thou hast received Christ; and by thy prayers, O holy one, thou grantest unto all the forgiveness of transgressions.

*Ikos:*[^122]
Desiring Christ the King, Who laid down His life for the life of the world, the soldier hastened to die for Him; and having great divine zeal in his heart, he brought himself forward Therefore let us all also hymn with faith the divine George as a fervent intercessor, as a glorious servant of Christ, clearly imitating his Master, and coming to everyone who prays; for he ever hastens and prays the Savior to grant unto all the forgiveness of transgressions.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*[^123]
The godless decree of the lawless tormentor raised up a lofty flame; but Christ spread out spiritual dew unto the pious children, being blessed and supremely glorified!

*Troparion:*
Imitating thy Master, O martyr, thou didst willingly hasten to the struggles, O glorious one; and having won the victory, thou didst become a guardian of Christ's Church: ever preserve her by thy protection.

*Troparion:*
As an invincible martyr,[^124] as a sufferer, as an unconquerable champion of the faith, be thou now an unshakable pillar unto them that praise thee, O most wise George, fencing them by thy prayers.

*Troparion:*
Wisely cultivating the divine seed, thou didst multiply it, having watered it with the streams of thy blood, O blessed one, and by the diligence of thy sufferings and diverse wounds, whereby thou didst extinguish the ferocity of the tormentors.

*Theotokion:*
A Virgin thou didst remain even after giving birth, for thou didst bear God Who nourishes all things, Who through ineffable mercy and abundant compassion became man, O pure one: pray Him now that we all may be saved.

*Another Heirmos:*[^125]
The Abrahamic children once in Babylon trampled upon the flame of the furnace, crying out with hymns: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Seeing the prevalent idolatrous delusion, inflamed with the zeal of piety, O George, thou didst deliver thyself unto the struggles, O most blessed one, crying out: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Preaching, thou wast revealed as the faith unto the persecuted, and rebuking the mist of delusion, O George, thou didst say: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst cut down delusion by the root, and didst cultivate a greater faith through piety, O George, rejoicing and saying: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*
Higher than all creatures art thou, as having borne the Creator and Lord. Therefore I cry unto thee, O Mother of God: Blessed art thou, the Lord of hosts is with thee!

*Katavasia:*[^126]
*He Who delivered the children from the furnace:*

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*[^127]
The fiery furnace once in Babylon divided its operation by the divine command, burning up the Chaldeans, but bedewing the faithful, who sang: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:*
The torrent of cruel passions and tribulations, and of turbulent distress, and the falls of diseases, and the wiles of demons, and the assaults of the wicked, subdue by thy prayers, O crown-bearer George.

*Troparion:*
All purely illuminated with the light of the Trinity, O blessed one, as an invincible martyr, as a champion of piety, as a God-crowned victor, save those who honor thee by thy prayers, O inhabitant of heaven.

*Troparion:*
Variously adorned with an intelligible crown, and beautified with a diadem of the kingdom and a scepter, and robed in purple dyed with thine own blood, O blessed one, thou reignest together with the King of hosts.

*Theotokion:*
Him Who was timelessly begotten of the Father, and shone forth before the ages, Who made all things visible and invisible, thou didst bear, O Virgin Mother. Therefore all nations glorify thee as the Mother of God.

*Another Heirmos:*[^128]
Rejoicing after the condemnation of the tormentor, that they might not serve all the vile gods, but the living God, the children dared the fire; and being bedewed by the angel, they sang the hymn: Praise the Lord, ye works of the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Strengthened by the Spirit, thou didst heed not the pains from weapons: for sending up praise not unto deaf idols, but unto Christ God, in Whom thou didst acquire beloved faith, O George, thou didst say: Praise the Lord, ye works of the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Despising transient beauty as non-existent, thou didst endure diverse afflictions, O George, rejoicing in the hope of divine life; and heeding not the terrible crushing of limbs, with steadfastness of soul thou didst say: Praise the Lord, ye works of the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Seeing thy body lacerated by copper vessels, crushed member by member by sharp knives, divinely inspired by the Spirit, thou didst say that the present sufferings are unworthy to be compared with the future glory: Praise the Lord, ye works of the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Theotokion:*
He Who created thee from the rib of Adam was incarnate of thy virginity, even the Lord of all. Hymning Him, we cry aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works, praise the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Katavasia:*[^129]
*This chosen and holy day:*

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*[^130]
The Son of the unoriginate Father, God and the Lord, being incarnate of the Virgin, hath appeared unto us, to enlighten them that are darkened, to gather the dispersed: therefore we magnify the all-hymned Mother of God!

*Troparion:*
Ceaselessly pray the Lord for us who hymn thee, O blessed one, as Christ's martyr, as the vanquisher of the tormentor, as the expeller of evil spirits, as a sleepless guardian, as an unashamed protector.

*Troparion:*
The Master of all richly bestowed upon thee the rewards of thy sufferings. Standing before Him with martyr's boldness, O thrice-blessed one, preserve in this those who with rejoicing call upon thee.

*Troparion:*
The earth concealed thee, and heaven received thee, and the gate of paradise was clearly opened unto thee; wherein rejoicing and exulting with joy, O passion-bearer, preserve by thy prayers those who faithfully hymn thee.

*Theotokion:*
The tabernacle of testimony prefigured thee, O all-immaculate one, wherein were the tablets, the urn, and the golden ark; for as it did, so thou also didst contain in thy womb the unoriginate Word being incarnate, O Mother of God.

*Another Heirmos:*[^131]
Born of earthborn mortals, yet having given birth to the Creator, our boast, O pure Mother of God: thee, as reigning over creation, do we magnify!

*Troparion:*
Priests sing of, and kings praise, and peoples proclaim the glory of thy sufferings, O martyr George: therefore we all call thee blessed.

*Troparion:*
All the heavenly powers sang of thy struggles; for having offered thyself in sacrifice unto God, thou didst well-please Him, O martyr George: therefore we all call thee blessed. *(Twice)*

*Theotokion:*
Having sprung from earthborn mortals, and having given birth to the Creator, rejoice, our boast, O pure Mother of God: thee, as having dominion over creation, do we magnify.

*Katavasia:*[^132]
*Shine, shine, O new Jerusalem:*

*Exapostilarion. Podoben: "While the disciples looked on" (Оу҆чн҃кѡ́мъ зря́щымъ):*[^133]
The spring of God is for us the bright shining and divine Resurrection, which again translates us from earth: which the radiant memory of the all-glorious martyr George enlightens; which let us brightly celebrate, that we may be accounted worthy of divine grace from Christ the Savior.

*Glory... Podoben: "O unwaning Light" (Свѣ́те неизмѣ́нный):*

As a light-bearing sun, by the beams of miracles thou dost enlighten all creation, O George, passion-bearer. Therefore, celebrating thy memory, we sing of Him Who rose from the tomb.

*Both now and ever, of the Feast:*

*At the Praises, Stichera on 4, Tone 2. Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Є҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва):*[^134]
Come, all ye who have celebrated the all-festal, bright, glorious Resurrection, let us again brightly celebrate the festival of the martyr George, and crown him with spring flowers, an invincible faith, that by his entreaties we may receive deliverance from tribulations and remission of transgressions.

*Sticheron:*
Thou didst offer thyself entirely unto Him Who gave thee, O all-blessed one, whole and complete life: as a living and animate whole-burnt offering, and an acceptable and all-pure sacrifice. Therefore thou didst become a most fervent intercessor, snatching from the tempest all who with faith hymn thee, O martyr, and call upon thee, O George.

*Sticheron:*
Having diligently cultivated the seed of the Word sown in thy pure soul, thou didst multiply it by the pains of suffering; and being most wisely laid up in the heavenly granaries, thou didst find incorruptible delight. Whereof now being filled, O blessed one, by thy prayers unto God, save those who hymn thee with faith.

*Sticheron:*
O martyr, trophy-bearer of Christ, save those who are in grievous misfortunes, delivering them by thy supplications from every adversity, and driving away cruel grief; and ask grace and mercy for us: that being saved by thy prayers, we all may rejoicingly honor thy honorable struggles, O George.

*Glory, Tone 2:*[^135]
Let the eyelids of men remember righteous things, said Solomon; for he that looks on righteousness shall be shown mercy by Christ God, as he foretold. Readily giving ear unto these things, O long-suffering George, thou didst follow the Master's teachings; and delivering thyself unto the lawless, thou didst endure, and didst spit upon the sacrifices of delusion as abominable: for having given thy body for the love of the Creator to be cut entirely limb from limb, Belial was all the more put to shame and wounded, seeing thee a perfected crown-bearer. Therefore, dwelling in the courts of thy Lord with the hosts on high, pray, O passion-bearer of Christ, that our souls may be saved.

*Both now and ever, of the Feast:*

*Great Doxology.*

*After the Trisagion, Troparion of the Saint.*

*Glory* both now and ever, of the Feast.*

*Litanies, and the Dismissal. And holy oil is given unto the brethren from the lamp of the Saint; and the First Hour. At the First Hour, Troparion of the Feast. Glory, of the Saint. Both now and ever, the Theotokion of the Hours. After the Trisagion, we say the Kontakia of the Feast and of the Saint alternately.*

*Stichera at the Anointing, Tone 2. Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Є҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва):*[^136]
Despising all things in life, only to acquire the love of the One didst thou bring thy soul, O great George, luminary of the universe; and manly confronting boundless torments, thou didst receive a fair crown of victory. Therefore we pray thee, O blessed one: pray that all who sing of thee may be delivered from tribulations.

*Sticheron:*
Tribulations and cruel sorrows, the tempestuous waves and storms of life now encompass me; I am held fast by the turmoil of evil passions: O my great protector, blessed George, stand forth and ever help me by thy protection as I flee unto thee with faith, that with joy of heart, being saved, I may call thee blessed, O martyr.

*Glory, Tone 4:*[^137]
Come, ye assemblies of the faithful, come, let us institute a feast: for today the most bright memory of the passion-bearer George hath appeared, invisibly enlightening our hearts, shining with virtues. Therefore with one mind singing, let us say: Rejoice, soldier of Christ the great King! Rejoice, all-radiant boast of the faith! Rejoice, richly and supremely enriched! Pray Christ God, the Master of all, for us, O most blessed one, that we may be preserved from the temptations of the evil one, and that our souls may be saved.

*Both now and ever, of the Feast.*

*At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes of the Feast, the appointed Ode, and of the Canon of the Saint, Ode 3. Prokeimenon of the Feast, and of the Saint, Tone 7:*
The righteous man shall be glad in the Lord, and shall hope in Him.[^138]
*Verse:* Hear, O God, my voice, when I pray unto Thee.[^139]
*Epistle of the day, and of the Saint: Acts, Section 24.*[^140]
*Alleluia of the day, and of the Saint, Tone 4:*
The righteous man shall flourish like a palm tree.[^141]
*Verse:* Planted in the house of the Lord, in the courts of our God they shall flourish.[^142]
*Gospel of the day, and of the Saint: John, Section 52.*[^143]
*Communion Hymn of the Feast, and of the Saint:* In everlasting remembrance shall the righteous man be.[^144]
*At the trapeza, consolation for the brethren.*

*Rubric Directive concerning the Holy Great-Martyr George:*[^145]
Beginning from the day of Great Friday, and extending unto Thursday of the 5th Week after Pascha. And if the feast of the Great-Martyr George falls on Great Friday, or on Great Saturday, or on Pascha itself: the Stichera of the Saint, and the Canon, and the rest of the service are sung on Bright Monday, together with the service of the day.

*If the feast of the Great-Martyr George falls on one of the days of Bright Week:*
At Vespers, at "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Resurrection, 3; and of the Saint, 3. Glory, of the Saint, Tone 6: "Worthily named" (*Досто́йнѡ и҆мени*); Both now and ever, the 1st Theotokion of the Tone. Entrance. "O gladsome Light." Prokeimenon of the day. And the 3 Readings of the Saint. If it be on the evening of Pascha Sunday itself, the priest says, "And that we may be accounted worthy," and the rest. Behold: And the Gospel is read according to the order. At the Aposticha, the Sticheron of the Resurrection; then the Paschal Stichera, with their verses. Glory, of the Saint, Tone 4: "O spiritual adamant" (*Раꙁꙋ́мнагѡ а҆дама́нта*); Both now and ever, of Pascha, Tone 5: "The day of Resurrection"; "Christ is risen," thrice. Glory, Troparion of the Saint; Both now and ever, the 1st Theotokion of the Tone. Priest: "Wisdom." And the Dismissal.
At Matins, after "Christ is risen" and after the 4 verses: Glory, Troparion of the Saint; Both now and ever, the 1st Theotokion of the Tone. Then the customary Litany, and the Polyeleos. Then, Sessional hymn of the Saint, 2; Glory, both now and ever, Theotokion; and reading. Then the Gradual, 1st Antiphon of Tone 4. Prokeimenon, "Let every breath"; Gospel of the Saint. "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ," thrice; Glory, "Through the prayers of the Passion-Bearer"; Both now and ever, "Through the prayers of the Theotokos." Then the verse: "The righteous man shall be glad in the Lord, and shall hope in Him." And we sing the Idiomelon Sticheron of the Saint, Tone 6: "Today the whole universe"; Then the priest says: "O God, save Thy people"; and we, "Lord, have mercy," 12 times. After the exclamation, the Paschal Canon with the Heirmos on 8; and of the Saint, on 6. Paschal Katavasia, both choirs together. After Ode 3, Kontakion of Pascha, and Ikos, and reading. After Ode 6, Kontakion of the Saint, and Ikos, and reading. "Jesus having risen," thrice: we sing on both choirs. After Ode 9, Exapostilarion of Pascha. Glory, of the Saint; Both now and ever, of Pascha. At the Praises, Stichera of the Resurrection, 3; and of the Saint, 3. Then the Paschal Stichera, with their verses. Glory, of the Saint; Both now and ever, of Pascha: "The day of Resurrection"; "Christ is risen," thrice. Glory, Troparion of the Saint; Both now and ever, the 1st Theotokion. Litanies, and the Dismissal. The Hours are sung of Pascha according to the custom. At the Liturgy, Antiphons of Pascha. After the Entrance, "Christ is risen is risen," thrice. Then Troparion of the Saint; Glory, his Kontakion; Both now and ever, of Pascha. And instead of the Trisagion, we sing: "As many as have been baptized into Christ." Prokeimenon, Epistle, Alleluia, and Gospel, and Communion hymn, first of the day, then of the Saint.

*Concerning the memory of Saint George: If it falls on Thomas Sunday:*[^146]
On Saturday of Bright Week, at Small Vespers: At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Feast, on 4. Glory, of the Saint; Both now and ever, of the Feast. At the Aposticha, Stichera of the Saint, with his verses, from Great Vespers. Glory, of the Saint; Both now and ever, of the Feast. Troparion of the Saint; Glory, both now and ever, of the Feast. Small Litany, and Dismissal.
At Great Vespers, after the customary Kathisma: At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Feast, on 6; and of the Saint, on 4. Glory, of the Saint; Both now and ever, of the Feast. Entrance, and the Readings of the Saint. At the Litiya, Stichera of the Feast, and of the Saint. Glory, of the Saint; Both now and ever, of the Feast. At the Aposticha, of the Feast. Glory, of the Saint; Both now and ever, of the Feast. At the Blessing of the Loaves, Troparion of the Feast, twice; and of the Saint, once; and reading in the Acts of the Apostles.
At Matins, at "God is the Lord," Troparion of the Feast, twice; Glory, of the Saint; Both now and ever, of the Feast. After the Kathismata, Sessional hymns of the Feast; and reading. Polyeleos, and Sessional hymn; and reading of the Feast. Gradual, 1st Antiphon of Tone 4. Prokeimenon, "Let every breath"; Gospel of the Feast: Matthew, Section 116: "The eleven disciples."[^147] "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ," thrice. Psalm 50. Glory, "Through the prayers of the Apostles"; Both now and ever, "Through the prayers of the Theotokos"; Sticheron: "Jesus having risen." Canon of the Feast with the Heirmos, on 8; and of the Saint, on 6. Paschal Katavasia: "The day of Resurrection," both choirs together. After Ode 3, Kontakion of the Saint, and Ikos, and Sessional hymn, twice; Glory, both now and ever, of the Feast. And reading in the Theologian, whereof the beginning is: "It is an ancient statute to celebrate renewals."[^148] After Ode 6, Kontakion of the Feast, and Ikos; and reading. After Ode 9 we sing: "Holy is the Lord our God," in Tone 1. Exapostilarion of the Feast; Glory, of the Saint; Both now and ever, of the Feast. At the Praises, Stichera of the Feast, 3; and of the Saint, 3. We sing also the Idiomelon of the Saint: "Let the eyelids of men remember righteous things," with his verse: "The righteous man shall be glad in the Lord." Glory, of the Feast; Both now and ever: "Most blessed art thou, O Virgin Theotokos." Great Doxology. After the Trisagion, Troparion of the Saint; Glory, both now and ever, of the Feast. Litanies, and the Dismissal, and the Studite Catechesis. The customary procession. Glory, both now and ever, the Gospel Sticheron. And the First Hour in the narthex First Hour in the narthex. At the Hours, Troparion of the Feast; Glory, of the Saint; Both now and ever, Theotokion of the Hours. After the Our Father, we say the Kontakia alternately.
At the Liturgy: The Beatitudes of the Feast, Ode 3, on 4; and of the Saint, Ode 6, on 4. After the Entrance, Troparion of the Feast, and of the Saint. Glory, Kontakion of the Saint; Both now and ever, of the Feast. Prokeimenon, Epistle, Alleluia, Gospel, and Communion hymn, first of the Feast, then of the Saint.

*If the memory of the holy Great-Martyr George falls on the Sunday of the Myrrh-bearers, or of the Paralytic, or of the Samaritan Woman:*[^149]
On Saturday, at Small Vespers: At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Resurrection, 4; Glory, of the Saint; Both now and ever, Theotokion. At the Aposticha, Sticheron of the Resurrection, and of the Saint from the Aposticha of Great Vespers, with his verses. Glory, of the Saint; Both now and ever, Theotokion. Troparion of the Resurrection; Glory, of the Saint; Both now and ever, Theotokion. Small Litany, and Dismissal.
At Great Vespers, after the customary Kathisma: At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Resurrection of the Tone, 3; and of the Feast, 3; and of the Saint, 4. Glory, of the Feast, Idiomelon of the Triodion; Both now and ever, the 1st Theotokion of the Tone. Entrance. Prokeimenon of the day; and the Readings of the Saint, 3. At the Litiya, Sticheron of the temple, and the Idiomela of the Saint. Glory, of the Saint; Both now and ever, of the Feast. At the Aposticha, Sticheron of the Resurrection, and of Pascha. Glory, of the Saint; Both now and ever, of the Triodion. At the Blessing of the Loaves, Troparion: "O Theotokos and Virgin," twice; and of the Saint, once; and reading in the Acts of the Apostles.
If it is the Sunday of the Myrrh-bearers: At "God is the Lord," Troparion of the Resurrection: Behold: "When Thou didst descend unto death," 1; "The noble Joseph," 1; Glory, of George; Both now and ever, "Unto the Myrrh-bearing Women."
If it is another Sunday: Troparion of the Resurrection, twice; Glory, of the Saint; Behold: Both now and ever, the 1st Theotokion in the same Tone. After the Kathismata, Sessional hymns of the Resurrection, and their Theotokia; and reading in the Commentary Gospel. Polyeleos, and both Sessional hymns of the Saint; Glory, Sessional hymn after the Polyeleos; Both now and ever, Theotokion; and reading of the Saint. Gradual, and Prokeimenon of the Tone. "Let every breath"; Gospel of the Resurrection. "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ," thrice. Psalm 50. Sticheron of the Resurrection. Canon of Pascha, with the Heirmos and with their Theotokia, on 6; and of the Saint, on 4; and of the Triodion, on 4.
