And we sing thus: at each Ode, Troparion of the Resurrection, 1; and of the Feast, one; and the Triadicon, and the Theotokion. Katavasia: "The day of Resurrection." After Ode 3, Kontakion of the Saint, and Ikos, and Sessional hymn his Sessional hymn, twice; Glory, both now and ever, of the Feast; and reading of the Saint. After Ode 6, Kontakion of the Feast, and Ikos. At Ode 9, "More honorable than the Cherubim" is not sung. Exapostilarion of Pascha; Glory, of the Saint; Both now and ever, of the Feast. At the Praises, Stichera of the Resurrection, 4; and of the Saint, 4, with the Doxasticon, and with his verses. Glory, of the Feast; Both now and ever: "Most blessed art thou, O Virgin Theotokos." Great Doxology. After the Trisagion, Troparion of the Resurrection; litanies, and catechism, and dismissal, and the customary procession into the narthex; Glory, both now and ever, the Gospel Sticheron; and the First Hour. At the Hours, Troparion of the Resurrection; Glory, of the Saint; Both now and ever, Theotokion of the Hours. After the Our Father, we say the Kontakia alternately.
At the Liturgy: The Beatitudes of the Tone, on 4; and of the Triodion, Ode 3, on 4; and of the Saint, Ode 6, on 4. After the Entrance, Troparion of the Resurrection, and of the Saint. Glory, Kontakion of the Saint; Both now and ever, of the Feast. Prokeimenon, Epistle, Alleluia, Gospel, and Communion hymn, first of the day, then of the Saint.

*If the feast of the holy Great-Martyr George falls on Wednesday of Mid-Pentecost:*[^150]
On Tuesday evening, we sing "Blessed is the man," the first antiphon. At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Feast, 3; and of the Saint, 3. Glory, of the Saint; Both now and ever, of the Feast. Entrance. Prokeimenon of the day; and the Readings of the Feast, 3; and of the Saint, 3. At the Aposticha, Stichera of the Feast, with their verses. Glory, of the Saint; Both now and ever, of the Feast. After the Trisagion, Troparion of the Saint; Glory, both now and ever, of the Feast; and the Dismissal.
At Matins, at "God is the Lord," Troparion of the Feast, twice; Glory, of the Saint; Both now and ever, of the Feast. After the Kathismata, Sessional hymns; and reading of the Feast. Polyeleos, and all the Sessional hymns of the Saint; and reading. Gradual, 1st Antiphon of Tone 4. Prokeimenon, "Let every breath"; Gospel of the Saint. "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ," once. Psalm 50. Sticheron of the Saint. First Canon of the Feast, Tone 4, with the Heirmos on 6; and of the Saint, on 4; and the other Canon of the Feast, Tone 8, on 4. Katavasia, the Heirmos of the last Canon of the Feast, both choirs together. After Ode 3, Kontakion of the Saint, and Ikos, and his Sessional hymn, twice; Glory, both now and ever, of the Feast. After Ode 6, Kontakion of the Feast, and Ikos. At Ode 9, "More honorable than the Cherubim" is not sung. Exapostilarion of the Feast; Glory, of the Saint; Both now and ever, of the Feast. At the Praises, Stichera of the Feast, 3; and of the Saint, 3. Glory, of the Saint; Both now and ever, of the Feast. Great Doxology. After the Trisagion, Troparion of the Saint; Glory, both now and ever, of the Feast; then the litanies, and the Dismissal.

...and the Dismissal, and the First Hour. At the Hours, Troparion of the Feast; Glory, of the Saint; Both now, Theotokion of the Hours. After the Our Father, Kontakion of the Feast and of the Saint, we say alternately.
At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes of the Feast, Tone 4, Ode 3, on 4; and of the Saint, Ode 6, on 4. After the Entrance, Troparion of the Feast, and of the Saint; Glory, Kontakion of the Saint; Both now, of the Feast. Prokeimenon, Epistle, Alleluia, Gospel, and Communion hymn, first of the Feast, then of the Saint.

*If the feast of the holy Great-Martyr George falls on the Sunday of the Samaritan Woman:*[^151]
The service of Mid-Pentecost is omitted, and the services of the Resurrection, of the Samaritan Woman, and of Saint George are sung.

*In the same month, on the 24th day: The Holy Martyr Sabbas Stratelates.*
*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera, Tone 4. Podoben: "As one valiant" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*
An invincible commander, a brave sufferer, and a universal beacon hast thou been, enlightening the ends of the earth with the dawnings of thy struggles, and driving away the heavy demonic darkness, O great Sabbas, adornment of martyrs, fellow-citizen of the holy bodiless powers, and intercessor for our souls.

Comely wast thou, an exceedingly rich youth, ever admired by all; and thou didst separate thyself unto the labors of sufferings without fear, and didst raise up victories over all enemies by thy great holy struggles. Therefore in praise we honor thee with joy, O thou who art worthy of marvel.

Shut up in prisons, and burned with fire, and tormented with many tortures, and stretched upon the tree, and afflicted with cold, and beaten unceasingly with frequent stripes, and receiving with fervor a blessed end in the waters, O passion-bearer, thou didst remain unshaken.

*Glory, both now and ever, Theotokion:*
With the showers of the All-Holy Spirit, O all-immaculate one, bedew my mind, thou who hast given birth unto the drop, Christ, Who by His compassions hath incalculably washed away the immeasurable iniquities of men: dry up the fountain of my passions, and vouchsafe me ever the stream of the living nourishment, by thy prayers.

*Stavrotheotokion:*
When the ewe-lamb that bare Thee beheld Thee upon the Tree, the Lamb and Shepherd, she wept aloud and maternally cried out unto Thee: O my longed-for Son, how art Thou suspended upon the tree of the Cross, O longsuffering One? How have Thy hands and Thy feet, O Word, been nailed by the lawless, and how hast Thou poured out Thy blood, O Master?

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
Thy martyr, O Lord...

*Or this, Tone 5:*
Having left the pomp[^152] and honors of earthly rank, O sufferer, before the tormentors thou didst confess Christ as being truly God and King, and for His sake thou didst endure many sufferings, O glorious one. Therefore thou hast been adorned with a fair crown of victory by the King of all that reign; and standing before Him brightly adorned with the heavenly hosts, O Sabbas, pray that He may save our souls.

*Canon, Tone 2:*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*[^153]
Come, ye peoples, let us sing a hymn unto Christ God, Who divided the sea and guided the people whom He brought out of the bondage of Egypt, for He hath been glorified.

*Troparion:*
Having delivered our nature from the gates of hades, O Lord, Thou didst shut it up in eternal prisons; and having put death to death, Thou didst pour forth life.

*Troparion:*
Having left the earthly warfare, O glorious one, thou wast gloriously numbered among the heavenly ranks and armies by divine labors, O Sabbas, the glory of Christ.

*Troparion:*
Clothed with power from on high against the enemies, O wise one, thou didst show thyself unshakable and invincible, valiantly conquering their warfare, O most glorious one.

*Troparion:*
Most brightly radiant didst thou shine forth, a beacon in the night of deception, rightly proclaiming Christ the Sun unto all, and driving away the darkness of godlessness.

*Theotokion:*
From the nethermost earth the Son of God Who was born of thee hath snatched us, as One full of compassion, by His Resurrection unto life eternal, us who ever hymn thee.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*[^154]
Establish us in Thee, O Lord, Who by the Tree didst put sin to death, and plant Thy fear in the hearts of us who sing of Thee.

*Troparion:*
Christ cast down the power of the adversary and the dominion of death; and by His Resurrection He brought our nature to life and strengthened it.

*Troparion:*
The warm love of Christ, having divinely kindled thee, O most wise one, separated thee from all earthly and corruptible things, and showed thee an adversary of deceit showed thee an adversary of deceit.

*Troparion:*
Having known the Creator as the only Lord, Who gave Himself as a ransom for men, thou gavest thy blood for Him, O Sabbas, for the sake of the future recompense.

*Theotokion:*
The torment of death hath ceased, O all-immaculate one, and the treasures of hades have been brought to naught by the rising of Him Who was born of thee, and the treasures of life have been filled.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4. Podoben: "Thou Who wast raised up" (Вознесы́йся):*[^155]
Thou didst show thyself an unshakable commander; thou didst conquer the barbarian wiles, and having valiantly suffered, O glorious one, thou didst rebuke the hosts of invisible enemies. Therefore thou hast been crowned with a crown of victory. Fervently pray for us, O most blessed Sabbas, who hymn thee with faith.

*Theotokion:*
After God, I, the lowly one, flee unto thy divine protection, O Theotokos, and falling down, I pray: Have mercy, O all-immaculate one, for my sins have exceeded my head,[^156] and I fear torment, O Sovereign Lady, and I tremble: make supplication, O pure one, unto thy Son, to deliver me from them.

*Stavrotheotokion:*
She who in these last times bare Thee in the flesh Who was begotten of the unoriginate Father, beholding Thee hanging upon the Cross, O Christ, cried out: Woe is me, O most beloved Jesus! How is it that Thou, Who art glorified as God by the angels, art now of Thine own will crucified by lawless men, O Son? I sing of Thee, O longsuffering One!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*[^157]
I have heard, O Lord, the report of Thy dispensation, and I have glorified Thee, O only Lover of mankind.

*Troparion:*
With the knife of thy true words thou didst valiantly cut off the entanglements of falsehood, O most glorious Sabbas.

*Troparion:*
The blooming beauty of thy body truly revealed, O passion-bearer, the divine comeliness of thy soul.

*Troparion:*
Through thy growing spiritual courage, O glorious one, thou didst conquer the ancient adversary by thy sufferings.

*Theotokion:*
Having snatched mortal man as from a wild beast, O Virgin, thy Son hath translated him unto life.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*[^158]
O Giver of light and Creator of the ages, O Lord, guide us in the light of Thy commandments: for we know no other God save Thee.

*Troparion:*
By the burial of Thy flesh Thou didst bury our adversary, and as God having despoiled hades, by Thy Resurrection Thou didst raise up the dead who have glorified Thee, O Christ.

...Adorned, O wise one, with the beauty of creation, thou didst recognize the Master of all. Therefore having suffered for His sake, O Sabbas, thou wast accounted worthy of crowns.

*Troparion:*
Having received the gifts of Christ's mysteries, O glorious one, and being divinely illumined by His ungrudging radiance, thou didst show thyself a beacon in the world, O glorious one.

*Theotokion:*
The Fountain of life, having taken flesh of thee and risen, hath poured forth the nature of the earthborn, O Theotokos, and given it to drink of these waters of immortality.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*[^159]
Wallowing in the abyss of sin, I call upon the unfathomable abyss of Thy compassion: bring me up from corruption, O God!

*Troparion:*
Having by death destroyed death, Thou Who for our sake wast willingly numbered among the dead, O Christ, didst bring the dead to life and deliver them from these bonds.

*Troparion:*
Sprinkled with the divine blood of Christ, thou didst turn away from the defilements of idol offerings, O glorious Sabbas, and wast clothed with the light of grace.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst flee the winter of deception and didst reach the tranquil haven through the knowledge of Christ, wherewith also Thou wast illumined, O most glorious one.

*Theotokion:*
Us who were corrupted by transgressions, O all-immaculate one, thy Son by His Resurrection hath renewed and made heavenly, and hath made us gods by grace.

*Kontakion, Tone 4. Podoben: "Thou Who wast raised up" (Вознесы́йся):*[^160]
Thou didst show thyself an invincible commander; thou didst conquer the barbarian wiles, and having valiantly suffered, O glorious one, thou didst overthrow the multitude of invisible enemies. Therefore thou hast woven a crown of victory: pray to Christ for us, O most blessed Sabbas, who honor thee with faith.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*[^161]
Unto the golden image worshipped in the plain of Dura, the three children of Thine disregarded the godless command; and cast into the midst of the fire, bedewed they sang: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Christ having willingly fallen asleep as dead in the flesh in the tomb, awakened the dead who slept in hades from the ages, and by His life-bearing rising He raised them up, who sing: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Holding the weapon of divine words like a sword, O wise one, thou didst plunge it into the hearts of the ungodly, and didst wound the false iniquities of the adversaries. And thou didst cry aloud: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Seized by the enemies, thou didst not shake thy soul, nor wast thou cast down; for thou hadst Christ looking upon thee and strengthening thee unshaken. Unto Whom also thou didst cry out, O Sabbas, and thankfully sang: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst lay the dead enemy under thy feet, O sufferer, and as spoil from him thou didst take the deceived and gavest them unto the Master of all through knowledge, who cry: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*
Human weak nature was clothed with sovereign might, O most glorious Virgin, at the rising of Him Who was born of thee; and armed valiantly against death, it glorifies thee, the pure Mother of immortality and life.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*[^162]
Unto Him Who descended into the fiery furnace unto the Hebrew children, and changed the flame into dew, praise God as Lord, ye works, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages.

*Troparion:*
Having slain the finder of death by Thy bodily death, O compassionate One, Thou didst show him unto all men to be eternal death: Thee do we supremely exalt, O Life and Incorruption!

*Troparion:*
Having separated thyself from all godless corruption and from the worship of idols by the bath of baptism, O passion-bearer, thou didst honorably approach the Creator, O Sabbas, enduring valiantly in the struggles.

*Troparion:*
With honey-flowing word, O wise one, and with sweet countenance astonishing the tormentor, thou didst set up works of victory over them, and didst cry out: We supremely exalt Thee, O Lord, unto the ages.

*Theotokion:*
The Author of my life, having approached unto death in the flesh, put it to death: give life, O Virgin, unto them that love thee and sing of thee, and supremely exalt Him unto the ages.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*[^163]
God the Word, Who in ineffable wisdom came to renew Adam who had fallen grievously into corruption through eating, and Who for our sake took flesh ineffably of the holy Virgin: with one accord, ye faithful, in songs do we magnify Him.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst rend the belly of hades, O compassionate One, and as the Mighty One Thou didst despoil it; and the impoverished world Thou didst fill, having raised up and brought to life the dead, and by Thy understanding Thou didst exalt all men.

*Troparion:*
Having ascended spiritually unto the height of the divine knowledge of Christ, O passion-bearer, the deception that drags down the deception that drags down thou didst abandon, and upon the wings of suffering thou didst mount up unto Him, fervently magnifying and glorifying Him as supremely good.

*Troparion:*
Accounting the missiles of the tormentors as arrows of infants,[^164] arming them with the power of Christ, O most glorious Sabbas, thou hadst Him as thy fellow-warrior, Who ever helped thee: Whom with faith and love thou didst unceasingly magnify.

*Theotokion:*
Radiant, O Virgin, thy Son and God, having risen from the tomb, filled the universe with divine rays and glory; and with the radiance of incorruption He hath enlightened those who glorify thee with faith as a radiant cloud.

*In the same month, on the 25th day: The Holy Apostle and Evangelist Mark.*
*At Vespers, we sing "Blessed is the man," the first antiphon. At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera on 8: of the Feast, 3; and of the Apostle, 5: Tone 4. Podoben: "Thou of the heavenly ranks" (И҆́же и҆з̾вы́шнихъ чинѡ́въ):*
The writer of divinely eloquent words, ye faithful, and the great teacher of Egypt let us worthily hymn, crying aloud: O wise Mark, by thy teachings and prayers cease not to guide us all unto the immaterial life, O disciple of Christ. (Twice)

Thou wast a fellow-traveler of the chosen vessel,[^165] and with him thou didst traverse all Macedonia; and in Rome having become a disciple of Peter, thou didst show thyself a sweet narrator, and having suffered divinely in Egypt thou didst fall asleep, O Mark, friend of Christ. (Twice)

The souls that were parched and grown old thou didst quicken with the bright sprinklings of the Gospel. Therefore, O divine Mark, brightly with us today doth Alexandria celebrate thy memory and praise it, bowing down before thy relics.

*Glory, Tone 6:*
Grace was poured out upon thy lips,[^166] O Mark the Apostle, and thou wast a pastor of the Church of Christ, teaching the rational sheep to believe in the consubstantial Trinity in one Godhead.

*Both now and ever, of the Feast.*

*Entrance. Prokeimenon of the day. The three Readings are written in this month on the thirtieth day.*[^167]

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 1. Podoben:* "All-praised martyrs" (Всехва́льнїи мꙋ́нцы):*
O divinely wise Mark, thou hast been the reed of a most wise scribe and quick writer,[^168] divinely inspiredly writing the incarnation of Christ, and clearly uttering the words of unending life: wherein pray that they may be written who hymn thee and honor thy glorious memory.

*Verse:* Their sound hath gone forth into all the earth, and their words unto the ends of the world.[^169]

O all-praised Mark, having preached the Gospel of Christ, thou didst traverse the universe like the sun, destroying all the darkness of polytheism with the rays of faith. And now pray that peace and great mercy be granted unto our souls.

*Verse:* The heavens declare the glory of God, and the firmament proclaims the work of His hands.[^170]

O Mark the Apostle, among whom the madness of impiety had formerly multiplied, thou didst preach, driving away the Egyptian darkness with the illumination of thy words, O most rich proclaimer of God. And now pray that peace and great mercy be granted unto our souls.

*Glory, Tone 8:*
Come, all ye, let us praise Mark in psalms and hymns, the preacher of the heavenly mystery and minister of the Gospel; for he hath appeared as a river of the noetic paradise, watering the furrows of the soul with heavenly showers, and showing them forth fruitful unto Christ God, Who by his prayers grants unto the pious purification and enlightenment and great mercy.

*Both now and ever, of the Feast.*

*Troparion, Tone 3:*
O holy apostle and evangelist Mark, pray the merciful God that He grant remission of transgressions unto our souls.

*Another Troparion, in the same Tone:*
Taught by the supreme Peter, thou wast an apostle of Christ, and didst shine forth like the sun upon the nations, having become the adornment of Alexandria, O blessed one; through thee Egypt was delivered from deception, when thou didst enlighten all by thy Gospel teaching like a light-bearing pillar of the Church. Therefore honoring thy memory, brightly we celebrate, O Mark of divine speech: pray the God proclaimed by thee, that He grant remission of transgressions unto our souls.

*Glory, both now and ever, of the Feast.*

*At Matins, at "God is the Lord," Troparion of the Feast, twice; Glory, of the Apostle; Both now and ever, of the Feast. After the 1st Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4. Podoben: "Thy tomb" (Гро́бъ тво́й):*
Like the dawn thou didst divinely shine forth upon the world, and by faith thou didst drive away the deep darkness of deception, O apostle, and didst enlighten the souls and didst enlighten the souls: therefore the whole Church today radiantly sings of thy light-bearing memory, magnifying Christ. (Twice)

*Glory, both now and ever, of the Feast.*

*After the 2nd Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4. Podoben: "Thou Who wast raised up" (Вознесы́йся):*
Instructed in wisdom from the Fountain of life, thou didst make wise those who formerly in ignorance served mental idols, O most wise one. Therefore art thou blessed unto ages of ages, standing before the throne of the Holy Trinity, and fervently praying, O Mark the Apostle, that we may find remission of transgressions. (Twice)

*Glory, both now and ever, of the Feast.*

*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 8. Podoben: "Of wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*
Having cast down the pride of idols, desiring the Savior's passions, O Mark the Apostle, thou didst appear unto the world from heaven, pouring forth miracles unto all, and thou wast a teacher of the nations, O most blessed one. Therefore worthily do we honor thy memory, glorifying in hymns and faithfully magnifying. O most rich one of divine speech, pray Christ God to grant remission of sins unto them that love thy holy memory. (Twice)

*Glory, both now and ever, of the Feast.*

*Gradual, 1st Antiphon of Tone 4. Prokeimenon, Tone 4:* Their sound hath gone forth into all the earth, and their words unto the ends of the world.
*Verse:* The heavens declare the glory of God.
*Let every breath praise the Lord.*
*Gospel according to Luke, Section 50.*[^171]
*After Psalm 50, Sticheron, Tone 6: "Grace was poured out": It is written at "Lord, I have cried," at Glory.*

*Canon of the Feast, with the Heirmos on 6; and of the Saint, on 8, Tone 1.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*[^172]
Thy triumphant right hand hath been glorified in strength in a manner befitting God; for it, O immortal One, as all-powerful hath shattered the adversaries, fashioning for the Israelites a new path through the depth.

*Troparion:*
Praising Mark who beheld God, I invoke the grace of the Spirit: that brightly breathing into me, He may drive away ignorance and grant the divine wealth of wisdom.

*Troparion:*
Illumining him with the light of Thy divine speech through grace, O Savior, Thou didst send him into the world, O Word of God, as the Author of good things, to proclaim Thy coming unto us.

*Troparion:*
Having been a disciple of the most wise Peter, and being enriched by his adoption, O most blessed Mark, thou wast revealed as an initiate of the divine mysteries, and thou becamest an heir of his glory.

*Theotokion:*
He Who in the depth drowned the Egyptians with the cloud of the Spirit He came from thee, O pure one, unto the Egyptians later, and through Mark He changed them from deception.

*Katavasia:* The day of Resurrection:[^173]

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*[^174]
O Thou Who alone knowest the weakness of human nature, and in Thy compassion didst take form therein: gird me from on high with power, that I may cry aloud unto Thee, O holy One: O animate temple of Thine ineffable glory, O Lover of mankind!

*Troparion:*
Proclaiming Thine hypostatic wisdom, O Christ, illumined by Thy rays, which appeared unto the whole world, O Master, giving life and enlightening men, was Thy glorious servant Mark, O Lover of mankind.

*Troparion:*
Having received the ever-flowing stream of wisdom, O Christ, like a rushing river he watered the ends of the earth with the light of the knowledge of God, manifesting Thine ancient truly foretold prophecy, Thy witness Mark, O Lover of mankind.

*Troparion:*
Manifestly following Peter, O wise one, thou didst set forth the Gospel as his disciple, having received the light of theology from him, O apostle, and clearly proclaiming the bodily coming of God.

*Theotokion:*
Carried formerly in Egypt by human hands, Thou didst shake the temples of the demons; and thither again didst Thou send Mark, to make known unto all Thy becoming man, and Thy divine mystery, O Christ.

*Kontakion and Ikos of the Feast.*

*Sessional Hymn of the Apostle, Tone 4. Podoben: "Thy tomb" (Гро́бъ тво́й):*
Having mounted up unto the height of virtues, O divine Mark, thou didst thunder forth unto us the great mysteries of saving dogmas, having become a divine preacher. Therefore with faith we pray thee, O most blessed one: Deliver us from every affliction, transgression, and peril.

*Glory, both now and ever, of the Feast.*

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*[^175]
Perceiving with foresighted eyes the mountain overshadowed by the grace of God, Habakkuk proclaimed that the Holy One of Israel should come forth from thee, for our salvation and renewal.

*Troparion:*
Into all the earth thy sound hath gone forth, and unto the ends of the world, O most wise one, the power of thy clear words, actively and brightly proclaiming our salvation and renewal.

*Troparion:*
The most wise apostle appeared unto the universe like lightning, O Savior, and preached the joy of truth, illumined by the divine radiance of light-shedding, unto our salvation and renewal Prophetically were thy feet made beautiful:[^176] for beyond word thou didst gloriously preach the Gospel unto us, O God-receiver, peace in the flesh appearing unto the ends of the earth, for our salvation and renewal.

*Theotokion:*
Sitting upon the swift cloud, the Word,[^177] having overthrown the Egyptian counsels and god-fighting thoughts, destroyed them by the teachings of the divine Mark, as One full of compassion, for our salvation and renewal.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*[^178]
O Christ Who hast enlightened by the illumination of Thy coming, and hast illumined the ends of the world with Thy Cross: enlighten the hearts with the light of Thy divine knowledge, of those who orthodoxly hymn Thee.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst drop the sweetness of piety, the shower of thy divine words, being left as a ray in the water, having manifestly appeared by the grace of the spiritual Sun, O most blessed God-receiver Mark.

*Troparion:*
From the house of the Lord thou didst go forth as a fountain, and having watered the parched hearts with rich spiritual streams, thou didst teach them to bear fruit instead of barrenness, O apostle.

*Troparion:*
Thou wast a son of the great Peter, and ever illumined by his teachings, thou didst enlighten the souls of those who warmly approach thee, O blessed apostle who beheld God.

*Theotokion:*
As a Man born of the Virgin, Thou wast sent unto the Egyptians, O Christ, Who didst shine forth from the Father before all creation: with Whom Thou didst anoint Mark, O wise One, who taught them the mysteries of the knowledge of God.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*[^179]
The outermost abyss hath surrounded us, there is none to deliver: we have been accounted as sheep for the slaughter: save Thy people, O our God; for Thou art the strength of the weak, and the restoration.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst lay low the grave of the lawless, O Lord, and Thou didst humble the proud insolence, showing Thine apostle to be victorious by Thy power; for Thou art the strength of the weak and their restoration.

*Troparion:*
The crown of hope, O glorious one, wherewith we now are crowned, thou didst preach by thy words, O wise Mark: the Creator of creation, Who for our glory was clothed with the nature of flesh.

*Troparion:*
The supreme Peter, O glorious one, clearly and mystically taught thee to write the honorable Gospel, showing thee to be a minister of divine grace; for thou hast stretched forth the light of the knowledge of God unto us.

*Theotokion:* 
O Lord Who lovest mankind, Thou Who didst formerly enlighten Egypt that was in darkness by Thy twofold divine wisdom, as an Infant going thither Thou didst expose its vanity by the teachings of the divinely-speaking Mark.

*Kontakion, Tone 2. Podoben: "Seeking the highest" (В вышних ищя):*
Having received the grace of the Spirit from on high, O apostle, thou didst break the entanglements of the rhetors, and having caught all the nations, O all-glorious Mark, thou didst bring them unto thy Master, by preaching the divine Gospel.

*Ikos:*
Having become the disciple of the supreme apostle, with him thou didst proclaim Christ the Son of God, establishing upon the rock of truth those who were shaken by deceit; whereon do thou establish me also, and make straight the steps of my soul, so that, being delivered from the snares of the enemy, I may unhindered glorify thee; for thou, O wise Mark, hast enlightened all, by preaching the divine Gospel.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
We the faithful perceive thee, O Theotokos, as a spiritual furnace; for just as the Exalted One saved the three Children, so the All-powerful One renewed the world in thy womb: the praised and supremely glorified God of our fathers.

*Troparion:*
Illumined with divine splendors, by mystical imposition thou sendest forth dawn-like rays, O most blessed Mark; for thou didst preach the Word, Who is before all ages, taking flesh: the praised and supremely glorified God of our fathers.

*Troparion:*
Having armed the divinely speaking disciple with powers of grace, O Seer of all, Thou didst make him a wonderworker; for he drove away diseases and healed wounds, preaching Thee, the supremely praised and glorified God.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst show thyself like unto the elder teacher of the choir of disciples, the divine man Peter; for thou didst set in order the whole fullness of the Church, [preaching] the supremely praised and glorified God of our fathers.

*Theotokion:*
Thou thyself hast set up a pillar in Egypt, the writing of thy Gospel, for Him Who was born without seed of the Mother of God, following the words of Isaiah,[^180] O all-glorious one, proclaiming the supremely praised and glorified God.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
In the furnace the children of Israel, as in a crucible, shone purer than gold with the beauty of piety, saying: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord; sing unto the Lord, and exalt Him supremely unto all ages.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst preach, O glorious one, Christ the Word begotten of the Father before all ages, Who clothed Himself in human nature, and thou didst cry aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord; sing unto the Lord, and exalt Him supremely unto all ages.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst glorify Christ, O glorious one, Who gave Himself as a ransom for our sin, and Who raised the fallen from passion, crying aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord; sing unto the Lord, and exalt Him supremely unto all ages.

*Troparion:*
Obeying the most wise teaching of Peter, O Mark, thou didst most wisely depict in writings the honorable Gospel for the faithful who cry aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord; sing unto the Lord, and exalt Him supremely unto all ages.

*Theotokion:*
The Virgin, carrying Emmanuel as an Infant, appeared unto the Egyptians, unto whom Mark is sent as a bright star, crying aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord; sing unto the Lord, and exalt Him supremely unto all ages.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
The unburnt bush, ablaze with fire, prefigured the image of thy pure childbearing; and now we pray thee to quench the furnace of tribulations raging against us, that we may unceasingly magnify thee, O Theotokos.

*Troparion:*
Having reached the ineffable fountain of the thrice-radiant divine brightness, O divinely revealed one, thou dost enjoy the purest and clearest deification which is beyond the mind, dancing with the angels unceasingly, O most blessed one.

*Troparion:*
Having been a partaker of the teaching and divine preaching of Peter, thou dwellest in the heavenly mansions with him, O divinely speaking and most blessed Mark the apostle, an intercessor for us.

*Troparion:*
Preaching piously the Light united in three splendors, yet marvelously ordered as One, O venerable one, illumined by the ray of grace: therefore we entreat thee, pray ever for us.

*Theotokion:*
Mark the honorable apostle is the hierarch and friend unto the Egyptians, unto whom the Lord of glory came incarnate as an Infant from the pure Virgin Mother of God, Whom we worthily magnify.

*Exapostilarion. Podoben: "Give ear, ye women" (Жены услышите):*
Having been instructed in heavenly wisdom, O apostle, thou didst destroy the entanglements of rhetors and the reckonings of astrologers by thy tongue, O all-glorious Mark; and having become a disciple of Peter, thou didst preach to the world the words of the Gospel. *(Twice)*

*Glory, and now, of the feast.*
*At the Praises, stichera of the feast, 3; and of the apostle, 3. Tone 1. Podoben (Подо́бенъ):*

*Podoben: "O all-praised martyrs" (Всехвальнии муницы):*
O most blessed Mark, thou didst drink from the torrent of delight rushing forth as from Eden; as a most bright and pure river of peace, abundant with the waters of the preaching of thy Gospel, watering the face of the earth and the firmaments of the Church with divinely inspired teachings.

*Sticheron:*
O all-glorious Mark, Moses indeed formerly drowned the Egyptians in the sea; but thou, O most wise one, didst deliver them from the sea of delusion, by the power of Him Who came unto them in the flesh, and cast down their handmade idols with a high arm.

*Sticheron:*
O all-consecrated Mark, living in the light of the Spirit, thou wast wholly spiritual; and now, O most wealthy one, being deified by participation in unfailing visions in the Good, and by most blessed illuminations, thou hast found the supreme and much-desired end of the mysteries.

*Glory, Tone 6. Idiomelon:*
O most wise fisher, holy disciple, laborer of the Savior, and narrator of His passions, who didst traverse the creation in faith, and having gathered the deluded nations and brought them unto God, ascended to the heavens as sweet incense. Therefore, standing before the Judge, pray that we may be delivered from our iniquities, and in the day of judgment be delivered from torment.

*And now, of the feast.*
*Great Doxology, and dismissal.*
*At the Liturgy: The Beatitudes from the canon of the feast, whichever ode is appointed,[^181] and of the saint, Ode 3.*
*Prokeimenon, Tone 8: Into all the earth [their sound hath gone forth]: Verse: The heavens declare the glory of God.*
*Epistle: Section 63 [1 Peter 5:6-14].*
*Alleluia, Tone 1: The heavens shall confess Thy wonders.*
*Gospel of Mark: Section 23 [Mark 6:7-13].*
*Communion Verse: Into all the earth [their sound hath gone forth].*

*In the same month, on the 26th day.*
*The holy hieromartyr Basil, Bishop of Amasea.*
*At "Lord, I have cried," stichera, Tone 4. Podoben: "As noble" (Яко добля):*
*Sticheron:*
Thou didst reign over all passions, O blessed one, being strengthened by the divine law, O glorious one; and having clearly made thy mind sovereign, and having become God's intercessor, according to thy worth- -iness thou didst receive holy unction, and wast a leader unto the rational flock, an adornment of hierarchs and sufferers.

*Sticheron:*
As a sheep from the flock, O most blessed Basil, the lawless king separated thee, and when thou didst preach Christ the King, our God, Who was sacrificed as a Lamb, he unjustly slaughtered thee, and commanded thee to be cast into the sea, thereby mediating for thee the incorruptible Kingdom and everlasting glory.

*Sticheron:*
Thou didst most gloriously make thy paths in the waters, O glorious Basil; for having been cast into them after thy demise, even as thou didst foretell, having mightily swum across, thou gavest thyself as an honorable treasure unto thy city of Amasea, an unashamed intercessor, a river of healings, and a cleansing of diverse diseases.

*Glory, and now, of the feast; or this Theotokion:*
O thou who didst contain in thy womb the uncontainable God, Who in His love for mankind became man, taking our compound nature from thee and manifestly deifying it: despise me not, O all-pure Theotokos, who am now in sorrow; but have mercy speedily, and deliver me from diverse enmities and from the harm of the evil one.

*Stavrotheotokion:*
When the ewe that bare Thee beheld Thee upon the Tree as Lamb and Shepherd, she wept and cried out to Thee maternally: O desired Son, how art Thou suspended upon the Wood of the Cross, O longsuffering One? How are Thy hands and Thy feet nailed by the lawless, O Word? And Thou hast poured forth Thy blood, O Master!

*Canon, Tone 4. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
I shall open my mouth, and it shall be filled with the Spirit, and I shall pour forth a word unto the Queen Mother; and I shall appear radiantly keeping festival, and rejoicing I shall sing of her wonders.

*Troparion:*
Grant me wisdom, O glorious one, as I zealously strive to praise thee, and by thy prayers grant me a word of understanding, to extol thy divine struggles which thou didst mightily display for the sake of the love of Christ.

*Troparion:*
Anointed with the chrism of grace, thou didst appear as a hierarch,[^182] O holy one, worthily [offering] thyself to Christ as a pure sacrifice through martyrdom, and offering bloodless sacrifices.

*Troparion:*
Adorned with the victorious purple robe of thy sufferings, and crowned with a crown of blood, thou didst show thyself a king over passions, O hierarch; having driven away delusion, thou reignest with Christ.

*Theotokion:*
He Who first formed man from the earth, in thy womb- -is incarnate, and becomes perfect man. Therefore we know Him, O all-immaculate Theotokos, to be perfect in two natures.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
We glory not in wisdom, nor in power, nor in riches, but in Thee, the hypostatic Wisdom of the Father, O Christ; for there is none holy save Thee, O Lover of mankind.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst offer thyself as a sacrifice unto Christ, as one without blemish, consumed by suffering in the fire of thy conscience, O wise one. *(Twice)*

*Troparion:*
By the word of wisdom Christ established thee upon the foundation of the rock; He immovably exalted thee, O glorious Basil, as a wise shepherd.

*Theotokion:*
By thy childbearing, O all-pure one, the earthly race was truly loosed from the ancient curse. Therefore we venerate the likeness of thy divine image, O Sovereign Lady.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 3. Podoben: "Of the divine faith" (Божественныя веры):*
Illumined by the Divine Spirit, with pastoral boldness thou didst eloquently confound the pride of the tormentor, and having crossed the abyss of delusion, thou didst reach the divine haven. O venerable father, pray unto Christ God to grant us great mercy.

*Glory, and now, Theotokion:*
Thou wast the divine tabernacle of the Word, O only all-pure Virgin Mother, surpassing the angels in purity: cleanse me who above all men have become dust, defiled by carnal transgressions, with the divine waters of thy prayers, granting great mercy, O pure one.

*Stavrotheotokion:*
The spotless ewe of the Word, the incorrupt Virgin Mother, beholding Him Who sprang forth from her without pain suspended upon the Cross, maternally wept as was meet, crying out: Woe is me, my Child! How art Thou suffering willingly, desiring to deliver man from shameful passions?

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Contemplating the unsearchable divine counsel of Thine incarnation from the Virgin, O Thou Most High, the prophet Habakkuk cried aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
Beside the living water of the divine fountains, O father, and upon the grass of faith, by the staff of thy teachings, O wise one, thou didst guide the flock of Christ, O most blessed Basil.

*Troparion:*
With divinely wise words, O martyr of Christ, with noble boldness thou didst rebuke the lawless and ill-named Licinius for his God-hated folly, and wast crowned with the honor of victory.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst establish an indestructible foundation upon the rock of faith, O wise one; mightily didst thou endure the grievous torments of idolatrous deceit, O hieromartyr, destroying it by the power of Christ.

*Theotokion:*
Heal the crushing of human nature, O Mother of God, who ineffably gavest birth in two natures without division unto the unoriginate Word, O all-immaculate one; we venerate the likeness of His flesh.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
All things were amazed at thy divine glory; for thou, O unwedded Virgin, didst hold within thy womb the God Who is over all, and gavest birth to the timeless Son, bestowing peace on all who praise thee.

*Troparion:*
Strengthened by Christ, O glorious hierarch, thou didst rebuke idolatrous deceit; and thou didst mock the author thereof as a vile bird, having mightily completed the course of faith, O wise martyr. *(Twice)*

*Troparion:*
As another Moses, O most blessed Basil, thou didst manifestly divide the sea of impiety with the rod of faith, and didst lead the new Israel into the Promised Land, unto the light of the knowledge of God.

*Theotokion:*
Possessing with reverent beauty the divine likeness of thy form for veneration, O pure Theotokos, we ever drive away the gathering of heretics, being enriched by thy supplications and help, O all-immaculate one.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Prefiguring the three-day burial, the prophet Jonah cried aloud, praying in the whale: Deliver me from corruption, O Jesus, King of hosts!

*Troparion:*
The cruel and lawless tormentor could not endure the boldness in thy words, O martyr; and the devil fiercely armed Licinius against thee, O glorious one. *(Twice)*

*Troparion:*
Thinking to diminish[^183] thy steadfastness and courage by starvation, that evil tormentor failed of his hope, O martyr, and was mightily crushed under thy feet.

*Theotokion:*
Through thy childbearing, O Theotokos, Eve was delivered from pangs; for thou didst bear the Deliverer for the human race, O only adornment, all-pure Sovereign Lady.

*Kontakion, Tone 2. Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Явился еси днесь):*[^184]
Having despised the emperor's command, thou didst obtain the heavenly Kingdom, O Basil; wherein exulting, remember us who honor thy memory, O hieromartyr.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
O Thou Who didst save Thy children of Abraham in the fire and didst slay the Chaldeans- *Heirmos (continued):*
-whom justice justly snared: supremely praised Lord God of our fathers, blessed art Thou.

*Troparion:*
The proud enemy, boasting greatly that he would despoil the earth like a fledgling, was mightily vanquished by thy resistance, and manifestly fell low beneath thy holy feet, O hieromartyr.

*Troparion:*
With the light of faith thou didst manifestly drive away the darkness of idols, O wise one, singing praises to Christ, O blessed one, even as the Children of old: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers.

*Troparion:*
Neither by the flatteries nor by the threats of the tormentors, O hierarch Basil, didst thou in any wise weaken the strength of thy soul, O glorious one; but unwounded, thou didst endure temptations as though they were flowers.

*Theotokion:*
Thou didst transcend the laws of nature, O pure one, having manifestly given birth unto the Creator of all creation. Therefore we the faithful now most piously confess thee as the true Theotokos, O all-hymned one.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
The childbearing of the Theotokos saved the pious Children in the furnace; prefigured then, but now fulfilled in truth, it moves the whole universe to sing unto Thee: Sing unto the Lord, all ye works, and exalt Him supremely unto all ages.

*Troparion:*
Having cast down deceit beneath thy feet by the arrows of wisdom, O most blessed hierarch, thou wast crowned with victorious crowns as a most glorious champion of the faith, crying aloud in the Divine Spirit: Sing unto the Lord, all ye works, and exalt Him supremely unto all ages.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst sever the neck of delusion with the sword of faith, O hierarch; for when thy head was cut off, O glorious one, thou didst receive the crown of victory from Christ, crying aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and exalt Him supremely unto all ages.

*Troparion:*
Thy head was joined unto thy body after its beheading,[^185] O blessed one, which was most marvelous, and the boundless deep bore it ashore under the guidance of the Divine Spirit, O holy one; and now rejoicing with the angels, thou glorifiest the Son and the Divine Spirit together with the Father.

*Theotokion:*
By thy childbearing, O Virgin, thou didst cut off the serpent's venom which poisoned Eve; for thou didst bear the hypostatic Word in the flesh, Who was dreadfully united within thy womb, O all-pure one—the Word hypostatic to the Father—Whose bodily likeness we venerate.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Eve indeed, through the disease of disobedience, brought in the curse; but thou, O Virgin Theotokos, by the offshoot of thy womb-bearing didst blossom forth blessing unto the world. Therefore- *Heirmos (continued):*
-do we all magnify thee.

*Troparion:*
Hastening manifestly unto the haven, O all-glorious one, thou didst pass through the grievous tempests of the idolatrous storm by the divine guidance of the Spirit; and exulting with the celestial powers, remember them that honor thee, O blessed one. *(Twice)*

*Troparion:*
Divinely dancing with the angels, O glorious Basil, singing the hymns of victory before the throne of God and Master of all, thou dost joyfully and mercifully watch over us who sing unto thee, as our shepherd and hierarch.

*Theotokion:*
Having immutably given birth unto the unoriginate Son, God the Word, O Virgin Mother, thou hast wholly renewed our nature which had grown old through the transgression of sin. Therefore we unceasingly magnify thee, O Theotokos.

*In the same month, on the 27th day.*
*The holy hieromartyr Symeon, Kinsman of the Lord.*[^186]
*At "Lord, I have cried," stichera of the feast, 3; and of the apostle, 3. Tone 4. Podoben: "As noble" (Яко добля):*
*Sticheron:*
Thou didst show thyself a most holy priest, a lawful sufferer, a true kinsman of the Creator, a marvelous wonderworker, a most bright beacon, a guide of the erring, O Symeon: an unshakable pillar of the honorable Church, an heir of the Kingdom on high, and equal in honor to the angels.

*Sticheron:*
After bodily pangs, O most glorious hierarch, after every most painful torment, after very many struggles, thou wast nailed unto the cross, being likened unto Christ Who was crucified willingly. Therefore, as we joyfully celebrate thy holy memory, O Symeon, we receive remission of sins through thy prayers.

*Sticheron:*
Thou didst holily enter into the heavenly church, holily dyed with thy blood, O blessed one; and thou standest before the Trinity, richly shining with splendors pouring forth from thence, O most opulent one. Therefore today, O Symeon, as we celebrate thy luminous memory, we enlighten the senses of the soul.

*Glory, and now, of the feast; or this Theotokion:*
Bedew my mind with the rays of the All-Holy Spirit, O all-pure one, who didst ineffably give birth to Christ the drop of dew that hath washed away the boundless iniquities- -of men together by His compassions: dry up the fountain of my passions, and vouchsafe me ever to taste of the torrent of living food, by thy prayers.

*Stavrotheotokion:*
When Thine ewe-lamb and Mother beheld Thee nailed upon the Cross, O Lord, she was amazed and cried aloud: What sight is this, O beloved Son? Hath the unfaithful and lawless assembly rendered this unto Thee, which had enjoyed Thy many miracles? But glory to Thine ineffable condescension, O Master!

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
As the kinsman of Christ, O hierarch Symeon, and as a steadfast martyr, we holily extol thee, who didst destroy delusion and preserve the faith. Therefore, as we celebrate thy most holy memory today, we receive remission of sins through thy prayers.

*Canon of the feast, with the heirmos, for 6; and of the saint, for 6. The composition of Joseph. Tone 4. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
I shall sing unto Thee, O Lord my God, for Thou hast led Thy people out of Egyptian bondage, and Thou didst cover the chariots of Pharaoh and his power.

*Troparion:*
O holy Symeon, standing before the Master as a crown-bearer, ask remission of sins for them that lovingly celebrate thy most consecrated memory.

*Troparion:*
Winged by the sail of the Cross, as it were truly by the cool breeze of the Spirit, thou didst pass through the storm of delusion, O all-glorious one.

*Troparion:*
Christ Who sprang forth from the tribe of Judah anointed thee with divine chrism as a priest for the people, O holy Symeon, converser with the angels.

*Theotokion:*
As thou art higher than the creatures, O Virgin, thou didst conceive in thy womb the Creator and God of all, and gavest birth unto Him for the redemption of men.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
The bow of the mighty hath grown weak, and the weak have girded themselves with strength; therefore my heart is established in the Lord.

*Troparion:*
Deified by yearning toward God, O holy Symeon, Thou wast wholly luminous, flashing with the radiance of martyrdom.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst shine forth as dawn unto them that were in darkness, showing forth the Sun of righteousness, Who shone forth unto men from the cloud of the Virgin Maiden.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst hate worldly pleasures, and didst become a comely beauty of hierarchs, and by suffering wast vouchsafed heavenly glory.

*Theotokion:*
Every law of praises is overcome in thee, O all-immaculate Mother of God; for God, as He alone knows, became man from thee for our sake.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4. Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Явился еси днесь):*
Having acquired the divinely speaking Symeon as a great star today, the Church is enlightened, crying aloud: Rejoice, O honorable leader of martyrs! *(Twice)*

*Glory, and now, of the feast; or this Theotokion:*
Stretching forth thy most pure hands, O Virgin Mother, shelter them that hope in thee, and that cry unto thy Son: Grant Thy mercies unto all, O Christ!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
Beholding Thy Son lifted up upon the Tree, O all-pure one, torn in thy maternal womb, thou didst plaintively cry aloud: Woe is me! How hast Thou set, O my timeless Light?

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
For the sake of the love of Thine image, O Merciful One, Thou didst stand upon Thy Cross, and the nations melted away; for Thou, O Lover of mankind, art my strength and praise.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst minister, offering bloodless sacrifices unto Him Who was sacrificed for thee, and wast sacrificed as a lamb, being brought unto Him, O divinely wise and most opulent one.

*Troparion:*
The mountains of impiety and the hills of demons were crushed before thy face, O hierarch; they all melted away, with Christ strengthening thee.

*Troparion:*
Having a most exalted life, O most blessed one, thou willingly didst ascend upon the Tree, being likened unto the divine Passion[^187] of Him Who exalted the world.

*Theotokion:*
Foreseeing thy childbearing, O all-pure one, Habakkuk proclaimed thee as the overshadowed mountain, out of which our only God hath come forth.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Send down Thy light upon us, O Lord, and loose us from the darkness of transgressions, O Good One, granting Thy peace.

*Troparion:*
Willingly, O blessed one, thou didst surrender thyself, wholly consumed by the fire of martyrdom, O blessed Symeon, mystically preserved in the good pleasure [of God].

*Troparion:*
As an unblemished sheep thou didst give thyself over to suffer zealously, being likened unto Christ Who suffered for us in the flesh and drove away passions.

*Troparion:*
Having become a temple of the Divine Spirit, O most wise one, thou didst cast down the temples of the idols by the power of thy prayers, and didst guide the erring unto the light.

*Theotokion:*
Pray to Christ for us, O all-pure Maiden, Who from thy pure blood clothed Himself in material flesh and renewed mankind.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Prefiguring the three-day burial, the Prophet Jonah cried aloud, praying within the whale: Deliver me from corruption, O Jesus, King of hosts!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst learn most gloriously from the divine radiance those things which guide thee unto the divine life, O most opulent one; and thou didst show thyself, O hierarch, to be full of ineffable understanding.

*Troparion:*
Having thee full of spiritual, life-bearing waters, as another river flowing forth from Eden, O Symeon, the divine Church rejoices.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst sit upon an exalted throne, having become the successor of James, and a partaker of his manner of life, having illumined thy suffering[^188] through martyrdom, O blessed one.

*Theotokion:*
Heal the incurable passions of my soul, O Bride of God, who didst bear Christ the Physician unto men, Who by His passions healed our passions.

*Kontakion, Tone 4. Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Явился еси днесь):*
Having today the divinely speaking Symeon as a most great star, the Church is guided by light, crying aloud: Rejoice, O honorable crown of martyrs!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
Of old in Babylon the Abrahamite children trod down the flame of the furnace, crying aloud with hymns: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Having zealously surrendered thy body unto torments for the sake of Christ, thou wast neither shaken nor weakened, crying aloud, O marvelous one: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Being ignited in mind with divine love, O most blessed one, thou didst cry aloud, stronger than material fire: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Even as the Master, O father, thou wast lifted up upon the cross, being adorned by the likeness of His passions, and singing with gladness: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*
As Mother of God, O Maiden, terror[^189] of demons, and adornment of the angels, the salvation of mankind: deliver my humble soul from the deceit of the enemy.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Him Who was nailed in the flesh upon the Cross, and Who showed forth unto us a weapon for salvation: ye children, exalt Christ our God supremely unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Having received this dawn of the Comforter into thy pure heart, O Symeon, thou didst consume the gloomy darkness of the demons, having enlightened the faithful;

having dried up the sea of fierce evil belief with the streams of thy divine teachings, thou didst water every soul that bears the wheat-ear of faith.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst appear as a priest by thine own blood, O martyr, having entered into the divine Church, where thou ever beholdest Him Who for thy sake was sacrificed as an unblemished Lamb.

*Theotokion:*
That we may hymn thee with voices of thanksgiving, who hast magnified our race: shelter and preserve us, O Bride of God, from every harm.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Thy childbearing was shown to be incorrupt: God came forth from thy loins, and appeared upon earth as a bearer of flesh, and dwelt among men. Therefore we all magnify thee, O Theotokos.

*Troparion:*
Even as a pillar of fire thou didst appear, O most blessed one, leading the new people out of Egyptian deceit, and piously bringing them unto the land of divine promise, O martyr and sacred initiate of the mysteries.

*Troparion:*
Simon-Symeon, a twofold name thou didst possess,[^190] having before the godless theologized the Word Who is twofold by nature, and having drawn near unto the cross. Therefore we all, having come together, call thee blessed.

*Troparion:*
Today the Church celebrates the festive remembrance of thee, suppliantly honoring thee together with divine James, O Symeon, whose throne thou didst receive, O holy one.

*Theotokion:*
Illumine me with divine light, O Virgin, dwelling-place of Light, driving away the darkness of my passions and the deep night of blindness, O all-pure Mother of God.

*At the Liturgy: Prokeimenon, Tone 7: Into all the earth is their sound gone forth, and their words unto the ends of the world. Verse: The heavens declare the glory of God.*
*To the Corinthians: Section 131 [1 Cor. 4:9-16].*
*Alleluia, Tone 4: The heavens shall confess Thy wonders, O Lord.*
*Gospel according to Matthew: Section 56 [Matt. 13:54-58].*
*Communion Verse: Into all the earth [is their sound gone forth].*

*In the same month, on the 28th day. The Holy Apostles Jason and Sosipater; and the Holy Martyrs Dadas, Maximus, and Quintilian.*

*At "Lord, I have cried," stichera of the apostles, Tone 2. Podoben: "Thy martyrs, O Lord" (Мученицы твои Господи):*
The blessed Jason, flowing as a saving stream unto the fullness of the earth, watered the hearts held by evil burning and bedewed them with faith; therefore after his demise he is called blessed. By his prayers, O Lord, grant great mercy unto all.

*Sticheron:*
The divinely inspired Jason became a ladder reaching unto the heavens, leading up the faithful by his teaching unto heavenly life; and he lives after his demise, and the glorious one stands rejoicing before the Trinity. By his prayers, O Lord, grant great mercy unto all.

*Sticheron:*
The most opulent Jason ever pours forth healings unto them that approach his shrine, and stays the diseases of souls, and drives away seductive spirits, and calms storms. By his prayers, O Lord, grant great mercy unto all.

*Other stichera, of the holy martyrs, Tone 1. Podoben: "O all-praised martyrs" (Всехвальнии мученицы):*
Having confessed the uncreated Trinity, ye three holy martyrs before the judgment seat were put to death, and now have been accounted worthy of ageless life, and have inherited the torrent of delight. Therefore pray that peace and great mercy may be granted unto our souls.

*Sticheron:*
O invincible martyrs, by the streams of your shed blood ye dried up the torrents of delusion, and pour forth gouts[^191] of healings unto all that run [unto you] with faith, and take away the power of diseases. Therefore pray that peace and great mercy may be granted unto our souls.

*Sticheron:*
Let us all honor in hymns today divine Dadas, Maximus, and Quintilian, the steadfast armor-bearers, the most wise martyrs, inhabitants of the paradise on high, who now pray that peace and great mercy may be granted unto our souls.

*Glory, and now, of the feast; or this Theotokion:*
O all-hymned Sovereign Lady, hope and confirmation of the faithful, refuge and help, we pray thee:

preserve thy servants from every calamity, who with faith bow down before thy childbearing; and entreat Him to grant peace and great mercy unto our souls.

*Stavrotheotokion:*
Beholding her own Lamb upon the Cross, having neither form nor comeliness, the ewe-lamb and undefiled Sovereign Lady, weeping, cried aloud: Woe is me! Whither hath Thy comeliness set, O Sweetest One? Whither Thy majesty? Whither the radiant grace of Thine image, O my most beloved Son?

*Troparion, Tone 3:*
O holy apostles, entreat the merciful God, that He may grant remission of transgressions unto our souls.

*Canon of the Apostles, the composition of Joseph. Tone 2. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
The cleft deep was cut through the indivisible, and the sun beheld the earth which it had not seen; the water drowned the fierce enemy, and Israel traversed the impassable; and the hymn was sung: Let us sing unto the Lord, for He is gloriously glorified!

*Troparion:*
Dwelling with the angelic choirs, and ever filled with the unwaning light, and deified by true participation, O most opulent one: enlighten them that with love celebrate thy holy memory, singing unto the Lord: For He is gloriously glorified!

*Troparion:*
Fervent divine desire utterly kindled thee, O Jason; therefore thou didst follow Him Who came to cast fire upon the earth, O glorious one; enlightened by Whom, thou didst brightly cry aloud: Let us sing unto the Lord, for He is gloriously glorified!

*Troparion:*
Likened in manner unto the immaterial minds, O blessed one, thou didst minister, O most blessed one, unto Him Who for our sake mercifully appeared upon earth in the substance of the body; and having become His sacred minister, thou didst cry: Let us sing unto the Lord, for He is gloriously glorified!

*Theotokion:*
The Incomprehensible One was seen taking flesh from the unwedded Virgin for the sake of His manifold tender mercy, manifestly leading up unto dispassion through the holiness of His flesh those who had wretchedly succumbed to the passions of the body; unto Whom we customarily sing: For He is gloriously glorified!

*Canon of the Martyrs, the composition of Joseph. Tone 1. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Let us all sing a song of victory unto God, Who hath wrought marvelous wonders with a high arm, and saved Israel, for He is glorified!

*Troparion:*
Standing ever with boldness before God the Almighty, O fair-victorious martyrs, pray that cleansing of transgressions may be sent down unto us.

*Troparion:*
Ye were crowned with divine beauty, O martyrs, having finished the contest, and having vanquished the wily enemy;

therefore with hymns we joyfully and unceasingly glorify you.

*Troparion:*
By the power of the Most Holy Spirit ye destroyed the might of the deceitful serpent, O divinely blessed ones, and now enjoy immortal gifts.

*Theotokion:*
The Full One emptied Himself out of tender mercy and became flesh, O pure one, raising us up unto our primal beauty, who with faith praise thee upon the earth.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
Thou art the confirmation of them that flee unto Thee, O Lord; Thou art the light of the darkened, and my spirit sings of Thee.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst show thyself to be a lute of the Spirit, sounding forth unto us divinely fair teachings, cutting away seductive songs.

*Troparion:*
Thy tongue poured forth fountains of life as a river, and dried up the bitter waters of delusion, O glorious one.

*Troparion:*
Thy sacred relics, O wise one, pour forth diverse healings by the operation and grace of the Holy Spirit.

*Theotokion:*
Put to death, O all-pure one, the sin that dwells in me by thy life-giving intercession, O thou that didst bear Life!

*Canon of the Martyrs. Ode 3. Heirmos:*
Let my heart be established in Thy will, O Christ God, Who didst establish the second heaven above the waters, and didst found the earth upon the waters, O All-powerful One!

*Troparion:*
By grievous imprisonment, O holy ones, having become prisoners for the sake of Christ, ye loosed the evil bond of delusion by grace, and being loosed from the flesh, ye passed over unto life.

*Troparion:*
Desiring to behold the divine beauty, O all-praised sufferers, ye endured wounds and revilings,[^192] and a violent death, being strengthened by steadfastness of mind.

*Troparion:*
Lawfully contending with steadfastness of character, O all-praised martyrs, ye together were clothed with the crown of victory, and now stand rejoicing before the honorable Trinity.

*Theotokion:*
He Who is uncircumscribed by nature, having become incarnate of thee, is circumscribed, O pure one full of divine grace; Whom unceasingly entreat to have compassion upon them that sing of thee, and to save them.

*Sessional Hymn of the Apostle, Tone 8. Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Премудрости):*
With radiant dawns of honorable words thou didst godly enlighten all, O Apostle Jason, and didst drive away darkness; and having suffered, O blessed one, thou pourest forth healings, and stayest the fierce diseases of bodies. Therefore having gathered together, we all celebrate thy holy festival, glorifying the Savior, and faithfully cry unto Thee:

*Sessional Hymn (continued):*
Entreat Christ God to grant remission of sins unto them that with love honor thy holy memory.

*Glory, Tone 4. Podoben: "Thy tomb, O Savior" (Гроб твой Спасе):*
Approaching pains with steadfastness of soul, undefeated by the manifold wiles of the deceiver, ye appeared, O martyrs, equal in honor unto the angels. Therefore the whole universe honors your struggles and your honorable sufferings, whereby ye were glorified.

*And now, of the feast; or this Theotokion:*
O Hope of Christians, most holy Virgin, Whom thou didst bear as God beyond mind and word, Him unceasingly entreat together with the heavenly hosts, to grant remission of all our sins and amendment of life unto them that ever glorify thee with faith and love.

*Stavrotheotokion:*
Beholding her Lamb and Shepherd hanged dead upon the Tree, the spotless ewe-lamb, weeping, cried aloud, maternally lamenting: How shall I endure Thy descent which is beyond word, O my Son, and Thy voluntary passions, O most good God?

*Canon of the Apostles. Ode 4. Heirmos:*
I heard, O Lord, the mystery of Thy dispensation; I considered Thy works, and I glorified Thy Divinity.

*Troparion:*
Having become a disciple of most wise Paul, the sacred herald, and an imitator of Christ, O glorious one, thou didst obey His divine commands.

*Troparion:*
Thou wast adorned with the garment of the bridal chamber of the divine Church, having betrothed [souls] unto it by the Spirit, O God-bearer, through the sweet word of thy wisdom.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst become an expert physician of the ailing through thy divine gifts that drive away evil, healing grievous passions.

*Theotokion:*
O Sovereign Lady who knew not wedlock, who didst bear the Savior Word: save me, and deliver me from perils and from eternal condemnation.

*Canon of the Martyrs. Ode 4. Heirmos:*
I heard Thy report, O Lord, and was afraid; I considered Thy works, said the prophet, and I glorified Thy power.

*Troparion:*
Upholding the divine law, the sufferers cast down the iniquity of the godless, having piously suffered and well-pleased the Lord.

*Troparion:*
Let us call blessed today the valiant Dadas and Maximus, who suffered lawfully, and let us crown all-honorable Quintilian with praises.

*Troparion:*
Having remained unswerving in the time of suffering, O holy ones, ye cast down all deceit, and received the crown of victory from God our Savior.

*Theotokion:*

Raise me up who am held fast by despondency, O Mother of God, to do the divine will, that I may call thee blessed, O blameless protectress of all.

*Canon of the Apostles. Ode 5. Heirmos:*
From the night of ignorance, wandering ever upon the soul-destroying[^193] path: guide me by the light of Thy knowledge, O Lord, leading me forward unto the holy [path] of Thy commandments.

*Troparion:*
Those men who were held by deceit and foolishly enslaved to idols, O most blessed one, thou didst set free by thy divine teachings.

*Troparion:*
Set on high as a light of divine knowledge, O Jason, thou didst draw unto the divine harbor them that were sorely drowning.

*Troparion:*
Them that were of old slain by the bite of the serpent, thou didst revive, O blessed one, having astonished [all] by thy life-bearing word.

*Theotokion:*
Thou hast shown thyself holier than the Cherubim, having in the flesh brought forth the Master of all upon the earth, O pure and all-immaculate Sovereign Lady.

*Canon of the Martyrs. Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Make to shine upon us the radiant and ever-existing light, who rise early at dawn unto the judgments of Thy commandments, O Master, Lover of mankind, Christ our God!

*Troparion:*
Having confessed the divine and saving Name before the tormentors, O holy ones, ye endured the trial of many torments and an unjust death.

*Troparion:*
Strengthened by the grace of the Divine Spirit, ye feared not bodily torments, nor did ye offer honor unto carved idols, O fair-victorious martyrs.

*Troparion:*
Having received an unfading crown, O holy martyrs, ye three now stand before the Trinity, accounted worthy of the unsetting radiance, and now are called blessed.

*Theotokion:*
Heal the fierce and incurable passions of my heart, O all-immaculate Sovereign Lady, and drive away the heavy darkness of my mind, O all-pure one.

*Canon of the Apostles. Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Even as Thou didst deliver the prophet from the nethermost deep, O Christ God, so deliver me also from my sins, as Thou art the Lover of mankind, and direct my life, I pray Thee.

*Troparion:*
Unto the marvelous beauty of Him Who drew nigh unto us out of tender mercy, O Apostle Jason: Him didst thou follow, and didst forsake worldly beauty.

*Troparion:*
Having been taught divine things, thou didst by grace teach the trinitarian radiance, O glorious one, unto them that formerly bore blindness, the folly of incomprehension;

driving away diverse diseases and chasing away malicious spirits, thou didst turn the people unto the knowledge of the Lord, having delivered them from the deceit of the wicked one.

*Theotokion:*
As rain upon the fleece, the saving Word descended into thy womb and became man of thee; save me, O Maiden, by thine intercession.

*Canon of the Martyrs. Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Even as the Prophet Jonah, raise up my life from corruption, O Christ God; I cry unto Thee, O Lover of mankind: for with Thee is life, and incorruption, and power!

*Troparion:*
Seen cloudlessly as stars, ye enlighten the earthly world, O all-praised martyrs, driving away the gloom of godlessness by grace.

*Troparion:*
Utterly casting aside the decrees of the godless, and manifestly established by the law of the Lord, ye lawfully suffered for Christ, O holy martyrs.

*Troparion:*
Being well displayed by most exalted understandings and deeds, ye subjected under your feet him that fell from the heights, being adorned with the beauties of martyrdom, O all-praised ones.

*Theotokion:*
Beyond the laws of nature, thou didst bear the Creator in the flesh according to nature, O Maiden, beyond understanding, O all-immaculate one; Whom the fair-victorious martyrs confessing, suffered.

*Kontakion of the Apostles, Tone 4. Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Явился еси днесь):*
Ye appeared as most radiant stars, enlightening the whole universe with the light of preaching: O divine apostles, Jason and Sosipater, save them that honor you with faith.

*Canon of the Apostles. Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The pious children in Babylon bowed not down unto the golden image, but bedewed in the midst of the fiery furnace, they sang a hymn, saying: Blessed art Thou, O supremely exalted God of our fathers and our God!

*Troparion:*
Approaching incessant afflictions, O Jason, thou didst lighten the sicknesses of the faithful, and didst guide them unto painless reception, who piously sing: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst stay the corruption of malice, O blessed one, and didst plant saving knowledge in human thoughts; enlightened by which, thou didst cry unto the Master of all: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*
Holding Christ in thine arms as a throne of fiery appearance, O all-immaculate one, entreat Him as thy Son and Lord to give peace unto the world that cries: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Canon of the Martyrs. Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The fire touched not nor troubled Thy children in the furnace, O Savior; then the three, as with one mouth, sang and blessed, saying: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Kindling your soul with the fire of the divine fear, O wise ones, ye burned up every substance of bitter polytheism, and were taken up unto the unwaning radiance, granting remission of our transgressions.

*Troparion:*
Having quenched the conflagration of polytheism with streams of blood, O blessed ones, ye truly pour forth unto us a shower of wonders, who ever flee with faith unto the shrine of your relics.

*Troparion:*
Having shown yourselves to be a dwelling-place of the Spirit, O wise and fair-victorious martyrs, the temple wherein ye now lie is made a fountain of wonders; fleeing wherein, we are delivered from every defilement of passions.

*Theotokion:*
Thou didst bear unto us in the flesh the Word consubstantial with the Father and the Spirit, O all-immaculate Mother of God; Whom entreat to save them that sing of thee from all passions and distress.

*Canon of the Apostles. Ode 8. Heirmos:*
The divinely speaking children in the furnace, trampling down the flame together with the fire, sang: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst uproot the thorns of evil vaunting with the seed of the word, O Jason; and thou didst cultivate a hundredfold harvest of the multitude of the saved.

*Troparion:*
As an illumination of them that sit in darkness, Tarsus sets thee forth as a beacon, O blessed one, whose throne thou didst govern sacredly and righteously, O Apostle Jason.

*Troparion:*
Thou wast a lawful teacher unto them that were subject to worldly[^194] deceit, to honor the one uncreated God; and them that offered honors unto creations, thou didst save.

*Theotokion:*
Having borne the incarnate God the Word, O Maiden, heal the passions of our flesh and of our soul, that with faith and love we all may glorify thee.

*Canon of the Martyrs. Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Him before Whom angels and all hosts tremble as Creator and Lord: ye priests, hymn; ye children, glorify; ye people, bless, and exalt Him supremely unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Loving the Lord with your whole soul, ye were slaughtered as lambs for the rational Shepherd, and were offered unto the divine banquet, and dwell in the tents of the firstborn.

*Troparion:*
With faith let us honor Quintilian, Dadas, and Maximus, martyrs of Christ, divine stars;

enlightening the hearts of all with the rays of wonders.

*Troparion:*
Despising the reproaches[^195] of the godless and wicked, and rejoicing in your suffering, O martyrs of Christ: ye were numbered with the divine passion-bearers from the beginning of time, with whom ye are ever honored.

*Theotokion:*
As a throne of fiery appearance thou didst carry Christ, Who mingled Himself with us by the grossness of the flesh; Him entreat, O pure Theotokos, to have compassion upon them that honor thee with love, and to save them.

*Canon of the Apostles. Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Thou didst surpass the ordinances of nature, having conceived the Creator and Lord, and becamest a door of salvation unto the world. Therefore we unceasingly magnify thee, O Theotokos!

*Troparion:*
Let us all assemble sacred choirs, sacredly calling blessed great Jason, the apostle of Christ, who ever rejoices with the bodiless choirs.

*Troparion:*
Creation leaps, keeping choir now in thine honorable triumph, O glorious one; apostles, prophets, and all martyrs rejoice, O Jason, with whom we magnify thee with faith.

*Troparion:*
The shrine wherein lies thy patient and much-suffering body, O glorious Jason, ever sends forth rays and dispels the darkness of diseases, O most blessed one.

*Theotokion:*
Thou wast revealed, O all-immaculate Mother, as Mother of Emmanuel, Who was incarnate of thy womb and deified human nature. Therefore we call thee blessed, O most holy Mother of God.

*Canon of the Martyrs. Ode 9. Heirmos:*
The light-bearing cloud, wherein the Master of all descended from heaven as rain upon the fleece, and for our sake was incarnate, becoming man, the Unoriginate One: we all magnify as the pure Mother of our God!

*Troparion:*
Following the passion of the Passionless One, O passion-bearers, ye together submitted unto the trial of intricate torments, and by death inherited life, abiding unto the age of the age. Therefore ye are ever called blessed.

*Troparion:*
Having known the beautiful pathway of martyrdom, and having trodden it well upon paths leading unto noetic resting-places, O glorious martyrs, ye received your end by the sword, and ever dance with the angels.

*Troparion:*
Let us stand in the house of the Lord and call blessed Dadas, Maximus, and Quintilian, the valiant warriors who destroyed the enemies; and let us cry with faith: O Lover of mankind, by their prayers deliver all from intricate afflictions!

*Troparion:*
Your sacred memory, O most glorious victorious ones, brightly illuminates creation like the sun: we pray you, deliver us who celebrate it from the darkening of passions by your sacred intercessions, O passion-bearers of the Savior and God.

*Theotokion:*
Being a lover of sin, in repentance I fall down before Thy compassions, O God, and with faith I cry aloud: By the supplications of her who purely gave birth to Thee on earth, have compassion on me, and deliver me from eternal torments, O most good Christ.

*The same month, on the 29th day.*
*Of the Holy Ten Martyrs who were in Cyzicus:*[^196]
*And of our Venerable Father Memnon the Wonderworker.*

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Martyrs, Tone 2, Podoben: "O marvelous wonder!" (Ѡ҆ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ!):*
O all-praised martyrs of Christ, when Hellenic madness once prevailed, dragging all into lawless pits, ye forsook not the right way and joined not yourselves to godlessness; but ye suffered by the bravery of your disposition, and being slain by the sword, ye inherited life.

Let us with faith honor and with hymns call blessed Theognis and Rufus, Antipater, Theostichus[^197] and Artemas, Magnus and Theodotus, the glorious Thaumasius, and the divine Philemon, who shone brightly through sufferings and enlightened those who were in darkness, as we celebrate their sacred and all-glorious memory.

The fair regiment of the nine martyrs,[^198] the sacred host, the assembly gathered by God, the excellent church, the God-chosen gathering, the holy choir, the blessed company, having contended for the Trinity: these pray Thee, O Savior, to intercede for us, that we may receive remission of sins.

*Other Stichera of the Venerable One, in the same tone and melody:*
O venerable and blessed Memnon, having pre-purified thy soul through abstinence, thou becamest a pure and beautiful dwelling-place of the Spirit; therefore thou drivest away the spirits of malice, and healest ever the infirmities of those who run to thee, praying to Christ for us who praise thee.

O glorious venerable Memnon, having gained mastery over the passions of the soul, thou becamest a leader of monks, zealously anointing them unto the labors of abstinence, whom also thou didst present unto the all-seeing Word; and having lived a blessed life, unmingled with defilement: with them remember ever us who praise thee.

O most rich and venerable Memnon, of old thou didst cause a spring of water to flow forth, unto the praise of the Lord and the preservation of souls; from the depth of the sea thou didst save ships, from the harm of locusts thou didst preserve [fields] by thy prayers, and thou workest ever immeasurable wonders: praying to Christ for us who praise Thee.

*Glory, both now, of the feast.*
*Or Theotokion:*
Having taken flesh from thy blood, the pre-eternal God showed thee forth as an intercessor for men, O pure one; therefore deliver thy servants from every calamity and circumstance, and from the snares of the crafty enemy, and vouchsafe that those who glorify God and bow down before thee may partake of the radiance of the elect.

*Stavrotheotokion:*
Seeing Thee suspended upon the Cross, O Jesus, and accepting sufferings willingly, the Virgin and Thy Mother, O Word, cried out: Woe is me! O sweet Child, how dost Thou endure wounds unjustly, O Physician Who didst heal human infirmity and didst deliver all from corruption by Thy tender mercy?

*Canon of the Martyrs, Tone 4.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Drown Thou, I pray Thee, the three parts of my soul in the depth of dispassion, O Thou Who wast born of the Virgin, so that in the mortification of the body, as upon a timbrel, I may sing unto Thee a song of victory, [Who didst drown] the mighty captains.[^199]

*Troparion:*
Delighting together in divine beauties, and drinking richly of the torrent of pleasure, and being deified by participation, O crown-bearing martyrs, deliver from cruel afflictions those who call you blessed.

*Troparion:*
Having shone forth like sunbeams by the rays of your steadfast contests, O sufferers, and by the radiances of wonders, ye enlighten all; ye loose the night of passions, and destroy the gloom of demons.

*Troparion:*
Tormented on earth by the wounds of suffering, together ye passed over unto the painless life, O much-suffering ones together; therefore ye pour forth healings, easing every sickness by your God-pleasing prayers, O most rich ones.

*Theotokion:*
Thou didst give birth to God from thy pure blood, Who beyond understanding was incarnate in our likeness, O all-immaculate Mother; therefore all generations of generations call thee blessed, the confirmation of holy martyrs the confirmation.

*Another Canon, of the Venerable One, in the same tone.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
