Having traversed the watery deep of the Red Sea with dryshod feet, ancient Israel of old, by the crosswise-stretched hands of Moses, conquered the power of Amalek in the wilderness.

*Troparion:*
Shining with the divine grace of the All-Holy Spirit, O light-bearing Memnon, by thy bright prayer clear away the gloom of passions for those who celebrate thy honorable dormition.

*Troparion:*
O venerable one, having taken up thy cross, thou didst follow the Word Who was crucified for our sake, and by abstinence and lying on the ground, O venerable one, thou didst mortify the motions of the flesh.

*Troparion:*
Uniting thyself to God through fasting and prayers and tears, thou receivedst grace from Him to heal diseases and drive away spirits, O God-wise and most blessed Memnon.

*Theotokion:*
Having clothed Himself in man from thee because of goodness, O most pure and undefiled one, God came forth twofold in energies and natures; Him ever beseech, that He may enlighten those who praise thee.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
Not in wisdom, nor in power, nor in riches do we boast, but in Thee, O Christ, the Father's hypostatic Wisdom; for there is none holy save Thee, O Lover of mankind.

*Troparion:*
Refusing in any way to sacrifice unto graven images, ye offered yourselves as a living sacrifice unto Him Who was sacrificed for our sake, O all-fair youths, holy martyrs.

*Troparion:*
Strengthened by divine laws through the power of the Spirit, ye cast down the counsels and entanglements of the lawless, and having lawfully suffered, ye attained glory.

*Troparion:*
Let Artemas and Philemon be praised, Theostichus and Magnus, Rufus the glorious, and Antipater, and Thaumasius, Theognis and Theodotus, the renowned ones.

*Theotokion:*
All the weapons of the enemy have now utterly failed, since thou, O all-immaculate one, didst give birth to Christ, Who was wounded by the spear, for Whose sake the martyrs were wounded and suffered.

*Another [Canon]. Heirmos:*
Thy Church rejoices in Thee, O Christ, crying: Thou art my strength, O Lord, and my refuge and confirmation.

*Troparion:*
Like the sun upon earth, thou didst shine forth by the enlightenment of virtues, enlightening the souls of all, O venerable one, by spiritual grace.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst have thy loins girded with iron, manifestly unto the mortification of passions, unto the mortification of passions manifestly, O venerable Memnon, having clothed thyself in dispassion.

*Troparion:*
Having become compassionate toward the infirm, thou receivedst abundant grace from God, O venerable one, to heal incurable diseases.

*Theotokion:*
The Word of God dwelt in thy undefiled womb, O most pure one, and took away every sin from earthborn men.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 1, Podoben: "Thy tomb, [O Savior]" (Гро́бъ тво́й):*
The radiant choir of the sacred sufferers, the beautiful gathering, the God-assembled council, the ninefold band, having suffered steadfastly, trampled underfoot the enemy's tyranny, and were numbered among the angelic choirs, standing before the Lord.

*Glory, of the Venerable One, Tone 8, Podoben: "The Wisdom [and Word]" (Премꙋ́дрости):*
As a light-like star enlightening the world with virtues and with the rays of wonders, we all praise thee, O blessed one; for thou becamest a partaker of the divine splendor, and didst pass, O Memnon, unto the unwaning radiance; therefore we ever honor thy light-bearing and holy memory, glorifying the Savior, and with faith we cry unto thee: Beseech Christ God that He grant remission of sins unto those who honor thy holy memory with love.

*Both now, of the feast, or Theotokion:*
Having fallen into manifold temptations from enemies visible and invisible, and held fast by the tempest of mine innumerable transgressions: as unto my fervent protection and shelter, O pure one, I run unto the haven of thy goodness. Therefore, O most pure one, fervently beseech Him Who without seed was incarnate of thee, for all thy servants who unceasingly pray unto thee, O all-pure Theotokos, ever beseeching Him to grant remission of sins unto those who worthily sing thy glory.

*Stavrotheotokion:*
The ewe-lamb, beholding the Lamb and Shepherd and Deliverer upon the Cross, cried out weeping, and bitterly lamenting, exclaimed: The world indeed rejoices, receiving deliverance through Thee, but my womb burns, beholding Thy Crucifixion, which Thou endurest for the tenderheartedness of Thy mercy, O long-suffering Lord! O Abyss of mercy and inexhaustible Spring, take compassion and grant remission of sins unto those who with faith hymn Thy divine passions.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Sitting in glory upon the throne of Godhead, Jesus the most divine came upon a light cloud, and by an incorrupt hand saved those who cry: Glory to Thy power, O Christ to Thy power, O Christ.

*Troparion:*
As burnt-offerings, O sufferers, and God-consecrated slaughters, a perfect offering, acceptable sacrifices, ye were brought unto Him Who was willingly sacrificed and put death to death; therefore we call you blessed.

*Troparion:*
Showing forth a great wonder, O sufferers, ye straightened the contracted hands [of a man], and delivered from the dropsical affliction[^200] a notable man who once fled with love unto your shrine, O most marvelous ones.

*Troparion:*
Let Artemas and Antipater, Theostichus and Rufus, Magnus and Philemon, and Dion and Theognis, with the glorious Theodotus, be worthily called blessed; having suffered, they cry: Glory to Thy power, O Christ.

*Theotokion:*
He Who ineffably sits in the bosom of the Father sits in thine embrace, O Virgin, as an infant; depicting Whose blessed passion, the fair-victorious youths chose to die.

*Another [Canon]. Heirmos:*
Beholding Thee, the Sun of righteousness, lifted up upon the Cross, the Church stood in her station, worthily crying aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lord.

*Troparion:*
Having cleansed thy heart with streams of tears, O venerable Memnon, thou didst cause waters to gush forth in a waterless place, God having glorified thee as His pleasing servant.

*Troparion:*
Shining like a star by thy most glorious life, through thy sacred wonders thou didst show thyself to be another heaven, O blessed one, unto those who cry with faith: Glory to Thy power, O Lord.

*Troparion:*
Obeying the Master's laws, O venerable one, thou didst instruct and shepherd those who were under thee, O venerable one; and like an obedient servant, thou didst multiply manifold the talent given unto thee.

*Theotokion:*
Strengthen, I pray thee, by thine intercession my mind that is drawn unto bodily pleasures, O Parent of God, who alone didst give birth to God the Giver of grace.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
The ungodly shall not see Thy glory, O Christ; but waking early from the night, we praise Thee, O Only-begotten One, the Radiance of the Father's glory of Godhead, O Lover of mankind.

*Troparion:*
Having kindled your mind like noetic coals, O blessed ones, ye truly burned up all bramble-like delusion, and by the dew of faith extinguished the burning heat of idolatry.

*Troparion:*
Though in many bodies, ye had no division, O sufferers, as ye went to suffer for the undivided Trinity; therefore ye destroyed every host of the devil did ye destroy.

*Troparion:*
He that was loosed from the evil demonic legion sings of your wonders, O God-beholding martyrs, having come with zeal unto your honorable shrines.[^201]

*Theotokion:*
Show thyself delivering us from calamities and corrupting transgressions, from passions, afflictions, and distresses, who with faith praise thee, O Theotokos Ever-Virgin.

*Another [Canon]. Heirmos:*
Thou, my Lord, camest as a light into the world, a holy light, turning from dark ignorance those who praise Thee with faith.

*Troparion:*
Thy mind being wholly directed toward God with pure contemplations, O God-blessed one, thou showedst thyself above carnal pleasures.

*Troparion:*
Being a physician of bodies, O most rich one, burn away the brutish sicknesses of souls by the grace of the Spirit.

*Troparion:*
Blessed art thou, and well is it with thee, having conquered the malice of the enemy and having put the passions to death, O most blessed one.

*Theotokion:*
Thou didst give birth to God, and remainedst a virgin, O Bride of God; therefore the whole human race calls thee blessed, rejoicing.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
I am come into the depths of the sea, and the tempest of my many sins has drowned me; but as God, lead up my life from the abyss, O greatly Merciful One.

*Troparion:*
Precious was the death of the divine sufferers before Thee, O God of all; honoring them now, Thou hast shown them forth as a free dispensary of healing for the sick.

*Troparion:*
Having elevated judgment and hearts with a lofty disposition toward the highest, O God-bearing martyrs, ye manfully cast down the haughty exaltation of the evil one.

*Theotokion:*
As a heap of wheat, O pure one, thy womb appeared, bearing the Wheat of immortality, which nourishes the souls of all the faithful and enlightens the martyrs.

*Another [Canon]. Heirmos:*
I will sacrifice unto Thee with the voice of praise, O Lord, the Church cries unto Thee, having been cleansed from the demonic blood by the blood which flowed for mercy's sake from Thy ribs.

*Troparion:*
By thy name alone the locusts are driven away, and every demonic harm; for thou, O Memnon, wast known as a great servant of God, Who greatly glorified thee, O father.

*Troparion:*
Having conquered the mind of the carnal assault, thou receivedst the grace of the Spirit to drive away diseases, and to heal souls that are sick with sins, O most marvelous one.

*Troparion:*
[O marvelous] wonder, how by the mere calling upon thy name alone, O most glorious Memnon, the faithful who sail are saved, beholding thee hastening to their aid and calming the tumult of the waves!

*Theotokion:*
Mortify the mind of my flesh, O Virgin, having conceived and given birth beyond word and understanding unto the Slayer of death and the Giver of life, O most pure one.

*Kontakion, Tone 2, Podoben: "Seeking the highest" (Вы́шнихъ и҆щѧ̀):*
The steadfast and all-radiant choir of the nine martyrs,[^202] confessors of the tri-radiant Godhead, at the tribunal cried out to the judge:[^203] Our blood and our souls with our bodies, as an immaculate sacrifice we offer unto Thee, O Master: number us among Thy heavenly choirs, as merciful God.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The three youths in Babylon, having turned the tyrant's command into foolishness, cried out in the midst of the flame: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers.

*Troparion:*
Your labors, O martyrs, ever drop divine streams of healings, drying up rivers of diseases and washing away every defilement of passions, O most marvelous ones.

*Troparion:*
The passion-bearers rejoiced when they suffered with ineffable joy, singing as they were cut down by the sword: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers.

*Troparion:*
Ease the burden of diseases, the passions of heart, the infirmities of body, and every sorrow that comes upon us, O glorious sufferers.

*Theotokion:*
Having appeared as a great strength unto all the martyrs, O pure Mother of God, thou didst appear as protection unto men who ever praise thee with sacred hymns.

*Another [Canon]. Heirmos:*
In the Persian furnace, Abraham's children, burned by love of piety rather than by flame, cried out: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord.

*Troparion:*
With showers of tears thou quenchedst the furnace of passions; and now truly by the fire of thy wonders, O blessed one, thou ever burnest away the harm of diseases by grace.

*Troparion:*
Zealously didst thou love thy Creator, O blessed one; therefore, O wise Memnon, seeking [Him], thou becamest outside the flesh and the world: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord.

*Troparion:*
Like a tree planted by the streams of abstinence, O father, thou yieldedst the fruits of the operation of wonders, O Memnon O most rich Memnon, abounding in due season.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst stop the oncoming harm of locusts, O wise Memnon, by divine commands, drowning them in river waters; crying out: Blessed art Thou, O my God, thou didst exclaim.

*Theotokion:*
From boundless transgressions deliver me, O Maiden, the only strong help of the humble and cleansing of sins: Blessed art thou among women, O all-immaculate Sovereign Lady.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
O Almighty Deliverer of all, having descended into the midst of the flame, Thou didst bedew those who acted piously, and didst teach them to sing: All ye works, bless and praise the Lord.

*Troparion:*
Let us offer joyful praise unto Him Who granted us the great protectors, the passion-bearing martyrs, as they cry: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord.

*Troparion:*
Having nobly taken root upon the rock of faith, like branches, the martyrs brought forth unto us the fruits of suffering, as with faith we sing: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord.

*Troparion:*
O renowned ones in suffering, heal the passions of my soul, and deliver me from Gehenna and from the outer darkness, praying to Christ our God.

*Theotokion:*
Thou didst give birth to Christ the Lawgiver, Who crowned the passion-bearing martyrs who suffered lawfully; Him beseech, O Maiden, to heal me who am conquered by the law of sin.

*Another [Canon]. Heirmos:*
Daniel, having stretched forth his hands, stopped the mouths of the lions in the pit; and the youths, lovers of piety, girded with virtue, quenched the power of fire, crying aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord.

*Troparion:*
Like the morning didst thou shine forth, like a star, like a great sun, O God-inspired Memnon, enlightening souls by grace, as those who appointed thee an excellent guide, a sacred teacher, and a marvelous instructor cry aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord.

*Troparion:*
Thou gavest a deep, death-bearing wound to the enemy, O most blessed one, when by the grace of the Spirit thou didst acquire humble meekness, humbling his haughty disposition to the earth, and trampling upon his soul-corrupting snares.

*Troparion:*
Thou wast vouchsafed to see the blessedness there, and the everlasting delight, O holy one, and the splendors of the saints, having lived thy life well; and now thou criest with joy: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, bless the Lord.

*Troparion:*
The place that was waterless before, and now is full of water, proclaims, O venerable one, Thy faith toward the Lord and the grace of Thy wonders, which thou didst work, crying unto the Giver of good things: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord.

*Theotokion:*
Vouchsafe visitation, O Sovereign Lady full of divine grace, unto my humble soul that is sick from the burden of cruel things and drowned, and show it wholly healthy, as it cries: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Eve indeed, through the disease of disobedience, brought in the curse; but thou, O Virgin Theotokos, through the blossoming of thy bearing in the womb, didst cause blessing to blossom forth unto the world: therefore we all magnify thee.

*Troparion:*
Lo, the heavenly gates are opened unto you who have suffered, Christ said unto the passion-bearers: Enter and receive rejoicing the worthy rewards of your sufferings, ye who have valiantly contended of old!

*Troparion:*
Fair were ye in bodily wounds, and ye were made like unto the angels; and now ye stand before the Beauty of all, ever rejoicing, visibly adorned by the participation of fair things, O all-honored ones.

*Troparion:*
Let us stand in the house of God, praising the sufferings of Antipater, Theostichus, Rufus, and Philemon, the wonders of divine Thaumasius, and the invincible fortitude of Magnus, Artemas, Theognis, and Theodotus.[^204]

*Troparion:*
The celebration of the saints shone forth upon us as a day of salvation; celebrating which suppliantly, we cry unto them with faith: O radiant crown of martyrs, illuminate the hearts of all!

*Theotokion:*
Clothed from thee in the bodily purple, the King came forth all-beautiful from thy womb, and conquered all enemies, and granted victory unto the sufferers, O pure and only Ever-Virgin.

*Another [Canon]. Heirmos:*
A cornerstone was cut out from thee, the unhewn mountain, O Virgin, without hands of man: Christ, Who joined together the separated natures; therefore rejoicing, we magnify thee, O Theotokos.

*Troparion:*
Strengthened by the divine sovereign power, O God-wise Memnon, thou wast able to overcome all the enemy's might; therefore, having received the crown as a victor, pray to Christ for us.

*Troparion:*
As from a spring, streams of healings flow from thy shrine, and by the power of the divine Spirit they cleanse diseases and defilements, and water the thoughts of those who honor thee, O venerable one.

*Troparion:*
Today thy memory, O father, like the sun enlightens every age and understanding; for thou didst work the works of light, O Memnon, and having now departed, thou hast taken up thy dwelling in the unwaning Light.

*Troparion:*
Thou hast been taken up unto the eternal mansions, worthily beholding God; and thou wast numbered among the flocks of the venerable, O blessed Memnon, with whom remember us who remember thee on earth.

*Theotokion:*
With joy we cry unto thee the archangel's voice, O Virgin: Rejoice, loosening of the curse, glory of the venerable, praise of the martyrs, preaching of the prophets, and salvation of men!

*The same month, on the 30th day.*
*Of the Holy Apostle James, Brother of the Holy John the Theologian.*

*At Vespers, we sing "Blessed is the man," 1st antiphon. At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera on 8: of the feast, 3; and of the Apostle, 5, Tone 4, Podoben: "As noble" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*
With the rod of grace, from the depth of vanity thou drewest forth men, O most marvelous James, obeying the nod of the Teacher Who enlightened thy mind in all things, and Who showed thee forth, O most blessed one, as an apostle and honorable God-proclaimer of this incomprehensible Godhead. *(Twice)*

The spiritual radiance descended upon thee in the form of fire, and made thee, O blessed one, a divine receptacle, steadfastly driving away the darkness of godlessness, and enlightening the world with the brightness of thy most wise words, O initiate of mysteries, crown of the apostles, eyewitness of Christ, James! *(Twice)*

Having enlightened with the radiances of preaching those sleeping in the darkness of ignorance, O glorious James, thou showedst them forth through faith as sons of the Master and God; Whose passion and death thou didst emulate, and of Whose glory thou becamest a partaker, as most wise, and as a God-proclaimer, and as a true disciple.

*Glory, Tone 2:*
Come, let us all praise with psalmic hymns James, the preacher of the heavenly mystery and narrator of the Gospel; for he appeared as a river of the noetic paradise, watering the furrows of the soul with heavenly showers, and showing them forth fruitful unto Christ God, Who grants unto all unto all by his prayers, cleansing and enlightenment and great mercy.

*Both now, of the feast.*

*Entrance. Prokeimenon of the day, and three readings:*

*Reading from the General Epistle of James. Chapter 1:*[^205]
James, a servant of God and of the Lord Jesus Christ, to the twelve tribes which are in the dispersion, greeting. Count it all joy, my brethren, when ye fall into manifold temptations; knowing that the trying of your faith works patience. But let patience have her perfect work, that ye may be perfect and entire, lacking in nothing. If any of you lack wisdom, let him ask of God, Who gives to all men simply, and upbraids not; and it shall be given him. But let him ask in faith, nothing doubting; for he that doubts is like a wave of the sea, driven with the wind and tossed. For let not that man think that he shall receive anything from God. A double-minded man is unstable in all his ways. Let the brother of low degree boast in his exaltation, but the rich, in his humiliation; because as the flower of the grass he shall pass away. For the sun is no sooner risen with a burning heat, but it withers the grass, and the flower thereof falls, and the grace of the fashion of it perishes; so also shall the rich man fade away in his ways. Blessed is the man that endures temptation; for when he is tried, he shall receive the crown of life, which God hath promised to them that love Him.

*Reading from the General Epistle of James. Chapter 1, verse 13:*
Let no man when he is tempted say: I am tempted of God; for God cannot be tempted with evil, neither tempts He any man; but every man is tempted, when he is drawn away of his own lust, and enticed. Then when lust hath conceived, it brings forth sin; and sin, when it is finished, brings forth death. Do not err, my beloved brethren: Every good gift and every perfect gift is from above, and comes down from the Father of lights, with Whom is no variableness, neither shadow of turning. For of His own will begat He us with the word of truth, that we should be a kind of firstfruits of His creatures. Therefore, my beloved brethren, let every man be swift to hear, slow to speak, slow to wrath; for the wrath of man works not the righteousness of God. Therefore laying aside all filthiness and superfluity of malice, receive with meekness the engrafted word, which is able to save your souls your souls. But be ye doers of the word, and not hearers only, deceiving your own selves. For if any be a hearer of the word, and not a doer, he is like unto a man beholding his natural face in a mirror; for he beholds himself, and goes his way, and straightway forgets what manner of man he was. But whoso looks into the perfect law of liberty, and continues therein, he being not a forgetful hearer, but a doer of the work, this man shall be blessed in his deed. If any man among you seem to be devout,[^206] and bridles not his tongue, but deceives his own heart, this man's religion is vain. Pure religion and undefiled before God and the Father is this: To visit the fatherless and widows in their affliction, and to keep oneself unspotted from the world.

*Reading from the General Epistle of James. Chapter 2:*
My brethren, have not the faith of our Lord Jesus Christ, the Lord of glory, with respect of persons. For if there come unto your assembly a man with a gold ring, in fine apparel, and there come in also a poor man in vile raiment; and ye have respect to him that wears the fine clothing, and say unto him: Sit thou here in a good place; and say to the poor: Stand thou there, or sit here under my footstool: are ye not then partial in yourselves, and are become judges of evil thoughts? Give ear, my beloved brethren: Hath not God chosen the poor of this world rich in faith, and heirs of the kingdom which He hath promised to them that love Him? But ye have despised the poor. Do not rich men oppress you, and draw you before the judgment seats? Do not they blaspheme that worthy name by which ye are called? If ye indeed fulfill the royal law according to the scripture: Thou shalt love thy neighbor as thyself, ye do well. But if ye have respect to persons, ye commit sin, being convicted by the law as transgressors. For whosoever shall keep the whole law, and yet offend in one point, he is guilty of all. For He that said: Do not commit adultery, said also: Do not kill. Now if thou commit no adultery, yet if thou kill, thou art become a transgressor of the law. So speak ye, and so do, as they that shall be judged by the law of liberty. For judgment is without mercy to him that hath showed no mercy; and mercy boasts against judgment.

*At the Aposticha, Stichera of the Saint, Tone 2, Podoben: "O marvelous wonder!" (Ѡ҆ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ!):*
Blessed James, beholder of God, having straightway received the voice of the Word, thou neglectedst paternal pleasing and love; having forsaken the tumult of life, and having entered into the noetic sea, thou didst trouble it with the teachings of piety and with thy surpassing beams, O God-blessed one.

*Verse: Their sound hath gone forth into all the earth, and their words unto the ends of the world.*

O James, eyewitness of the Word, the Prince of life and Father of the age to come: by deeds pleasing [to God], O blessed one, thou deprivedst all-defiled Ishmael of the birthright,[^207] who accounted his belly as a god; whom also thou didst trip up and showedst him forth stripped of paternal shelter, having no part in the blessing and inheritance.

*Verse: The heavens declare the glory of God, and the firmament proclaims the work of His hands.*

Thou wast appointed a prince, O glorious one, now over all the earth, as was written concerning thee of old; having become a disciple of Him Who made all things, and by thy most fervent zeal and unconquerable boldness thou didst endure slaughter, being the first to be slain from the honorable company of the twelve disciples, O blessed one.

*Glory, Tone 4:*
Thou followedst Christ, O divine James, and hatedst the world; having hidden His teachings in thy treasury, thou appearedst as an apostle; therefore, going unto the nations, and having enlightened with the commandments of the Savior their souls that were darkened by delusion and passions, [thou taughtest them] to believe in the consubstantial Trinity.

*Both now, of the feast.*

*Troparion, Tone 3:*
O holy Apostle James, entreat the merciful God, that He grant remission of transgressions unto our souls.

*Another Troparion, in the same tone:*
Thou wast a chosen apostle of Christ, and uterine brother of the beloved Theologian, O all-praised James; for those who praise thee ask remission of sins, and great mercy unto our souls.

*Glory, both now, of the feast.*

*At Matins, at "God is the Lord," Troparion of the feast, twice. Glory, of the Apostle. Both now, of the feast. After the 1st Kathisma, Sessional Hymn of the Apostle. Glory, both now, of the feast. After the 2nd Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4:*
Like a ray, Christ the Sun of righteousness sent thee forth to enlighten all the earth, O glorious Apostle James; by thy divine prayers thou illuminest and with divine unwaning light thou enlightenest all who celebrate with faith thy holy thy memory.

*Glory, both now, of the feast.*

*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "The Wisdom [and Word]" (Премꙋ́дрости):*
Filled from on high with rays and enlightened with divine brightness, O Apostle James, thou didst behold upon Mount Tabor the Creator and Lord being transfigured, and wast worthily accounted to hear the Father's voice. Therefore also thou wast vouchsafed great recompense, having become an eyewitness and disciple of grace, and a witness of the divine mysteries. Beseech Christ God to grant remission of sins unto those who honor thy holy memory with love. *(Twice)*

*Glory, both now, of the feast.*

*Then the Gradual Hymns, 1st antiphon of the 4th Tone. Prokeimenon, Tone 4: Their sound hath gone forth into all the earth, and their words unto the ends of the world. Verse: The heavens declare the glory of God. Let every breath: Gospel according to John, pericope 67. After Psalm 50, Sticheron, Tone 4:*
*Thou followedst Christ:*
*It is written at the Aposticha Glory.*

*Canon of the feast, with the heirmos, on 6; and of the Apostle, on 8, Tone 8.*

*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
The wonderworking rod of Moses of old, striking crosswise and dividing the sea, drowned Pharaoh the charioteer; but it saved fugitive Israel, fleeing on foot, who sang a song unto God.

*Troparion:*
Having drawn up my soul from the depth of transgressions with thy net, like a skilled fisher, O blessed one: enlighten it with ray-emitting beams, and vouchsafe me worthily to praise thy memory, O God-blessed disciple James.

*Troparion:*
The pre-eternal God, co-unoriginate with the Father, and most perfect, having become incarnate, appeared on earth as man, and showed thee forth, O blessed one, as a co-worker of grace and a most wise minister, strengthened by His power.

*Troparion:*
God the Foreknower perceived the nobility of thy soul, O disciple, and the steadfastness and invincible mind, O glorious one; and He appointed thee the best of His ministers, proclaiming unto the nations.

*Theotokion:*
The Angel of Great Counsel of the Father appeared unto the world from the Virgin Mother, having taken flesh; He shows thee forth as a disciple adorned with virtues, O God-proclaiming James, who eloquently preachest His words. Katavasia of the feast.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
Thou Who in the beginning established the heavens with understanding, and founded the earth upon the waters founded upon the waters: establish me, O Christ, upon the rock of Thy commandments; for there is none holy save Thee, O only Lover of mankind.

*Troparion:*
The violent breath of the Comforter from on high, having kindled thee, showed thee forth as a wise God-proclaimer, openly preaching the majesty of the incarnate Word, of Whom thou wast also an eyewitness.

*Troparion:*
Like a chosen arrow sharpened by the word, [Christ] shot thee into the hearts of the enemies, tearing asunder the souls of the adversaries, O most wise one, and taking captive their spoils, O initiate of mysteries, James.

*Troparion:*
The meekness of the undefiled parts of thy soul was seen by the Master, and before thy calling, O blessed one, thou appearedst well-pleasing unto Him, O James, and becamest a disciple of His dispensation.

*Troparion:*
By thy sacred and light-bearing life, O wise one, and clearly adorned with kinship to the Lord, O all-blessed one, thou showedst thyself a companion; for from thy youth thou appearedst as a dwelling of the Word.

*Theotokion:*
Thou appearedst as a noetic gate of the Dayspring from on high, Who appeared unto those on earth, O Virgin; for through thee the Word came unto us, to save all from irrationality, O all-pure one.

*Kontakion and Ikos of the feast.*

*Sessional Hymn of the Apostle, Tone 2:*
Having caught the nations, the glorious Apostle taught the ends of the earth to worship Thee with the Father and the Spirit, O Christ God; for his sake establish Thy Church, and send down Thy blessing upon the faithful, O only Merciful One and Lover of mankind.

*Glory, both now, of the feast.*

*Or Theotokion:*
Having conceived Wisdom and the Word in thy womb without being consumed, O Mother of God, thou didst give birth unto the world to Him Who holds the world, and heldest in thine embrace Him Who upholds all, the Nourisher of all and Creator of creation. Therefore I pray thee, O all-holy Virgin, and with faith I glorify thee, that I may be delivered from transgressions; and in the day of judgment, when I am to stand before the face of my Creator, O Sovereign Lady, pure Virgin, grant me then thy help: for thou canst do all things whatsoever thou desirest, O all-hymned one.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Thou art my strength, O Lord, Thou art my power, Thou art my God, Thou art my rejoicing, Who leftest not the bosom of the Father, and visitedst our poverty; therefore with the Prophet Habakkuk I cry unto Thee: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind.

*Troparion:*
Having become a preacher of Christ, O divine James, being called thou straightway followedst, having despised paternal love, and exchanged corruptible things for eternal ones; therefore thou wast vouchsafed the ineffable heavenly inheritance, O blessed and ever-memorable one.

*Troparion:*
O thy fervent desire toward the Master Christ! For Him didst Thou greatly desire, and being enlightened by His radiance, so as to be transfigured and appear unto all as a second light, being illuminated by the rays and glory of the First [Light], O all-honored, blessed James.

*Troparion:*
Thou hadst an honorable and most marvelous life; for having received uncontrollable desire by desire, thou attainedst, O beholder of God, the perfect blessedness of desires in the Divine Principle, crying with the bodiless hosts: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind.

*Troparion:*
Having given thyself wholly over to the Master's nod, O God-radiant disciple, thou wast manifestly borne up by virtues unto the height, and truly unto the divine summit; and from thence thou pourest forth rivers of healings unto those who faithfully glorify thy memory.

*Theotokion:*
Thou art the boast of the faithful, O unwedded one; thou art the protectress, thou also the refuge of Christians, a rampart and a haven; for thou bearest supplications unto thy Son, O all-immaculate one, and savest from perils those who with faith and love know thee as the pure Mother of God.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Why hast Thou cast me away from Thy face, O Light never-setting, and alien darkness hath covered me, wretch that I am? But turn me, and direct my paths toward the light of Thy commandments, I pray Thee.

*Troparion:*
Kindled with zeal like a new Elijah, thou desiredst, O glorious one, to consume with fire those who submitted not to thy preaching; but the Lover of mercy forbade thee, teaching thee graceful meekness.[^208]

*Troparion:*
Having ascended the utmost summit by virtue, for the sake of the love of the best things thou desiredst, O most wise one, to hold the primary thrones of the Master,[^209] not as desiring vain glory, but to behold at the last Him Whom thou desiredst.

*Troparion:*
James surpassed the laws of humanity, O Savior; for clothed in Thy power as in a garment, he pours forth rivers of healings and wonders, and enlightens the ends of the earth through faith.

*Troparion:*
The bright cloud of the Word, shining with the glory of deification, overshadowed thee on Mount Tabor, O blessed James; and thou wast vouchsafed to hear the Father's voice, testifying to His Sonship.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
The abyss of sins and the tempest of transgressions trouble me, and drag me into the depth of violent despair; but stretch forth unto me Thy strong hand, as unto Peter, O Helmsman, and save me.

*Troparion:*
Ceaselessly pouring forth streams of healings as from a spring, and piously gushing forth the enlightenment of doctrine, O wise James, thou illuminest the souls of those who piously approach with love, O most blessed one.

*Troparion:*
Having found thee, O most blessed one, a receptive vessel of His rich and honorable giving, the Master enriches thee with gifts, an ever-standing minister of the mysteries that are beyond understanding.

*Troparion:*
When for our sake the Master willed to receive mortification, the Cause of the true resurrection of the dead, He took thee as a wise initiate of mysteries, O blessed one, [to be] a fellow participant on the night in which He was betrayed.[^210]

*Theotokion:*
Having given birth to the abyss of compassions, O Virgin Theotokos, save my soul from the sorrows of life, and open unto me the doors of spiritual joy; for upon thee alone have I placed my hope.

*Kontakion, Tone 2, Podoben: "Seeking the highest" (Вы́шнихъ и҆щѧ̀):*
Having heard the divine voice calling thee, thou neglectedst paternal love, and rannest unto Christ, O James, with thy kinsman, O glorious one; with whom thou wast vouchsafed to see the divine Transfiguration of the Lord.

*Ikos:*
Being together with thy brother in the heavenly mansions, by your divine prayers drive away the Hagarenes who now trample holy places, walking with defiled and unclean feet, cutting off the devices of the tormentors; and those who are now held captive by them because of the multitude of our sins, vouchsafe deliverance:[^211] so that gathered together into one we may behold the divine Transfiguration of the Lord.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The fire in Babylon of old was ashamed before the condescension of God; therefore the youths in the furnace, dancing with rejoicing feet as in a meadow of flowers, sang: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers.

*Troparion:*
Thou drankest of the cup, as thou promisedst the Master Christ, and with His baptism wast thou baptized, O God-blessed one;[^212] unto Whom thou now criest fervently, rejoicing: Blessed is the God of our fathers.

*Troparion:*
Enlightened with the radiance of divine contemplation, thou appearedst, O James; therefore Christ the Giver of grace adorns thee with the crown of the kingdom, as with the angels thou singest with the angels: Blessed is the God of our fathers.

*Troparion:*
In thee the Great Giver fulfilled the promises, when thou wast borne up unto Him by the likeness of His sufferings; standing before Whom now, thou criest rejoicing: Blessed is the God of our fathers.

*Theotokion:*
In two natures indeed, but not in two persons, we preach Him Who was incarnate of thee, O Mother; Whom we depict in the flesh and worship the depiction of His form, having through thee been reconciled unto God, O pure one.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Sevenfold the Chaldean tyrant frantically kindled the furnace for the pious; but having seen them saved by a higher power, he cried unto the Creator and Deliverer: Ye youths, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt [Him] unto all ages.

*Troparion:*
Thou acquiredst Abraham's disposition, O beholder of God, following Christ Who called thee; and thou wast a most-bright minister unto Him, singing unto the Creator and Deliverer: Ye youths, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt [Him] unto all ages.

*Troparion:*
Thy proclamation was showed forth mightier than thunder,[^213] [silencing] the sound of the legal shadow; for thou didst thunder Christ unto all the universe, singing unto God, the Creator and Deliverer: Ye youths, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt [Him] unto all ages.

*Troparion:*
Thou wast marvelous, O glorious one, by the brightness of martyrdom and the true enlightenment of piety, as thou wast appointed a prince of the apostles over all the earth, singing unto the Creator and Deliverer: Ye youths, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt Christ unto all ages.

*Theotokion:*
The tongues of orators cannot praise thee, O Parent of God, Mary, Bride of God; for thou gavest birth to God Who is over all, O pure Maiden, the Creator and Deliverer; unto Whom we cry: Ye youths, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt [Him] unto all ages.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Heaven was amazed at this, and the ends of the earth were astonished, that God appeared in the flesh unto men, and thy womb became more spacious than the heavens; therefore the ranks of angels and men magnify thee, O Theotokos.

*Troparion:*
Having holily passed thy life, thou hast attained unto the dwellings of the saints, where rejoicing thou beholdest the tri-radiant radiance, delighting wherein, O most wise one, filled with gladness: show forth those who hymn thee [partakers] of joy, O most blessed one show forth those who hymn thee [partakers] of joy, O most blessed one, by thy prayers, O James.

*Troparion:*
The evildoers and murderers could not endure the sight of thee, as they could not endure thy Master before; for by rebuking their deeds, thou convictedst them by the example of thy life. They slay with the sword the emulator of Christ Who was crucified in the flesh for mankind,[^214] O God-proclaiming, most blessed one.

*Troparion:*
Tormented by heavenly poverty, Herod perished with plagues;[^215] for the wretched one was not satisfied with slaying Thy ministers and disciples, O Word, whom Thou namedst apostles; therefore, marveling at Thy righteous providence, O Benefactor, we magnify Thee.

*Theotokion:*
Thou appearedst, O Virgin Mother of God, having beyond nature given birth in the flesh unto God the Word, Whom the Father poured forth from His heart before all ages as Good; Whom now also we understand to be above bodies, though He was clothed in a body.

*Exapostilarion, Podoben: "Give ear, ye women" (Жены̀ ᲂу҆слы́шите):*
Let us all hymn James the son of Zebedee; for he ascended with Christ onto Mount Tabor, and was a spectator of the divine Transfiguration, and having descended, he preached unto the four corners of all [the earth], with Peter and John. *(Twice)*

*Glory, both now, of the feast.*

*At the Praises, Stichera of the feast, 3; and of the Apostle, 3, Tone 8, Podoben: "O marvelous wonder!" (Ѡ҆ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ!):*
Blessed James, beholder of God, having straightway received the voice of the Word, thou neglectedst paternal pleasing and love; having forsaken the tumult of life, and having entered into the noetic sea, thou didst trouble it with the teachings of piety and with thy surpassing beams, O God-blessed one.

O James, eyewitness of the Word, the Prince of life and Father of the age to come: by deeds pleasing [to God], O blessed one, thou deprivedst all-defiled Ishmael of the birthright,[^216] who accounted his belly as a god; whom also thou didst trip up and showedst him forth stripped of paternal shelter, having no part in the blessing and inheritance.

Thou wast appointed a prince, O glorious one, now over all the earth, as was written concerning thee of old; having become a disciple of Him Who made all things, and by thy most fervent zeal and unconquerable boldness thou didst endure slaughter, being the first to be slain from the honorable company of the twelve disciples, O blessed one.

*Glory, of the Apostle, Tone 6. Idiomelon:*
O all-wise fisher, holy disciple, laborer of the Savior, and narrator of His sufferings: who traversedst creation by faith, and having gathered the deceived nations and brought them unto God, thou ascendedst unto heaven like sweet incense. Therefore, standing before the Judge, pray that we may be delivered from our iniquities, and in the day of judgment be saved from torment.

*Both now, of the feast. Great Doxology.*

*At the Liturgy: The Beatitudes of the feast, the appointed ode; and of the Apostle, Ode 3, on 4. Prokeimenon, Tone 8: Their sound hath gone forth into all the earth, and their words unto the ends of the world. Verse: The heavens declare the glory of God. Epistle: Acts of the Apostles, pericope 24.[^217] Alleluia, Tone 4: The heavens shall confess Thy wonders, O Lord. Gospel according to Luke, pericope 17. Communion Hymn: Their sound hath gone forth into all the earth.*

*Resurrectional Theotokia for the Eight Tones.*[^218]

*Tone 1. Theotokion:*
Let us hymn the Virgin Mary, the glory of the whole world, who blossomed forth from mankind and gave birth to the Master, the gate of heaven, the song of the bodiless hosts, and the adornment of the faithful. For she appeared as heaven and the temple of the Godhead; she, having broken down the middle wall of enmity, brought in peace and opened the kingdom. Having her, therefore, as the confirmation of our faith, we have as champion the Lord born of her. Be of good courage, therefore, be of good courage, O people of God; for He shall conquer the enemies, for He is all-powerful.

*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*
Lo, the prophecy of Isaiah is fulfilled; for a Virgin thou hast given birth, and after giving birth, even as before birth, hast thou remained a Virgin, for God was He that was born. Therefore also He renewed nature. But, O Mother of God, despise not the supplications of thy servants, offered unto thee in thy temple; but as thou bearest the Tender-hearted One in thine arms, have compassion upon thy servants, and pray that our souls may be saved.

*Tone 2. Theotokion:*
The shadow of the Law has passed away, now that grace has come; for even as the bush burned with fire and was not consumed, so as a Virgin hast thou given birth, and a Virgin hast thou remained. Instead of the pillar of fire, the Sun of righteousness has shone forth; instead of Moses, Christ, the salvation of our souls.

*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*
O wonder new among all ancient wonders! For who has known a mother to give birth without a husband, and to bear in her arms Him Who upholds all creation? It is the counsel of God, He that was born. Whom as an Infant having borne in thine arms, O all-pure one, and having maternal boldness toward Him, cease not praying for those who honor thee: that He have mercy and save our souls.

*Tone 3. Theotokion:*
How shall we not marvel at thy God-man childbirth, O all-honored one? For not having received trial of man, O all-immaculate one, thou gavest birth in the flesh without a father unto the Son, Who was begotten of the Father without a mother before the ages; Who in no wise suffered change, or confusion, or division but preserved intact the property of each nature. Therefore, O Sovereign Lady, Virgin Mother, entreat Him that the souls of those who confess thee as Theotokos in orthodox fashion may be saved.

*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*
Without seed from the Holy Spirit, but by the Father's will, thou didst conceive the Son of God, Who exists before the ages from the Father without a mother; and for our sake, having come from thee without a father, thou didst give birth unto Him in the flesh, and nourished the Infant with milk. Therefore cease not praying, that our souls may be delivered from tribulations.

*Tone 4. Theotokion:*
David the Prophet, forefather of God, because of thee in song proclaimed concerning thee unto Him that has done great things for thee: The queen stood at Thy right hand.[^219] For God, Who was well-pleased to become man of thee without a father, showed thee forth as mother, mediatress of life; that He might renew His own image, which was corrupted by passions; and having found the sheep that went astray upon the mountains, taking it upon His shoulder, He will bring it unto the Father, and according to His own will will unite it with the heavenly hosts, and will save the world, O Theotokos, Christ Who has great and rich mercy.

*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*
Look upon the supplications of thy servants, O all-immaculate one, quelling the fierce uprisings against us, releasing us from every affliction. For thee alone do we have as a firm and sure confirmation, and we have acquired thy protection, that we who call upon thee may not be put to shame, O Theotokos. Speed unto the supplication of those who faithfully cry unto thee: Rejoice, O Theotokos, help of all, joy and protection, and salvation of our souls.

*Tone 5. Theotokion:*
In the Red Sea an image of the unwedded Bride was prefigured of old: there Moses, the divider of the water; but here Gabriel, the minister of the wonder. Then did Israel traverse the deep dryshod; but now the Virgin has given birth unto Christ without seed. The sea after the passage of Israel remained impassable; the immaculate one after the childbirth of Emmanuel remained incorrupt. O Thou Who art, and art before all, Who didst appear as man, O God, have mercy on us.

*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*
Thou art a temple and a door, a palace and a royal throne, O all-honored Virgin; through whom my Deliverer, Christ the Lord, appeared unto those sleeping in darkness, being the Sun of righteousness, desiring to enlighten that which He fashioned according to His own image by His own hand. Therefore, O all-hymned one, as having maternal boldness toward Him, unceasingly pray that our souls may be saved.

*Tone 6. Theotokion:*
Who shall not call thee blessed, O all-holy Virgin? Who shall not praise thy most pure childbirth? For the only-begotten Son, Who shone forth timelessly from the Father, the same came forth from thee, pure one, having ineffably become incarnate. Being God by nature, and having become man by nature for our sake; not divided into two persons, but known in two natures without confusion. Entreat Him, O pure and all-blessed one, that our souls may have mercy.

*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*
My Creator and Deliverer, Christ the Lord, having come forth from thy womb, O all-pure one, and having clothed Himself in me, delivered Adam from the first curse. Therefore unto thee, O all-pure one, as truly both the Mother of God and a Virgin, we unceasingly cry out the angelic "Rejoice!": Rejoice, O Theotokos, protection, shelter, and salvation of our souls.

*Tone 7. Theotokion:*
Thou wast known as a Mother beyond nature, O Theotokos, and didst remain a Virgin beyond speech and understanding; and the wonder of thy childbirth no tongue is able to tell. For the conception being most glorious, O pure one, the manner of the childbirth is incomprehensible; for where God so wills, the order of nature is overcome. Therefore, knowing thee as the Mother of God, we all fervently pray thee: pray that our souls may be saved.

*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*
Fleeing unto thy good protection, all we earthborn cry unto thee: O Theotokos, our hope, deliver us from our measureless transgressions, and save our souls.

*Tone 8. Theotokion:*
The King of heaven, through love of mankind, appeared on earth and dwelt with men; for having taken flesh from a pure Virgin, and having come forth from her with the assumption [of humanity], one is the Son, twofold in nature, but not in hypostasis. Therefore, truly proclaiming Him as perfect God and perfect man, we confess Christ our God. Entreat Him, O unwedded Mother, that our souls may have mercy.

*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*
O unwedded Virgin, who ineffably didst conceive God in the flesh, Mother of God Most High: accept the supplications of thy servants, O all-immaculate one, granting unto all cleansing of transgressions. Now receiving our supplications, pray that we all may be saved.

*Theotokia of the Eight Tones, sung when there is Glory to the Saint in the Menaion, and Both now in the tone of the Saint.*[^220]

*Tone 1:*
*On Sunday evening, Theotokion:*
O rejoicing of the heavenly ranks, mighty protection of men upon earth, O all-pure Virgin, save us who flee unto thee; for next to God, O Theotokos, we have placed our hope in thee.

*On Monday at Matins, Theotokion:*
Most holy of all holy powers, more honorable than all creation, O Theotokos, Sovereign Lady of the world: save us who didst give birth to the Savior, from our ten-thousandfold transgressions and tribulations, as thou art good, by thy prayers.

*On Monday evening, Theotokion:*
O all-hymned Virgin, Moses with prophetic eyes beheld the mystery that is in thee: the bush burning and unconsumed; for the fire of the Godhead, O pure one, burned not thy womb. Therefore we pray thee, as Mother of our God, ask peace for the world, and great mercy.

*On Tuesday at Matins, Theotokion:*
The harlot and the prodigal, and the thief have I surpassed, and the publican in transgressions have I exceeded, and the Ninevites. Woe is me, what shall become of me? How shall I, wretch that I am, escape torment? O pure one, I fall down before thee: have compassion upon me by thy mercy, even as thy Son has saved them.

*On Wednesday evening, Theotokion:*
Rejoice, joy of the forebears, gladness of the apostles and martyrs, and protection of us, thy servants, O Virgin.

*On Thursday at Matins, Theotokion:*
Rejoice, O Virgin Theotokos; rejoice, boast of the whole universe; rejoice, all-pure, blessed Mother of God.

*On Friday evening, Theotokion:*
Truly beyond understanding are thy marvelous great things, O Bride of God, of thy childbirth which all the prophets proclaimed, all most glorious! The conception and childbirth, O all-hymned one, are incomprehensible and ineffable; by Whom He has saved the world, as He is merciful.

*On Saturday at Matins, Theotokion:*
Rejoice from us, O holy Virgin Theotokos, pure vessel of the whole universe, unquenchable lamp, dwelling-place of the Incomprehensible, unconquerable temple! Rejoice, of whom was born the Lamb of God, Who takes away the sins of the whole world!

*Tone 2:*
*On Sunday evening, Theotokion:*
Rejoice, O Mary Theotokos, indestructible temple, and most holy, as the Prophet cries: Holy is Thy temple, marvelous in righteousness.[^221]

*On Monday at Matins, Theotokion:*
In thee have we placed our hope, O Theotokos; let us not fall away from our expectation: save us from tribulations, O helper of those in perplexity, and destroy the counsels of the adversaries; for thou art our salvation, O blessed one.

*On Monday evening, Theotokion:*
Impassable gate, mystically sealed, O blessed Virgin Theotokos: receive our supplications, and bear them unto thy Son and God, that through thee He may save our souls.

*On Tuesday at Matins, Theotokion:*
Rejoice, O Mary Theotokos, indestructible temple, and most sacred, as the Prophet cries: Holy is Thy temple, marvelous in righteousness.

*On Wednesday at Matins, Theotokion:*
As a fruitful olive tree, O Virgin, thou hast brought forth for thee the Fruit of life, to bear the fruit of great and rich mercy unto the world.

*On Thursday at Matins, Theotokion:*
All my hope I place in thee, O Mother of God: preserve me under thy protection.

*On Friday evening, Theotokion:*
Save thy servants from tribulations, O Virgin Theotokos; for next to God we all flee unto thee, as unto an indestructible rampart and protection.

*On Saturday at Matins, Theotokion:*
Come, with unceasing hymns crying out, let us all glorify the Mother of light, for she gave birth to our salvation; and let us offer "Rejoice!" as having alone given birth unto the Pre-eminent over all, Who is God before the ages. Rejoice, thou that didst rebuild fallen Eve! Rejoice, all-pure unwedded Virgin!

*Tone 3:*
*On Sunday evening, Theotokion:*
O Theotokos, protectress of all who pray unto thee, in thee do we take courage, and in thee do we boast, and in thee is all our hope: entreat Him that was born of thee for thine unworthy servants thine unworthy servants.

*On Monday at Matins, Theotokion:*
O all-immaculate boast of the heavenly ranks, song of the apostles, and fulfillment of the prophets, O Theotokos, receive our prayers.

*On Monday evening, Theotokion:*
Holy among women, unwedded Mother of God, entreat Him Whom thou didst bear, the King and God, that He may save us, as He is the Lover of mankind.

*On Tuesday at Matins, Theotokion:*
Without seed thou didst conceive of the Holy Spirit, and giving glory we hymn thee: Rejoice, O all-holy Virgin!

*On Wednesday evening, Theotokion:*
Snatch me, who have greatly sinned, O Maiden, by thy great mercy from the violent flame; and guide me aright, O pure one, by thy supplications, directing me unto saving paths by thy maternal prayers.

*On Thursday at Matins, Theotokion:*
Without seed thou didst conceive of the Holy Spirit, and giving glory we hymn thee: Rejoice, O all-holy Virgin!

*On Friday evening, Theotokion:*
Holy among women, unwedded Mother of God, entreat Him Whom thou didst bear, the King and God, that He may save us, as He is the Lover of mankind.

*On Saturday at Matins, Theotokion:*
Without seed thou didst conceive of the Holy Spirit, and giving glory we hymn thee: Rejoice, O all-holy Virgin!

*Tone 4:*
*On Sunday evening, Theotokion:*
Rejoice, cloud of the Light! Rejoice, radiant candlestick! Rejoice, jar wherein is the manna! Rejoice, rod of Aaron! Rejoice, unburnt bush! Rejoice, bridal chamber! Rejoice, throne! Rejoice, holy mountain! Rejoice, refuge! Rejoice, divine table! Rejoice, mystical door! Rejoice, joy of all!

*On Monday at Matins, Theotokion:*
O Theotokos, Queen of all, boast of the Orthodox: destroy the haughtiness of the heretics, and put to shame the faces of those who bow not down, nor venerate, O all-pure one, thine honorable icon.[^222]

*On Monday evening, Theotokion:*
Deliver us from our necessities, O Mother of Christ God, who gavest birth to the Creator of all, that we all that we all may cry unto thee: Rejoice, sole protection of our souls!

*On Tuesday at Matins, Theotokion:*
Preserve thy servants from all tribulations, O blessed Theotokos, that we may glorify thee, the hope of our souls.

*On Wednesday evening, Theotokion:*
Having thee, O Theotokos, as our hope and protection, we fear not the snares of the enemy, for thou savest our souls.

*On Thursday at Matins, Theotokion:*
We have acquired thee as a rampart, O all-pure Theotokos, and a calm haven and confirmation; therefore I pray thee, who am storm-tossed in life: pilot and save me.

*On Friday evening, Theotokion:*
Unquenchable lamp, righteous throne, O all-pure Sovereign Lady: pray that our souls may be saved.

*On Saturday at Matins, Theotokion:*
O only pure and all-pure Virgin, who without seed didst give birth unto God: pray that our souls may be saved.

*Tone 5:*
*On Sunday evening, Theotokion:*
Fearful and most glorious and great is the mystery: the Incomprehensible was contained in a womb, and the Mother after childbirth remained again a Virgin; for she gave birth unto God incarnate of her. Unto Him let us cry out, unto Him let us recite a song, singing with the angels: Holy art Thou, O Christ God, Who becamest man for our sake; glory to Thee!

*On Monday at Matins, Theotokion:*
O full of grace, intercede by thy prayers, and ask for our souls a multitude of compassions and cleansing of many transgressions, we pray thee.

*On Monday evening, Theotokion:*
Soothe the afflictions of my much-sighing soul, thou that hast wiped away every tear from the face of the earth; for thou drivest away the diseases of men, and dispellest the sorrows of sinners; for we all have acquired thee as hope and confirmation, O all-holy Virgin Mother.

*On Tuesday at Matins, Theotokion:*
O full of grace, intercede by thy prayers, and ask for our souls a multitude of compassions and cleansing of many transgressions, we pray thee.

*On Wednesday evening, Theotokion:*
We call thee blessed, O Virgin Theotokos, and we the faithful glorify thee as is meet, an unshakable city, an unassailable rampart, a firm protection, and the refuge of our souls.

*On Thursday at Matins, Theotokion:*
We call thee blessed, O Virgin Theotokos, for from thee shone forth the Sun of righteousness, Christ, Who has great mercy.

*On Friday evening, Theotokion:*
We pray unto thee as Mother of God, O blessed one: pray for the salvation of our souls.

*On Saturday at Matins, Theotokion:*
O wretched soul, what answer shalt thou make unto the Judge in that hour, when the thrones are set for judgment, and the Judge shall come from the heavens, having descended with myriads of angels?[^223] When He shall sit upon the judgment seat, to make reckoning with unprofitable servants like unto me, what shalt thou answer? And what shalt thou offer then? Truly nothing, having defiled thy mind and body. Therefore fall down before the Virgin, and cry unceasingly, that rich forgiveness of sins may be granted thee.

*Tone 6:*
*On Sunday evening, Theotokion:*
Let us the faithful hymn in archangelic fashion the heavenly bridal chamber, and the truly sealed door: Rejoice, through whom blossomed forth for us Christ the Savior of all, the Giver of life and God! Cast down the tormentors, O Theotokos, our godless enemies, by thy hand, O all-pure one, hope of Christians.

*On Monday at Matins, Theotokion:*
Thou didst receive the archangelic word, and wast showed forth as the cherubic throne, and in thine embrace thou didst bear, O Theotokos, the Hope of our souls.

*On Monday evening, Theotokion:*
No one who flees unto thee goes away from thee ashamed, O all-pure Virgin Theotokos, but asks for grace, and receives a gift according to his profitable petition.

*On Tuesday at Matins, Theotokion:*
Of great gifts wast thou accounted worthy, O pure Virgin Mother of God: for thou gavest birth in the flesh unto One of the Trinity, Christ the Giver of life, unto the salvation of our souls.

*On Wednesday evening, Theotokion:*
The eye of my heart do I cast up unto thee, O Theotokos: despise not my meager sighing in the hour when thy Son shall judge the world; be unto me a protection and a helper.

*On Thursday at Matins, Theotokion:*
Thou art the relief of those in sorrow, the healing of the sick, O all-hymned Theotokos; save the city and the people, O pacification of those who are assailed, calm of the storm-tossed, sole protectress of the faithful.

*On Friday evening, Theotokion:*
By the prayers of her that bare Thee, O Christ, of Thy martyrs and apostles, and prophets, and hierarchs, venerable and righteous ones, and of all the saints: give rest unto Thy departed servants.[^224]

*On Saturday at Matins, Theotokion:*
We have come to know God Who was incarnate of thee, O Virgin Theotokos: entreat Him for the salvation of our souls.

*Tone 7:*
*On Sunday evening, Theotokion:*
"Rejoice!" we cry unto thee with the angel, O Bride of God, calling thee bridal chamber and door, and fiery throne, and unhewn mountain, and unburnt bush.

*On Monday at Matins, Theotokion:*
Bring peace unto our life by the prayers of the Theotokos, as we cry unto Thee: O Merciful Lord, glory to Thee!

*On Monday evening, Theotokion:*
Rejoice, cloud of the noetic and ineffable Sun, O Sovereign Lady! Rejoice, all-radiant candle! Rejoice, all-golden lampstand! Through thee, O all-pure one, Eve was delivered from the curse. But as having boldness toward thy well-pleased Son and God, fail not to pray with thy maternal prayer, O all-pure one...

*On Tuesday at Matins, Theotokion:*
A Light, O Christ, hast Thou blossomed forth from the Virgin, and hast enlightened the human race: O Lord, glory to Thee!

*On Wednesday evening, Theotokion:*
Let us hymn the one all-pure Virgin after childbirth as Mother of God the Word, saying: Glory to Thee!

*On Thursday at Matins, Theotokion:*
Thou wast ineffably born of the Virgin, O Christ, and hast enlightened those who were in darkness, who cry: O Lord, glory to Thee!

*On Friday evening, Theotokion:*
O thou who alone didst receive the Incomprehensible, and didst give birth to God the Word the Word incarnate: pray that our souls may be saved.

*On Saturday at Matins, Theotokion:*
Pray, O Virgin, with the apostles and martyrs, that those who have departed may find great mercy at the judgment.[^225]

*Tone 8:*
*On Sunday evening, Theotokion:*
Having received the voice of the Archangel Gabriel, let us say: Rejoice, O Mother of God, who gavest birth to Christ the Giver of life unto the world!

*On Monday at Matins, Theotokion:*
Heaven sings thy praises,[^226] O Mother full of grace, who knewest not wedlock, and we glorify thine unsearchable childbirth, O Theotokos: pray that our souls may be saved.

*On Monday evening, Theotokion:*
Rejoice, boast of the universe! Rejoice, temple of the Lord! Rejoice, overshadowed mountain! Rejoice, refuge of all! Rejoice, golden lampstand! Rejoice, venerable glory of the Orthodox! Rejoice, Mary, Mother of Christ God! Rejoice, paradise! Rejoice, divine table! Rejoice, tabernacle! Rejoice, all-golden jar! Rejoice, hope of all!

*On Tuesday at Matins, Theotokion:*
Thy protection, O Virgin Theotokos, is a spiritual medicine; for fleeing beneath it, we are delivered from spiritual infirmities.

*On Wednesday evening, Theotokion:*
I flee unto thy protection, O holy Virgin Theotokos; I know that through thee I shall find salvation, for thou art able, O pure one, to help me.

*On Thursday at Matins, Theotokion:*
Snatch me, O Sovereign Lady, from the hand of the manslaying serpent, who desires by cunning to swallow me up utterly; crush his jaws, I pray thee, and destroy his snares, that having escaped from his claws, I may magnify thy defense.

*On Friday evening, Theotokion:*
O pure Virgin, gate of the Word, Mother of our God: pray that we may be saved.

*On Saturday at Matins, Theotokion:*
O Sovereign Lady, receive the prayers of thy servants, and deliver us from every necessity and sorrow.

*Dismissal Resurrectional Theotokia of the Eight Tones.*[^227]

*Tone 1. Theotokion:*
When Gabriel said unto thee: "Rejoice!", O Virgin, with the voice the Master of all became incarnate in thee, the holy ark, as the righteous David said. Thou wast shown to be more spacious than the heavens, having borne thy Creator. Glory to Him Who dwelt in thee! Glory to Him Who came forth from thee! Glory to Him Who has freed us through thy childbirth!

*Tone 2. Theotokion:*
All beyond understanding, all most glorious are thy mysteries, O Theotokos! Sealed in purity and preserved in virginity, thou wast known as a true Mother, having given birth unto the true God. Entreat Him that our souls may be saved.

*Tone 3. Theotokion:*
Thee, who didst mediate the salvation of our race, do we hymn, O Virgin Theotokos; for in the flesh assumed from thee, thy Son and our God, having accepted the Passion through the Cross, delivered us from corruption, as the Lover of mankind.

*Tone 4. Theotokion:*
The mystery hidden from the ages and unknown unto the angels, through thee, O Theotokos, was revealed unto those upon earth: God becoming incarnate in unconfused union, and willingly accepting the Cross for our sake, whereby having resurrected the first-fashioned, He saved our souls from death.

*Tone 5. Theotokion:*
Rejoice, impassable gate of the Lord! Rejoice, wall and protection of those who flee unto thee! Rejoice, stormless haven and unwedded one, who gavest birth in the flesh to thy Creator and God: cease not praying for those who hymn and worship thy childbirth!

*Tone 6. Theotokion:*
Calling Thy Mother blessed, Thou camest to the Passion by Thy will, shining forth upon the Cross, desiring to seek out Adam, saying unto the angels: "Rejoice with Me, for the lost drachma is found!" O our God, Who hast wisely ordered all things, glory to Thee!

*Tone 7. Theotokion:*
As the treasury of our resurrection, O all-hymned one, lead up from the pit and the abyss of transgressions those who hope in thee for thou hast saved those guilty of sin, having given birth to our Salvation: who before childbirth wast a Virgin, and in childbirth a Virgin, and after childbirth still remainest a Virgin.

*Tone 8. Theotokion:*
O Good One, Who for our sake wast born of a Virgin, and didst endure crucifixion, Who by death didst overthrow death, and manifested the resurrection as God: despise not those whom Thou hast fashioned with Thine own hand. Show Thy love for mankind, O Merciful One. Accept the Theotokos who gave Thee birth, who prays for us, and save, O our Savior, a despairing people.

*Dismissal Theotokia, after the troparia of the Saints, sung throughout the whole year, at Vespers, and at "God is the Lord" at Matins, and again at the end of Matins.*[^228]

*Tone 1:*
*On Sunday evening, and on Monday at "God is the Lord":*
Wonder of wonders, O full of grace! Seeing thee, creation rejoices; for thou didst conceive without seed, and gavest birth ineffably unto Him Whom the ranks of angels cannot behold: Him do thou entreat for our souls.

*At the end of Matins:*
All-pure Theotokos, blessed in heaven and glorified upon earth: rejoice, O unwedded Bride!

*On Monday evening, and on Tuesday at "God is the Lord":*
Thou who without being consumed didst conceive the divine fire, and gavest birth without seed unto the Lord, the Fountain of life: O Theotokos full of grace, save those who magnify thee.

*At the end of Matins:*
Him Who without a mother is in heaven beyond understanding and hearing, thou gavest birth to upon earth without a father: Him do thou entreat, O Theotokos, for our souls.

*On Tuesday evening, and on Wednesday at "God is the Lord":*
Having thy protection, O all-pure one, and being delivered from tribulations by thy prayers, preserved everywhere by the Cross of thy Son, as is meet, we all piously magnify thee.

*At the end of Matins:*
All-pure Theotokos, blessed in heaven...

*On Wednesday evening, and on Thursday at "God is the Lord":*
Thou who without being consumed didst conceive the divine fire...

*At the end of Matins:*
Him Who without a mother is in heaven Him Who without a mother is in heaven.

*On Thursday evening, and on Friday at "God is the Lord":*
Having thy protection...

*At the end of Matins:*
All-pure Theotokos...

*On Friday evening, and on Saturday at "God is the Lord":*
When Gabriel said unto thee: "Rejoice!", O Virgin, with the voice the Master of all became incarnate in thee, the holy ark, as the righteous David said. Thou wast shown to be more spacious than the heavens, having borne thy Creator. Glory to Him Who dwelt in thee! Glory to Him Who came forth from thee! Glory to Him Who has freed us through thy childbirth!

*At the end of Matins:*
Him Who without a mother is in heaven...

*Tone 2:*
*On Sunday evening, and on Monday at "God is the Lord":*
Being a fountain of tender compassion, O Mother, vouchsafe us [thy mercy], O Theotokos: look upon a sinning people, show as always thy power; for hoping in thee, we cry unto thee "Rejoice!", as of old Gabriel, commander of the bodiless hosts.

*At the end of Matins:*
Holy Mother of the ineffable Light, honoring thee with angelic hymns, we piously magnify thee.

*On Monday evening, and on Tuesday at "God is the Lord":*
We have become partakers of the divine nature through thee, O Theotokos, Ever-Virgin; for thou didst give birth for us unto God become incarnate. Therefore, as is meet, we all piously magnify thee.

*At the end of Matins:*
Thee do we magnify, O Theotokos, crying: Rejoice, cloud of the unwaning Light, who didst bear in thy womb the Lord of glory!

*On Tuesday evening, and on Wednesday at "God is the Lord":*
Thou art most glorious, O Virgin Theotokos, we hymn thee; for by the Cross of thy Son Hades was cast down, and death was put to death; we who were dead are risen, and have been deemed worthy of life, receiving paradise, the ancient enjoyment. Therefore, giving thanks, we glorify Christ our God as mighty and alone abundantly merciful.

*At the end of Matins:*
Thee do we magnify, O Theotokos, crying: Rejoice, rod from which without seed God sprouted forth, destroying death upon the Tree!

*On Wednesday evening, and on Thursday at "God is the Lord":*
We have become partakers of the divine nature...

*At the end of Matins:*
Thee do we magnify, O Theotokos, crying Thee do we magnify, O Theotokos, crying:

*On Thursday evening, and on Friday at "God is the Lord":*
Thou art most glorious, O Virgin Theotokos...

*At the end of Matins:*
Thee do we magnify, O Theotokos, crying: Rejoice, rod...

*On Friday evening, and on Saturday at "God is the Lord":*
All beyond understanding, all most glorious are thy mysteries, O Theotokos! Sealed in purity and preserved in virginity, thou wast known as a true Mother, having given birth unto the true God. Entreat Him that our souls may be saved.

*At the end of Matins:*
Holy Mother of the ineffable Light...

*If Alleluia occurs in any Tone, these are said:*[^229]
*Troparion:*
Apostles, martyrs, and prophets, hierarchs, venerable ones, and righteous, who have accomplished the good fight and kept the faith, having boldness before the Savior: entreat Him, as He is good, on our behalf, that our souls may be saved, we pray.
*Another Troparion, for the Departed:*
Remember, O Lord, as Thou art good, the souls of Thy servants, and forgive whatever they have sinned in life; for none is sinless save Thee, Who art able to grant rest unto the departed.
*Theotokion:*
Holy Mother of the ineffable Light...

*Tone 3:*
*On Sunday evening, and on Monday at "God is the Lord":*
Marveling at the beauty of thy virginity and thy most radiant purity, Gabriel cried aloud unto thee, O Theotokos: "What worthy praise shall I offer thee? What shall I call thee? I am in perplexity and I am amazed! Therefore, as I was commanded, I cry unto thee: Rejoice, O full of grace!"

*At the end of Matins:*
Whithersoever one is saved, thither does he rightly flee. And what other such refuge is there as thou, O Theotokos, who shelterest our souls?

*On Monday evening, and on Tuesday at "God is the Lord":*
Our refuge and strength, O Theotokos, mighty help of the world, by thy prayers shelter thy servants from every necessity, O only blessed one.

*At the end of Matins:*
Whithersoever one is saved...

*On Tuesday evening, and on Wednesday at "God is the Lord":*
Having acquired the rod of strength, the Cross of thy Son, O Theotokos, thereby do we cast down the insolence of the enemies, unceasingly magnifying thee with love.

*At the end of Matins:*
Our refuge and strength Our refuge and strength.

*On Wednesday evening, and on Thursday at "God is the Lord":*
The prophets proclaimed, the apostles taught, the martyrs confessed, and we have believed that thou art truly the Theotokos: therefore do we magnify thine ineffable childbirth.

*At the end of Matins:*
Whithersoever one is saved...

*On Thursday evening, and on Friday at "God is the Lord":*
Having acquired the rod of strength...

*At the end of Matins:*
Our refuge and strength, O Theotokos...

*On Friday evening, and on Saturday at "God is the Lord":*
Thee, who didst mediate the salvation of our race, do we hymn, O Virgin Theotokos; for in the flesh assumed from thee, thy Son and our God, having accepted the Passion through the Cross, delivered us from corruption, as the Lover of mankind.

*At the end of Matins:*
The prophets proclaimed...

*Tone 4:*
*On Sunday evening, and on Monday at "God is the Lord":*
Unto her who was nurtured in the temple in the Holy of Holies, clothed with faith and wisdom, and immeasurable virginity, the archangel Gabriel brought the salutation from heaven, and the "Rejoice!": Rejoice, blessed one! Rejoice, most glorified one, the Lord is with thee!

*At the end of Matins:*
As thou art higher than all creations, being in perplexity how worthily to hymn thee, O Theotokos, therefore we pray thee: have mercy on us.

*On Monday evening, and on Tuesday at "God is the Lord":*
Unto the Theotokos let us now earnestly flee, we sinners and humble ones, and fall down, in repentance crying from the depth of the soul: O Sovereign Lady, help, having compassion on us! Make haste, we are perishing from the multitude of our transgressions! Turn not thy servants empty away, for thee alone have we as hope.

*At the end of Matins:*
Thee do we magnify, O Theotokos, crying: Thou art the bush, wherein Moses beheld unconsumed the flame, the fire of the Godhead!

*On Tuesday evening, and on Wednesday at "God is the Lord":*
O all-immaculate Virgin, Mother of Christ God, a sword pierced through thy most holy soul when thou didst behold thy Son and God willingly crucified: entreat Him unceasingly, O blessed one, to grant us forgiveness of transgressions.

*At the end of Matins:*
Thee do we magnify, O Theotokos, crying Thee do we magnify, O Theotokos, crying:
Thou art the mountain, from which a stone was cut without hands, and crushed the gates of Hades.

*On Wednesday evening, and on Thursday at "God is the Lord":*
The Word of the Father, Christ our God, Who became incarnate of thee, have we known, O Virgin Theotokos, only pure, only blessed one: therefore unceasingly hymning thee, we magnify thee.

*At the end of Matins:*
As thou art higher than all creations...

*On Thursday evening, and on Friday at "God is the Lord":*
O all-immaculate Virgin, Mother...

*At the end of Matins:*
Thee do we magnify, O Theotokos, crying: Thou art the mountain...

*On Friday evening, and on Saturday at "God is the Lord":*
The mystery hidden from the ages and unknown unto the angels, through thee, O Theotokos, was revealed unto those upon earth: God becoming incarnate in unconfused union, and willingly accepting the Cross for our sake, whereby having resurrected the first-fashioned, He saved our souls from death.

*At the end of Matins:*
Thee do we magnify, O Theotokos, crying: Thou art the bush...

*Tone 5:*
*On Sunday evening, and on Monday at "God is the Lord":*
With the angels things heavenly, and with men things earthly, with the voice of rejoicing we cry unto thee, O Theotokos: Rejoice, gate more spacious than the heavens! Rejoice, only salvation of the earthborn! Rejoice, pure one full of grace, who gavest birth to God incarnate!

*At the end of Matins:*
O Christ God, Who didst shine forth unto the world from a Virgin, showing [us] to be sons of light through her: have mercy on us.

*On Monday evening, and on Tuesday at "God is the Lord":*
Show thy speedy protection, and help, and mercy upon thy servants; and calm the waves of vain thoughts, O pure one, and raise up my fallen soul, O Theotokos; for I know, yea, I know, O Virgin, that thou canst do whatsoever thou desirest.

*At the end of Matins:*
Most holy Mother of God, wall of Christians, deliver thy people as is thy wont, who earnestly cry unto thee: oppose shameful and proud thoughts, that we may cry unto thee: Rejoice, Ever-Virgin!

*On Tuesday evening, and on Wednesday at "God is the Lord":*
By the Cross of thy Son By the Cross of thy Son, O Mary full of divine grace, all the deceit of idols has been abolished, and the power of demons trampled down: therefore, as is meet, we the faithful ever hymn and bless thee, and confessing thee truly as Theotokos, we magnify thee.

*At the end of Matins:*
O Christ God, Who didst shine forth unto the world from a Virgin...

*On Wednesday evening, and on Thursday at "God is the Lord":*
Strange and saving unto the world was the Virgin's mystery shown forth; for without seed wast Thou born of her, and in the flesh wast Thou shown without corruption, O Joy of all: Lord, glory to Thee!

*At the end of Matins:*
Most holy Mother of God, wall of Christians...

*On Thursday evening, and on Friday at "God is the Lord":*
By the Cross of thy Son...

*At the end of Matins:*
O Christ God, Who didst shine forth unto the world from a Virgin...

*On Friday evening, and on Saturday at "God is the Lord":*
Rejoice, impassable gate of the Lord! Rejoice, wall and protection of those who flee unto thee! Rejoice, stormless haven and unwedded one, who gavest birth in the flesh to thy Creator and God: cease not praying for those who hymn and worship thy childbirth!

*At the end of Matins:*
O Christ God, Who didst shine forth unto the world from a Virgin...

*Tone 6:*
*On Sunday evening, and on Monday at "God is the Lord":*
The beginning of salvation was Gabriel's greeting unto the Virgin; for she heard the "Rejoice!", and shrank not back from the salutation. She doubted not as Sarah in the tent, but thus she said: "Behold the handmaid of the Lord, be it unto me according to thy word."

*At the end of Matins:*
Thou didst receive the archangelic word, and wast shown forth as the cherubic throne, and in thine arms didst bear, O Theotokos, the Hope of our souls.

*On Monday evening, and on Tuesday at "God is the Lord":*
O good Virgin Theotokos, Hope of the world, we ask thy sole and sovereign intercession: have compassion upon a people bereft of help, entreat the merciful God, that our souls may be delivered from every menace, O only blessed one.

*At the end of Matins:*
None that flees unto thee departs from thee ashamed, O all-pure Virgin Theotokos: but asks for grace, and receives a gift profitable unto the request receives a gift profitable unto the request.

*On Tuesday evening, and on Wednesday at "God is the Lord":*
O Virgin Theotokos, entreat thy Son, Who was willingly nailed to the Cross, and freed the world from deceit, Christ our God, to have mercy on our souls.

*At the end of Matins:*
The Son and Word of God, begotten of the Father before the ages without a mother, in these latter times hast thou borne, incarnate of thy pure blood, O Parent of God without a husband: Him do thou entreat, that forgiveness of sins may be granted us before the end.

*On Wednesday evening, and on Thursday at "God is the Lord":*
Holy Sovereign Lady, pure Mother of our God, who ineffably didst give birth unto the Creator of all: entreat His goodness ever with the sacred apostles, to deliver us from passions, and to grant us forgiveness of sins.

*At the end of Matins:*
Of what great gifts, O pure Virgin Mother of God, wast thou deemed worthy! For thou didst give birth in the flesh unto One of the Trinity, Christ the Giver of life, unto the salvation of our souls.

*On Thursday evening, and on Friday at "God is the Lord":*
O Virgin Theotokos, entreat thy Son...

*At the end of Matins:*
The Son and Word of God, begotten of the Father before the ages without a mother...

*On Friday evening, and on Saturday at "God is the Lord":*
